Then (*ZY*)⁻¹ρ is a rotation that fixes **e**₃, hence a rotation about the third axis, and ρ = *ZY*(*ZY*)⁻¹ρ. (ii) This is shown in the proof of Lemma 5.2. (iii) For every matrix with Λᵀ*η*Λ = *η*, the determinant is 1 or −1, and the component 00 of the equation gives (Λ⁰₀)² = 1 + Σ_j (Λ^j₀)² ≥ 1. The determinant and Λ⁰₀ are continuous functions of the matrix, so along a continuous path of such matrices the determinant cannot pass from 1 to −1 nor Λ⁰₀ from the values ≥ 1 to the values ≤ −1: a path that begins at the identity stays in 𝒟₊. Conversely, by (i) and (ii) each Λ in 𝒟₊ is a product of rotations about coordinate axes by angles *θ*₁, …, *θ*_n and one boost with a velocity *v*; replacing the angles by *τθ*₁, …, *τθ*_n and the velocity by *τv* gives, for 0 ≤ *τ* ≤ 1, a continuous path of matrices with Λᵀ*η*Λ = *η* from the identity to Λ. □

**Theorem 6.36 (The Euclidean Klein pair of a spatial slice).** (i) The elements of ISO⁺(1,3) that leave the time *t* of every event unchanged are the maps (*t*, **x**) → (*t*, ρ**x** + **a**) with ρ a rotation matrix, ρᵀρ = 1 and det ρ = 1, and **a** a vector of ℝ³. (ii) They form a group, ISO(3), which acts on each slice *t* = constant, carries every point of the slice to every other, and preserves the distance |**x** − **y**|, which is the interval of two events of the slice. The elements fixing the point **x** = 0 are the rotations. Hence (ISO(3), SO(3)) is a Klein pair with space ℝ³.

*Proof.* (i) An element of ISO⁺(1,3) is *x* → Λ*x* + *b* with Λᵀ*η*Λ = *η*, Λ⁰₀ ≥ 1 and det Λ = 1 (Lemma 6.26 and Lemma 6.35). The time is unchanged for every event exactly when *b*⁰ = 0, Λ⁰₀ = 1 and Λ⁰_j = 0. The component 00 of Λᵀ*η*Λ = *η* reads −(Λ⁰₀)² + Σ_j (Λ^j₀)² = −1, so Λ^j₀ = 0. Then Λ consists of the entry 1 and a 3 × 3 block ρ, the spatial components of Λᵀ*η*Λ = *η* read ρᵀρ = 1, and det Λ = det ρ = 1. Conversely such a Λ satisfies the three conditions. (ii) The composition of **x** → ρ**x** + **a** after **x** → ρ′**x** + **a**′ is **x** → ρρ′**x** + ρ**a**′ + **a**, and the inverse is **x** → ρ⁻¹**x** − ρ⁻¹**a**; both are of the same form. The translation by **y** − **x** carries **x** to **y**. Since ρᵀρ = 1, |ρ**x** − ρ**y**| = |**x** − **y**|, and for two events with the same *t* the interval is −*c*²·0 + |**x** − **y**|². The map fixes **x** = 0 exactly when **a** = 0. □

**Theorem 6.37 (The Galilean Klein pair as the limit of large *c*).** (i) For a rotation ρ, vectors **u**, **a** of ℝ³ and a real number *s*, let Γ(*s*, **a**, **u**, ρ) be the map (*t*, **x**) → (*t* + *s*, ρ**x** + **u***t* + **a**) of the set ℝ⁴ of events. These maps form a group, the Galilean group. It carries every event to every other, and the elements fixing the event (0, 0) are the maps (*t*, **x**) → (*t*, ρ**x** + **u***t*), which form the group ℝ³ ⋊ SO(3) of the pairs (**u**, ρ). Hence the Galilean group with this subgroup is a Klein pair with space ℝ⁴. (ii) Every Galilean map preserves the difference of the times of two events, and the distance |**x** − **y**| of two events with the same time. (iii) Let **u** have the length *w* and the direction of the first axis, and let *B*_c be the transformation of Theorem 8.1.7 with the velocity −*w*: *t*′ = *γ*(*t* + *wx*₁/*c*²), *x*₁′ = *γ*(*x*₁ + *wt*), *x*₂′ = *x*₂, *x*₃′ = *x*₃, with *γ* = (1 − *w*²/*c*²)^(−1/2). For *c* ≥ 2*w*, |*t*′ − *t*| ≤ (*w*²/*c*²)|*t*| + 2*w*|*x*₁|/*c*² and |*x*₁′ − *x*₁ − *wt*| ≤ (*w*²/*c*²)(|*x*₁| + *w*|*t*|). Hence for each fixed event, as *c* → ∞ with *w* fixed, *B*_c(*t*, **x**) tends to (*t*, **x** + **u***t*). (iv) For every Galilean map Γ there are elements *P*_c of ISO⁺(1,3), defined for all *c* > |**u**|, such that *P*_c(*t*, **x**) tends to Γ(*t*, **x**) for every event as *c* → ∞.

*Proof.* (i) The composition of Γ(*s*, **a**, **u**, ρ) after Γ(*s*′, **a**′, **u**′, ρ′) sends (*t*, **x**) to (*t* + *s*′ + *s*, ρρ′**x** + (ρ**u**′ + **u**)*t* + ρ**a**′ + **u***s*′ + **a**), which is Γ(*s* + *s*′, ρ**a**′ + **u***s*′ + **a**, ρ**u**′ + **u**, ρρ′). Solving *t*′ = *t* + *s*, **x**′ = ρ**x** + **u***t* + **a** for (*t*, **x**) gives the inverse Γ(−*s*, ρ⁻¹(**u***s* − **a**), −ρ⁻¹**u**, ρ⁻¹). The map Γ(*s*, **a**, 0, 1) carries (0, 0) to (*s*, **a**), so with inverses every event is carried to every other. Γ fixes (0, 0) exactly when *s* = 0 and **a** = 0, and by the law of composition these elements multiply as (**u**, ρ)(**u**′, ρ′) = (ρ**u**′ + **u**, ρρ′). (ii) The times of two events both increase by *s*. For two events with the same *t* the images differ by ρ**x** − ρ**y**, of length |**x** − **y**|. (iii) From *γ*² − 1 = *γ*²*w*²/*c*², *γ* − 1 = *γ*²(*w*²/*c*²)/(*γ* + 1). For *c* ≥ 2*w*, *γ*² ≤ 4/3, so *γ* ≤ 2 and *γ* − 1 ≤ *w*²/*c*². Then *t*′ − *t* = (*γ* − 1)*t* + *γwx*₁/*c*² and *x*₁′ − *x*₁ − *wt* = (*γ* − 1)(*x*₁ + *wt*) give the two bounds, and both bounds tend to 0 as *c* → ∞. (iv) Let σ be a rotation that turns **u** into the direction of the first axis, and let *w* = |**u**|. By Theorem 6.36 the rotations and the translations in space and time belong to ISO⁺(1,3), and so does *B*_c for *c* > *w* (Theorem 8.1.7). Put *P*_c = *T* σ⁻¹ *B*_c σ ρ, where ρ and σ act on **x** alone and *T* is the translation by (*s*, **a**). By (iii) and the continuity of σ⁻¹ and *T*, *P*_c(*t*, **x**) tends to the image under *T* of the event with the time *t* and the place σ⁻¹ applied to σρ**x** + *wt***e**₁, where **e**₁ is the unit vector of the first axis. Since σ⁻¹ carries *w***e**₁ to **u**, that place is ρ**x** + **u***t*, and the image under *T* is (*t* + *s*, ρ**x** + **u***t* + **a**). □

**The McGucken Principle *dx*₄/*dt* = i*c* reading of Theorem 6.37.** The rate *c* of the expansion of *x*₄ is finite, and the group of nature is that of Theorem 6.28. The Galilean group is what that group becomes for motions slow compared with *c*: by part (iii) the difference between *B*_c and the Galilean map is of the order *w*²/*c*² and *wx*₁/*c*². Its two invariants of part (ii) are what remains of the one interval: the interval divided by −*c*² tends to the square of the difference of the times, and for equal times the interval is the square of the distance.

**Theorem 6.38 (The spin Klein pair: SL(2, ℂ) covers SO⁺(1,3) twice).** Let σ₁, σ₂, σ₃ be the Pauli matrices and, for an event *x*, let *M*(*x*) = *x*⁰ + *x*¹σ₁ + *x*²σ₂ + *x*³σ₃, a Hermitian 2 × 2 matrix; every Hermitian 2 × 2 matrix is *M*(*x*) for exactly one *x*, and det *M*(*x*) = −*η*(*x*, *x*). Let SL(2, ℂ) be the group of complex 2 × 2 matrices *A* with det *A* = 1. (i) For *A* in SL(2, ℂ) there is exactly one real 4 × 4 matrix Λ(*A*) with *AM*(*x*)*A*† = *M*(Λ(*A*)*x*) for all *x*; it belongs to SO⁺(1,3), and Λ(*AB*) = Λ(*A*)Λ(*B*). (ii) Λ(*A*) = 1 exactly when *A* = 1 or *A* = −1. (iii) For a unit vector **n** and a real *θ*, the matrix *A* = cos(*θ*/2) − i sin(*θ*/2)(*n*₁σ₁ + *n*₂σ₂ + *n*₃σ₃) has Λ(*A*) equal to the rotation by the angle *θ* about **n**, and the diagonal matrix *A* with the entries exp(*s*/2), exp(−*s*/2) has Λ(*A*) equal to the boost *x*⁰ → *x*⁰ cosh *s* + *x*³ sinh *s*, *x*³ → *x*⁰ sinh *s* + *x*³ cosh *s*. (iv) Every element of SO⁺(1,3) is Λ(*A*) for exactly two matrices, *A* and −*A*. (v) The pairs (*a*, *A*), with *a* in ℝ⁴ and *A* in SL(2, ℂ), acting on spacetime by *x* → Λ(*A*)*x* + *a*, form a group *G* with the law (*a*, *A*)(*b*, *B*) = (*a* + Λ(*A*)*b*, *AB*). *G* carries every event to every other, and the elements fixing *x* = 0 are the (0, *A*). Hence (*G*, SL(2, ℂ)) is a Klein pair with space the spacetime of Theorem 6.28, and (*a*, *A*) → (*a*, Λ(*A*)) maps *G* onto ISO⁺(1,3), two elements to one.

*Proof.* The matrices 1, σ₁, σ₂, σ₃ are a basis of the real vector space of Hermitian 2 × 2 matrices, and *M*(*x*) has the rows (*x*⁰ + *x*³, *x*¹ − i*x*²) and (*x*¹ + i*x*², *x*⁰ − *x*³), with determinant (*x*⁰)² − (*x*¹)² − (*x*²)² − (*x*³)². (i) *AM*(*x*)*A*† is Hermitian and depends linearly on *x*, so it is *M*(Λ*x*) for one real matrix Λ = Λ(*A*). Its determinant is |det *A*|² det *M*(*x*) = det *M*(*x*), so *η*(Λ*x*, Λ*x*) = *η*(*x*, *x*) for all *x*, and by the identity 4*η*(*x*, *y*) = *η*(*x* + *y*, *x* + *y*) − *η*(*x* − *y*, *x* − *y*), Λᵀ*η*Λ = *η*. Since (*AB*)*M*(*AB*)† = *A*(*BMB*†)*A*†, Λ(*AB*) = Λ(*A*)Λ(*B*). The component 0 of *x* is half the trace of *M*(*x*), so Λ(*A*)⁰₀ is half the trace of *AA*†, which is positive; with Λᵀ*η*Λ = *η* this gives Λ⁰₀ ≥ 1. For the determinant three facts about 2 × 2 matrices are used; each rests on part (iii), whose proof below does not use the determinant. First, a Hermitian matrix is *α* + *rN* with *α* real, *r* ≥ 0 and *N* = *n*₁σ₁ + *n*₂σ₂ + *n*₃σ₃ for a unit vector **n**, because 1, σ₁, σ₂, σ₃ are a basis. A rotation about an axis perpendicular to **n** and to the third axis carries **n** to the unit vector of the third axis; by (iii) the corresponding matrix *C* satisfies *CNC*† = σ₃, and *CC*† = 1 because *C* = cos(*θ*/2) − i sin(*θ*/2)*N*′ with (*N*′)² = 1. So *C*(*α* + *rN*)*C*† is diagonal with the entries *α* + *r*, *α* − *r*. If the Hermitian matrix is positive, both entries are positive, and *C*† times the diagonal matrix of their square roots times *C* is a positive Hermitian matrix whose square is the given one. Second, a unitary matrix *V* with det *V* = 1 has the rows (*u*, −*v*∗) and (*v*, *u*∗) with |*u*|² + |*v*|² = 1, because its inverse is both *V*† and, the determinant being 1, the matrix with the rows (*V*₂₂, −*V*₁₂) and (−*V*₂₁, *V*₁₁). With *u* = *a* − i*s*₃ and *v* = *s*₂ − i*s*₁, *a* and the *s*_j real, this is *V* = *a* − i(*s*₁σ₁ + *s*₂σ₂ + *s*₃σ₃) with *a*² + *s*₁² + *s*₂² + *s*₃² = 1, that is, *V* = cos(*θ*/2) − i sin(*θ*/2)*N* for some *θ* and unit vector. Third, since *N*² = 1, (cos *a* − i sin *a* *N*)(cos *b* − i sin *b* *N*) = cos(*a* + *b*) − i sin(*a* + *b*)*N*, and det(cos *a* − i sin *a* *N*) = cos²*a* + sin²*a* = 1; so *V* is the square of the matrix cos(*θ*/4) − i sin(*θ*/4)*N* of SL(2, ℂ). Now let *A* be in SL(2, ℂ). *A*†*A* is Hermitian, and it is positive because the product of a column *z* ≠ 0 with *A*†*Az* is the squared length of *Az*, which is not zero. Let *Q* be its positive Hermitian square root and *V* = *AQ*⁻¹; then *V*†*V* = *Q*⁻¹*A*†*AQ*⁻¹ = 1. Since det *Q* > 0, |det *V*| = 1 and det *V* det *Q* = 1, both determinants are 1. *Q* then has the diagonal form with entries *d*, 1/*d*, and it is the square of *C*† times the diagonal matrix with the entries *d*^(1/2), *d*^(−1/2) times *C*, a matrix of SL(2, ℂ). So *A* = *VQ* is a product of two squares of elements of SL(2, ℂ), and det Λ(*A*), a product of two squares of numbers ±1, is 1.

(ii) If *AM*(*x*)*A*† = *M*(*x*) for all *x*, then *x* = (1, 0, 0, 0) gives *AA*† = 1, hence *AM* = *MA* for every Hermitian *M*, and for every complex 2 × 2 matrix, since each is *M*₁ + i*M*₂ with *M*₁, *M*₂ Hermitian. A matrix commuting with all matrices is a multiple *λ* of the identity, and det *A* = *λ*² = 1. (iii) Write *N* = *n*₁σ₁ + *n*₂σ₂ + *n*₃σ₃ and, for a vector **x**, *S* = *x*¹σ₁ + *x*²σ₂ + *x*³σ₃. From σ_jσ_k = δ_jk + i Σ_l ε_jkl σ_l follow *N*² = 1, *NS* − *SN* = 2i times the matrix of **n** × **x**, and *NSN* = 2(**n**·**x**)*N* − *S*. With *A* = cos(*θ*/2) − i sin(*θ*/2)*N* and *A*† = cos(*θ*/2) + i sin(*θ*/2)*N*, *ASA*† = cos²(*θ*/2)*S* + sin²(*θ*/2)*NSN* − i sin(*θ*/2) cos(*θ*/2)(*NS* − *SN*), which is the matrix of the vector cos*θ* **x** + (1 − cos*θ*)(**n**·**x**)**n** + sin*θ* **n** × **x**; and *A*·1·*A*† = 1, so *x*⁰ is unchanged. For **n** along the third axis this vector is (*x*¹cos*θ* − *x*²sin*θ*, *x*¹sin*θ* + *x*²cos*θ*, *x*³), the rotation by *θ*; for a general **n** the vector is unchanged if **x** is along **n** and is turned by *θ* in the plane perpendicular to **n**. For the diagonal matrix, *AM*(*x*)*A*† multiplies *x*⁰ + *x*³ by exp(*s*), *x*⁰ − *x*³ by exp(−*s*) and leaves *x*¹ ± i*x*² unchanged, which is the stated boost.

(iv) By (iii) the image of Λ contains the rotations about the three axes. By Lemma 6.35(i) every rotation is a product of such. By (iii) the image contains the boosts along the third axis, with every value of tanh *s* between −1 and 1, and hence, conjugating by a rotation, the boosts along the first axis. By Lemma 6.35(ii) every element of SO⁺(1,3) is a product of a rotation, a boost along the first axis and a rotation. So Λ maps SL(2, ℂ) onto SO⁺(1,3), and by (i) and (ii) the matrices Λ(*A*) and Λ(*B*) are equal exactly when *B*⁻¹*A* is 1 or −1. (v) The composition of *x* → Λ(*A*)*x* + *a* after *x* → Λ(*B*)*x* + *b* is *x* → Λ(*AB*)*x* + Λ(*A*)*b* + *a*, by (i); the inverse of (*a*, *A*) is (−Λ(*A*⁻¹)*a*, *A*⁻¹). The element (*y* − *x*, 1) carries *x* to *y*, and (*a*, *A*) fixes 0 exactly when *a* = 0. The last statement is (iv). □

**The McGucken Principle *dx*₄/*dt* = i*c* reading of Theorem 6.38.** In the matrices of Theorem 8.5.3, *γ*¹*γ*² consists of two blocks −iσ₃, so the rotation *R*(*θ*) = cos(*θ*/2) + *γ*¹*γ*² sin(*θ*/2) of Theorem 8.3.7 consists of two blocks cos(*θ*/2) − i sin(*θ*/2)σ₃, the matrix *A* of part (iii) for the third axis. The two-to-one covering of part (iv) is the fact of Theorem 8.3.7 that a turn by 2π multiplies the amplitude by −1 and a turn by 4π restores it: *A* = −1 for *θ* = 2π, and Λ(−1) = 1. The matrix *M*(*x*) holds the interval as its determinant, so the group of the spin pair is fixed by the interval of §9.1, with the i of the Pauli matrix σ₂ the i of *x*₄ = i*ct*.

**Theorem 6.39 (The conformal Klein pair of the null cone).** Let ℝ⁶ consist of the triples *ξ* = (*y*, *κ*, *ν*) with *y* in ℝ⁴ and *κ*, *ν* real, with the symmetric form ⟨*ξ*, *ξ*′⟩ = *η*(*y*, *y*′) − ½(*κν*′ + *κ*′*ν*), so that ⟨*ξ*, *ξ*⟩ = *η*(*y*, *y*) − *κν*. Let O(2,4) be the group of the linear maps *g* of ℝ⁶ with ⟨*gξ*, *gξ*′⟩ = ⟨*ξ*, *ξ*′⟩, and let *X* be the set of null lines, the sets of the multiples of a *ξ* ≠ 0 with ⟨*ξ*, *ξ*⟩ = 0. For an event *x* put *j*(*x*) = (*x*, 1, *η*(*x*, *x*)). (i) The form has two negative and four positive squares. *j*(*x*) is null, the null lines with *κ* ≠ 0 are exactly the lines of the *j*(*x*), each for one *x* = *y*/*κ*, and ⟨*j*(*x*), *j*(*x*′)⟩ = −½*η*(*x* − *x*′, *x* − *x*′). Two events have interval zero exactly when their null lines are orthogonal. (ii) O(2,4) acts on *X* and preserves orthogonality of null lines. It contains maps that act on the events through *j* as the translations *x* → *x* + *a*, the Lorentz transformations *x* → Λ*x*, the dilations *x* → *λx* with *λ* > 0, and the inversion *x* → *x*/*η*(*x*, *x*), defined for *η*(*x*, *x*) ≠ 0. (iii) O(2,4) carries every null line to every other. With *H* the subgroup fixing the line of *j*(0), (O(2,4), *H*) is a Klein pair with space *X*; *H* contains the Lorentz transformations, the dilations and the maps obtained by performing the inversion, a translation and the inversion. (iv) For *g* in O(2,4) and an event *x*, write *gj*(*x*) = (*y*, *κ*, *ν*) and, when *κ* ≠ 0, *g*·*x* = *y*/*κ* and *κ*_g(*x*) = *κ*. Then for events *x*, *x*′ with *κ*_g(*x*), *κ*_g(*x*′) ≠ 0,

*η*(*g*·*x* − *g*·*x*′, *g*·*x* − *g*·*x*′) = *η*(*x* − *x*′, *x* − *x*′)/(*κ*_g(*x*) *κ*_g(*x*′)).

(v) Consequently *x* → *g*·*x* carries events with interval zero to events with interval zero, and its derivative Δ at *x* satisfies *η*(Δ*h*, Δ*h*) = *η*(*h*, *h*)/*κ*_g(*x*)² for every *h*: it multiplies the interval of neighbouring events by a positive factor. (vi) If *ψ* satisfies (□ − *μ*²)*ψ* = 0, then *ψ*(*x*/*λ*) satisfies the same equation with *μ*/*λ* in place of *μ*; the dilations preserve the equation exactly when *μ* = 0.

*Proof.* (i) −*κν* = ((*κ* − *ν*)/2)² − ((*κ* + *ν*)/2)², so with the one negative and three positive squares of *η* the form has two negative and four positive squares. ⟨*j*(*x*), *j*(*x*)⟩ = *η*(*x*, *x*) − *η*(*x*, *x*) = 0. A null *ξ* with *κ* ≠ 0 has *ν* = *η*(*y*, *y*)/*κ*, so *ξ* = *κ* *j*(*y*/*κ*), and *j*(*x*), *j*(*x*′) are proportional only for *x* = *x*′, their components *κ* both being 1. Finally ⟨*j*(*x*), *j*(*x*′)⟩ = *η*(*x*, *x*′) − ½(*η*(*x*′, *x*′) + *η*(*x*, *x*)) = −½*η*(*x* − *x*′, *x* − *x*′). (ii) A linear map preserving the form carries null vectors to null vectors, lines to lines and orthogonal vectors to orthogonal vectors. The following maps are linear and preserve ⟨*ξ*, *ξ*⟩, hence the form. Translation: (*y*, *κ*, *ν*) → (*y* + *κa*, *κ*, *ν* + 2*η*(*a*, *y*) + *κη*(*a*, *a*)), for which *η*(*y* + *κa*, *y* + *κa*) − *κ*(*ν* + 2*η*(*a*, *y*) + *κη*(*a*, *a*)) = *η*(*y*, *y*) − *κν*, and which sends *j*(*x*) to *j*(*x* + *a*). Lorentz transformation: (*y*, *κ*, *ν*) → (Λ*y*, *κ*, *ν*), which sends *j*(*x*) to *j*(Λ*x*). Dilation: (*y*, *κ*, *ν*) → (*y*, *κ*/*λ*, *λν*), which sends *j*(*x*) to (*x*, 1/*λ*, *λη*(*x*, *x*)) = (1/*λ*) *j*(*λx*). Inversion: (*y*, *κ*, *ν*) → (*y*, *ν*, *κ*), which sends *j*(*x*) to (*x*, *η*(*x*, *x*), 1) = *η*(*x*, *x*) *j*(*x*/*η*(*x*, *x*)) when *η*(*x*, *x*) ≠ 0, because *η*(*x*/*q*, *x*/*q*) = 1/*q* for *q* = *η*(*x*, *x*).

(iii) Let *ξ*, *ζ* be null vectors with ⟨*ξ*, *ζ*⟩ ≠ 0 and put *w* = *ξ* − *ζ*; then ⟨*w*, *w*⟩ = −2⟨*ξ*, *ζ*⟩ ≠ 0, and the reflection *r*(*θ*) = *θ* − 2⟨*w*, *θ*⟩*w*/⟨*w*, *w*⟩ is linear, preserves the form, and sends *ξ* to *ξ* − *w* = *ζ*, since ⟨*w*, *ξ*⟩ = −⟨*ζ*, *ξ*⟩ = ½⟨*w*, *w*⟩. Let *ζ* = (*z*, *κ*, *ν*) be any null vector other than 0. If *κ* ≠ 0 its line is that of *j*(*z*/*κ*). If *κ* = 0, then ⟨*j*(*x*), *ζ*⟩ = *η*(*x*, *z*) − ½*ν*, and this is not zero for a suitable *x*: for *x* = 0 if *ν* ≠ 0, and otherwise *z* ≠ 0 and *x* can be chosen with *η*(*x*, *z*) ≠ 0. The reflection just described then carries the line of *ζ* to the line of *j*(*x*). The translation by −*x* carries the line of *j*(*x*) to that of *j*(0). So every null line is carried to the line of *j*(0), and with inverses every null line to every other; by Definition 6.32 this is a Klein pair. The Lorentz transformations and the dilations send *j*(0) = (0, 1, 0) to a multiple of itself. The inversion exchanges the lines of (0, 1, 0) and (0, 0, 1), and a translation fixes (0, 0, 1); so inversion, translation, inversion fixes the line of *j*(0).

(iv) By the definitions *gj*(*x*) = *κ*_g(*x*) *j*(*g*·*x*), since a null vector with *κ* ≠ 0 is *κ* times *j*(*y*/*κ*). Hence, by (i) twice, −½*η*(*x* − *x*′, *x* − *x*′) = ⟨*j*(*x*), *j*(*x*′)⟩ = ⟨*gj*(*x*), *gj*(*x*′)⟩ = *κ*_g(*x*)*κ*_g(*x*′) ⟨*j*(*g*·*x*), *j*(*g*·*x*′)⟩ = −½*κ*_g(*x*)*κ*_g(*x*′) *η*(*g*·*x* − *g*·*x*′, *g*·*x* − *g*·*x*′). (v) The first statement is (iv). The components of *gj*(*x*) are polynomials in *x*, so *g*·*x* is a quotient of polynomials, differentiable where *κ*_g(*x*) ≠ 0. Put *x*′ = *x* + *εh* in (iv), divide by *ε*² and let *ε* → 0: the left side tends to *η*(Δ*h*, Δ*h*) and the right side to *η*(*h*, *h*)/*κ*_g(*x*)². (vi) Each derivative of *ψ*(*x*/*λ*) is 1/*λ* times the derivative of *ψ* at *x*/*λ*, so □ applied to *ψ*(*x*/*λ*) is *λ*⁻² times (□*ψ*)(*x*/*λ*) = *λ*⁻²*μ*²*ψ*(*x*/*λ*). The equation is the same for every *λ* exactly when *μ*²/*λ*² = *μ*² for every *λ* > 0, that is, when *μ* = 0. □

**The McGucken Principle *dx*₄/*dt* = i*c* reading of Theorem 6.39.** The null cone of an event *p* is the set of events with interval zero from *p*; its section at the time *t* is the McGucken Sphere of *p* of radius *c*|*t* − *t*_p| (Theorem 8.1.9). The invariant of this geometry is the relation of interval zero, that is, the family of all McGucken Spheres of all events, as the interval itself is the invariant of Theorem 6.28; part (iv) shows that O(2,4) preserves it and part (v) that the interval is multiplied by a positive factor. Part (vi) states the range of the pair: the Compton frequency *ω*_C = *mc*²/ℏ of a body of rest mass *m* fixes a scale, so the group of a massive body is that of Theorem 6.28, and the larger group belongs to the wavefront of the McGucken Sphere itself, the case *μ* = 0. The six-dimensional construction is that of Dirac [88]. That every map of a region of spacetime with the property of part (v) is one of the maps *x* → *g*·*x* is Liouville’s theorem, proved below as Theorem 6.41; Corollary 6.42 then determines the subgroup *H*.

**Lemma 6.40 (Linear maps that preserve the null cone).** Let *M* be a real 4 × 4 matrix with *η*(*Mh*, *Mh*) = 0 for every *h* with *η*(*h*, *h*) = 0. Then *M*ᵀ*ηM* = *bη* with a real number *b*, and *b* > 0 if *M* is invertible.

*Proof.* Let *B* = *M*ᵀ*ηM*, a symmetric matrix with *h*ᵀ*Bh* = 0 for every null *h*. Let **e**₀, …, **e**₃ be the unit vectors of the axes. The vectors **e**₀ + **e**_j and **e**₀ − **e**_j are null, which gives *B*₀₀ + *B*_jj + 2*B*_0j = 0 and *B*₀₀ + *B*_jj − 2*B*_0j = 0; hence *B*_0j = 0 and *B*_jj = −*B*₀₀. For *j* ≠ *k* the vector **e**₀ + (**e**_j + **e**_k)/2^(1/2) is null, which gives *B*₀₀ + ½(*B*_jj + *B*_kk) + *B*_jk = 0, hence *B*_jk = 0. So *B* = −*B*₀₀*η*. If *M* is invertible, *B* is invertible, so *b* ≠ 0, and by Sylvester’s law of inertia *B* has one negative and three positive squares, as *η* has; *bη* with *b* < 0 has three negative squares. □

**Theorem 6.41 (Liouville’s theorem for spacetime: O(2,4) is the largest group of the null cone).** Let *E* be a connected open region of spacetime and *f* a map of *E* into spacetime, four times continuously differentiable, whose derivative at every point is invertible and carries null vectors to null vectors. Then there is an element *g* of O(2,4) with *κ*_g(*x*) ≠ 0 on *E* and *f*(*x*) = *g*·*x* for all *x* in *E*, in the notation of Theorem 6.39(iv).

*Proof.* Write *f*_i = ∂_i*f*, *f*_ij = ∂_i∂_j*f*, and ⟨*u*, *v*⟩ = *η*(*u*, *v*). Step 1. By Lemma 6.40, ⟨*f*_i, *f*_j⟩ = *λ*²*η*_ij with a function *λ* > 0, and *λ*² = ⟨*f*₁, *f*₁⟩ is three times continuously differentiable. Put *σ* = ln *λ*, *σ*_i = ∂_i*σ*, *σ*_ij = ∂_i∂_j*σ*, *σ*^k = *η*^kl*σ*_l and *s* = *σ*^k*σ*_k. Differentiating ⟨*f*_i, *f*_j⟩ = *λ*²*η*_ij with respect to *x*^k gives ⟨*f*_ik, *f*_j⟩ + ⟨*f*_i, *f*_jk⟩ = 2*λ*²*σ*_k*η*_ij. Writing this for the three cyclic orders of *i*, *j*, *k*, adding two and subtracting the third, ⟨*f*_ij, *f*_k⟩ = *λ*²(*σ*_i*η*_jk + *σ*_j*η*_ik − *σ*_k*η*_ij). The vectors *f*₀, …, *f*₃ are a basis with ⟨*f*_k, *f*_l⟩ = *λ*²*η*_kl, so

*f*_ij = *σ*_i*f*_j + *σ*_j*f*_i − *η*_ij*σ*^k*f*_k. (L)

Step 2. Differentiate (L) with respect to *x*^k and replace the second derivatives of *f* on the right by (L). The term −*η*_ij*σ*^l*f*_lk becomes −*η*_ij(*s* *f*_k + *σ*_k*σ*^l*f*_l − *σ*_k*σ*^l*f*_l) = −*η*_ij *s* *f*_k, and the result is *f*_ijk = *σ*_ik*f*_j + *σ*_jk*f*_i − *η*_ij*σ*^l_k*f*_l + 2*σ*_i*σ*_j*f*_k + *σ*_i*σ*_k*f*_j + *σ*_j*σ*_k*f*_i − (*σ*_i*η*_jk + *σ*_j*η*_ik)*σ*^l*f*_l − *η*_ij *s* *f*_k, where *σ*^l_k = *η*^lm*σ*_mk. The left side is unchanged when *j* and *k* are exchanged. Subtracting the expression with *j*, *k* exchanged, and writing *S*_ij = *σ*_ij − *σ*_i*σ*_j and *S*^l_k = *η*^lm*S*_mk, gives (*S*_ik + *η*_ik *s*)*f*_j − (*S*_ij + *η*_ij *s*)*f*_k − *η*_ij*S*^l_k*f*_l + *η*_ik*S*^l_j*f*_l = 0. Since the *f*_l are a basis, the coefficient of each *f*_m vanishes: (*S*_ik + *η*_ik *s*)δ^m_j − (*S*_ij + *η*_ij *s*)δ^m_k − *η*_ij*S*^m_k + *η*_ik*S*^m_j = 0. Setting *m* = *j* and summing over *j* from 0 to 3, with *S*^j_j = *τ*: 4(*S*_ik + *η*_ik *s*) − (*S*_ik + *η*_ik *s*) − *S*_ik + *η*_ik*τ* = 0, that is, 2*S*_ik + 3*η*_ik *s* + *η*_ik*τ* = 0. Contracting with *η*^ik: 2*τ* + 12*s* + 4*τ* = 0, so *τ* = −2*s*, and therefore *S*_ik = −½ *s* *η*_ik.

Step 3. Let *ρ* = 1/*λ* = exp(−*σ*). Then ∂_i*ρ* = −*ρσ*_i and ∂_i∂_j*ρ* = *ρ*(*σ*_i*σ*_j − *σ*_ij) = −*ρS*_ij = *κη*_ij with *κ* = ½*ρs* = *η*^kl∂_k*ρ*∂_l*ρ*/(2*ρ*). Since *ρ* is three times continuously differentiable, ∂_k∂_i∂_j*ρ* = (∂_k*κ*)*η*_ij is unchanged when *j* and *k* are exchanged; taking *i* = *j* ≠ *k* gives ∂_k*κ* = 0. So *κ* is constant on the connected region *E*, the second derivatives of *ρ* are constant, and *ρ* is a polynomial of the second degree on *E*, determined by the values at one point of *ρ* and of its first derivatives, *κ* being fixed by these.

Step 4, uniqueness. Let *f* and *h* both satisfy the hypotheses on *E*, and at a point *p* let *f*(*p*) = *h*(*p*), let the derivatives of *f* and *h* be equal, and let the first derivatives of their functions *ρ* be equal. The values of *ρ* at *p* are equal, since *λ*² = ⟨*f*₁, *f*₁⟩. By Step 3 the two functions *ρ* are the same polynomial, so the functions *σ* are the same. Along a continuously differentiable path *x*(*τ*) from *p*, by (L) the four vectors *f*_j(*x*(*τ*)) satisfy the linear system of ordinary differential equations *df*_j/*dτ* = (*dx*^i/*dτ*)(*σ*_i*f*_j + *σ*_j*f*_i − *η*_ij*σ*^k*f*_k), and the *h*_j satisfy the same system with the same initial values. By the uniqueness of the solution of a linear system, *f*_j = *h*_j along the path. Every point of *E* is joined to *p* by such a path, so *f* − *h* has zero derivative on *E* and vanishes at *p*: *f* = *h*.

Step 5, existence. For elements of O(2,4), *g*₁*g*₂*j*(*x*) = *κ* *g*₁*j*(*g*₂·*x*), where *κ* is the number *κ*_g(*x*) belonging to the factor *g*₂, so (*g*₁*g*₂)·*x* = *g*₁·(*g*₂·*x*) and the functions *κ* multiply. By Theorem 6.39(v) the map *x* → *g*·*x* satisfies the hypotheses where *κ*_g ≠ 0, with *ρ* = |*κ*_g|. Let *C*_b be the element inversion, translation by *b*, inversion of Theorem 6.39(iii). It sends *j*(*x*) = (*x*, 1, *q*), *q* = *η*(*x*, *x*), successively to (*x*, *q*, 1), to (*x* + *qb*, *q*, 1 + 2*η*(*b*, *x*) + *qη*(*b*, *b*)) and to (*x* + *qb*, *P*(*x*), *q*) with *P*(*x*) = 1 + 2*η*(*b*, *x*) + *η*(*b*, *b*)*η*(*x*, *x*). So *C*_b·*x* = (*x* + *qb*)/*P*(*x*): it fixes 0, its derivative at 0 is the identity, and its *ρ* is *P* near 0, with first derivatives 2*η*_ik*b*^k at 0. Now fix *p* in *E*. The derivative *M* of *f* at *p* satisfies *M*ᵀ*ηM* = *λ*₀²*η* with *λ*₀ = *λ*(*p*), so Λ₀ = *M*/*λ*₀ preserves *η*. Let *b*^k = ½*λ*₀*η*^kl∂_l*ρ*(*p*), and let *g* be the product of the translation by −*p*, then *C*_b, then the Lorentz transformation Λ₀, then the dilation by *λ*₀, then the translation by *f*(*p*), all of Theorem 6.39(ii). Its map is *h*(*x*) = *f*(*p*) + *λ*₀Λ₀ *C*_b·(*x* − *p*), and *κ*_g(*x*) = *P*(*x* − *p*)/*λ*₀, the dilation contributing the factor 1/*λ*₀. At *p*: *h*(*p*) = *f*(*p*), the derivative of *h* is *λ*₀Λ₀ = *M*, and the function *P*(*x* − *p*)/*λ*₀ has the value 1/*λ*₀ = *ρ*(*p*) and the first derivatives 2*η*_ik*b*^k/*λ*₀ = ∂_i*ρ*(*p*). This function is a polynomial of the second degree with second derivatives (2*η*(*b*, *b*)/*λ*₀)*η*_ij, and 2*η*(*b*, *b*)/*λ*₀ is the value of *η*^kl∂_k*ρ*∂_l*ρ*/(2*ρ*) at *p*, which is *κ*. By Step 3 it coincides with *ρ* on *E*. Hence *κ*_g = *ρ* > 0 on *E*, the map *h* is defined on all of *E* and satisfies the hypotheses there with the same *ρ*, and by Step 4, *f* = *h* on *E*. □

**Corollary 6.42 (The kernel of the action and the subgroup fixing an event).** (i) An element of O(2,4) that fixes the null line of *j*(*x*) for every *x* in a non-empty open set is 1 or −1. Hence two elements of O(2,4) give the same map *x* → *g*·*x* on an open set exactly when they are equal or opposite, and the element *g* of Theorem 6.41 is determined up to sign. (ii) The subgroup *H* of Theorem 6.39(iii) consists of the elements ±*D*_λ*L*_Λ*C*_b, with *D*_λ a dilation, *λ* > 0, *L*_Λ a Lorentz transformation, Λᵀ*η*Λ = *η*, and *C*_b as in Step 5; *λ*, Λ and *b* are determined by the element. (iii) The group of the transformations of regions of spacetime that carry null directions to null directions is therefore the group of the maps *x* → *g*·*x*, which is O(2,4) with *g* and −*g* identified.

*Proof.* (i) Let *gj*(*x*) = *a*(*x*)*j*(*x*) for *x* in the open set *W*. For *x*, *x*′ in *W*, ⟨*gj*(*x*), *gj*(*x*′)⟩ = *a*(*x*)*a*(*x*′)⟨*j*(*x*), *j*(*x*′)⟩, so *a*(*x*)*a*(*x*′) = 1 whenever the interval of *x*, *x*′ is not zero (Theorem 6.39(i)). The events with interval zero from a given event form the zero set of a polynomial that is not identically zero, so for *x*, *x*′ in *W* there is *x*″ in *W* with non-zero interval from both. From *a*(*x*)*a*(*x*″) = 1 and *a*(*x*′)*a*(*x*″) = 1 follows *a*(*x*) = *a*(*x*′); so *a* is constant on *W*, and its square is 1. A linear form on ℝ⁶ vanishing on all *j*(*x*), *x* in *W*, is a polynomial *η*(*α*, *x*) + *β* + *γη*(*x*, *x*) vanishing on an open set, so it is zero: the *j*(*x*) span ℝ⁶, and *g* = *a*. The second statement follows by applying this to the product of one element with the inverse of the other. (ii) Each of *D*_λ, *L*_Λ, *C*_b fixes the line of *j*(0) (Theorem 6.39(iii)). Conversely let *g* be in *H*. Then *gj*(0) is a non-zero multiple of *j*(0), so *κ*_g(0) ≠ 0, and *x* → *g*·*x* fixes 0 and satisfies the hypotheses of Theorem 6.41 on a ball about 0. By Step 5 of its proof with *p* = 0 and *f*(*p*) = 0, this map is that of *D*_λ*L*_Λ*C*_b for *λ* and Λ given by its derivative at 0 and *b* by the first derivatives of its *ρ* at 0, and by (i) *g* is this element or its opposite. (iii) is Theorem 6.41 with (i). □

**Theorem 6.43 (The de Sitter and anti-de Sitter Klein pairs).** (i) Let a real vector space carry a symmetric form ⟨ , ⟩, let *G* be the group of its linear maps preserving the form, and let *ξ*, *ζ* be vectors with ⟨*ξ*, *ξ*⟩ = ⟨*ζ*, *ζ*⟩ = *N* ≠ 0. Then some element of *G* carries *ξ* to *ζ*. (ii) On ℝ⁵ with the form of one negative and four positive squares, the group O(1,4) carries every point of the set *Y* of the vectors with ⟨*ξ*, *ξ*⟩ = *c*²/*H*² to every other, and the subgroup fixing a point is the group O(1,3) of its orthogonal complement. The map sending (*t*, *r*, **n**), with **n** a unit vector of ℝ³ and *r* < *c*/*H*, to the vector with the components *w* sinh *Ht*, *r***n**, *w* cosh *Ht*, where *w* = (*c*²/*H*² − *r*²)^(1/2), has its values in *Y*, and the form induces on them the metric of Theorem 8.6.17(ii), the solution of the vacuum field equations with Λ = 3*H*²/*c*². Hence (O(1,4), O(1,3)) is a Klein pair whose space *Y* contains that solution, and each element of O(1,4) preserves the metric. (iii) On ℝ⁵ with the form of two negative and three positive squares, the same holds for O(2,3) and the set *Z* of the vectors with ⟨*ξ*, *ξ*⟩ = −*L*²: the subgroup fixing a point is O(1,3), and the map sending (*t*, *r*, **n**) to the vector with the components *w* sin(*ct*/*L*), *w* cos(*ct*/*L*), *r***n**, where *w* = (*L*² + *r*²)^(1/2), has its values in *Z* and induces the metric −*F* *c*²*dt*² + *dr*²/*F* + *r*²(*dθ*² + sin²*θ* *dφ*²) with *F* = 1 + *r*²/*L*², which satisfies the vacuum field equations with Λ = −3/*L*².

*Proof.* (i) For a vector *w* with ⟨*w*, *w*⟩ ≠ 0 the reflection *θ* → *θ* − 2⟨*w*, *θ*⟩*w*/⟨*w*, *w*⟩ is linear and preserves the form. Since ⟨*ξ* − *ζ*, *ξ* − *ζ*⟩ + ⟨*ξ* + *ζ*, *ξ* + *ζ*⟩ = 4*N* ≠ 0, one of the two terms is not zero. If the first, take *w* = *ξ* − *ζ*: then ⟨*w*, *ξ*⟩ = *N* − ⟨*ζ*, *ξ*⟩ = ½⟨*w*, *w*⟩ and the reflection sends *ξ* to *ξ* − *w* = *ζ*. If the second, take *w* = *ξ* + *ζ*: then ⟨*w*, *ξ*⟩ = ½⟨*w*, *w*⟩, the reflection sends *ξ* to −*ζ*, and the map −1 sends −*ζ* to *ζ*. (ii) The first statement is (i). An element fixing *ξ*₀ maps the orthogonal complement *E* of *ξ*₀ onto itself, and every vector is a multiple of *ξ*₀ plus a vector of *E*, since ⟨*ξ*₀, *ξ*₀⟩ ≠ 0; so the element is determined by its action on *E*, which may be any linear map of *E* preserving the form. As ⟨*ξ*₀, *ξ*₀⟩ > 0, the form on *E* has one negative and three positive squares. For the map: −(*w* sinh *Ht*)² + *r*² + (*w* cosh *Ht*)² = *w*² + *r*² = *c*²/*H*². The differentials of the first and last components give −*d*(*w* sinh *Ht*)² + *d*(*w* cosh *Ht*)² = *dw*² − *w*²*H*²*dt*², the differential of *r***n** gives *dr*² + *r*²(*dθ*² + sin²*θ* *dφ*²), and *dw* = −*r* *dr*/*w*. The sum is −*w*²*H*²*dt*² + (1 + *r*²/*w*²)*dr*² + *r*²(*dθ*² + sin²*θ* *dφ*²), and *w*²*H*² = *c*²(1 − *H*²*r*²/*c*²), 1 + *r*²/*w*² = 1/(1 − *H*²*r*²/*c*²). A linear map preserving the form preserves the metric it induces on *Y*. (iii) The same argument applies, with ⟨*ξ*₀, *ξ*₀⟩ < 0 removing one negative square, so that *E* again has one negative and three positive squares. For the map: −*w*² + *r*² = −*L*²; the first two components give −*dw*² − *w*²*c*²*dt*²/*L*², with *dw* = *r* *dr*/*w*, and the sum is −(*w*²/*L*²)*c*²*dt*² + (1 − *r*²/*w*²)*dr*² + *r*²(*dθ*² + sin²*θ* *dφ*²), with *w*²/*L*² = *F* and 1 − *r*²/*w*² = 1/*F*. By Theorem 8.2.1(ii) with *A* = *F*, *B* = 1/*F*, as in the proof of Theorem 8.6.17(ii), *F*″ + 2*F*′/*r* = 6/*L*² and 1 − *F* − *rF*′ = −3*r*²/*L*², so *R*_μν = −(3/*L*²)*g*_μν and *G*_μν − (3/*L*²)*g*_μν = 0. □

**The McGucken Principle *dx*₄/*dt* = i*c* reading of Theorem 6.43.** The field equations of §9.2 contain the constant Λ, the ratio *β*/*α* of Theorem 9.2.2, whose value the present paper does not derive. For Λ = 0 the solution without matter is the spacetime of Theorem 6.28 with its pair (ISO⁺(1,3), SO⁺(1,3)); for Λ > 0 and Λ < 0 the solutions of greatest symmetry carry the pairs of this theorem, with the same subgroup O(1,3) fixing an event. Which of the three is the pair of the universe is the question of the value of Λ.

**Theorem 6.44 (Curved spacetime: the pair of Theorem 6.28 at each event, and the condition for a Klein pair of the whole).** (i) Let *g*_μν be a Lorentzian metric. At each event the coordinate systems adapted to it (Lemma 9.2.1(i)) exist, and the Jacobian matrix at the event of the change from one adapted system to another is a matrix Λ with Λᵀ*η*Λ = *η*; every such Λ occurs, and each is the Jacobian matrix of exactly one linear change. So the group O(1,3), the group fixing an event in Theorem 6.28 together with the reflections, acts on the adapted systems at each event, carrying each to every other. (ii) If a group of transformations preserving the metric carries every event to every other, then every scalar formed from the metric and its derivatives by the rules of tensor algebra has the same value at all events. (iii) For the Schwarzschild metric of Theorem 8.2.1 with *r*_s > 0 the scalar *R*_μνρσ*R*^μνρσ equals 12*r*_s²/*r*⁶. Hence no group of transformations preserving that metric carries every event to every other: this spacetime is the space of no Klein pair of such transformations.

*Proof.* (i) In two adapted systems the metric at the event is *η* in both, and the transformation law of the metric at the event reads Λᵀ*η*Λ = *η* for the Jacobian matrix Λ. Conversely, if *y* is adapted and Λᵀ*η*Λ = *η*, the linear change *y*′ = Λ⁻¹*y* gives the metric Λᵀ*g*Λ, which equals *η* at the event and has vanishing first derivatives there. (ii) Such a scalar is defined by the same formula in all coordinates, and a transformation preserving the metric carries the coordinates at one event to coordinates at the image event in which the metric is the same function; so the scalar has the same value at both. (iii) With the connection coefficients of Theorem 8.2.1(i) and *A* = 1/*B* = 1 − *r*_s/*r*, the components of the curvature tensor referred to the unit vectors along the coordinate lines are, up to the symmetries of the tensor: *r*_s/*r*³ in magnitude for the pair of directions (*t*, *r*) and for the pair (*θ*, *φ*), and *r*_s/(2*r*³) in magnitude for the four pairs (*t*, *θ*), (*t*, *φ*), (*r*, *θ*), (*r*, *φ*). Each such component occurs four times in the full contraction, so the scalar is 4[2(*r*_s/*r*³)² + 4(*r*_s/(2*r*³))²] = 12*r*_s²/*r*⁶. It takes different values at different *r*, and (ii) applies. □

**The McGucken Principle *dx*₄/*dt* = i*c* reading of Theorem 6.44.** Mass curves and stretches *x*₁, *x*₂, *x*₃, and part (iii) shows that the curved spacetime of a mass is the space of no Klein pair of its own. What the Principle supplies at every event of every solution is part (i): the group of Theorem 6.28 acting on the systems in which the interval at that event is *dx*₁² + *dx*₂² + *dx*₃² + *dx*₄². This is the sense in which Cartan generalized Klein’s programme. The theory of the connection that joins the groups at neighbouring events, and the group of all smooth transformations of spacetime, are not treated here.

## **Mathematical Physics on a Categorical Foundation.**

The consequence for mathematical physics is a change of foundation rather than of notation. Standard formulations posit each arena — Minkowski space, Hilbert space, phase space, gauge bundle, Fock space, operator algebra — as a separate object, with morphisms (Wick rotation, geometric quantization, second quantization, Dirac factorization, gauge covariantization) added by hand. Inside **McG** these arenas appear as objects of one category and the morphisms as canonical functors generated by the McGucken Operator *D_M*. The arena hierarchy is no longer a list of axioms; it is a diagram in **McG**, and each arrow in the diagram is an instance of the source law.

This is what is meant by completing the Erlangen Programme: not extending its catalogue of geometries, but supplying the source from which the Klein pairs of spacetime, of the space of states and of phase space, with the Euclidean, Galilean, spin and conformal pairs (Theorems 6.28, 6.30, 6.33, 6.34 and 6.36–6.39) — and the arenas built on them — are co-generated by a single law [63, 66, 67, 68]. Klein gave the classification; Cartan, Weyl, and Ehresmann gave the differential geometry; Mac Lane, Eilenberg, and Grothendieck gave the categorical vocabulary; the McGucken Principle supplies the source from which the classified objects arise.

## **Why This Completion Is Unique.**

Several features distinguish this completion from the partial extensions that came before it. *First*, it is generated by a single one-line law rather than by a list of axioms; the primitive count of the framework, examined in the next section, is one. *Second*, the law generates the **pair** (McGucken Space *M_G*, McGucken Operator *D_M*) simultaneously and reciprocally — a feature with no precedent in the Erlangen tradition, where space and operator are always introduced separately. *Third*, the completion holds in two independent languages at once: the group-theoretic language of 1872 and the category-theoretic language of 1945. *Fourth*, the law is simultaneously a *mathematical axiom* — the source of the formal category **McG** — and a *physical principle* — the statement that the fourth dimension expands at the velocity of light in a spherically symmetric manner. The completion is mathematical and physical at the same time, by the same one-line equation.

It is worth emphasizing how unusual this is. Klein in 1872 had a meta-principle (geometry = invariants of a group) but no law generating the group. Hilbert in 1900 had the demand for a generating law but no candidate. The constructive programme of the twentieth century supplied region-specific axiomatic systems but no unifying source. The Erlangen Programme stood open for 154 years, the Sixth Problem for 126. The McGucken Principle closes both at once, in both languages, with one equation. That convergence — one law completing two long-standing programmes in two independent formal frameworks — is the fact celebrated in this section.

With the categorical foundation in place we may now ask the quantitative question Hilbert posed first: how many independent primitives does the McGucken *dx*₄/*dt* = i*c* framework actually require? We turn to that count in the next section.

## 7. Comparative Axiomatics: Counting Primitives

The McGucken Principle asserts that the fourth dimension *x*₄ is expanding at the velocity of light: *dx*₄/*dt* = i*c*, with *x*₄ = i*ct*, as a spherically symmetric McGucken Sphere whose wavelength sets the action *h* and whose volume defines a nonlocality.

Hilbert’s first demand on the Sixth Problem was that the axiomatization proceed “by a small number of axioms.” To make this requirement quantitative we count *independent primitive structures*: objects, relations, or operators that must be specified before any theorem can be stated. We count a *law* as one primitive only when its variables are quantities defined elsewhere in the framework; if the variables are themselves uninterpreted symbols, they are also primitives. By this rule, Newton’s *F* = *m***a** is one primitive law over the previously defined kinematic primitives (position, time, mass), not four primitives {*F*, *m*, **a**, =}. The McGucken Principle is one primitive law over the quantities (*x*₄, *t*) and the constants (*i*, *c*), all of which are previously available in any physics curriculum. The action *h* is in the Principle itself, set by the wavelength of the expanding McGucken Sphere; Newton’s constant *G* enters only as the measured coupling *κ* = 8*πG*/*c*⁴ fixed by the Newtonian limit in §8.2.

Table 7.1 below tabulates the count for the major foundational programmes of the twentieth and early twenty-first centuries. The qualitative point is that no prior programme reaches one primitive; the McGucken Principle does.

| Programme                         | Year          | Count        | Primitives                                                      | Derives arena?                                               |
|-----------------------------------|---------------|--------------|-----------------------------------------------------------------|--------------------------------------------------------------|
| **McGucken Principle**            | **2008–2026** | **1**        | ***dx*₄/*dt* = i*c***                                           | **Yes (McGucken Space *M_G*, *H*, McGucken Operator *D_M*)** |
| Newton (mechanics)                | 1687          | 4+           | absolute space, absolute time, mass, force law                  | No (space & time given)                                      |
| Hilbert (1899) geometry           | 1899          | 5 groups     | incidence, order, congruence, parallels, continuity             | Plane only                                                   |
| Kolmogorov (probability)          | 1933          | 3 + 4        | sample space, σ-algebra, measure; K1–K4                         | No (sample space given)                                      |
| von Neumann (QM)                  | 1932          | 5+           | Hilbert space, observables, states, evolution, measurement      | No (*H* given)                                               |
| Wightman / Gårding–Wightman (QFT) | 1956 / 1964   | 6            | Hilbert space, Poincaré rep, vacuum, fields, locality, spectrum | No (Minkowski given)                                         |
| Haag–Kastler (algebraic QFT)      | 1964          | 4 + 4 axioms | net of C*-algebras + isotony, locality, covariance, spectrum   | No (Minkowski given)                                         |
| Causal sets                       | 1987          | 2            | set, partial order                                              | Lorentzian causal structure only                             |
| Spectral triples (Connes)         | 1980s         | 3            | algebra *A*, Hilbert space *H*, Dirac operator *D*              | Geometry only; no QM dynamics                                |

*Table 7.1. Primitive count and arena-generation status of foundational programmes.*

Two clarifications. First, the count assigned to McGucken is one only if the symbols (*x*₄, *t*, *i*, *c*) are taken from prior physical and mathematical vocabulary—the same way *F*, *m*, **a** are taken in *F* = *m***a**. To insist they be re-derived from outside is to demand the law be derived, which Hilbert’s notion of axiomatization does not require (“the axioms so set up are at the same time the definitions of those elementary ideas”). Second, the entry under “derives arena” is the distinguishing feature: every other entry takes the manifold or the Hilbert space as a separate primitive in addition to its dynamical axioms. The McGucken Principle generates both arena and dynamics from a single law, which is exactly the step Hilbert’s Sixth Problem requires.

**The McGucken Principle *dx*₄/*dt* = i*c* Reading of Axiom Counting: Primitive-Law Complexity *C* = 1.**

**McGucken Principle anchor.** The McGucken Principle *dx*₄/*dt* = i*c* states that the fourth dimension is expanding at the velocity of light in a spherically symmetric manner from every spacetime event, as a McGucken Sphere whose wavelength sets the action *h*. For the count of axioms the Principle predicts the value one: the proper axiom is the single statement *dx*₄/*dt* = i*c*, and the carrier *E*₄, the McGucken Sphere and the origin convention *x*₄(0) = 0 specify the framework in which that statement is written. Definitions 6, 9 and 21 and Theorem 22 of the companion paper [75] state the count as a definition and a theorem.

**Definition 7.1 (Derivational closure; Definitions 6 and 9 of [75]).** The derivational closure Der(McGucken Space *M_G*) is the set of mathematical structures obtainable from (McGucken Space *M_G*, McGucken Operator *D_M*) by finite sequences of applications of the closure operations: (O1) constraint imposition, (O2) quotient projection, (O3) slicing along the flow parameter, (O4) bundle formation, (O5) section formation, (O6) cotangent lift, (O7) complexification, (O8) representation formation, (O9) quantization, (O10) completion, (O11) tensor product, (O12) Fock construction, (O13) operator-algebra construction, (O14) connection formation.

**Definition 7.2 (Primitive-law complexity; Definition 21 of [75]).** For a foundational mathematical system *X*, the primitive-law complexity *C*(*X*) is the minimum number of independent primitive axioms required to generate the closure Der(*X*) associated with *X*, where independence is measured in the standard sense of axiomatic systems (no axiom is derivable from the others).

**Theorem 7.3 (Minimal primitive-law complexity; Theorem 22 of [75]).** *C*(McGucken Space *M_G*) = 1 ≤ *C*(*X*) for every nontrivial generative system *X*.

*Proof.* *Lower bound.* Any nontrivial generative system requires at least one proper axiom specific to itself; a system with zero proper axioms generates only the structure already present in its underlying logical or mathematical apparatus, which does not constitute a generative system in the sense of Definition 7.2. Hence *C*(*X*) ≥ 1.

*Upper bound for the McGucken* dx*₄/*dt *= i*c *framework, with a careful counting convention.* We adopt the standard counting convention used in foundational mathematics: *C*(*X*) counts the proper axioms specific to *X*, with the underlying logical apparatus and the universal mathematical apparatus (sets, functions, the standard operations on them) not counted. By this convention: ZFC has *C* = 9, counting the axioms of extensionality, pairing, union, power set, infinity, separation, replacement, foundation, choice [each axiom schema counted once]; the underlying first-order logic with equality is not counted. Peano arithmetic has *C* = 9 [Peano’s 1889 list, four of whose nine axioms concern equality] or *C* = 5 (second-order formulation), counting the axioms about 0, the successor function, and induction [in first-order Peano arithmetic induction is a schema, counted once]; the underlying logic is not counted. Hilbert’s geometry (*Grundlagen der Geometrie*) has *C* = 5 groups of axioms.

By the same counting convention applied to the McGucken *dx*₄/*dt* = i*c* framework: the proper axiom specific to the framework is the single statement *dx*₄/*dt* = i*c*. The closure operations (O1)–(O14) are part of the universal mathematical apparatus (constraint imposition is selection of a subset by a definable predicate, available in any sufficiently rich set-theoretic foundation; complexification, Cauchy completion, tensor product, Fock construction, and operator-algebra construction are standard constructions of differential geometry and functional analysis; bundle and connection formation are standard differential-geometric constructions). [Quantization (O9) applied to the McGucken Operator *D_M* is the term iℏ · *D_M* of the formal language ℒ_M of Definition 11.1, formed with the scalar-operator multiplication and the constant ℏ of that language; it is a term of the language and adds no axiom.] The framework structures (the carrier *E*₄ and the McGucken Sphere) and Convention κ are framework-specifying components analogous to the choice of vocabulary in ZFC (the symbol ∈) or the choice of a base point in pointed categories; they are not counted as proper axioms. Hence *C*(McGucken Space *M_G*) = 1 by the standard counting convention. Combined with the lower bound: *C*(McGucken Space *M_G*) = 1, the absolute floor. □

**The two inputs of the Hilbert-space sector.** Theorem 14 of [75] constructs the complex Hilbert space from the McGucken Space *M_G* together with two inputs, which [75] names the Born postulate (B) and Huygens’ principle (H), and the remark following that theorem states that with both reduced to theorems the construction is unconditional. Both are theorems in the sources of the present paper. (B) is Theorem 7.2 of [73], proved in §9.3 from the conservation law of the McGucken Sphere. (H) is the strong Huygens principle for the d’Alembertian in three space dimensions, a theorem of classical analysis through Kirchhoff’s formula [79], listed in the corpus as Theorem 2.8 of [72]. In [24, Part IV] the retarded Green’s function of the wave operator is *G* = *δ*(*t* − *t*′ − |**x** − **x**′|/*c*)/|**x** − **x**′|, supported on the sphere |**x** − **x**′| = *c*(*t* − *t*′), and the solution at (**x**, *t*) from data on the surface *t*′ is *ψ*(**x**, *t*) = (1/4*πc*²) ∫ [*G* ∂*ψ*/∂*t*′ − *ψ* ∂*G*/∂*t*′] *d*³*x*′. Kirchhoff’s formula states that the solution at an event is an integral of the data over a sphere; the McGucken Principle *dx*₄/*dt* = i*c* provides the physical mechanism for Huygens’ principle, the spherically symmetric expansion of *x*₄ at rate *c* from every event. With (B) and (H) as theorems, the count of Theorem 7.3 covers the Hilbert-space sector with no added axiom.

**The McGucken Principle *dx*₄/*dt* = i*c* reading of Table 7.1.** Table 7.1 counts primitive structures (objects, relations and operators that must be specified before a theorem can be stated); Definition 7.2 counts proper axioms. The McGucken Principle has the value one under both conventions: one law, *dx*₄/*dt* = i*c*, in Table 7.1, and one proper axiom in Theorem 7.3. The programmes in the remaining rows of Table 7.1 take their arena as a separate primitive, which Theorem 4.1 derives from the same law.

## 8. The Derivation Lattice: How Physics Emerges from *dx*₄/*dt* = i*c*

The McGucken Principle asserts that the fourth dimension *x*₄ is expanding at the velocity of light: *dx*₄/*dt* = i*c*, with *x*₄ = i*ct*, as a spherically symmetric McGucken Sphere whose wavelength sets the action *h* and whose volume defines a nonlocality.

We outline the principal derivation chains. Each is established as an explicit theorem chain in McGucken’s papers—general relativity in [56, 71], quantum mechanics in [58], the joint GR–QM unification in [57], thermodynamics and the second law in [31, 62], the symmetries and the Erlangen completion in [59], the action and the Lagrangian in [61], spacetime’s foundational atoms together with its sphere–amplituhedron structure in [60], the source-pair reciprocal generation in [26, 27, 64, 65, 66], and the joint Space–Operator co-generation underwriting the categorical foundation in [63, 67, 68]. We present the structure rather than the full computation, as the latter occupies several hundred pages across the source documents.

**8.1 Special Relativity.** From the McGucken Principle *dx*₄/*dt* = i*c* the line element *ds*² = *dx*₄² + *d***x**² = *d***x**² − *c*²*dt*² follows (§9.1). With proper time defined by *c*²*dτ*² = −*ds*², the master equation *u*^μ *u*_μ = −*c*² follows, expressing that every object’s four-velocity has fixed magnitude *c*. The four-velocity budget (*dx*₄/*dτ*)² + |*d***x**/*dτ*|² = −*c*², equivalently (*c* *dτ*/*dt*)² + |*d***x**/*dt*|² = *c*² in coordinate time, where *c* *dτ*/*dt* = *c*/*γ* is the standard time-dilation factor, yields, by elementary algebra, time dilation *dt*/*dτ* = γ, length contraction *L* = *L*₀/γ, mass-energy equivalence *E*₀ = *mc*² (from the temporal component of four-momentum *p*₄ = iγ*mc* = i*E*/*c*), and the Lorentz transformation. None of these are postulates; all follow from *dx*₄/*dt* = i*c* by direct calculation. The full derivation, with the Lorentz transformation obtained as an explicit theorem rather than a postulate, is presented in McGucken [28, 56, 57].

**The McGucken Principle *dx*₄/*dt* = i*c* Reading of Special Relativity: The Interval, Inertial Coordinates, Proper Time, Time Dilation, the Master Equation, the Lorentz Transformation, Length Contraction, the Constancy of *c*, and *E* = *mc*², with Proofs.**

**McGucken Principle anchor.** The McGucken Principle *dx*₄/*dt* = i*c* states that the fourth dimension is expanding at the velocity of light in a spherically symmetric manner from every spacetime event, as a McGucken Sphere whose wavelength sets the action *h*. For the kinematics of special relativity the Principle predicts the following: because *x*₄ advances at the rate i*c* against *t* and is perpendicular to *x*₁, *x*₂, *x*₃, distances in the four coordinates are measured by *dx*₁² + *dx*₂² + *dx*₃² − *c*²*dt*², and every relation of special relativity is a property of that one quadratic form. The statements below follow GR Theorem 1 and GR Corollary 1 of [57] and Parts I–II of [24]; every proof is carried out here in full.

**Lemma 8.1.1 (The interval).** With *x*₄ = i*ct*, the four-coordinate form *dx*₁² + *dx*₂² + *dx*₃² + *dx*₄² equals *ds*² = *dx*₁² + *dx*₂² + *dx*₃² − *c*²*dt*².

*Proof.* This is Lemma 6.25: *dx*₄ = i*c* *dt*, so *dx*₄² = −*c*²*dt*². □

**Definition 8.1.2 (Inertial coordinate system).** The McGucken Principle *dx*₄/*dt* = i*c* is a statement about the fourth dimension *x*₄; it refers to no state of motion and holds from every event. An inertial coordinate system is a system of coordinates (*t*, *x*₁, *x*₂, *x*₃) on the events, with *x*₄ = i*ct*, in which the Principle reads *dx*₄/*dt* = i*c* and the four-coordinate form is *dx*₁² + *dx*₂² + *dx*₃² + *dx*₄². By Lemma 8.1.1 the interval between two neighbouring events is *ds*² = *d***x**² − *c*²*dt*² in every inertial coordinate system.

**Lemma 8.1.3 (Transformations between inertial coordinate systems).** Let (*t*, **x**) and (*t*′, **x**′) be two inertial coordinate systems on the same events, and write *x*^μ = (*ct*, *x*₁, *x*₂, *x*₃), *η* = diag(−1, 1, 1, 1). Then *x*′ = *Λx* + *a* with *a* constant and *Λ* a constant matrix satisfying *Λ*ᵀ*ηΛ* = *η*.

*Proof.* Both systems assign to a pair of neighbouring events the same interval (Definition 8.1.2), so with *J*^α_μ = ∂*x*′^α/∂*x*^μ, *η*_αβ *J*^α_μ *J*^β_ν = *η*_μν at every event. Differentiate with respect to *x*^ρ and write *S*_ρμν = (∂_ρ *J*^α_μ) *η*_αβ *J*^β_ν. Since ∂_ρ *J*^α_μ = ∂_ρ ∂_μ *x*′^α, *S*_ρμν is symmetric in its first two indices, and the differentiated identity reads *S*_ρμν + *S*_ρνμ = 0. Then *S*_ρμν = −*S*_ρνμ = −*S*_νρμ = *S*_νμρ = *S*_μνρ = −*S*_μρν = −*S*_ρμν, so *S*_ρμν = 0. The matrix *η*_αβ *J*^β_ν is invertible because its product with *J*ᵀ is *η*, so ∂_ρ ∂_μ *x*′^α = 0: the map is affine, *x*′ = *Λx* + *a*, and the identity for *J* = *Λ* is *Λ*ᵀ*ηΛ* = *η*. □

**Definition 8.1.4 (Proper time).** Along a worldline **x**(*t*) with speed *v* = |*d***x**/*dt*| < *c*, the proper time *τ* is defined by *c*²*dτ*² = −*ds*², with *τ* increasing with *t*. By Lemma 8.1.3 the interval is the same in every inertial coordinate system, so *τ* is the same in every inertial coordinate system.

**Theorem 8.1.5 (Time dilation; equation (XII) of [24]).** Along a worldline of speed *v* < *c*, *dt*/*dτ* = *γ* with *γ* = (1 − *v*²/*c*²)^(−1/2).

*Proof.* By Lemma 8.1.1 and Definition 8.1.4, *c*²*dτ*² = *c*²*dt*² − *d***x**² = *c*²*dt*² (1 − *v*²/*c*²). Hence (*dτ*/*dt*)² = 1 − *v*²/*c*², and since *τ* increases with *t*, *dτ*/*dt* = (1 − *v*²/*c*²)^(1/2) = 1/*γ*. A clock moving at speed *v* advances by *dτ* = *dt*/*γ* while the coordinate time advances by *dt*. □

**Theorem 8.1.6 (Master equation and four-velocity budget; GR Theorem 1 and GR Corollary 1 of [57]).** Along a worldline of speed *v* < *c* let *u*₄ = *dx*₄/*dτ* and **u** = *d***x**/*dτ*. Then (i) *u*₄ = i*cγ* and **u** = *γ***v**; (ii) the sum *u*₄² + |**u**|² equals −*c*², which in the coordinates *x*^μ = (*ct*, **x**) is the master equation *u*^μ *u*_μ = −*c*²; (iii) the budget |*dx*₄/*dτ*|² − |*d***x**/*dτ*|² = *c*² holds, and in coordinate time (*c* *dτ*/*dt*)² + |*d***x**/*dt*|² = *c*².

*Proof.* (i) By the chain rule and the Principle, *dx*₄/*dτ* = (*dx*₄/*dt*)(*dt*/*dτ*) = i*c* *γ*, using Theorem 8.1.5; likewise *d***x**/*dτ* = **v** *γ*. (ii) Squaring, *u*₄² + |**u**|² = (i*cγ*)² + *γ*²*v*² = −*γ*²(*c*² − *v*²) = −*c*², because *γ*²(1 − *v*²/*c*²) = 1. With *u*⁰ = *c* *dt*/*dτ* = *cγ*, *u*₄ = i*u*⁰ and *u*^μ *u*_μ = −(*u*⁰)² + |**u**|² = *u*₄² + |**u**|² = −*c*². (iii) Here |*u*₄|² = *c*²*γ*² and |**u**|² = *γ*²*v*², so |*u*₄|² − |**u**|² = *c*². Multiplying the identity *c*²*dτ*² = *c*²*dt*² − *d***x**² of Theorem 8.1.5 by 1/*dt*² gives (*c* *dτ*/*dt*)² + *v*² = *c*². □

**The McGucken Principle *dx*₄/*dt* = i*c* reading of the budget.** The quantity *c* *dτ*/*dt* = *c*/*γ*, the standard time-dilation factor multiplied by *c*, is the share of the fixed total *c* that the worldline does not spend on spatial motion. For a body at rest in space it equals *c*. For light, *v* = *c* and the budget gives *c*² − *v*² = 0: the photon is at rest in *x*₄, while the expansion *dx*₄/*dt* = i*c* holds at every event of its worldline. The proper-time relation *dx*₄/*dτ* = i*cγ* of part (i) is a consequence of the Principle through the chain rule; the Principle itself is *dx*₄/*dt* = i*c* in the coordinate time *t*.

**Theorem 8.1.7 (Lorentz transformation; equation (XVI) of [24]).** Let (*t*, *x*, *y*, *z*) and (*t*′, *x*′, *y*′, *z*′) be inertial coordinate systems whose origins coincide at *t* = *t*′ = 0, with *y*′ = *y*, *z*′ = *z*, with (*t*′, *x*′) depending on (*t*, *x*) alone, with *x*′ increasing with *x* and *t*′ increasing with *t*, and with the spatial origin *x*′ = 0 moving along *x* = *vt*, |*v*| < *c*. Then *x*′ = *γ*(*x* − *vt*) and *t*′ = *γ*(*t* − *vx*/*c*²).

*Proof.* By Lemma 8.1.3 and the coincidence of origins the transformation is linear and preserves *ds*². With *y*′ = *y*, *z*′ = *z* it preserves *x*² + *x*₄², where *x*₄ = i*ct* and *x*₄′ = i*ct*′. Write *x*′ = *ax* + *bx*₄ and *x*₄′ = *ex* + *fx*₄. Equating *x*′² + *x*₄′² with *x*² + *x*₄² for all (*x*, *x*₄) gives *a*² + *e*² = 1, *b*² + *f*² = 1 and *ab* + *ef* = 0. The third equation states that the vector (*b*, *f*) is orthogonal to the vector (*a*, *e*), which is nonzero because *a*² + *e*² = 1; hence (*b*, *f*) = *k*(−*e*, *a*) for a constant *k*. The second equation then gives *k*²(*e*² + *a*²) = *k*² = 1. Hence *e* = −*kb* and *f* = *ka* with *k* = ±1, and *a*² + *b*² = *a*² + *e*² = 1. The origin *x*′ = 0 moves along *x* = *vt*, where *x*₄ = i*ct*: 0 = *a* *vt* + *b* i*ct* for all *t*, so *b* = −*av*/(i*c*) = i*av*/*c*. Then *a*² + *b*² = *a*²(1 − *v*²/*c*²) = 1, so *a* = ±*γ*, and *a* = *γ* because *x*′ increases with *x*. Hence *b* = i*γv*/*c* and *x*′ = *γx* + (i*γv*/*c*)(i*ct*) = *γ*(*x* − *vt*). Further *x*₄′ = *k*(−*bx* + *ax*₄) = *k*(−i*γvx*/*c* + i*γct*) = *k* i*cγ*(*t* − *vx*/*c*²), so *t*′ = *kγ*(*t* − *vx*/*c*²), and *k* = +1 because *t*′ increases with *t*. The coefficients *a* = *γ* and *b* = i*γv*/*c* are those of a rotation of the (*x*, *x*₄) plane through the angle *θ* with cos *θ* = *γ*, sin *θ* = i*γv*/*c*, as in Appendix A. □

**Theorem 8.1.8 (Length contraction; equation (XIII) of [24]).** A rod at rest along the *x*′-axis of the second system of Theorem 8.1.7, of length *L*₀ there, has length *L* = *L*₀/*γ* in the first system.

*Proof.* The ends of the rod are at *x*′ = 0 and *x*′ = *L*₀ for all *t*′. Its length in the first system is the difference *Δx* of the positions of its ends at one time *t*. By Theorem 8.1.7, *x*′ = *γ*(*x* − *vt*) for each end at that *t*, so *L*₀ = *γ* *Δx* and *L* = *Δx* = *L*₀/*γ*. □

**Theorem 8.1.9 (Constancy of the velocity of light and invariance of the McGucken Sphere).** A signal that moves with speed *c* in one inertial coordinate system moves with speed *c* in every inertial coordinate system. The McGucken Spheres of an event *p*, the spheres of radius *c*(*t* − *t*₀) about *p* at the times *t* > *t*₀, are in every inertial coordinate system with *t*′ increasing with *t* the spheres of radius *c*(*t*′ − *t*₀′) about *p* at the times *t*′ > *t*₀′.

*Proof.* A signal of speed *c* satisfies |*d***x**| = *c* *dt*, that is *ds*² = 0. By Lemma 8.1.3, the interval *ds*² is the same in every inertial coordinate system, so *d***x**′² − *c*²*dt*′² = 0 and |*d***x**′/*dt*′| = *c*. For the second statement, an event *q* lies on the McGucken Sphere of *p* exactly when |**x**_q − **x**_p|² − *c*²(*t*_q − *t*_p)² = 0 with *t*_q > *t*_p. The left side is the quadratic form *η* evaluated on the difference of the coordinates of *q* and *p*, which is preserved by *x*′ = *Λx* + *a* because *Λ*ᵀ*ηΛ* = *η*. It remains to show *t*′_q > *t*′_p. Write *n* for the difference of the coordinates of *q* and *p*, so *n*⁰ = |**n**| > 0, and let *λ*₀ = *Λ*⁰₀ and **λ** = (*Λ*⁰₁, *Λ*⁰₂, *Λ*⁰₃). The inverse of *Λ* is *η*⁻¹*Λ*ᵀ*η*, so *Λη*⁻¹*Λ*ᵀ = *η*⁻¹, whose 00 component reads *λ*₀² = 1 + |**λ**|²; with *t*′ increasing with *t*, *λ*₀ > |**λ**|. Then (*Λn*)⁰ = *λ*₀*n*⁰ + **λ**·**n** ≥ (*λ*₀ − |**λ**|) *n*⁰ > 0. Hence |**x**′_q − **x**′_p| = *c*(*t*′_q − *t*′_p). □

**Theorem 8.1.10 (Energy, momentum and *E*₀ = *mc*²; equation (XV) of [24]).** For the free particle of mass *m* with the action *S* = −*mc*² ∫ *dτ* of Proposition 8.7.1: (i) the momentum is **p** = *γm***v** and the conserved energy is *E* = *γmc*²; (ii) the four-momentum *m*(**u**, *u*₄) has fourth component *p*₄ = i*γmc* = i*E*/*c*; (iii) the relation *E*² = |**p**|²*c*² + *m*²*c*⁴ holds; (iv) at rest, *E*₀ = *mc*².

*Proof.* (i) By Theorem 8.1.5 the action is ∫ *L* *dt* with *L* = −*mc*²(1 − *v*²/*c*²)^(1/2). The momentum is **p** = ∂*L*/∂**v** = *m***v**(1 − *v*²/*c*²)^(−1/2) = *γm***v**. The energy is *E* = **p**·**v** − *L* = *γmv*² + *mc*²/*γ* = *γm*(*v*² + *c*² − *v*²) = *γmc*². It is conserved: *dE*/*dt* = (*d***p**/*dt*)·**v** + **p**·(*d***v**/*dt*) − (∂*L*/∂**v**)·(*d***v**/*dt*) = (*d***p**/*dt*)·**v**, and the Euler–Lagrange equation *d***p**/*dt* = ∂*L*/∂**x** = 0 gives *dE*/*dt* = 0. (ii) By Theorem 8.1.6(i), *m***u** = *γm***v** = **p** and *mu*₄ = i*γmc* = i*E*/*c*. (iii) By Theorem 8.1.6(ii), *p*₄² + |**p**|² = *m*²(*u*₄² + |**u**|²) = −*m*²*c*², that is −*E*²/*c*² + |**p**|² = −*m*²*c*². (iv) At *v* = 0, *γ* = 1 and *E* = *mc*². □

**The McGucken Principle *dx*₄/*dt* = i*c* reading of Einstein’s two postulates.** Einstein’s 1905 derivation took the principle of relativity and the constancy of the velocity of light as two postulates. Here the first is Definition 8.1.2, the statement that the Principle *dx*₄/*dt* = i*c* holds from every event and refers to no state of motion, and the second is Theorem 8.1.9. The rest energy *E*₀ = *mc*² of Theorem 8.1.10 is the energy of a body at rest in space, whose whole budget *c* of Theorem 8.1.6 lies in the advance of *x*₄.

**8.2 General Relativity.** The McGucken-Invariance Lemma asserts that *dx*₄/*dt* is metric-independent: ∂(*dx*₄/*dt*)/∂*g*_μν = 0. In the chart adapted to the foliation by spatial slices, where the invariant *x*₄-advance gives *g*₄₄ = 1 and *g*₄ᵢ = 0 (the synchronous, lapse-fixed gauge of [71, §1.4]), the Christoffel symbols carrying both lower indices in the *x*₄ direction vanish, Γ^λ₄₄ = ½*g*^λσ(2∂₄*g*_σ4 − ∂_σ*g*₄₄) = 0 (writing 4 as shorthand for *x*₄), and every remaining connection coefficient is built from the spatial metric *g*ᵢⱼ alone. The geodesic principle *S* = −*mc* ∫ √−*g*_μν ẋ^μẋ^ν *dλ* emerges as the variational extremum of proper-time arc length along *x*₄. The Einstein field equations *G*_μν + Λ*g*_μν = (8π*G*/*c*⁴) *T*_μν follow from the factorisation of diffeomorphism invariance by the McGucken-Invariance Lemma (Theorem 9 of [71], reproduced in §9.2) and Lovelock’s 1971 uniqueness theorem (the unique divergence-free symmetric (0,2)-tensor in 4D constructed from the metric and its first two derivatives is *G*_μν + Λ*g*_μν) with the coupling *κ* = 8π*G*/*c*⁴ fixed by the Newtonian limit. The McGucken Principle supplies what Lovelock requires: the 4-dimensional Lorentzian metric arena, generated by integration of the McGucken Axiom *dx*₄/*dt* = i*c*. Schwarzschild geometry, gravitational time dilation, redshift, light bending Δφ = 4*GM*/*c*²*b*, the Mercury perihelion precession Δφ = 6π*GM*/*c*²*a*(1 − *e*²), gravitational waves (transverse-traceless polarizations only), the FLRW cosmology, and Bekenstein–Hawking thermodynamics all follow as downstream theorems. Each of these is given an explicit theorem-chain derivation from *dx*₄/*dt* = i*c* in McGucken [56, 71], with the joint GR–QM unification developed in [57] and the underlying Light–Time–Dimension framework in [28].

**The McGucken Principle *dx*₄/*dt* = i*c* Reading of the Schwarzschild Geometry and the Classical Tests: The Static Spherically Symmetric Solution, Gravitational Time Dilation and Redshift, the Orbit Equation, the Perihelion Advance and the Bending of Light, with Proofs.**

**McGucken Principle anchor.** The McGucken Principle *dx*₄/*dt* = i*c* states that the fourth dimension is expanding at the velocity of light in a spherically symmetric manner from every spacetime event, as a McGucken Sphere whose wavelength sets the action *h*. In the presence of mass the advance of *x*₄ keeps its invariant rate and the three spatial dimensions are curved; the field equations of §9.2 state how. For a single spherical mass they predict the metric of Theorem 8.2.1, and from that metric the four classical tests follow. The statements follow GR Theorems 12–16 of [57]; every proof is carried out here in full.

**Theorem 8.2.1 (The static spherically symmetric vacuum solution; GR Theorem 12 of [57]).** Consider the metric *ds*² = −*A*(*r*) *c*²*dt*² + *B*(*r*) *dr*² + *r*²(*dθ*² + sin²*θ* *dφ*²) with *A*, *B* > 0, and the field equations of §9.2 with *T*_μν = 0 and Λ = 0, which are *R*_μν = 0. (i) The non-zero connection coefficients are Γ^t_tr = *A*′/(2*A*), Γ^r_tt = *c*²*A*′/(2*B*), Γ^r_rr = *B*′/(2*B*), Γ^r_θθ = −*r*/*B*, Γ^r_φφ = −*r* sin²*θ*/*B*, Γ^θ_rθ = Γ^φ_rφ = 1/*r*, Γ^θ_φφ = −sin *θ* cos *θ*, Γ^φ_θφ = cos *θ*/sin *θ*. (ii) The Ricci tensor is diagonal, with *R*_tt = *c*²[*A*″/(2*B*) − (*A*′/(4*B*))(*A*′/*A* + *B*′/*B*) + *A*′/(*rB*)], *R*_rr = −*A*″/(2*A*) + (*A*′/(4*A*))(*A*′/*A* + *B*′/*B*) + *B*′/(*rB*), *R*_θθ = 1 − 1/*B* − (*r*/(2*B*))(*A*′/*A* − *B*′/*B*), and *R*_φφ = sin²*θ* *R*_θθ. (iii) *R*_μν = 0 holds exactly when, after a constant rescaling of *t*, *A* = 1 − *r*_s/*r* and *B* = 1/*A* for a constant *r*_s. (iv) The motion of a slow body far from the centre is the Newtonian motion in the potential −*GM*/*r* exactly when *r*_s = 2*GM*/*c*².

*Proof.* (i) and (ii) follow from Γ^λ_μν = ½ *g*^λσ(∂_μ *g*_σν + ∂_ν *g*_σμ − ∂_σ *g*_μν) and *R*_μν = ∂_λ Γ^λ_μν − ∂_ν Γ^λ_μλ + Γ^λ_λσ Γ^σ_μν − Γ^λ_νσ Γ^σ_μλ by direct substitution of the diagonal metric. (iii) From (ii), *R*_tt/(*c*²*A*) + *R*_rr/*B* = (1/(*rB*))(*A*′/*A* + *B*′/*B*). If *R*_μν = 0 this vanishes, so (ln(*AB*))′ = 0 and *AB* is a constant *K*; replacing *t* by *t*/√*K* makes *AB* = 1. Then *B*′/*B* = −*A*′/*A*, and *R*_θθ = 1 − *A* − *rA*′ = 1 − (*rA*)′. Its vanishing gives *rA* = *r* − *r*_s with a constant *r*_s, that is *A* = 1 − *r*_s/*r*. Conversely, with *B* = 1/*A* the formula for *R*_tt becomes (*c*²*A*/(2*r*²))(*r*²*A*′)′, and *r*²*A*′ = *r*_s is constant, so *R*_tt = 0; then *R*_rr = 0 by the identity at the start of (iii), and *R*_θθ = *R*_φφ = 0 by construction. (iv) The action of a body is *S* = −*mc* ∫ (−*g*_μν *dx*^μ *dx*^ν)^(1/2) = ∫ *L* *dt* with *L* = −*mc*² (*A* − *w*²/*c*²)^(1/2), where *w*² = (*dr*/*dt*)²/*A* + *r*²((*dθ*/*dt*)² + sin²*θ* (*dφ*/*dt*)²). For *r*_s/*r* and *w*²/*c*² small, (1 − *r*_s/*r* − *w*²/*c*²)^(1/2) = 1 − *r*_s/(2*r*) − *w*²/(2*c*²) to first order and *w*² = *v*² to the same order, so *L* = −*mc*² + *mv*²/2 + *mc*²*r*_s/(2*r*). This is the Newtonian Lagrangian with potential energy −*mc*²*r*_s/(2*r*), which equals −*GMm*/*r* exactly when *r*_s = 2*GM*/*c*². □

**Theorem 8.2.2 (Gravitational time dilation and redshift; GR Theorems 13 and 14 of [57]).** In the metric of Theorem 8.2.1: (i) a clock at rest at radius *r* advances by *dτ* = *A*(*r*)^(1/2) *dt*; (ii) light emitted with frequency *ν*_e by a source at rest at *r*_e is received by an observer at rest at *r*_o with frequency *ν*_o = *ν*_e (*A*(*r*_e)/*A*(*r*_o))^(1/2).

*Proof.* (i) For a clock at rest *dr* = *dθ* = *dφ* = 0, so *c*²*dτ*² = −*ds*² = *A* *c*²*dt*². (ii) The metric does not depend on *t*, so if one signal follows the path **x**(*t*) from emitter to receiver, the path **x**(*t* − Δ*t*) shifted in *t* is also a signal path. Two successive wave crests therefore leave and arrive with the same separation Δ*t* in coordinate time. By (i) the proper period at the emitter is *A*(*r*_e)^(1/2) Δ*t* and at the receiver *A*(*r*_o)^(1/2) Δ*t*; the frequencies are the reciprocals. □

**Theorem 8.2.3 (The orbit equation).** For motion in the plane *θ* = *π*/2 in the metric of Theorem 8.2.1, let *u* = 1/*r* as a function of *φ*. (i) Along a geodesic, parametrized by proper time for a body and by an affine parameter for light, *A* *dt*/*dλ* = *e* and *r*² *dφ*/*dλ* = *ℓ* are constant. (ii) For a body, *d*²*u*/*dφ*² + *u* = *GM*/*ℓ*² + 3*GMu*²/*c*². (iii) For light, *d*²*u*/*dφ*² + *u* = 3*GMu*²/*c*².

*Proof.* (i) The geodesics are the extremals of ∫ ½ *g*_μν (*dx*^μ/*dλ*)(*dx*^ν/*dλ*) *dλ*. The integrand does not contain *t* or *φ*, so the Euler–Lagrange equations give the constancy of −*Ac*² *dt*/*dλ* and of *r*² sin²*θ* *dφ*/*dλ*; the equation for *θ* is satisfied by *θ* = *π*/2. (ii) and (iii) Along the geodesic *g*_μν (*dx*^μ/*dλ*)(*dx*^ν/*dλ*) = −*κc*², with *κ* = 1 for a body and *κ* = 0 for light. With (i) this reads −*c*²*e*²/*A* + (*dr*/*dλ*)²/*A* + *ℓ*²/*r*² = −*κc*², so (*dr*/*dλ*)² = *c*²*e*² − *A*(*κc*² + *ℓ*²*u*²). Since *dr*/*dλ* = (*dr*/*dφ*)(*ℓ*/*r*²) = −*ℓ* *du*/*dφ*, this is *ℓ*²(*du*/*dφ*)² = *c*²*e*² − (1 − *r*_s*u*)(*κc*² + *ℓ*²*u*²). Differentiating with respect to *φ* and dividing by 2*ℓ*² *du*/*dφ* gives *d*²*u*/*dφ*² = *κc*²*r*_s/(2*ℓ*²) + (*r*_s/2)*u*² − *u* + *r*_s*u*², that is *d*²*u*/*dφ*² + *u* = *κc*²*r*_s/(2*ℓ*²) + (3/2)*r*_s*u*². With *r*_s = 2*GM*/*c*² this is (ii) for *κ* = 1 and (iii) for *κ* = 0. □

**Theorem 8.2.4 (Perihelion advance; GR Theorem 16 of [57]).** To first order in *GM*/(*c*²*r*), a bound orbit of semi-major axis *a* and eccentricity *e* advances its perihelion by Δ*φ* = 6*πGM*/(*c*²*a*(1 − *e*²)) per revolution.

*Proof.* Without the term 3*GMu*²/*c*² the solution of Theorem 8.2.3(ii) is the Kepler ellipse *u*₀ = (*GM*/*ℓ*²)(1 + *e* cos *φ*), for which *a*(1 − *e*²) = *ℓ*²/(*GM*). Put *u* = *u*₀ + *u*₁ with *u*₁ of first order. Then *u*₁″ + *u*₁ = 3*GMu*₀²/*c*² = (3*G*³*M*³/(*c*²*ℓ*⁴))(1 + 2*e* cos *φ* + *e*² cos²*φ*). The constant and the cos²*φ* terms produce bounded periodic terms in *u*₁. The term 2*e* cos *φ* produces *e* *φ* sin *φ*, since (*φ* sin *φ*)″ + *φ* sin *φ* = 2 cos *φ*. Hence, apart from bounded periodic terms, *u* = (*GM*/*ℓ*²)[1 + *e* cos *φ* + *δ* *e* *φ* sin *φ*] with *δ* = 3*G*²*M*²/(*c*²*ℓ*²), and to first order in *δ* this is (*GM*/*ℓ*²)[1 + *e* cos((1 − *δ*)*φ*)]. Successive perihelia occur when (1 − *δ*)*φ* increases by 2*π*, that is when *φ* increases by 2*π*(1 + *δ*) to first order. The advance per revolution is 2*πδ* = 6*πG*²*M*²/(*c*²*ℓ*²) = 6*πGM*/(*c*²*a*(1 − *e*²)). □

**Theorem 8.2.5 (Bending of light; GR Theorem 15 of [57]).** To first order in *GM*/(*c*²*b*), a light ray passing a mass *M* with impact parameter *b* is deflected through the angle 4*GM*/(*c*²*b*).

*Proof.* Without the right side the solution of Theorem 8.2.3(iii) is the straight line *u*₀ = sin *φ*/*b*. Put *u* = *u*₀ + *u*₁. Then *u*₁″ + *u*₁ = (3*GM*/(*c*²*b*²)) sin²*φ*, and a solution is *u*₁ = (*GM*/(*c*²*b*²))(1 + cos²*φ*), since (1 + cos²*φ*)″ + (1 + cos²*φ*) = −2 cos 2*φ* + 1 + cos²*φ* = 3 sin²*φ*. The ray is at infinity where *u* = 0. Near *φ* = 0, with *φ* = −*α* small, *u* = −*α*/*b* + 2*GM*/(*c*²*b*²) to first order, which vanishes at *α* = 2*GM*/(*c*²*b*). By the symmetry of the solution under *φ* → *π* − *φ* the other end is at *φ* = *π* + *α*. The two directions differ from a straight line by 2*α* = 4*GM*/(*c*²*b*). □

**The McGucken Principle *dx*₄/*dt* = i*c* reading of the classical tests.** In Theorem 8.2.2 the clock rate *A*^(1/2) is the factor by which the proper time of a clock at rest in the curved spatial geometry differs from the coordinate time. In Theorems 8.2.4 and 8.2.5 the term 3*GMu*²/*c*² of the orbit equation is the contribution of the spatial curvature *B* = 1/*A*; without it the orbit equation is Newton’s. The Schwarzschild metric used in Theorem 8.6.12 is that of Theorem 8.2.1.

**The McGucken Principle *dx*₄/*dt* = i*c* Reading of Gravitational Waves and of the Friedmann–Lemaître–Robertson–Walker Cosmology, with Proofs.** **McGucken Principle anchor.** The McGucken Principle *dx*₄/*dt* = i*c* states that the fourth dimension is expanding at the velocity of light in a spherically symmetric manner from every spacetime event, as a McGucken Sphere whose wavelength sets the action *h*. By the McGucken-Invariance Lemma the rate of *x*₄ is unchanged by gravitation, and in the chart of §8.2 the metric has *g*₀₀ = −1 and *g*_{0j} = 0 with *x*⁰ = *ct*: mass curves and stretches *x*₁, *x*₂, *x*₃. For weak fields the Principle therefore predicts waves that distort the three spatial dimensions alone, transverse to their direction of travel, moving at the rate *c* of the expansion of *x*₄ (Theorem 8.2.6). For a uniform universe it predicts a spatial scale factor *a*(*t*) governed by the Friedmann equations, with the coordinate *t* of the Principle as cosmic time (Theorem 8.2.7). These are GR Theorems 19 and 20 of [57].

**Theorem 8.2.6 (Linearized field equations and gravitational waves; GR Theorem 19 of [57]).** Let *g*_μν = *η*_μν + *s* *h*_μν with *h*_μν a smooth symmetric field, and denote by a superscript (1) the coefficient of *s* in the expansion in powers of *s*. Write *h* = *η*^μν*h*_μν, □ = *η*^μν∂_μ∂_ν, and *H*_μν = *h*_μν − ½*η*_μν*h*, the trace-reversed field. (i) The Ricci tensor is *R*_μν^(1) = ½(∂^ρ∂_μ*h*_ρν + ∂^ρ∂_ν*h*_ρμ − ∂_μ∂_ν*h* − □*h*_μν) and *R*^(1) = ∂^ρ∂^σ*h*_ρσ − □*h*. (ii) The change of coordinates *x*^μ → *x*^μ + *s* *ξ*^μ replaces *h*_μν by *h*_μν − ∂_μ*ξ*_ν − ∂_ν*ξ*_μ and leaves *R*_μν^(1) unchanged. (iii) The linearized Einstein tensor is *G*_μν^(1) = −½□*H*_μν + ½(∂_μ∂^ρ*H*_ρν + ∂_ν∂^ρ*H*_ρμ − *η*_μν∂^ρ∂^σ*H*_ρσ). Hence, when ∂^ρ*H*_ρν = 0, the field equations with Λ = 0 and a matter tensor *s* *T*_μν are, to first order, □*H*_μν = −(16π*G*/*c*⁴) *T*_μν. (iv) In the chart of the McGucken-Invariance Lemma, *h*_00 = 0 and *h*_{0j} = 0. Let *h*_jk be the real part of *ε*_jk exp(i(**k**·**x** − *ωt*)) with *ω* ≠ 0 and a constant symmetric matrix *ε*_jk ≠ 0. Then *h* satisfies the vacuum equations *R*_μν^(1) = 0 if and only if *ε*_jj = 0, *k*_j*ε*_jk = 0 and *ω*² = *c*²|**k**|². For **k** along the third axis these matrices are the combinations of the two with *ε*₁₁ = −*ε*₂₂ = 1 and with *ε*₁₂ = *ε*₂₁ = 1, all other entries zero.

*Proof.* (i) To first order the connection coefficients are Γ^ρ_μν = ½*s*(∂_μ*h*^ρ_ν + ∂_ν*h*^ρ_μ − ∂^ρ*h*_μν), and the Ricci tensor ∂_ρ Γ^ρ_μν − ∂_ν Γ^ρ_μρ plus products of two connection coefficients has the first-order part ½(∂_ρ∂_μ*h*^ρ_ν + ∂_ρ∂_ν*h*^ρ_μ − □*h*_μν) − ½∂_ν∂_μ*h*, because Γ^ρ_μρ = ½*s*∂_μ*h*. Contracting with *η*^μν gives *R*^(1). (ii) The metric in the new coordinates is (∂*x*^α/∂*x*′^μ)(∂*x*^β/∂*x*′^ν)*g*_αβ, and ∂*x*^α/∂*x*′^μ = δ^α_μ − *s*∂_μ*ξ*^α to first order, which gives the stated change of *h*. Inserting ∂_μ*ξ*_ν + ∂_ν*ξ*_μ for *h*_μν in (i): the first two terms give □∂_μ*ξ*_ν + □∂_ν*ξ*_μ + 2∂_μ∂_ν∂^ρ*ξ*_ρ, the third gives −2∂_μ∂_ν∂^ρ*ξ*_ρ and the fourth −□∂_μ*ξ*_ν − □∂_ν*ξ*_μ; the sum is zero. (iii) Since *η*^μν*H*_μν = −*h*, *h*_μν = *H*_μν − ½*η*_μν*H* with *H* = *η*^μν*H*_μν. Inserting this in (i), the terms −½∂_μ∂_ν*H* − ½∂_μ∂_ν*H* from the first two terms cancel the term +∂_μ∂_ν*H* from the third, and *R*_μν^(1) = ½(∂_μ∂^ρ*H*_ρν + ∂_ν∂^ρ*H*_ρμ − □*H*_μν + ½*η*_μν□*H*). Its trace is *R*^(1) = ∂^ρ∂^σ*H*_ρσ + ½□*H*, and *G*_μν^(1) = *R*_μν^(1) − ½*η*_μν*R*^(1) is the stated expression. The field equations *G*_μν + Λ*g*_μν = (8π*G*/*c*⁴)*T*_μν hold at order zero for *η* with Λ = 0 and no matter, and at first order they read *G*_μν^(1) = (8π*G*/*c*⁴)*T*_μν.

(iv) Since *G*_μν^(1) = 0 is equivalent to *R*_μν^(1) = 0 (take the trace), use (i) with ∂₀ = *c*⁻¹∂ₜ, *h*_{0μ} = 0 and *h* = *h*_jj. The component 00 is *R*_00^(1) = −½∂₀∂₀*h*_jj, which for the wave is (*ω*²/2*c*²) times the real part of *ε*_jj exp(i(**k**·**x** − *ωt*)); it vanishes exactly when *ε*_jj = 0. The components 0*j* are *R*_{0j}^(1) = ½∂₀(∂_k*h*_kj − ∂_j*h*_kk), which for the wave is the real part of (*ω*/2*c*)(*k*_k*ε*_kj − *k*_j*ε*_kk) times the exponential; with *ε*_kk = 0 it vanishes exactly when *k*_k*ε*_kj = 0. With these two conditions the first three terms of (i) vanish in the components *jk*, and *R*_jk^(1) = −½□*h*_jk, which is ½(|**k**|² − *ω*²/*c*²) times *h*_jk; it vanishes exactly when *ω*² = *c*²|**k**|². For **k** = (0, 0, *k*) the condition *k*_j*ε*_jk = 0 leaves the entries with indices 1, 2, and *ε*₁₁ + *ε*₂₂ = 0 leaves the two stated matrices. □

**The McGucken Principle *dx*₄/*dt* = i*c* reading of Theorem 8.2.6.** In general relativity without the Principle the conditions *h*_00 = 0, *h*_{0j} = 0 are a choice of coordinates. Here they are the chart in which *x*₄ advances at its invariant rate, and part (iv) shows that in this chart the vacuum equations themselves make every wave transverse and traceless, with two polarizations, travelling at *c*. The wave is an oscillation of *x*₁, *x*₂, *x*₃; the rate of *x*₄ is unchanged by it.

**Theorem 8.2.7 (Friedmann–Lemaître–Robertson–Walker cosmology; GR Theorem 20 of [57]).** Consider the metric *ds*² = −*c*²*dt*² + *a*(*t*)²[*dr*²/(1 − *kr*²) + *r*²(*dθ*² + sin²*θ* *dφ*²)] with *a*(*t*) > 0 and a constant *k*, write *γ*_jk for the metric in the square brackets, and denote *d*/*dt* by a prime. (i) The metric *γ*_jk has the Ricci tensor 2*k* *γ*_jk: the slices *t* = constant have the same curvature at every point and in every direction. (ii) The curvature is *R*_tt = −3*a*″/*a*, *R*_jk = (*aa*″ + 2*a*′² + 2*kc*²)*γ*_jk/*c*², the mixed components vanish, *R* = (6/*c*²)(*a*″/*a* + *a*′²/*a*² + *kc*²/*a*²), *G*_tt = 3(*a*′² + *kc*²)/*a*² and *G*_jk = −(2*aa*″ + *a*′² + *kc*²)*γ*_jk/*c*². (iii) For matter of density *ρ*(*t*) and pressure *p*(*t*) at rest in these coordinates, *T*_μν = (*ρ* + *p*/*c*²)*u*_μ*u*_ν + *p* *g*_μν with *u*^μ = (1, 0, 0, 0), the field equations *G*_μν + Λ*g*_μν = (8π*G*/*c*⁴)*T*_μν are equivalent to the two Friedmann equations

(*a*′/*a*)² = (8π*G*/3)*ρ* − *kc*²/*a*² + Λ*c*²/3,

*a*″/*a* = −(4π*G*/3)(*ρ* + 3*p*/*c*²) + Λ*c*²/3.

(iv) They imply *ρ*′ = −3(*a*′/*a*)(*ρ* + *p*/*c*²). (v) For *k* = 0 and Λ = 0, with *a*(0) = 0 and *a*′ > 0: if *p* = 0 then *ρa*³ is constant, *a* is proportional to *t*^(2/3) and (*a*′/*a*)*t* = 2/3; if *p* = *ρc*²/3 then *ρa*⁴ is constant, *a* is proportional to *t*^(1/2) and (*a*′/*a*)*t* = 1/2.
