For *ρ* = 0, *p* = 0, *k* = 0 and Λ > 0, *a* is proportional to exp(±*Ht*) with *H* = *c*(Λ/3)^(1/2). (vi) On any interval of time on which (8π*G*/3)*ρ* + Λ*c*²/3 − *kc*²/*a*² > 0, *a*′ has one sign throughout.

*Proof.* (i), (ii) The connection coefficients that do not vanish are Γ^t_rr = *aa*′/(*c*²(1 − *kr*²)), Γ^t_θθ = *aa*′*r*²/*c*², Γ^t_φφ = *aa*′*r*² sin²*θ*/*c*², Γ^r_tr = Γ^θ_tθ = Γ^φ_tφ = *a*′/*a*, Γ^r_rr = *kr*/(1 − *kr*²), Γ^r_θθ = −*r*(1 − *kr*²), Γ^r_φφ = −*r*(1 − *kr*²) sin²*θ*, Γ^θ_rθ = Γ^φ_rφ = 1/*r*, Γ^θ_φφ = −sin*θ* cos*θ*, Γ^φ_θφ = cos*θ*/sin*θ*, and those obtained by exchanging the lower indices; each follows from Γ^λ_μν = ½*g*^λσ(∂_μ*g*_σν + ∂_ν*g*_σμ − ∂_σ*g*_μν) for the diagonal metric. Those with three spatial indices are the connection coefficients of *γ*_jk. Inserting them in *R*_μν = ∂_ρ Γ^ρ_μν − ∂_ν Γ^ρ_μρ + Γ^ρ_ρσ Γ^σ_μν − Γ^ρ_νσ Γ^σ_μρ gives for *γ*_jk the Ricci tensor 2*k* *γ*_jk and for the four-dimensional metric the stated components. For example, *R*_tt = −∂ₜ(3*a*′/*a*) − 3(*a*′/*a*)² = −3*a*″/*a*. The scalar is *R* = −*R*_tt/*c*² + *γ*^jk*R*_jk/*a*² with *γ*^jk*γ*_jk = 3, and *G*_μν = *R*_μν − ½*g*_μν*R* with *g*_tt = −*c*², *g*_jk = *a*²*γ*_jk. (iii) Lowering the index, *u*_t = −*c*² and *u*_j = 0, so *T*_tt = (*ρ* + *p*/*c*²)*c*⁴ − *pc*² = *ρc*⁴ and *T*_jk = *pa*²*γ*_jk. The component *tt* of the field equations is 3(*a*′² + *kc*²)/*a*² − Λ*c*² = 8π*Gρ*, the first Friedmann equation. The components *jk* are −(2*aa*″ + *a*′² + *kc*²)/*c*² + Λ*a*² = 8π*Gpa*²/*c*⁴; replacing *a*′² + *kc*² by its value from the first equation gives the second, and conversely. The mixed components vanish on both sides.

(iv) Differentiating the first equation, 2(*a*′/*a*)(*a*″/*a* − *a*′²/*a*²) = (8π*G*/3)*ρ*′ + 2*kc*²*a*′/*a*³. By the two equations, *a*″/*a* − *a*′²/*a*² = −4π*G*(*ρ* + *p*/*c*²) + *kc*²/*a*². Inserting this and cancelling the terms in *k* gives (8π*G*/3)*ρ*′ = −8π*G*(*a*′/*a*)(*ρ* + *p*/*c*²). (v) For *p* = 0, (iv) gives *ρ*′/*ρ* = −3*a*′/*a*, so *ρa*³ = *C*; then *a*′² = (8π*GC*/3)/*a*, so *a*^(1/2)*a*′ is constant and *a*^(3/2) is proportional to *t*. For *p* = *ρc*²/3, *ρ*′/*ρ* = −4*a*′/*a*, so *ρa*⁴ = *C*; then *aa*′ is constant and *a*² is proportional to *t*. For a power *a* = *Bt*^n, (*a*′/*a*)*t* = *n*. In the third case (*a*′/*a*)² = Λ*c*²/3. (vi) By the first Friedmann equation *a*′² is the stated positive quantity times *a*², so *a*′ has no zero on the interval, and a continuous function without a zero on an interval has one sign. □

**The McGucken Principle *dx*₄/*dt* = i*c* reading of Theorem 8.2.7.** The coordinate *t* of the metric is the *t* of *dx*₄/*dt* = i*c*: *g*_tt = −*c*² at every event, the invariant rate of *x*₄. The factor *a*(*t*) scales *x*₁, *x*₂, *x*₃ alone. That the slices have the form *γ*_jk is the statement of uniformity, part (i); the theorem that every three-dimensional metric with the same curvature at every point and in every direction has this form locally is an external theorem of Riemannian geometry and is not used in the proof.

**8.3 Quantum Mechanics.** The wave equation □*ψ* = 0 follows from Huygens’ principle applied to the spherically symmetric expansion of *x*₄. The de Broglie relation *p* = *h*/*λ* expresses the *x*₄-phase accumulated per unit of spatial motion; the Planck–Einstein relation *E* = *hν* expresses one quantum of action per *x*₄-cycle; the Compton frequency ω_C = *mc*²/ℏ expresses the rest-mass *x*₄-oscillation rate. From the Klein–Gordon equation (factored from the mass-shell condition *E*²/*c*² − |**p**|² = *m*²*c*²) the non-relativistic limit yields the Schrödinger equation iℏ ∂*ψ*/∂*t* = *Ĥψ*. The Dirac equation arises as the Clifford-square-root of Klein–Gordon, with the matter orientation condition (an even-grade multivector in Cl(1,3) carrying matter *x*₄-orientation at Compton frequency) yielding spin-1/2 via 4π-periodicity of the spinor representation. The canonical commutator [*q̂*, *p̂*] = iℏ follows by Stone’s theorem on the Hamiltonian side and by path-integral derivation on the Lagrangian side. The Born rule *P* = |*ψ*|² emerges as the unique phase-invariant quadratic measure on complex amplitudes—complex because of the i in *dx*₄/*dt* = i*c*. Tsirelson’s bound 2√2, the path integral ∫*𝒟x* exp(i*S*/ℏ) (as iterated McGucken Spheres), and second quantization with Pauli exclusion (via the same 4π-periodicity that produced spin) all follow. The full derivation of quantum mechanics from *dx*₄/*dt* = i*c*—wave equation, de Broglie, Planck–Einstein, Klein–Gordon, Schrödinger, Dirac, canonical commutator, and Born rule—is given as a theorem chain in McGucken [58], with the joint GR–QM unification in [57].

**The McGucken Principle *dx*₄/*dt* = i*c* Reading of Quantum Mechanics: The Action Clause, the Compton Frequency, the Planck–Einstein and de Broglie Relations, the Klein–Gordon Equation, the Schrödinger Equation, the Canonical Commutator and the Uncertainty Relation, with Proofs.**

**McGucken Principle anchor.** The McGucken Principle *dx*₄/*dt* = i*c* states that the fourth dimension is expanding at the velocity of light in a spherically symmetric manner from every spacetime event, as a McGucken Sphere whose wavelength sets the action *h*. For quantum mechanics the Principle predicts the following: because each wavelength of the expansion of *x*₄ carries the action *h*, a body whose action is *S* carries the phase *S*/ℏ, and the frequency, the wavelength, the wave equations and the commutator of quantum mechanics are properties of that phase. The statements below follow Proposition 2.2, Definition 2.2.bis.1, Proposition 2.2.bis.1, Theorem 5.1 and Theorem 9.0 of [72] and QM Theorems 2, 3, 5, 7 and 8 of [57]; every proof is carried out here in full.

**The action clause of the McGucken Principle (Proposition 2.2 of [72]).** The McGucken Principle *dx*₄/*dt* = i*c* has two dimensional parameters. (i) The constant *c* is the rate of the expansion: |*dx*₄/*dt*| = *c*. (ii) The constant *h* is the action carried per oscillation of that expansion: the expansion is a wavefront whose wavelength sets the action, each oscillatory cycle of *x*₄-advance carrying one quantum of action *h* = 2*π*ℏ. A cycle is a phase advance of 2*π*, so the phase of a wavefront that has accumulated the action *S* is *S*/ℏ, and the amplitude it contributes is exp(i*S*/ℏ). The McGucken wavefunction (Definition 2.6 of [73], §9.3) is the sum of these amplitudes over paths, *ψ* = Σ_γ exp(i*S*[*γ*]/ℏ); sums of such amplitudes are again amplitudes.

**Theorem 8.3.1 (Compton frequency and the Compton cycle; Definition 2.2.bis.1, Proposition 2.2.bis.1 and Corollary 2.2.bis.1 of [72]; QM Theorem 5 of [57]).** For a body of rest mass *m* at rest in an inertial coordinate system: (i) its amplitude is *ψ* = exp(−i*ω*_C *t*) with the Compton angular frequency *ω*_C = *mc*²/ℏ; (ii) one cycle lasts Δ*t*_C = 2*π*ℏ/(*mc*²); (iii) during one cycle *x*₄ advances by Δ*x*₄ = i*λ*_C, where *λ*_C = *h*/(*mc*) is the Compton wavelength; (iv) during one cycle the action accumulated has magnitude *h*, whatever the mass *m*.

*Proof.* (i) By Proposition 8.7.1 the action of the body is *S* = −*mc*² ∫ *dτ*, and at rest *τ* = *t* (Theorem 8.1.5 with *v* = 0), so *S* = −*mc*²*t*. By the action clause the amplitude is exp(i*S*/ℏ) = exp(−i*mc*²*t*/ℏ) = exp(−i*ω*_C *t*). (ii) The phase *ω*_C *t* advances by 2*π* in the time 2*π*/*ω*_C = 2*π*ℏ/(*mc*²). (iii) By the Principle *x*₄ advances at the rate i*c*, so in one cycle Δ*x*₄ = i*c* Δ*t*_C = 2*π*iℏ/(*mc*) = i*h*/(*mc*) = i*λ*_C. (iv) The magnitude |*S*| grows at the rate *mc*², so in one cycle it grows by *mc*² · 2*π*ℏ/(*mc*²) = 2*π*ℏ = *h*; the factors *mc*² cancel, so the result holds for every *m*. □

**Theorem 8.3.2 (Planck–Einstein relation *E* = *hν* and de Broglie relation *p* = *h*/*λ*; QM Theorems 2 and 3 of [57]).** Let a body of rest mass *m* move with velocity **v** in an inertial coordinate system (*t*, **x**), with energy *E* = *γmc*² and momentum **p** = *γm***v** (Theorem 8.1.10). (i) Its amplitude is *ψ* = exp(i(**p**·**x** − *Et*)/ℏ). (ii) The angular frequency of this wave is *ω* = *E*/ℏ and its wave vector is **k** = **p**/ℏ; hence *E* = ℏ*ω* = *hν* and |**p**| = ℏ|**k**| = *h*/*λ*, with *ν* = *ω*/2*π* the frequency and *λ* = 2*π*/|**k**| the wavelength. (iii) The frequency and wave vector satisfy *ω*² = *c*²|**k**|² + *ω*_C². (iv) The action of the straight worldline from the origin to the event (*t*, **x**), *S*(*t*, **x**) = −*mc* (*c*²*t*² − |**x**|²)^(1/2), has gradient ∇*S* = **p** and time derivative ∂ₜ*S* = −*E*.

*Proof.* (i) Let (*t*′, **x**′) be the inertial coordinate system in which the body is at rest, with the *x*-axis along **v**. By Theorem 8.3.1 the amplitude there is exp(−i*mc*²*t*′/ℏ). By Theorem 8.1.7, *t*′ = *γ*(*t* − **v**·**x**/*c*²). Hence −*mc*²*t*′ = −*γmc*²*t* + *γm* **v**·**x** = **p**·**x** − *Et*, and the amplitude is exp(i(**p**·**x** − *Et*)/ℏ). (ii) A wave exp(i(**k**·**x** − *ωt*)) has angular frequency *ω* and wave vector **k**; comparison with (i) gives *ω* = *E*/ℏ and **k** = **p**/ℏ. (iii) By Theorem 8.1.10(iii), *E*² = |**p**|²*c*² + *m*²*c*⁴; dividing by ℏ² gives *ω*² = *c*²|**k**|² + (*mc*²/ℏ)². (iv) Along the straight worldline from the origin to (*t*, **x**) the velocity is **v** = **x**/*t* and the proper time is *τ* = *t*/*γ* = (*t*² − |**x**|²/*c*²)^(1/2), so *S* = −*mc*²*τ* = −*mc* (*c*²*t*² − |**x**|²)^(1/2). Differentiating, ∇*S* = *mc* **x** (*c*²*t*² − |**x**|²)^(−1/2) = *m***x**/*τ* = *γm***v** = **p**, and ∂ₜ*S* = −*mc*·*c*²*t* (*c*²*t*² − |**x**|²)^(−1/2) = −*mc*²*t*/*τ* = −*γmc*² = −*E*. □

**The McGucken Principle *dx*₄/*dt* = i*c* reading of light.** For *m* → 0 at fixed **p**, part (iii) becomes *ω* = *c*|**k**| and the relations *E* = ℏ*ω*, **p** = ℏ**k** of part (ii) are unchanged, with *E* = |**p**|*c*. The Compton frequency is zero: the photon is at rest in *x*₄ (Theorem 8.1.6), and its wave advances with the McGucken Sphere at speed *c*.

**Theorem 8.3.3 (Klein–Gordon equation; Theorem 9.0 of [72]; QM Theorem 8 of [57]).** Write □ = ∇² − *c*⁻²∂ₜ² and *μ* = *mc*/ℏ. (i) Every amplitude of Theorem 8.3.2 satisfies (□ − *μ*²)*ψ* = 0. (ii) Every superposition *ψ*(*t*, **x**) = ∫ *a*(**k**) exp(i(**k**·**x** − *ω*(**k**)*t*)) *d*³*k*, with *a* a Schwartz function and *ω*(**k**) = *c* √(|**k**|² + *μ*²), satisfies (□ − *μ*²)*ψ* = 0. (iii) Conversely, every solution of (□ − *μ*²)*ψ* = 0 that is a Schwartz function of **x** at each *t* is *ψ* = ∫ [*a*(**k**) exp(−i*ω*(**k**)*t*) + *b*(**k**) exp(i*ω*(**k**)*t*)] exp(i**k**·**x**) *d*³*k*. (iv) For *m* = 0 the equation is the wave equation □*ψ* = 0.

*Proof.* (i) For *ψ* = exp(i(**k**·**x** − *ωt*)), ∇²*ψ* = −|**k**|²*ψ* and ∂ₜ²*ψ* = −*ω*²*ψ*, so □*ψ* = (*ω*²/*c*² − |**k**|²)*ψ*. By Theorem 8.3.2(iii), *ω*²/*c*² − |**k**|² = *ω*_C²/*c*² = *m*²*c*²/ℏ² = *μ*². (ii) For a Schwartz function *a* the integral and its derivatives of every order converge absolutely, so differentiation under the integral is permitted, and each integrand satisfies the equation by (i). (iii) Take the Fourier transform in **x**: *ψ*(*t*, **x**) = ∫ *A*(*t*, **k**) exp(i**k**·**x**) *d*³*k*. The equation becomes ∂ₜ²*A* = −*c*²(|**k**|² + *μ*²)*A* = −*ω*(**k**)²*A* for each **k**, an ordinary differential equation in *t* whose solutions are *a*(**k**) exp(−i*ω*(**k**)*t*) + *b*(**k**) exp(i*ω*(**k**)*t*). (iv) Set *μ* = 0. □

**The McGucken Principle *dx*₄/*dt* = i*c* reading of the Klein–Gordon equation.** The term *μ*² = *ω*_C²/*c*² is the square of the Compton frequency of Theorem 8.3.1 divided by *c*²: the mass term of the equation is the rate at which the body’s phase advances as *x*₄ expands. The equation is of second order because the interval of Lemma 8.1.1 is quadratic. In part (iii) the amplitudes exp(−i*ω*t) are those of Theorem 8.3.2, with phase *S*/ℏ for the orientation +i*c* of the Principle; the amplitudes exp(+i*ω*t) are their complex conjugates.

**Theorem 8.3.4 (Schrödinger equation; QM Theorem 7 of [57]; Theorem 9.1 of [72]).** Let *ψ* be a superposition of the form of Theorem 8.3.3(ii) and put *ψ* = exp(−i*ω*_C *t*) *φ*. (i) Exactly, iℏ ∂ₜ*φ* = −(ℏ²/2*m*) ∇²*φ* + (ℏ²/2*mc*²) ∂ₜ²*φ*. (ii) Exactly, iℏ ∂ₜ*φ* = −(ℏ²/2*m*) ∇²*φ* − *Rφ*, where *R* multiplies the Fourier transform of *φ* by *r*(**k**) with 0 ≤ *r*(**k**) ≤ ℏ⁴|**k**|⁴/(8*m*³*c*²). (iii) In a potential energy *V*(**x**), entering through the covariant derivative of §9.4 as ∂ₜ → ∂ₜ + i*V*/ℏ, the equation of part (i) becomes iℏ ∂ₜ*φ* = −(ℏ²/2*m*) ∇²*φ* + *Vφ* + (ℏ²/2*mc*²)(∂ₜ + i*V*/ℏ)²*φ*. The Schrödinger equation iℏ ∂ₜ*φ* = −(ℏ²/2*m*) ∇²*φ* + *Vφ* is the equation that remains when the terms carrying the factor 1/*c*² are dropped.

*Proof.* (i) We have ∂ₜ²*ψ* = exp(−i*ω*_C *t*)(−*ω*_C²*φ* − 2i*ω*_C ∂ₜ*φ* + ∂ₜ²*φ*) and ∇²*ψ* = exp(−i*ω*_C *t*) ∇²*φ*. Substituting in (□ − *μ*²)*ψ* = 0 and using *ω*_C²/*c*² = *μ*², the terms in *φ* without derivatives cancel, leaving ∇²*φ* + (2i*ω*_C/*c*²) ∂ₜ*φ* − *c*⁻²∂ₜ²*φ* = 0. Since 2*ω*_C/*c*² = 2*m*/ℏ, multiplying by ℏ²/2*m* gives iℏ ∂ₜ*φ* = −(ℏ²/2*m*) ∇²*φ* + (ℏ²/2*mc*²) ∂ₜ²*φ*. (ii) The Fourier transform of *φ* is *a*(**k**) exp(−i(*ω*(**k**) − *ω*_C)*t*), so iℏ ∂ₜ multiplies it by ℏ(*ω*(**k**) − *ω*_C). Put *x* = |**k**|²/*μ*². Then ℏ(*ω* − *ω*_C) = *mc*²((1 + *x*)^(1/2) − 1) and ℏ²|**k**|²/2*m* = *mc*² *x*/2, and for *x* ≥ 0, 0 ≤ 1 + *x*/2 − (1 + *x*)^(1/2) ≤ *x*²/8. [Both bounds follow from (1 + *x*/2)² − (1 + *x*) = *x*²/4 ≥ 0: dividing by (1 + *x*/2) + (1 + *x*)^(1/2) ≥ 2 gives the difference, which is at most *x*²/8.] Hence *r*(**k**) = ℏ²|**k**|²/2*m* − ℏ(*ω* − *ω*_C) satisfies 0 ≤ *r* ≤ *mc*² *x*²/8 = ℏ⁴|**k**|⁴/(8*m*³*c*²). (iii) Replacing ∂ₜ by ∂ₜ + i*V*/ℏ in the equation (□ − *μ*²)*ψ* = 0 and repeating the computation of (i) with ∂ₜ*φ* replaced by (∂ₜ + i*V*/ℏ)*φ* gives iℏ(∂ₜ + i*V*/ℏ)*φ* = −(ℏ²/2*m*) ∇²*φ* + (ℏ²/2*mc*²)(∂ₜ + i*V*/ℏ)²*φ*, and iℏ · i*V*/ℏ = −*V*. □

**Theorem 8.3.5 (Momentum operator and canonical commutator; Theorem 5.1 of [72]).** (i) There is exactly one linear vector operator *p̂* = (*p̂*₁, *p̂*₂, *p̂*₃) on the superpositions *f*(**x**) = ∫ *a*(**k**) exp(i**k**·**x**) *d*³*k*, *a* a Schwartz function, that multiplies each wave exp(i**k**·**x**) by its momentum ℏ**k** of Theorem 8.3.2: it is *p̂* = −iℏ∇. (ii) With *q̂*_j the multiplication by *x*_j, [*q̂*_j, *p̂*_k] *f* = iℏ δ_jk *f* for every Schwartz function *f*.

*Proof.* (i) An operator that is linear and multiplies exp(i**k**·**x**) by ℏ**k** sends *f* to ∫ ℏ**k** *a*(**k**) exp(i**k**·**x**) *d*³*k*, which determines it on every superposition. Since −iℏ∇ exp(i**k**·**x**) = ℏ**k** exp(i**k**·**x**), and differentiation under the integral is permitted for Schwartz *a*, that integral equals −iℏ∇*f*. Every Schwartz function *f* is such a superposition, by Fourier inversion. (ii) By the product rule, *p̂*_k(*x*_j *f*) = −iℏ δ_jk *f* − iℏ *x*_j ∂_k *f*, and *x*_j *p̂*_k *f* = −iℏ *x*_j ∂_k *f*. Subtracting, *x*_j *p̂*_k *f* − *p̂*_k(*x*_j *f*) = iℏ δ_jk *f*. □

**The McGucken Principle *dx*₄/*dt* = i*c* reading of iℏ.** In [*q̂*, *p̂*] = iℏ the factor i is the i of the phase exp(i*S*/ℏ), which is the i of *dx*₄/*dt* = i*c*, the mark of the perpendicularity of *x*₄ to the three spatial dimensions; the factor ℏ is the action per cycle of the action clause (Theorem 5.1 of [72]).

**Theorem 8.3.6 (Uncertainty relation).** For a Schwartz function *f* with ∫ |*f*|² *d*³*x* = 1, let ⟨*A*⟩ = ∫ *f*∗ *Af* *d*³*x*, and let *σ*_q² = ⟨(*q̂* − ⟨*q̂*⟩)²⟩ and *σ*_p² = ⟨(*p̂* − ⟨*p̂*⟩)²⟩ for *q̂* = *q̂*_j and *p̂* = *p̂*_j with the same index *j*. Then *σ*_q *σ*_p ≥ ℏ/2.

*Proof.* Put *A* = *q̂* − ⟨*q̂*⟩ and *B* = *p̂* − ⟨*p̂*⟩. Both are symmetric on Schwartz functions: for *q̂* because *x*_j is real, for *p̂* by integration by parts, so ⟨*q̂*⟩ and ⟨*p̂*⟩ are real and ∫ (*Ag*)∗ *h* = ∫ *g*∗ *Ah*, likewise for *B*. Constants commute with everything, so [*A*, *B*] = [*q̂*, *p̂*] = iℏ by Theorem 8.3.5. Then iℏ = ⟨[*A*, *B*]⟩ = ∫ (*Af*)∗ *Bf* − ∫ (*Bf*)∗ *Af* = 2i Im ∫ (*Af*)∗ *Bf*. By the Cauchy–Schwarz inequality, ℏ = 2 |Im ∫ (*Af*)∗ *Bf*| ≤ 2 ‖*Af*‖ ‖*Bf*‖ = 2 *σ*_q *σ*_p, since ‖*Af*‖² = ∫ *f*∗ *A*²*f* = *σ*_q² and ‖*Bf*‖² = *σ*_p². □

**The McGucken Principle *dx*₄/*dt* = i*c* reading of the 1900–1926 postulates.** Planck introduced *E* = *hν* in 1900 and de Broglie introduced *p* = *h*/*λ* in 1924 as postulates, and Schrödinger’s equation of 1926 was written down to fit them. Here *E* = *hν* and *p* = *h*/*λ* are Theorem 8.3.2, proved from the action clause of the Principle and the Lorentz transformation of Theorem 8.1.7; the Klein–Gordon and Schrödinger equations are Theorems 8.3.3 and 8.3.4; and the commutator and the uncertainty relation are Theorems 8.3.5 and 8.3.6.

**The McGucken Principle *dx*₄/*dt* = i*c* Reading of Spin, the Sum over Paths, the Tsirelson Bound and Exclusion, with Proofs.**

**McGucken Principle anchor.** The McGucken Principle *dx*₄/*dt* = i*c* states that the fourth dimension is expanding at the velocity of light in a spherically symmetric manner from every spacetime event, as a McGucken Sphere whose wavelength sets the action *h*. For the four results below the Principle supplies the objects on which they are proved: the Dirac matrices of Theorem 8.5.3, the amplitude exp(i*S*/ℏ) of the action clause, and the Hilbert space of Theorem 8.4.3. The statements follow QM Theorems 9, 13, 15 and 20 of [57].

**Theorem 8.3.7 (Spin ½ and the 4*π* period; QM Theorem 9 of [57]).** Let *γ*^μ be matrices as in Theorem 8.5.3, put *J* = *γ*¹*γ*² and *S*₃ = (iℏ/2)*J*, and let *R*(*θ*) = exp(−i*θS*₃/ℏ). (i) *J*² = −1 and *S*₃² = ℏ²/4, so the eigenvalues of *S*₃ are ±ℏ/2. (ii) *R*(*θ*) = cos(*θ*/2) + *J* sin(*θ*/2). (iii) *R*(*θ*) *γ*¹ *R*(*θ*)⁻¹ = *γ*¹ cos *θ* + *γ*² sin *θ*, *R*(*θ*) *γ*² *R*(*θ*)⁻¹ = *γ*² cos *θ* − *γ*¹ sin *θ*, and *R*(*θ*) commutes with *γ*⁰ and *γ*³: *R*(*θ*) acts on the Dirac matrices as the rotation through the angle *θ* in the (*x*₁, *x*₂) plane. (iv) *R*(2*π*) = −1 and *R*(4*π*) = 1.

*Proof.* (i) By the relation of Theorem 8.5.3, *γ*¹*γ*² = −*γ*²*γ*¹ and (*γ*¹)² = (*γ*²)² = −1. Hence *J*² = *γ*¹*γ*²*γ*¹*γ*² = −(*γ*¹)²(*γ*²)² = −1, and *S*₃² = (iℏ/2)² *J*² = ℏ²/4. A matrix with *S*₃² = ℏ²/4 has eigenvalues among ±ℏ/2. (ii) −i*θS*₃/ℏ = (*θ*/2) *J*, and since *J*² = −1 the exponential series separates into cos(*θ*/2) + *J* sin(*θ*/2). (iii) *J* *γ*¹ = *γ*¹*γ*²*γ*¹ = −(*γ*¹)²*γ*² = *γ*², and *γ*¹ *J* = (*γ*¹)²*γ*² = −*γ*², so *J* *γ*¹ = −*γ*¹ *J*. Therefore *γ*¹ *R*(*θ*)⁻¹ = *γ*¹ (cos(*θ*/2) − *J* sin(*θ*/2)) = *R*(*θ*) *γ*¹, and *R*(*θ*) *γ*¹ *R*(*θ*)⁻¹ = *R*(*θ*)² *γ*¹ = (cos *θ* + *J* sin *θ*) *γ*¹ = *γ*¹ cos *θ* + *γ*² sin *θ*. In the same way *J* *γ*² = *γ*¹(*γ*²)² = −*γ*¹ gives the second formula. *γ*⁰ and *γ*³ each anticommute with *γ*¹ and with *γ*², hence commute with *J* and with *R*(*θ*). (iv) *R*(2*π*) = cos *π* + *J* sin *π* = −1, and *R*(4*π*) = cos 2*π* = 1. □

**The McGucken Principle *dx*₄/*dt* = i*c* reading of spin.** A rotation of the coordinates through *θ* carries a solution *ψ* of the Dirac equation of Theorem 8.5.3 into *R*(*θ*)*ψ* evaluated at the rotated coordinates, by part (iii). A rotation through 2*π* returns the coordinates to themselves and multiplies *ψ* by −1; only a rotation through 4*π* returns *ψ*. The component of angular momentum carried by *ψ* itself has the values ±ℏ/2.

**Theorem 8.3.8 (The free propagator as a sum over paths; QM Theorem 15 of [57]).** For *t* > 0 let *U*(*t*) be the free evolution of Theorem 8.4.1, which multiplies the Fourier transform by exp(−iℏ|**k**|²*t*/2*m*), and let *K*_t(**x**) = (*m*/(2*π*iℏ*t*))^(3/2) exp(i*m*|**x**|²/(2ℏ*t*)). (i) The exponent of *K*_t is i*S*/ℏ, where *S* = *m*|**x**|²/(2*t*) is the action ∫ (*mv*²/2) *dt* of the straight path from the origin to **x** in the time *t*. (ii) For a Schwartz function *φ*₀, (*U*(*t*)*φ*₀)(**x**) = ∫ *K*_t(**x** − **y**) *φ*₀(**y**) *d*³*y*. (iii) *U*(*t*) = *U*(*t*/*N*)^N for every *N*, so that (*U*(*t*)*φ*₀)(**x**) is the integral over **x**₀, …, **x**_(N−1), with **x**_N = **x**, of (*m*/(2*π*iℏΔ))^(3*N*/2) exp((i/ℏ) Σ_j *m*|**x**_(j+1) − **x**_j|²/(2Δ)) *φ*₀(**x**₀), Δ = *t*/*N*: a sum, over the broken-line paths through **x**₀, …, **x**_N, of exp(i*S*/ℏ) with *S* the action of the path.

*Proof.* (i) The straight path has constant velocity **x**/*t*, so ∫ (*mv*²/2) *dt* = *m*|**x**|²/(2*t*); this is the action of Proposition 8.7.1 with the constant −*mc*² removed and terms of order *v*⁴/*c*² dropped. (ii) For *a* > 0 the function exp(−*a*|**x**|²/2) has Fourier transform, in the convention of Theorem 8.3.3, (2*πa*)^(−3/2) exp(−|**k**|²/(2*a*)); both sides are analytic in *a* for Re *a* > 0 and continuous up to *a* = −i*m*/(ℏ*t*), where the identity states that *K*_t has Fourier transform (2*π*)⁻³ exp(−iℏ|**k**|²*t*/2*m*). A convolution multiplies Fourier transforms, with the factor (2*π*)³ of the convention, so convolution with *K*_t multiplies the Fourier transform of *φ*₀ by exp(−iℏ|**k**|²*t*/2*m*). (iii) exp(−iℏ|**k**|²*t*/2*m*) = (exp(−iℏ|**k**|²Δ/2*m*))^N, so *U*(*t*) = *U*(Δ)^N; writing each factor *U*(Δ) as the convolution of (ii) gives the stated integral, in which the exponents add to (i/ℏ) times the sum of the actions of the *N* straight segments. □

**The McGucken Principle *dx*₄/*dt* = i*c* reading of the path integral.** Each factor *K*_Δ is the amplitude exp(i*S*/ℏ) of the action clause for one straight segment, and the composition of Theorem 8.3.8(iii) is the composition of expansions: each point reached is the origin of the next. With a potential, the same formula with *S* replaced by the action ∫ (*mv*²/2 − *V*) *dt* of the broken-line path holds in the limit *N* → ∞ by the Trotter product formula [16, Vol. I].

**Theorem 8.3.9 (CHSH inequality and Tsirelson bound; QM Theorem 13 of [57]).** (i) For numbers *a*₁, *a*₂, *b*₁, *b*₂ each equal to ±1, *a*₁*b*₁ + *a*₁*b*₂ + *a*₂*b*₁ − *a*₂*b*₂ = ±2; hence the mean of this expression over any probability distribution lies between −2 and 2. (ii) Let *A*₁, *A*₂, *B*₁, *B*₂ be self-adjoint operators on a Hilbert space with squares equal to 1 and with each *A*_j commuting with each *B*_k, and let *C* = *A*₁*B*₁ + *A*₁*B*₂ + *A*₂*B*₁ − *A*₂*B*₂. Then *C*² = 4 − [*A*₁, *A*₂][*B*₁, *B*₂], and |⟨*f*|*Cf*⟩| ≤ 2√2 for every unit vector *f*. (iii) The value 2√2 is attained.

*Proof.* (i) The expression is *a*₁(*b*₁ + *b*₂) + *a*₂(*b*₁ − *b*₂); one of *b*₁ + *b*₂, *b*₁ − *b*₂ is 0 and the other is ±2. (ii) Write *C* = *A*₁(*B*₁ + *B*₂) + *A*₂(*B*₁ − *B*₂). Using *A*_j² = *B*_k² = 1 and the commutation of the *A* with the *B*, *C*² = (*B*₁ + *B*₂)² + (*B*₁ − *B*₂)² + *A*₁*A*₂(*B*₁ + *B*₂)(*B*₁ − *B*₂) + *A*₂*A*₁(*B*₁ − *B*₂)(*B*₁ + *B*₂). The first two terms add to 4. (*B*₁ + *B*₂)(*B*₁ − *B*₂) = −[*B*₁, *B*₂] and (*B*₁ − *B*₂)(*B*₁ + *B*₂) = [*B*₁, *B*₂], so the last two terms add to −[*A*₁, *A*₂][*B*₁, *B*₂]. Each of *A*₁*A*₂, *A*₂*A*₁ has norm at most 1, so ‖[*A*₁, *A*₂]‖ ≤ 2, likewise for the *B*, and ‖*C*²‖ ≤ 4 + 4 = 8. *C* is self-adjoint, so ‖*C*‖² = ‖*C*²‖ ≤ 8 and |⟨*f*|*Cf*⟩| ≤ ‖*C*‖ ≤ 2√2. (iii) On ℂ² ⊗ ℂ² with the Pauli matrices *σ*_x, *σ*_z, take the unit vector *s* = (*e*₁ ⊗ *e*₂ − *e*₂ ⊗ *e*₁)/√2, for which (**σ** ⊗ 1 + 1 ⊗ **σ**)*s* = 0. Then for unit vectors **a**, **b**, ⟨*s*|(**a**·**σ** ⊗ **b**·**σ**)*s*⟩ = −⟨*s*|(1 ⊗ (**a**·**σ**)(**b**·**σ**))*s*⟩ = −**a**·**b**, because (**a**·**σ**)(**b**·**σ**) = **a**·**b** + i(**a** × **b**)·**σ** and ⟨*s*|(1 ⊗ **σ**)*s*⟩ = 0. With *A*₁ = *σ*_z ⊗ 1, *A*₂ = *σ*_x ⊗ 1, *B*₁ = −1 ⊗ (*σ*_z + *σ*_x)/√2, *B*₂ = 1 ⊗ (*σ*_x − *σ*_z)/√2, the four means are 1/√2, 1/√2, 1/√2 and −1/√2, and ⟨*s*|*Cs*⟩ = 4/√2 = 2√2. □

**Theorem 8.3.10 (Exclusion for antisymmetric amplitudes; QM Theorem 20 of [57]).** Let Ψ(*ξ*₁, *ξ*₂) be an amplitude for two bodies, *ξ* standing for the position and the spin component, with Ψ(*ξ*₂, *ξ*₁) = −Ψ(*ξ*₁, *ξ*₂). Then Ψ(*ξ*, *ξ*) = 0, and for one-body amplitudes *f*, *g* the antisymmetric combination *f*(*ξ*₁)*g*(*ξ*₂) − *g*(*ξ*₁)*f*(*ξ*₂) is identically zero when *f* = *g*.

*Proof.* Setting *ξ*₁ = *ξ*₂ = *ξ* in the hypothesis gives Ψ(*ξ*, *ξ*) = −Ψ(*ξ*, *ξ*). For *f* = *g* the two products are equal. □

That the amplitude of two identical bodies of spin ½ is antisymmetric is the spin–statistics theorem, an external theorem proved from the Wightman axioms in [10]. Its hypotheses are the six axioms listed in §5, which [72] states as theorems of *dx*₄/*dt* = i*c*; the sign −1 of Theorem 8.3.7(iv) under a rotation through 2*π* is the sign that the theorem relates to the sign under exchange.

**8.4 The Hilbert Space Itself.** The complex Hilbert space, which von Neumann had taken as primitive, is now *derived*. The i in *dx*₄/*dt* = i*c* supplies complex amplitudes ψ(*x*) ~ exp i(*k* · *x* − ω*t*). The McGucken Sphere *S_M*(τ) supplies linear superposition. The spherical measure on the McGucken Sphere supplies the inner product ⟨*ψ*|*φ*⟩ = ∫ *ψ*∗ *φ* *d*³*x* (Theorem 12.1 of [64]). Completion under the resulting norm yields *H* = *L*²(**R**³); the tensor/Fock construction yields multi-particle Hilbert space. The theorem is this (Theorem 6.1 of [72]): the Hilbert space of nonrelativistic single-particle quantum mechanics is the unique irreducible separable Hilbert-space representation of the Weyl form of the McGucken-derived canonical commutator algebra, *U*(*a*) *V*(*b*) = exp(−i(*a* · *b*)/ℏ) *V*(*b*) *U*(*a*), *a*, *b* ∈ **R**³, with iℏ supplied by Theorem 5.1 of [72] (i from *x*₄-perpendicularity; ℏ from the action-quantization clause of *dx*₄/*dt* = i*c*, Proposition 2.2 of [72]). By the Stone–von Neumann uniqueness theorem (Stone 1930; von Neumann 1931; refs. 108 and 109 of [72]), this representation is unitarily equivalent to the Schrödinger representation on *L*²(**R**³, *d*³*x*), with the Weyl unitaries acting as (*U*(*a*)*ψ*)(*x*) = exp(i(*a* · *x*)/ℏ) *ψ*(*x*) and (*V*(*b*)*ψ*)(*x*) = *ψ*(*x* + *b*). The Geometric Channel route, from the overlap of McGucken-Sphere wavefronts and with no use of the Stone–von Neumann theorem, is Theorem 6.1.5 of [72]. The von Neumann arena is no longer primitive; it is downstream of *dx*₄/*dt* = i*c*. The explicit co-generation of the joint Space–Operator pair, of which the complex Hilbert space is the spherical specialization, is the subject of McGucken [63, 64, 65].

**The McGucken Principle *dx*₄/*dt* = i*c* Reading of the Hilbert Space: The Conserved Norm, the Inner Product and the Completion, with Proofs.**

**McGucken Principle anchor.** The McGucken Principle *dx*₄/*dt* = i*c* states that the fourth dimension is expanding at the velocity of light in a spherically symmetric manner from every spacetime event, as a McGucken Sphere whose wavelength sets the action *h*. For the Hilbert space the Principle predicts each of its three defining features: the amplitudes are complex because the phase factor is exp(i*S*/ℏ) with the i of *dx*₄/*dt* = i*c*; they form a linear space because amplitudes add; and the norm is the total of the density |*ψ*|², which the expansion of *x*₄ conserves.

**Theorem 8.4.1 (Conservation of the total of |*φ*|²; Theorem 9.2 of [72]).** (i) For the free evolution of Theorem 8.3.4(ii), ∫ |*φ*(*t*, **x**)|² *d*³*x* is the same at every *t*. (ii) For a solution *φ* of the Schrödinger equation iℏ ∂ₜ*φ* = −(ℏ²/2*m*) ∇²*φ* + *Vφ* with real *V*, that is a Schwartz function of **x** at each *t*, ∫ |*φ*|² *d*³*x* is the same at every *t*.

*Proof.* (i) For *f*(**x**) = ∫ *a*(**k**) exp(i**k**·**x**) *d*³*k* with *a* a Schwartz function, Plancherel’s identity is ∫ |*f*|² *d*³*x* = (2*π*)³ ∫ |*a*|² *d*³*k*. The free evolution multiplies *a*(**k**) by exp(−i(*ω*(**k**) − *ω*_C)*t*), a number of modulus 1, so ∫ |*a*|² *d*³*k* is unchanged. (ii) Differentiating under the integral, (*d*/*dt*) ∫ |*φ*|² *d*³*x* = 2 Re ∫ *φ*∗ ∂ₜ*φ* *d*³*x* = (2/ℏ) Im ∫ *φ*∗ (−(ℏ²/2*m*) ∇²*φ* + *Vφ*) *d*³*x*. Integrating by parts, ∫ *φ*∗ ∇²*φ* *d*³*x* = −∫ |∇*φ*|² *d*³*x*, which is real, and ∫ *V* |*φ*|² *d*³*x* is real. The imaginary part is zero. □

**Theorem 8.4.2 (The inner product is determined by the conserved norm).** On the complex vector space of Schwartz functions on ℝ³ put *N*(*f*) = ∫ |*f*|² *d*³*x*, the total of the Born density of §9.3. There is exactly one form ⟨*f*|*g*⟩, linear in *g* and conjugate-linear in *f*, with ⟨*f*|*f*⟩ = *N*(*f*) for all *f*: it is ⟨*f*|*g*⟩ = ∫ *f*∗ *g* *d*³*x*.

*Proof.* The form ∫ *f*∗ *g* *d*³*x* has the stated linearity and gives *N*(*f*) at *g* = *f*. For uniqueness, let ⟨·|·⟩ be any such form. Expanding, *N*(*f* + *g*) − *N*(*f* − *g*) = 2⟨*f*|*g*⟩ + 2⟨*g*|*f*⟩ and *N*(*f* + i*g*) − *N*(*f* − i*g*) = 2i⟨*f*|*g*⟩ − 2i⟨*g*|*f*⟩. Hence 4⟨*f*|*g*⟩ = [*N*(*f* + *g*) − *N*(*f* − *g*)] − i[*N*(*f* + i*g*) − *N*(*f* − i*g*)]: the form is given by *N*. □

**Theorem 8.4.3 (The Hilbert space; Theorem 6.1 of [72]).** The completion of the Schwartz functions on ℝ³ in the norm ‖*f*‖ = *N*(*f*)^(1/2) is the complex separable Hilbert space *L*²(ℝ³, *d*³*x*). On it (i) the evolutions of Theorem 8.4.1 extend to unitary operators, and (ii) the operators *q̂*_j and *p̂*_j of Theorem 8.3.5 are symmetric on the Schwartz functions: ⟨*f*|*q̂*_j *g*⟩ = ⟨*q̂*_j *f*|*g*⟩ and ⟨*f*|*p̂*_j *g*⟩ = ⟨*p̂*_j *f*|*g*⟩.

*Proof.* The Schwartz functions are dense in *L*²(ℝ³), and *L*²(ℝ³) is complete (the Riesz–Fischer theorem) and separable [16, Vol. I]; a complete space in which a normed space is dense is its completion. (i) By Theorem 8.4.1 each evolution preserves the norm on a dense subspace and maps it onto itself, the inverse being the evolution for the opposite time; a norm-preserving linear map of a dense subspace onto a dense subspace extends uniquely to a norm-preserving map of the whole space onto itself, which is a unitary operator. (ii) For *q̂*_j: ∫ *f*∗ *x*_j *g* = ∫ (*x*_j *f*)∗ *g* because *x*_j is real. For *p̂*_j: integrating by parts, ∫ *f*∗ (−iℏ ∂_j *g*) = ∫ (iℏ ∂_j *f*∗) *g* = ∫ (−iℏ ∂_j *f*)∗ *g*. □

**The McGucken Principle *dx*₄/*dt* = i*c* reading of von Neumann’s arena.** Von Neumann’s 1932 axioms take the complex separable Hilbert space as given. Here it is constructed: the vectors are the amplitudes of Theorem 8.3.2 and their superpositions, the norm is the quantity conserved in Theorem 8.4.1, the inner product is fixed by that norm in Theorem 8.4.2, and the space is the completion of Theorem 8.4.3.

**8.5 The Operator Hierarchy.** Quantization of the McGucken operator yields *M̂* = iℏ*D_M* = *Ĥ* − i*c* *p̂*₄, and the quantum McGucken constraint *M̂*Ψ = 0 links energy and the fourth momentum, *Ĥ*Ψ = i*c* *p̂*₄Ψ (Definition 7.1 of [65]). Lorentzian projection (∂₄ = −i ∂ₜ/*c*) converts the Euclidean Δ₄ = ∂₄² + Δ into the d’Alembertian □_M = Δ − *c*⁻²∂ₜ², which is the □ of §2 (Definition 8.1 and Theorem 8.2 of [65]). The Clifford square-root yields the Dirac–McGucken operator 𝒟_M = iℏ*c* γ^μ∂_μ − *mc*², for which (iγ^μ∂_μ − *m*)(iγ^ν∂_ν + *m*) = □_M − *m*² in natural units, with the Clifford relation *γ*^μ*γ*^ν + *γ*^ν*γ*^μ = −2*η*^μν (Theorem 8.5.3; Definition 11.1 and Theorem 11.2 of [65]). Gauge covariantization *D*_μ → ∇_μ = ∂_μ + *A*_μ yields the gauge connection, in the convention of [65] in which the connection one-form *A*_μ absorbs the factor i*q*/ℏ of §9.4; the gauge-covariant McGucken operator is *D_M*^(A) = ∇_t + i*c*∇₄ = *D_M* + *A_M*, with *A_M* = *A*_t + i*cA*₄ the connection component along the fourth-dimensional flow (Definition 12.1 of [65]). Each of these—the Schrödinger operator, the d’Alembertian, the Dirac operator, the gauge covariant derivative—is a downstream descendant of the McGucken Operator *D_M*, which is itself co-generated with the McGucken Space *M_G* from the McGucken Axiom *dx*₄/*dt* = i*c*. The operator hierarchy as a theorem chain—and the symmetry derivations on which it rests—are developed in McGucken [27, 59, 65, 66].

**The McGucken Principle *dx*₄/*dt* = i*c* Reading of the Operator Hierarchy: The Quantized McGucken Operator, the d’Alembertian, the Dirac Factorization and the Gauge-Covariant McGucken Operator, with Proofs.**

**McGucken Principle anchor.** The McGucken Principle *dx*₄/*dt* = i*c* states that the fourth dimension is expanding at the velocity of light in a spherically symmetric manner from every spacetime event, as a McGucken Sphere whose wavelength sets the action *h*. For the operators of quantum theory the Principle predicts that each is obtained from the McGucken Operator *D_M* = ∂ₜ + i*c*∂₄, the derivative along the expansion: the energy operator by multiplying by iℏ, the wave operator by setting *x*₄ = i*ct* in the four-coordinate Laplacian, the Dirac operator as a first-order factor of the Klein–Gordon operator, and the gauge-covariant operator by requiring covariance under a change of phase from event to event. The statements follow Definitions 7.1, 8.1, 11.1 and 12.1 and Theorems 8.2 and 11.2 of [65]; the proofs are carried out here in full.

**Theorem 8.5.1 (Quantized McGucken Operator; Definition 7.1 of [65]).** Put *Ĥ* = iℏ∂ₜ, *p̂*₄ = −iℏ∂₄ and *M̂* = iℏ*D_M*. Then (i) *M̂* = *Ĥ* − i*c* *p̂*₄; (ii) for Ψ(*t*, *x*₄) differentiable in *t* and holomorphic in *x*₄, the three statements *M̂*Ψ = 0, *Ĥ*Ψ = i*c* *p̂*₄Ψ, and “Ψ is a function of *x*₄ − i*ct* alone” are equivalent.

*Proof.* (i) iℏ(∂ₜ + i*c*∂₄) = iℏ∂ₜ − *c*ℏ∂₄, and −i*c* *p̂*₄ = −i*c*(−iℏ∂₄) = −*c*ℏ∂₄. (ii) By (i), *M̂*Ψ = 0 is the same equation as *Ĥ*Ψ = i*c* *p̂*₄Ψ, and since iℏ ≠ 0 it is the same as *D_M*Ψ = 0, which by Lemma 6.10 holds exactly for the functions of *x*₄ − i*ct*. □

**Theorem 8.5.2 (The d’Alembertian from the four-coordinate Laplacian; Definition 8.1 and Theorem 8.2 of [65]; QM Theorem 1 of [57]).** Let *G*(**x**, *x*₄) be twice differentiable in **x** and holomorphic in *x*₄, and let *g*(*t*, **x**) = *G*(**x**, i*ct*) be its values on the constraint surface. Then (∂₄²*G* + ∇²*G*)(**x**, i*ct*) = ∇²*g* − *c*⁻²∂ₜ²*g* = □*g*.

*Proof.* By the chain rule, ∂ₜ*g* = i*c* (∂₄*G*)(**x**, i*ct*), and again ∂ₜ²*g* = (i*c*)² (∂₄²*G*)(**x**, i*ct*) = −*c*² (∂₄²*G*)(**x**, i*ct*). Hence (∂₄²*G*)(**x**, i*ct*) = −*c*⁻²∂ₜ²*g*, and the spatial derivatives of *G* at *x*₄ = i*ct* are those of *g*. □

**Theorem 8.5.3 (Dirac factorization of the Klein–Gordon operator; Definition 11.1 and Theorem 11.2 of [65]).** Write ∂₀ = *c*⁻¹∂ₜ, *η* = diag(−1, 1, 1, 1), so that □ = *η*^μν ∂_μ ∂_ν, and let *γ*⁰, *γ*¹, *γ*², *γ*³ be 4 × 4 matrices with *γ*^μ*γ*^ν + *γ*^ν*γ*^μ = −2*η*^μν. (i) Such matrices exist. (ii) (iℏ*γ*^μ∂_μ − *mc*)(iℏ*γ*^ν∂_ν + *mc*) = ℏ²(□ − *μ*²), with *μ* = *mc*/ℏ. (iii) Every solution *ψ* of the Dirac equation (iℏ*γ*^ν∂_ν + *mc*)*ψ* = 0, and every solution of (iℏ*γ*^μ∂_μ − *mc*)*ψ* = 0, has each of its four components satisfying the Klein–Gordon equation (□ − *μ*²)*ψ* = 0 of Theorem 8.3.3.

*Proof.* (i) With *σ*¹, *σ*², *σ*³ the Pauli matrices, which satisfy *σ*^j*σ*^k + *σ*^k*σ*^j = 2δ_jk, take in 2 × 2 blocks *γ*⁰ = [[1, 0], [0, −1]] and *γ*^j = [[0, *σ*^j], [−*σ*^j, 0]]. Then (*γ*⁰)² = 1 = −*η*⁰⁰; *γ*^j*γ*^k = [[−*σ*^j*σ*^k, 0], [0, −*σ*^j*σ*^k]], so *γ*^j*γ*^k + *γ*^k*γ*^j = −2δ_jk = −2*η*^jk; and *γ*⁰*γ*^j = [[0, *σ*^j], [*σ*^j, 0]] = −*γ*^j*γ*⁰, so *γ*⁰*γ*^j + *γ*^j*γ*⁰ = 0 = −2*η*^0j. (ii) The matrices are constant, so the cross terms iℏ*mc* *γ*^μ∂_μ − *mc* iℏ*γ*^ν∂_ν cancel and the product is −ℏ²*γ*^μ*γ*^ν∂_μ∂_ν − *m*²*c*². Because ∂_μ∂_ν is symmetric, *γ*^μ*γ*^ν∂_μ∂_ν = ½(*γ*^μ*γ*^ν + *γ*^ν*γ*^μ)∂_μ∂_ν = −*η*^μν∂_μ∂_ν = −□. The product is ℏ²□ − *m*²*c*² = ℏ²(□ − *μ*²). (iii) Apply the other factor to the equation and use (ii), the two factors commuting. □

The sign in the relation *γ*^μ*γ*^ν + *γ*^ν*γ*^μ = −2*η*^μν matters. With +2*η*^μν in its place the same computation gives the product −ℏ²(□ + *μ*²), and the equation (□ + *μ*²)*ψ* = 0 has plane-wave solutions with *ω*² = *c*²|**k**|² − *ω*_C², which is the relation of Theorem 8.3.2(iii) with the sign of the mass term reversed. The McGucken Principle *dx*₄/*dt* = i*c* gives *ω*² = *c*²|**k**|² + *ω*_C², for which the group velocity *dω*/*d*|**k**| = *c*²|**k**|/*ω* is less than *c*; the relation −2*η*^μν is the one consistent with it.

**Theorem 8.5.4 (Gauge-covariant McGucken Operator; Definition 12.1 of [65]).** Let *A*_t and *A*₄ be functions, put ∇_t = ∂ₜ + *A*_t, ∇₄ = ∂₄ + *A*₄ and *D_M*^(A) = ∇_t + i*c*∇₄. Then (i) *D_M*^(A) = *D_M* + *A_M* with *A_M* = *A*_t + i*cA*₄; (ii) if Ψ is replaced by exp(i*α*)Ψ for a function *α*, and *A*_t, *A*₄ by *A*_t − i∂ₜ*α*, *A*₄ − i∂₄*α*, then *D_M*^(A)Ψ is replaced by exp(i*α*) *D_M*^(A)Ψ.

*Proof.* (i) ∂ₜ + *A*_t + i*c*(∂₄ + *A*₄) = (∂ₜ + i*c*∂₄) + (*A*_t + i*cA*₄). (ii) By the product rule, ∂ₜ(exp(i*α*)Ψ) = exp(i*α*)(∂ₜΨ + i(∂ₜ*α*)Ψ), so (∂ₜ + *A*_t − i∂ₜ*α*)(exp(i*α*)Ψ) = exp(i*α*)(∂ₜ + *A*_t)Ψ; the same holds with ∂₄ and *A*₄. Adding the first to i*c* times the second gives the statement. □

**The McGucken Principle *dx*₄/*dt* = i*c* reading of the operator hierarchy.** The Schrödinger operator of Theorem 8.3.4, the d’Alembertian of Theorem 8.5.2, the Dirac operator of Theorem 8.5.3 and the gauge-covariant operator of Theorem 8.5.4 are each obtained from *D_M* = ∂ₜ + i*c*∂₄, which Theorem 4.1 obtains from *dx*₄/*dt* = i*c* as the derivative along the expansion of *x*₄.

**8.6 Thermodynamics, the Five Arrows of Time, and Black-Hole Entropy.** Thermodynamics, traditionally axiomatized independently of mechanics, is on the McGucken *dx*₄/*dt* = i*c* framework a deductive consequence of the same source-law that generated the McGucken Sphere *S_M*(τ) and the McGucken Operator *D_M*. McGucken (2026e) presents the full chain as eighteen theorems in four groups; we organize them here by their position in the operator-space hierarchy.

**(a) Foundations on the McGucken Sphere *S_M*(τ) (Th 1–6).** The wave equation (1/*c*²)∂²*ψ*/∂*t*² − ∇²*ψ* = 0 follows (Th 1) by applying Huygens’ principle to the spherical expansion *R*(*t*) = *ct* driven by *x*₄. The spatial isometry group *ISO*(3) = *SO*(3) ⋉ **R**³ is the algebraic content (the *dx*₄/*dt* = i*c* Algebraic Channel, Th 2); the Huygens-wavefront propagation on the McGucken Sphere *S_M*(τ) is the geometric content (the *dx*₄/*dt* = i*c* Geometric Channel, Th 3). Compton coupling ω_C = *mc*²/ℏ (Th 4) connects rest mass to *x*₄-cycle rate. The spatial projection of the *x*₄-driven displacement is instantaneously isotropic (Th 5), and iterated isotropic displacements yield Brownian motion with Wiener variance Var(**r**(*t*)) = 6*Dt* (Th 6). All six are direct consequences of the McGucken Operator *D_M* acting on the McGucken Sphere *S_M*(τ).

**(b) Closure of Einstein’s three statistical-mechanical gaps (Th 7–10).** Einstein’s 1903–1911 attempts to derive the second law from mechanics left three gaps: **T1**, the choice of probability measure was postulated rather than derived; **T2**, the ergodic hypothesis is generically false (KAM theorem, 1954–1963); **T3**, the second law was statistical rather than strict, requiring an auxiliary Past Hypothesis. The McGucken *dx*₄/*dt* = i*c* framework closes each:

• **T1 closed by Th 7.** The rotation group SO(3) is compact, and its unique invariant probability measure is the normalized Haar measure (Haar 1933) [32]; the translation subgroup **R**³ of ISO(3) = SO(3) ⋉ **R**³ is non-compact and carries Lebesgue measure, invariant and unique up to scale. On the McGucken Sphere *S_M*(*τ*) the SO(3)-invariant probability measure is the normalized area measure (Lemma 7.1.5 of [73], reproduced in §9.3), the rotationally invariant spherical measure used to define ⟨*ψ*|*φ*⟩ in §8.4. Extending from the displacement sphere to the spatial slice by the orbit decomposition **R**³ = ⊔_{*r* ≥ 0} *S*²(*r*), with translation invariance fixing the radial Lebesgue weight, gives the Lebesgue measure *d*³*q*, and the same argument on momentum space gives the Liouville measure *d*Γ = Π_i *d*³*q*_i *d*³*p*_i ([62], Theorem 7, Step 3). The Liouville measure is no longer postulated; it is fixed uniquely by the symmetry generated by the McGucken Operator *D_M*.

• **T2 closed by Th 8.** For any continuous observable *F* on phase space, the time-average along any trajectory equals the ensemble-average over the McGucken Sphere’s wavefront cross-section, taken in the SO(3)-invariant measure on the McGucken Sphere *S_M*(*τ*) ([62], Theorem 8); ergodicity is a geometric identity, independent of metric transitivity and unaffected by KAM-tori obstruction. The equidistribution follows from the spatial-projection isotropy of Th 5 by Kolmogorov’s strong law of large numbers; Birkhoff’s 1931 theorem [33], which requires metric transitivity, is not used.

• **T3 closed by Th 9 and Th 10.** By Th 5 the spatial projection of *D_M*-driven *x*₄-displacement is isotropic; by Th 6 iterated isotropic displacements give a Gaussian density *ρ*(**r**, *t*) = (4*πDt*)^−3/2 exp(−|**r**|²/(4*Dt*)). The Boltzmann–Gibbs entropy *S*(*t*) = −*k*_*B* ∫ *ρ* ln *ρ* *d*³*r* evaluates to (3/2)*k*_*B*[1 + ln(4*πDt*)], and direct differentiation gives *dS*/*dt* = (3/2)*k*_*B*/*t* > 0 *strictly*, for all *t* > 0. The second law is no longer statistical (“overwhelmingly probable to increase”); it is a geometric identity following from the Gaussian spreading forced by the McGucken Operator *D_M*. Th 10 supplies the photonic analogue: for a spherical photon shell of radius *ct* the entropy is *S*(*t*) = *k*_*B* ln(4*π*(*ct*)²), *dS*/*dt* = 2*k*_*B*/*t* > 0.

It is worth contrasting this derivation with the parallel programme of rigorous hydrodynamic limits. Deng, Hani, and Ma (arXiv:2503.01800, 3 March 2025) proved that Newton’s equations for a hard-sphere gas converge, on the 2D and 3D torus, to solutions of the Boltzmann equation; combined with the Boltzmann-to-fluid theorems of Bardos–Golse–Levermore [42] and Saint-Raymond [43], this yields the compressible Euler and incompressible Navier–Stokes–Fourier equations as scaling limits of microscopic Newtonian dynamics. The Deng–Hani–Ma derivation and the McGucken derivation of Theorems 7–10 above are addressing different layers of Hilbert’s charge: the former derives macroscopic fluid laws from microscopic Newton on a fixed Euclidean arena, while the latter derives the Liouville measure (Th 7), ergodicity (Th 8), and the strict second law (Th 9) from the McGucken Operator *D_M* acting on the McGucken Sphere *S_M*(τ)—an arena that is itself co-generated with the dynamics. The two results are complementary, and together cover both branches of the kinetic-theory subgoal Hilbert listed in 1900.

**The McGucken Principle *dx*₄/*dt* = i*c* Reading of Statistical Mechanics: The Isotropic Step, Brownian Motion, the Liouville Measure, Ergodicity of Directions, and the Strict Second Law for Matter and for Light, with Proofs.**

**McGucken Principle anchor.** The McGucken Principle *dx*₄/*dt* = i*c* states that the fourth dimension is expanding at the velocity of light in a spherically symmetric manner from every spacetime event, as a McGucken Sphere whose wavelength sets the action *h*. For statistical mechanics the Principle predicts the following: because the expansion is spherically symmetric from every event, a displacement driven by it has no preferred direction, the only probability measure it allows on directions is the uniform one, repeated displacements spread a distribution as a Gaussian of growing width, and the entropy of that distribution increases at every instant. The statements follow Theorems 5–10 of [62]; every proof is carried out here in full.

**Hypotheses on the step (Theorems 4 and 5 of [62]).** A body coupled to the expansion of *x*₄ changes its position by successive steps. (S1) Each step has a direction **n** on the unit sphere, distributed by a probability measure invariant under the rotations SO(3), because the McGucken Sphere of each event has no preferred direction. (S2) The directions of different steps are independent, each step starting from its own event. (S3) Each step has length *ℓ* and duration Δ*t*. (S1) is the spherical symmetry of the Principle. (S2) and (S3) are the coupling of matter to the expansion, which Theorem 4 of [62] introduces as the Compton-coupling ansatz; the constant *D* = *ℓ*²/(6Δ*t*) that results is the diffusion constant.

**Theorem 8.6.1 (The isotropic step; Theorem 5 of [62]).** Under (S1) the direction **n** is distributed by the normalized area measure *d*Ω/4*π* on the unit sphere. Its mean is zero, ∫ *n*_i *d*Ω/4*π* = 0, and its second moments are ∫ *n*_i *n*_j *d*Ω/4*π* = δ_ij/3.

*Proof.* The rotation-invariant probability measure on a round sphere is unique and equals the normalized area measure (the lemma on the invariant measure of the McGucken Sphere in §9.3). The rotation by the angle *π* about the *x*_j-axis, *j* ≠ *i*, sends *n*_i to −*n*_i and preserves the measure, so the mean of *n*_i equals its own negative and is zero. For *i* ≠ *j* the same rotation about the *x*_j-axis sends *n*_i *n*_j to −*n*_i *n*_j, so that moment is zero. A rotation by *π*/2 exchanging two axes shows that the three moments of *n*₁², *n*₂², *n*₃² are equal, and their sum is the moment of *n*₁² + *n*₂² + *n*₃² = 1; each is 1/3. □

**Theorem 8.6.2 (Brownian motion; Theorem 6 of [62]).** Under (S1)–(S3) let **r**_N be the position after *N* steps from the origin, at time *t* = *N*Δ*t*, and put *D* = *ℓ*²/(6Δ*t*). (i) Exactly, the mean of **r**_N is zero and the mean of |**r**_N|² is *Nℓ*² = 6*Dt*. (ii) The characteristic function of **r**_N, the mean of exp(−i**k**·**r**_N), is (sin(*ℓk*)/(*ℓk*))^N with *k* = |**k**|. (iii) As Δ*t* → 0 with *D* and *t* fixed, it converges for every **k** to exp(−*Dk*²*t*), so the distribution of the position converges to the Gaussian density *ρ*(**r**, *t*) = (4*πDt*)^(−3/2) exp(−|**r**|²/(4*Dt*)). (iv) This density satisfies the diffusion equation ∂ₜ*ρ* = *D* ∇²*ρ*.

*Proof.* (i) **r**_N = *ℓ*(**n**₁ + ⋯ + **n**(*N*)). By Theorem 8.6.1 each **n**(*a*) has mean zero, so **r**_N has mean zero. Expanding |**r**_N|² = *ℓ*² Σ_a Σ_b **n**(*a*)·**n**(*b*): for *a* = *b* the term is 1; for *a* ≠ *b*, by the independence (S2), the mean of **n**(*a*)·**n**(*b*) is the product of the means, which is zero. Hence the mean of |**r**_N|² is *Nℓ*². (ii) By (S2) the mean of the product exp(−i*ℓ***k**·**n**₁) ⋯ exp(−i*ℓ***k**·**n**(*N*)) is the product of the means. For one step, with *θ* the angle between **k** and **n** and *u* = cos *θ*, ∫ exp(−i*ℓk* cos *θ*) *d*Ω/4*π* = ½ ∫ exp(−i*ℓku*) *du* over −1 ≤ *u* ≤ 1, which equals sin(*ℓk*)/(*ℓk*). (iii) Put *x* = *ℓk*, so *x*² = 6*D*Δ*t* *k*² → 0. For |*x*| ≤ 1, sin *x*/*x* = 1 − *x*²/6 + *R*(*x*) with |*R*(*x*)| ≤ *x*⁴/120, and ln(1 − *y*) = −*y* + *Q*(*y*) with |*Q*(*y*)| ≤ *y*² for 0 ≤ *y* ≤ ½. Hence *N* ln(sin *x*/*x*) = −*Nx*²/6 + *N* *E*(*x*) with |*E*(*x*)| ≤ *x*⁴. Now *Nx*²/6 = (*t*/Δ*t*)(*D*Δ*t* *k*²) = *Dk*²*t*, and *Nx*⁴ = (*t*/Δ*t*)(6*D*Δ*t* *k*²)² = 36*D*²*k*⁴*t* Δ*t* → 0. So the characteristic function converges to exp(−*Dk*²*t*), which is the characteristic function of the stated Gaussian density, and by Lévy’s continuity theorem the distributions converge. (iv) Differentiating, ∂ₜ*ρ* = *ρ* (−3/(2*t*) + |**r**|²/(4*Dt*²)) and ∇²*ρ* = *ρ* (|**r**|²/(4*D*²*t*²) − 3/(2*Dt*)); the first is *D* times the second. □

**Theorem 8.6.3 (The Liouville measure as the invariant measure of the Poincaré group; Theorem 7 of [62]).** The states of a free body of mass *m* are its straight worldlines, labelled by the position **q** at *t* = 0 and the momentum **p**. (i) The Poincaré group ISO⁺(1,3) of Lemma 6.26 acts transitively on these states. (ii) The measure *d*³*q* *d*³*p* is invariant under this action. (iii) Every invariant measure that is finite on bounded sets is a constant multiple of *d*³*q* *d*³*p*.

*Proof.* (i) A translation carries **q** to any other position, and a rotation followed by a boost carries **p** to any other momentum, because the velocity of Theorem 8.1.7 may be chosen freely with |**v**| < *c*. (ii) Translations add a constant to **q** and leave **p** unchanged; rotations rotate **q** and **p**; both preserve *d*³*q* *d*³*p*. For the boost of Theorem 8.1.7 with velocity *u* along *x*, write *γ*_u = (1 − *u*²/*c*²)^(−1/2), *E* for the energy and **v** = **p***c*²/*E* for the velocity of the state. The momentum transforms as *p*′_x = *γ*_u(*p*_x − *uE*/*c*²), *p*′_y = *p*_y, *p*′_z = *p*_z, and since ∂*E*/∂*p*_x = *v*_x, the Jacobian is ∂*p*′_x/∂*p*_x = *γ*_u(1 − *uv*_x/*c*²). The worldline **x** = **q** + **v***t* meets the new surface *t*′ = 0, that is *t* = *ux*/*c*², at *x* = *q*_x/(1 − *uv*_x/*c*²), where *x*′ = *γ*_u(*x* − *ut*) = *γ*_u *x*(1 − *u*²/*c*²) = *q*_x/(*γ*_u(1 − *uv*_x/*c*²)); the coordinates *y*′ and *z*′ at that event are *q*_y and *q*_z plus multiples of *q*_x. So at fixed **p** the Jacobian of **q** ↦ **q**′ is 1/(*γ*_u(1 − *uv*_x/*c*²)). The momentum **p**′ does not depend on **q**, so the Jacobian of (**q**, **p**) ↦ (**q**′, **p**′) is the product of the two, which is 1. (iii) On a space on which a group acts transitively, an invariant measure finite on bounded sets is unique up to a constant factor when it exists [80]; by (ii) it exists. □

**The McGucken Principle *dx*₄/*dt* = i*c* reading of the equal a priori probabilities.** Boltzmann and Gibbs postulated the uniform measure *d*³*q* *d*³*p* on phase space. Theorem 8.6.3 derives it: the Poincaré group is the invariance group of the interval generated by *dx*₄/*dt* = i*c* (Theorem 6.28), and the measure invariant under it is the Liouville measure. For *N* bodies the measure is the product over the bodies.

**Theorem 8.6.4 (Ergodicity of directions; Theorem 8 of [62]).** Under (S1) and (S2), for every continuous function *F* on the unit sphere, the time average of *F* along the sequence of step directions equals its average over the McGucken Sphere, with probability one: (1/*N*)(*F*(**n**₁) + ⋯ + *F*(**n**(*N*))) → ∫ *F* *d*Ω/4*π* as *N* → ∞.

*Proof.* Put *X*_a = *F*(**n**(*a*)) − ∫ *F* *d*Ω/4*π*. The *X*_a are independent, have mean zero, and satisfy |*X*_a| ≤ *K* for a constant *K*, since a continuous function on the sphere is bounded. Let *S*_N = *X*₁ + ⋯ + *X*_N. Expanding *S*_N⁴ and taking means, every term containing some *X*_a to the first power has mean zero by independence, leaving the *N* terms *X*_a⁴ and the 3*N*(*N* − 1) terms *X*_a²*X*_b² with *a* ≠ *b*; hence the mean of *S*_N⁴ is at most 3*N*²*K*⁴. By Markov’s inequality the probability that |*S*_N/*N*| > *ε* is at most 3*K*⁴/(*ε*⁴*N*²), and the sum of these bounds over *N* is finite. By the Borel–Cantelli lemma, with probability one |*S*_N/*N*| > *ε* for only finitely many *N*. Applying this for *ε* = 1, 1/2, 1/3, … gives *S*_N/*N* → 0 with probability one. □

**The McGucken Principle *dx*₄/*dt* = i*c* reading of the ergodic hypothesis.** Boltzmann’s ergodic hypothesis asserts the equality of time averages and phase-space averages for the Hamiltonian flow, and Birkhoff’s theorem [33] proves it under metric transitivity, which the theorem of Kolmogorov, Arnold and Moser shows to fail on sets of positive measure. Theorem 8.6.4 uses neither: the average over the McGucken Sphere is reached because the successive directions are independent and uniformly distributed, which is (S1) and (S2).

**Theorem 8.6.5 (Strict second law for matter; Theorem 9 of [62]).** Let *ρ*(**r**, *t*) > 0 be a solution of ∂ₜ*ρ* = *D* ∇²*ρ* with ∫ *ρ* *d*³*r* = 1 that, with its derivatives, decays faster than any power of |**r**|, and let *S*(*t*) = −*k*_B ∫ *ρ* ln *ρ* *d*³*r*. (i) *dS*/*dt* = *k*_B *D* ∫ |∇*ρ*|²/*ρ* *d*³*r* > 0. (ii) For the Gaussian of Theorem 8.6.2, *S*(*t*) = (3/2)*k*_B[1 + ln(4*πDt*)] and *dS*/*dt* = 3*k*_B/(2*t*).

*Proof.* (i) *dS*/*dt* = −*k*_B ∫ (∂ₜ*ρ*)(ln *ρ* + 1) *d*³*r*. Since ∫ ∂ₜ*ρ* *d*³*r* = (*d*/*dt*) ∫ *ρ* *d*³*r* = 0, this is −*k*_B *D* ∫ (∇²*ρ*) ln *ρ* *d*³*r*. Integrating by parts, with no boundary term because of the decay, −∫ (∇²*ρ*) ln *ρ* = ∫ ∇*ρ*·∇(ln *ρ*) = ∫ |∇*ρ*|²/*ρ*. The integrand is non-negative, and it is zero everywhere only if ∇*ρ* = 0 everywhere, which is impossible for a function with ∫ *ρ* = 1. (ii) ln *ρ* = −(3/2) ln(4*πDt*) − |**r**|²/(4*Dt*), so −∫ *ρ* ln *ρ* = (3/2) ln(4*πDt*) + (1/(4*Dt*)) ∫ |**r**|² *ρ*. By Theorem 8.6.2(i) carried to the limit, ∫ |**r**|² *ρ* = 6*Dt*, so the second term is 3/2. Differentiating (3/2)*k*_B[1 + ln(4*πDt*)] gives 3*k*_B/(2*t*). □

**Theorem 8.6.6 (Entropy of light on the McGucken Sphere; Theorem 10 of [62]).** Light emitted at an event with no preferred direction is, at the time *t* after emission, distributed uniformly on the McGucken Sphere of radius *ct*. Its entropy is *S*(*t*) = *k*_B ln(4*π*(*ct*)²), and *dS*/*dt* = 2*k*_B/*t* > 0.

*Proof.* By Theorem 8.1.9 light from the event reaches exactly the points at distance *ct*, and by Theorem 8.6.1 its distribution over directions is uniform. The density with respect to area on the sphere of area *A* = 4*π*(*ct*)² is 1/*A*, so −*k*_B ∫ (1/*A*) ln(1/*A*) *dA* = *k*_B ln *A*. Differentiating, *dS*/*dt* = *k*_B (2/*t*). □

**The McGucken Principle *dx*₄/*dt* = i*c* reading of the second law.** Boltzmann’s *H*-theorem obtains *dS*/*dt* ≥ 0 from the assumption of molecular chaos, and Loschmidt objected that the underlying mechanics is reversible. In Theorems 8.6.5 and 8.6.6 the increase is strict at every instant and its source is named: the outward expansion of the McGucken Sphere, which has the orientation +i*c* of the Principle. The entropies here are those of a probability density; a change of the unit of length adds a constant to *S* and leaves *dS*/*dt* unchanged.

**The McGucken Principle *dx*₄/*dt* = i*c* Reading of the Arrows of Time, the Diffusion Constant, and Black-Hole Thermodynamics: The Outgoing Wave, Reversible Equations and the Irreversible Expansion, the Earliest State, the Langevin Theorem, the Thermal Period, the Hawking Temperature and the Bekenstein–Hawking Entropy, with Proofs.**

**McGucken Principle anchor.** The McGucken Principle *dx*₄/*dt* = i*c* states that the fourth dimension is expanding at the velocity of light in a spherically symmetric manner from every spacetime event, as a McGucken Sphere whose wavelength sets the action *h*. The orientation is +i*c*: the McGucken Sphere of an event exists at the times after the event and its radius grows. For the arrows of time the Principle predicts that radiation leaves its source on expanding spheres, that entropy is least at the earliest time, and that the equations which are unchanged under reversal of *t* are those that do not contain the expansion. The statements follow Theorems 11–17 of [62]; every proof is carried out here in full.

**Theorem 8.6.7 (The outgoing wave of a point source; Theorem 11, Step 4, of [62]).** Let *f* be a twice continuously differentiable function with *f*(*s*) = 0 for *s* ≤ 0, let *r* = |**x**|, and put *u*(*t*, **x**) = *f*(*t* − *r*/*c*)/(4*πr*). (i) For *r* > 0, □*u* = 0. (ii) The flux of ∇*u* through the sphere of radius *R* about the origin tends to −*f*(*t*) as *R* → 0, so *u* is the field of a point source of strength *f*(*t*) at the origin. (iii) *u* = 0 at every event with *t* < *r*/*c*, and the value of *u* at distance *r* at time *t* is fixed by the source at the time *t* − *r*/*c*: it is carried by the McGucken Sphere emitted at that time. (iv) The function *f*(*t* + *r*/*c*)/(4*πr*) also satisfies (i), and it is different from zero at times before the source begins to act.

*Proof.* (i) For a function of the form *g*(*t*, *r*)/*r*, ∇²(*g*/*r*) = (∂²*g*/∂*r*²)/*r* for *r* > 0. With *g* = *f*(*t* − *r*/*c*)/4*π*, ∂²*g*/∂*r*² = *f*″/(4*πc*²) and ∂ₜ²*u* = *f*″/(4*πr*), so ∇²*u* − *c*⁻²∂ₜ²*u* = 0. (ii) ∂*u*/∂*r* = −*f*′(*t* − *r*/*c*)/(4*πcr*) − *f*(*t* − *r*/*c*)/(4*πr*²). Multiplying by the area 4*πR*² at *r* = *R* gives −*R* *f*′(*t* − *R*/*c*)/*c* − *f*(*t* − *R*/*c*), which tends to −*f*(*t*). (iii) *f*(*t* − *r*/*c*) = 0 when *t* − *r*/*c* ≤ 0. (iv) The computation of (i) is unchanged when *r*/*c* is replaced by −*r*/*c*; and *f*(*t* + *r*/*c*) ≠ 0 wherever *t* + *r*/*c* > 0 and *f* ≠ 0 there, which includes events with *t* < 0. □

**The McGucken Principle *dx*₄/*dt* = i*c* reading of the radiative arrow.** Both functions of Theorem 8.6.7 solve the wave equation. The first is supported on the McGucken Spheres of the source events, which by the orientation +i*c* exist after those events and expand. The second is supported on spheres that contract onto the source and exist before it acts; the Principle has no such spheres. The observed fact that radiation is retarded is the orientation +i*c* of *dx*₄/*dt* = i*c*.

**Theorem 8.6.8 (Reversible equations and the irreversible expansion; Theorem 12 of [62]).** (i) If *φ*(*t*, **x**) satisfies the Schrödinger equation of Theorem 8.3.4 with real *V*(**x**), so does *χ*(*t*, **x**) = *φ*∗(−*t*, **x**). (ii) If *ψ*(*t*, **x**) satisfies the Klein–Gordon equation of Theorem 8.3.3, so does *ψ*(−*t*, **x**). (iii) If *ρ*(*t*, **x**) satisfies the diffusion equation ∂ₜ*ρ* = *D* ∇²*ρ* of Theorem 8.6.2, then *ρ*(−*t*, **x**) satisfies ∂ₜ*ρ* = −*D* ∇²*ρ*, a different equation, along whose solutions the entropy of Theorem 8.6.5 decreases.

*Proof.* (i) Taking the complex conjugate of iℏ ∂ₜ*φ* = −(ℏ²/2*m*) ∇²*φ* + *Vφ* gives −iℏ ∂ₜ*φ*∗ = −(ℏ²/2*m*) ∇²*φ*∗ + *Vφ*∗. Since (∂ₜ*χ*)(*t*) = −(∂ₜ*φ*∗)(−*t*), iℏ ∂ₜ*χ* is the left side evaluated at −*t*, and the right side evaluated at −*t* is −(ℏ²/2*m*) ∇²*χ* + *Vχ*. (ii) The equation contains ∂ₜ only through ∂ₜ², which is unchanged when *t* is replaced by −*t*. (iii) With *σ*(*t*) = *ρ*(−*t*), ∂ₜ*σ* = −(∂ₜ*ρ*)(−*t*) = −*D* ∇²*σ*. For *σ* the computation of Theorem 8.6.5(i) gives *dS*/*dt* = −*k*_B *D* ∫ |∇*σ*|²/*σ* < 0. □

**The McGucken Principle *dx*₄/*dt* = i*c* reading of Loschmidt’s objection.** Loschmidt objected in 1876 that equations unchanged under reversal of *t* cannot yield an entropy that always increases. Theorem 8.6.8 locates the two kinds of equation. The Schrödinger and Klein–Gordon equations are unchanged under reversal. The diffusion equation is changed by it, and it was derived in Theorem 8.6.2 from the hypotheses (S1)–(S3), in which each step starts from an event and proceeds to later times, the order in which the McGucken Sphere of that event expands. The increase of entropy in Theorem 8.6.5 is a consequence of the diffusion equation and so of that order; it is derived from (S1)–(S3) and makes no use of the reversible equations.

**Theorem 8.6.9 (The earliest state has the least entropy; Theorem 13 of [62]).** Let *t* > 0 be the time elapsed since the origin event of the ensemble. For the matter ensemble of Theorem 8.6.5(ii), *S*(*t*) = (3/2)*k*_B[1 + ln(4*πDt*)], and for the light of Theorem 8.6.6, *S*(*t*) = *k*_B ln(4*πc*²*t*²). In both cases *S*(*t*₁) < *S*(*t*₂) whenever 0 < *t*₁ < *t*₂, and *S*(*t*) → −∞ as *t* → 0.

*Proof.* The logarithm is strictly increasing and tends to −∞ as its argument tends to 0, and 4*πDt* and 4*πc*²*t*² are strictly increasing in *t* > 0 and tend to 0. □

**The McGucken Principle *dx*₄/*dt* = i*c* reading of the Past Hypothesis.** The Past Hypothesis is the separate assumption that the universe began in a state of very low entropy, with the improbability estimated by Penrose [38]. For the ensembles of Theorems 8.6.5 and 8.6.6 no such assumption is made: the McGucken Sphere of the origin event has radius zero at that event, and the entropy is lower at every earlier time by Theorem 8.6.9.

**Hypothesis on the force (Theorem 14 of [62]).** (S4) Along each axis a body of mass *m* is subject to the damping force −*mγv* and to a fluctuating force *ξ*(*t*) of mean zero and strength *D*_p, in the sense that for continuous functions *g*, *h* the mean of the product (∫ *g* *ξ* *ds*)(∫ *h* *ξ* *ds*) is 2*D*_p ∫ *g* *h* *ds*. This holds for independent momentum kicks of size ±*q* arriving at the mean rate *ν*, with 2*D*_p = *νq*². Theorem 14 of [62] takes, as the Compton-coupling ansatz, kicks of size *q* = *εmc* at the rate *ν* = Ω, where *ε* is a dimensionless modulation amplitude and Ω a modulation frequency of the expansion of *x*₄; then *D*_p = *ε*²*m*²*c*²Ω/2.

**Theorem 8.6.10 (Langevin theorem and the diffusion constant; Theorem 14 of [62]).** Under (S4), for the motion *m* *dv*/*dt* = −*mγv* + *ξ* with *v*(0) = 0 and *x*(0) = 0: (i) the mean of *x*(*t*)² is (2*D*_p/(*m*²*γ*²)) [*t* − (2/*γ*)(1 − exp(−*γt*)) + (1/2*γ*)(1 − exp(−2*γt*))]; (ii) the mean of *x*(*t*)²/(2*t*) tends to *D* = *D*_p/(*mγ*)² as *t* → ∞; (iii) the mean of *v*(*t*)² tends to *D*_p/(*m*²*γ*); (iv) for the kicks of [62], *D* = *ε*²*c*²Ω/(2*γ*²), in which the mass has cancelled; (v) for thermal kicks at temperature *T*, for which the mean of *mv*²/2 is *k*_B*T*/2, *D*_p = *mγk*_B*T* and *D* = *k*_B*T*/(*mγ*); (vi) for independent thermal and Compton-coupling kicks together, *D* = *k*_B*T*/(*mγ*) + *ε*²*c*²Ω/(2*γ*²).

*Proof.* The solution of the equation of motion is *v*(*t*) = (1/*m*) ∫ exp(−*γ*(*t* − *s*)) *ξ*(*s*) *ds* over 0 ≤ *s* ≤ *t*. Integrating, and exchanging the order of the two integrations, *x*(*t*) = (1/*m*) ∫ *ξ*(*s*) (1 − exp(−*γ*(*t* − *s*)))/*γ* *ds*. (i) By (S4) with *g* = *h* = (1 − exp(−*γ*(*t* − *s*)))/(*mγ*), the mean of *x*(*t*)² is (2*D*_p/(*m*²*γ*²)) ∫ (1 − exp(−*γu*))² *du* over 0 ≤ *u* ≤ *t*, and ∫ (1 − 2 exp(−*γu*) + exp(−2*γu*)) *du* is the bracket of the statement. (ii) The bracket divided by *t* tends to 1. (iii) By (S4) with *g* = *h* = exp(−*γ*(*t* − *s*))/*m*, the mean of *v*(*t*)² is (2*D*_p/*m*²)(1 − exp(−2*γt*))/(2*γ*), which tends to *D*_p/(*m*²*γ*). (iv) *D*_p/(*mγ*)² = *ε*²*m*²*c*²Ω/(2*m*²*γ*²). (v) Setting the limit of (iii) equal to *k*_B*T*/*m* gives *D*_p = *mγk*_B*T*, and then (ii) gives *D* = *k*_B*T*/(*mγ*). (vi) For independent forces of mean zero the means of the cross products vanish, so the strengths *D*_p add, and by (ii) so do the diffusion constants. □

**The McGucken Principle *dx*₄/*dt* = i*c* reading of the diffusion constant.** The constant *D* of Theorem 8.6.2 is *ℓ*²/(6Δ*t*); Theorem 8.6.10 expresses it through the force. The thermal part *k*_B*T*/(*mγ*) vanishes as *T* → 0 and depends on the mass. The part *ε*²*c*²Ω/(2*γ*²) is independent of temperature and of mass: this is prediction (P2) of Appendix B.

**Theorem 8.6.11 (The thermal period in *τ*_E).** Let *H* be a self-adjoint operator on a finite-dimensional Hilbert space, *β* > 0, ⟨*X*⟩ = Tr(exp(−*βH*) *X*)/Tr(exp(−*βH*)), and for complex *z* let *A*(*z*) = exp(i*zH*/ℏ) *A* exp(−i*zH*/ℏ). Then ⟨*B* *A*(*t* + iℏ*β*)⟩ = ⟨*A*(*t*) *B*⟩ for all operators *A*, *B*. In the variable *τ*_E = i*t* of §9.5 the shift of *t* by iℏ*β* is the shift of *τ*_E by −ℏ*β*: the correlation functions of a state of temperature *T* = 1/(*k*_B*β*) repeat, with the order of the operators exchanged, when *τ*_E advances by the period ℏ/(*k*_B*T*).

*Proof.* *A*(*t* + iℏ*β*) = exp(−*βH*) *A*(*t*) exp(*βH*). Hence Tr(exp(−*βH*) *B* *A*(*t* + iℏ*β*)) = Tr(exp(−*βH*) *B* exp(−*βH*) *A*(*t*) exp(*βH*)), and moving exp(*βH*) to the front under the trace gives Tr(*B* exp(−*βH*) *A*(*t*)) = Tr(exp(−*βH*) *A*(*t*) *B*). □

**Theorem 8.6.12 (Hawking temperature; Theorem 16 of [62]).** Take the Schwarzschild metric of a mass *M*, *ds*² = −*f* *c*²*dt*² + *dr*²/*f* + *r*²*d*Ω² with *f* = 1 − *r*_s/*r* and *r*_s = 2*GM*/*c*² (Theorem 8.2.1), and let *τ*_E = i*t*. (i) With *ξ* the proper distance from the horizon *r* = *r*_s along a radius, the metric in the (*τ*_E, *r*) plane is, to leading order as *ξ* → 0, *dξ*² + *ξ*² *dθ*² with *θ* = *cτ*_E/(2*r*_s). (ii) This is the metric of the plane in polar coordinates, with no conical point at *ξ* = 0, exactly when *θ* has the period 2*π*, that is, when *τ*_E has the period 4*πr*_s/*c* = 2*πc*/*κ*, where *κ* = *c*²/(2*r*_s) = *c*⁴/(4*GM*) is the surface gravity. (iii) A state with this period has, by Theorem 8.6.11, the temperature *T*_H = ℏ*κ*/(2*πck*_B) = ℏ*c*³/(8*πGMk*_B).

*Proof.* (i) Since *dt* = −i *dτ*_E, −*f* *c*²*dt*² = *f* *c*²*dτ*_E². Near the horizon *f* = (*r* − *r*_s)/*r*_s to leading order, and the proper distance is *ξ* = ∫ *dr*/*f*^(1/2) = 2 (*r*_s(*r* − *r*_s))^(1/2) to leading order, so *f* = *ξ*²/(4*r*_s²) to leading order, and *dr*²/*f* = *dξ*² by the definition of *ξ*. The metric of the plane is *f* *c*²*dτ*_E² + *dξ*² = *ξ*² (*c* *dτ*_E/(2*r*_s))² + *dξ*². (ii) In the metric *dξ*² + *ξ*²*dθ*² with *θ* of period *P*, the circle of radius *ξ* has circumference *Pξ*; the plane has 2*πξ*, and for *P* ≠ 2*π* the point *ξ* = 0 is the vertex of a cone. *θ* = *cτ*_E/(2*r*_s) has period 2*π* when *τ*_E has period 4*πr*_s/*c*, and 2*πc*/*κ* = 2*πc* · 2*r*_s/*c*² = 4*πr*_s/*c*. (iii) Setting ℏ/(*k*_B*T*) = 2*πc*/*κ* gives *T* = ℏ*κ*/(2*πck*_B), and *κ* = *c*⁴/(4*GM*). □

**Theorem 8.6.13 (Bekenstein–Hawking entropy; Theorem 15 of [62]).** For the black hole of Theorem 8.6.12, with energy *E* = *Mc*², temperature *T*_H and horizon area *A* = 4*πr*_s², the entropy defined by *dS* = *dE*/*T*_H with *S* = 0 at *M* = 0 is *S* = 4*πGk*_B*M*²/(ℏ*c*) = *k*_B *A*/(4*ℓ*_P²), where *ℓ*_P = (ℏ*G*/*c*³)^(1/2).

*Proof.* *dS* = *c*² *dM* · 8*πGMk*_B/(ℏ*c*³) = (8*πGk*_B/(ℏ*c*)) *M* *dM*, and integrating from 0 to *M* gives 4*πGk*_B*M*²/(ℏ*c*). The area is *A* = 4*π*(2*GM*/*c*²)² = 16*πG*²*M*²/*c*⁴, so *k*_B *A* *c*³/(4ℏ*G*) = 4*πGk*_B*M*²/(ℏ*c*), and *c*³/(ℏ*G*) = 1/*ℓ*_P². □

**The McGucken Principle *dx*₄/*dt* = i*c* reading of black-hole thermodynamics.** The variable *τ*_E = *x*₄/*c* = i*t* is the fourth coordinate of the Principle divided by *c*. In Theorem 8.6.12 the temperature is fixed by the requirement that the geometry in *x*₄ be regular at the horizon, and in Theorem 8.6.13 the factor 1/4 follows from that temperature. The Schwarzschild metric is that of Theorem 8.2.1. One input is taken from outside: the regularity of the state at the horizon.

**(c) The five arrows of time (Th 11–14).** The thermodynamic, cosmological, radiative, psychological/biological, and quantum-measurement arrows are projections onto the spatial slice of the single +i*c*-directed expansion *dx*₄/*dt* = i*c* (Th 11). Loschmidt’s reversibility paradox is dissolved (Th 12) by the McGucken Duality: the Algebraic Channel (algebra) is time-symmetric, but the Geometric Channel (geometric propagation on the McGucken Sphere *S_M*(τ)) is monotone in *x*₄. The Past Hypothesis is dissolved (Th 13) because the *x*₄ origin at *R* = 0 is geometrically the lowest-entropy state; no 10^(−10¹²³) initial-condition fine-tuning (Penrose 1979) is required. Th 14 supplies the explicit Compton-coupling diffusion *Dₓ*^(McG) = *ε*²*c*²Ω/(2*γ*²) — the *x*₄-driven diffusion coefficient that appears in Appendix B as prediction (P2), where *ε* is the dimensionless modulation amplitude, Ω the modulation angular frequency, and *γ* here the environmental damping rate (not the Lorentz factor of §2).

**(d) Black-hole thermodynamics via the McGucken Wick rotation (Th 15–18).** Define the McGucken Wick rotation *τ*_E = *x*₄/*c*; this removes the i from the law (*dx*₄/*dτ*_E = *c*) and produces a Euclidean four-manifold on which the metric *c*²*dτ*_E² + *d***x**² is real and positive-definite and *D_M* = ∂ₜ + i∂_τE is the elliptic Cauchy–Riemann operator of *z* = *t* + i*τ*_E. At a Killing horizon, *x*₄-stationary modes are Planck-quantized at one mode per *ℓ*_*P*², yielding the Bekenstein–Hawking entropy *S*_BH = *k*_*B* *A*/(4*ℓ*_*P*²) (Th 15). The Euclidean cigar period fixes the Hawking temperature *T*_H = ℏ*κ*/(2*πck*_*B*) (Th 16). Th 17 gives the generalized second law *dS*ₜₒₜₐₗ/*dt* = *dS*_matter/*dt* + (*k*_*B*/(4*ℓ*_*P*²))*dA*/*dt* ≥ 0; Th 18 gives the FRW relations, including the reconstitution signature: with *ρ*(*t*) = *R*₄(*t*)*H*(*t*)/*c* the ratio of the McGucken horizon to the Hubble horizon, *ρ*(*t*_rec) ≈ 2.6, giving the entropy ratio *S*_McG/*S*_Hub = *ρ*²(*t*_rec) ≈ 7 ([62], Theorem 18). The Wick rotation *τ*_E = *x*₄/*c* is exactly the inverse of the i that makes the McGucken Operator *D_M* tangent to the McGucken hypersurface; black-hole thermodynamics is therefore not an additional axiom (as in semiclassical gravity) but a direct consequence of the i in the source-law.

**The McGucken Principle *dx*₄/*dt* = i*c* Reading of the Cosmological, Measurement and Psychological Arrows of Time and of the Thermodynamics of the Friedmann Universe, with Proofs.** **McGucken Principle anchor.** The McGucken Principle *dx*₄/*dt* = i*c* states that the fourth dimension is expanding at the velocity of light in a spherically symmetric manner from every spacetime event, as a McGucken Sphere whose wavelength sets the action *h*. The thermodynamic arrow is Theorems 8.6.5 and 8.6.6 and the radiative arrow is Theorem 8.6.7. For the three remaining arrows of Theorem 11 of [62] the Principle predicts: the radius of every McGucken Sphere grows at the rate *c*, and the sign of the rate of the cosmic scale factor cannot change (Theorem 8.6.14); a registration replaces a wavefunction by a distribution from which the wavefunction cannot be recovered, with non-negative entropy (Theorem 8.6.15); and a memory can be reset only by delivering heat to its surroundings (Theorem 8.6.16). Theorem 8.6.17 gives the thermodynamic relations of Theorem 18 of [62] that follow from the theorems of this paper. Each theorem names the hypothesis it uses beyond *dx*₄/*dt* = i*c*.

**Theorem 8.6.14 (Cosmological arrow; Theorem 11, Step 3, of [62]).** (i) The McGucken Sphere of an event at the time *t*₀ has at the time *t* > *t*₀ the radius *R*(*t*) = *c*(*t* − *t*₀), and *dR*/*dt* = *c* > 0. (ii) In the cosmology of Theorem 8.2.7 with *ρ* > 0, Λ ≥ 0 and *k* ≤ 0, the scale factor satisfies *a*′ ≠ 0 at every time; if *a*′ > 0 at one time then *a*′ > 0 at all times. (iii) Under the hypothesis (C) of [62], that the growth of *a*(*t*) is the reading in *x*₁, *x*₂, *x*₃ of the growth of the McGucken Sphere of the initial event, whose radius is *R*₄(*t*) = *ct*, the sign of *a*′ is the sign of *dR*₄/*dt* = *c*: the universe expands.

*Proof.* (i) By the McGucken Principle *x*₄ advances by i*c*(*t* − *t*₀) between the two times, and the McGucken Sphere is the set of points of space at the distance |*x*₄(*t*) − *x*₄(*t*₀)| = *c*(*t* − *t*₀) from the event (§6). (ii) The quantity (8π*G*/3)*ρ* + Λ*c*²/3 − *kc*²/*a*² is positive under the stated conditions, and Theorem 8.2.7(vi) applies on every interval. (iii) is the hypothesis combined with (i) and (ii). □

Hypothesis (C) is an input. Theorem 8.2.7 determines *a*(*t*) from the field equations and does not by itself fix the sign of *a*′; (ii) shows that the sign, once given, is permanent, and (C) gives it.

**Theorem 8.6.15 (Measurement arrow; Theorem 11, Step 5, of [62]).** Let *ψ* be a wavefunction with ∫ |*ψ*|² *d*³*x* = 1, and let space be divided into cells *V*₁, *V*₂, …, the cells of a detector. By the Born rule (Theorem 7.2 of [73], §9.3) the probability of registration in *V*_j is *p*_j = ∫ |*ψ*|² *d*³*x* over *V*_j. (i) The entropy of the registration, *S* = −*k*_B Σ_j *p*_j ln *p*_j, satisfies *S* ≥ 0, with *S* = 0 exactly when *ψ* vanishes outside one cell. (ii) The wavefunction that equals exp(i*θ*_j)*ψ* on *V*_j, for any real numbers *θ*_j, has the same probabilities *p*_j. The passage from *ψ* to the distribution (*p*_j) therefore has no inverse. (iii) The amplitude registered at an event is the amplitude carried there by the outgoing wavefront from the preparation (Theorems 8.6.7 and 9.3.5), so the registration is at a later time *t* than the preparation.

*Proof.* (i) Σ_j *p*_j = 1 and 0 ≤ *p*_j ≤ 1, so each term −*p*_j ln *p*_j is non-negative, and it is zero only for *p*_j = 0 or *p*_j = 1. All terms vanish exactly when one *p*_j is 1 and the others are 0. (ii) |exp(i*θ*_j)*ψ*| = |*ψ*|. Two wavefunctions with different relative phases *θ*_j − *θ*_l are different functions, and they evolve differently under Theorem 8.3.4, yet they give one distribution. (iii) By Theorem 8.6.7(iii) the outgoing wave vanishes at every event earlier than the arrival of the McGucken Sphere of its source. □

**Theorem 8.6.16 (Psychological arrow; Theorem 11, Step 6, of [62]; Landauer’s bound [86]).** Let a memory be a system with states 1, …, *N* occupied with probabilities *q*₁, …, *q*_N, in contact with surroundings at the temperature *T*. Hypothesis (L): in any process the entropy of the memory plus the entropy of the surroundings does not decrease, and the entropy of the surroundings changes by *Q*/*T* when they receive the heat *Q*. Then a process that resets the memory to one fixed state delivers to the surroundings the heat *Q* ≥ −*k*_B*T* Σ_j *q*_j ln *q*_j. For one binary digit with *q*₁ = *q*₂ = ½, *Q* ≥ *k*_B*T* ln 2.

*Proof.* Before the reset the entropy of the memory is −*k*_B Σ_j *q*_j ln *q*_j; after it the memory is in one state with probability 1 and its entropy is 0. By hypothesis (L), *Q*/*T* − (−*k*_B Σ_j *q*_j ln *q*_j) ≥ 0. For *q*₁ = *q*₂ = ½ the sum is −ln 2. □

**The McGucken Principle *dx*₄/*dt* = i*c* reading of Theorem 8.6.16.** A memory that is used more than once must be reset, and each reset raises the entropy of the surroundings. Hypothesis (L) is the second law for the memory with its surroundings; Theorems 8.6.5 and 8.6.6 prove the increase of entropy with *t* for matter diffusing by the steps of Theorem 8.6.1 and for light on the McGucken Sphere, and (L) extends it to the surroundings of a memory. Under (L) the making and remaking of records proceeds in the direction of *t* in which *x*₄ advances.

**Theorem 8.6.17 (Thermodynamics of the Friedmann universe; Theorem 18, items 1–3, of [62]).** (i) The McGucken Sphere of the initial event has the radius *R*₄(*t*) = *ct* and the area 4π*c*²*t*². Under the hypothesis (H) of [62], that this McGucken Sphere carries the entropy of Theorem 8.6.13 per unit area, its entropy is *S*₄(*t*) = *k*_B·4π*c*²*t*²/(4ℓ_P²) = π*k*_B*c*²*t*²/ℓ_P², with *dS*₄/*dt* = 2π*k*_B*c*²*t*/ℓ_P² > 0. With the rates 3*k*_B/(2*t*) of Theorem 8.6.5 and 2*k*_B/*t* of Theorem 8.6.6, every term of the total increases with *t*. (ii) The metric *ds*² = −*f* *c*²*dt*² + *dr*²/*f* + *r*²(*dθ*² + sin²*θ* *dφ*²) with *f* = 1 − *H*²*r*²/*c*² satisfies the vacuum field equations with Λ = 3*H*²/*c*². Its horizon is at *r* = *c*/*H*, with the area 4π*c*²/*H*². With *τ*_E = i*t*, the metric near the horizon has no conical point exactly when *τ*_E has the period 2π/*H*, and a state with this period has the temperature *T* = ℏ*H*/(2π*k*_B). Under (H) the entropy of the horizon is π*k*_B*c*²/(*H*²ℓ_P²). (iii) The ratio of *R*₄(*t*) to the Hubble radius *c*/(*a*′/*a*) is *ρ*₄(*t*) = *t* *a*′/*a*. In the two eras of Theorem 8.2.7(v) it is 2/3 for *p* = 0 and 1/2 for *p* = *ρc*²/3, and the ratio of the entropies assigned by (H) to the two radii is its square.

*Proof.* (i) Theorem 8.6.14(i) with *t*₀ = 0, and the area law *S* = *k*_B*A*/(4ℓ_P²) of Theorem 8.6.13. (ii) For a metric of this form with a function *f*(*r*), the Ricci tensor is *R*_tt = ½*c*²*f*(*f*″ + 2*f*′/*r*), *R*_rr = −(*f*″ + 2*f*′/*r*)/(2*f*), *R*_θθ = 1 − *f* − *rf*′, *R*_φφ = sin²*θ* *R*_θθ, the prime here denoting *d*/*dr* (Theorem 8.2.1(ii) with *A* = *f* and *B* = 1/*f*). For *f* = 1 − *H*²*r*²/*c*², *f*″ + 2*f*′/*r* = −6*H*²/*c*² and 1 − *f* − *rf*′ = 3*H*²*r*²/*c*², so *R*_μν = (3*H*²/*c*²)*g*_μν, *R* = 12*H*²/*c*² and *G*_μν + (3*H*²/*c*²)*g*_μν = 0. Let *r*_H = *c*/*H*. Near *r* = *r*_H, *f* = 2(*r*_H − *r*)/*r*_H to leading order, and the proper distance from the horizon is *ξ* = ∫ *dr*/*f*^(1/2) = (2*r*_H(*r*_H − *r*))^(1/2), so *f* = *ξ*²/*r*_H². With *τ*_E = i*t*, −*f* *c*²*dt*² + *dr*²/*f* = *dξ*² + *ξ*² *dϑ*² with *ϑ* = *cτ*_E/*r*_H = *Hτ*_E. As in Theorem 8.6.12(ii) this is the plane without a conical point exactly when *ϑ* has the period 2π. By Theorem 8.6.11 the period of *τ*_E is ℏ/(*k*_B*T*), so ℏ/(*k*_B*T*) = 2π/*H*. The entropy is the area law applied to 4π*r*_H². (iii) The Hubble radius is *ca*/*a*′, so the ratio is *ct* divided by *ca*/*a*′, which is *t* *a*′/*a*, and Theorem 8.2.7(v) gives the two values; the entropies of (H) are proportional to the squares of the radii. □

Two inputs of Theorem 8.6.17 are hypotheses: (H), and, as in Theorem 8.6.12, the regularity of the state at the horizon. The value *ρ*₄ ≈ 2.6 at recombination and the further items 4–6 of Theorem 18 of [62], quoted in paragraph (d) above and in Appendix B, are results of the cosmological model cited there, in which the Hubble rate is taken from the contraction of the spatial factor; they are not consequences of Theorem 8.2.7, for which *ρ*₄ = 2/3 in the era of matter.

We summarize the closure of Einstein’s three gaps:

| Gap | McGucken closure                                              | Theorem          | Standard problem                                |
|-----|---------------------------------------------------------------|------------------|-------------------------------------------------|
| T1  | Haar measure on SO(3), acting on the McGucken Sphere *S_M*(τ) | Th 7             | Liouville measure postulated                    |
| T2  | Huygens wavefront = ensemble                                  | Th 8             | Ergodicity false (KAM)                          |
| T3  | dS/dt = (3/2)k_B/t > 0 strict, geometric                     | Th 9, 10, 12, 13 | dS/dt ≥ 0 statistical; requires Past Hypothesis |

*Table 8.1. Closure of Einstein’s three statistical-mechanical gaps by the McGucken dx₄/dt = ic framework.*

Notation bridge. McGucken (2026e) writes the future light cone as *Σ*₊(*p*₀) with *R*(*t*) = *c*(*t* − *t*₀); this is the same surface we have called the McGucken Sphere *S_M*(τ) with radius *cτ*. Theorem 3 (Huygens propagation), Theorem 8 (wavefront ergodicity), and Theorem 10 (photon entropy via the 4*π*(*cτ*)² area) all live on the McGucken Sphere *S_M*(τ). The diffusion of Theorem 14 follows from the Compton coupling of Theorem 4, with rest-frame modulation *H*_mod(*τ*) = *εmc*² cos(Ω*τ*) in an environment of damping rate *γ* ([62], Theorem 14). Thermodynamics is thus not appended to the framework; it is the statistical content of the McGucken Operator *D_M* acting on the McGucken Sphere *S_M*(τ).

**The McGucken Principle *dx*₄/*dt* = i*c* Reading of the Inputs of the Second Law: Proposition 8.6.18.** **McGucken Principle anchor.** The McGucken Principle *dx*₄/*dt* = i*c* states that the fourth dimension is expanding at the velocity of light in a spherically symmetric manner from every spacetime event, as a McGucken Sphere whose wavelength sets the action *h*. For the steps of a body coupled to the expansion of *x*₄ the Principle predicts that the direction of each step has no preferred value, whatever path led to the event at which the step starts. The proposition shows that this one statement yields both (S1) and (S2), and it records which of the theorems of this section use (S3).

**Proposition 8.6.18 (The inputs of Theorems 8.6.1–8.6.9).** (i) Suppose that at each event at which a step starts, the direction of the step, conditionally on the directions of all earlier steps, is distributed by a rotation-invariant probability measure. Then (S1) and (S2) hold. (ii) Theorem 8.6.6, and Theorem 8.6.9 for light, use neither (S2) nor (S3). (iii) The rate *dS*/*dt* = 3*k*_B/(2*t*) of Theorem 8.6.5(ii) has the same value for every step length ℓ and every step duration Δ*t*.

*Proof.* (i) By Theorem 8.6.1 a rotation-invariant probability measure on the unit sphere is *d*Ω/4π, so the conditional distribution of the *N*-th direction, given the first *N* − 1 directions, is *d*Ω/4π for every value of those directions. Let *f*₁, …, *f*_N be bounded measurable functions on the sphere. Conditioning on the first *N* − 1 directions, the mean of the product of the *f*_a at the *a*-th directions, *a* = 1, …, *N*, equals the mean of the product for *a* = 1, …, *N* − 1 times the integral of *f*_N *d*Ω/4π.
