By induction on *N* the mean of the product is the product of the integrals of *f*_a *d*Ω/4π. This is (S1) for each step together with the independence (S2). (ii) The proof of Theorem 8.6.6 uses Theorem 8.1.9 and Theorem 8.6.1, and Theorem 8.6.9 for light uses the formula of Theorem 8.6.6. (iii) *S*(*t*) = (3/2)*k*_B[1 + ln(4π*Dt*)] has the derivative 3*k*_B/(2*t*), in which *D* = ℓ²/(6Δ*t*) does not appear. □

**The McGucken Principle *dx*₄/*dt* = i*c* reading of Proposition 8.6.18.** The hypothesis of (i) is the clause “from every spacetime event” of the Principle: the McGucken Sphere of the event at which a step starts is spherically symmetric, whatever path led to that event. With it, the inputs of Theorems 8.6.1–8.6.9 are the Principle and (S3), the existence of a step length ℓ and a step duration Δ*t* for a body of mass *m*. (S3) belongs to the Compton coupling of [62], Theorem 4, whose statement reads: “Massive matter couples to *x*₄’s expansion through the Compton angular frequency ω_C = *mc*²/ℏ.” By (iii) the strict second law for matter holds with the same rate for every value of ℓ and Δ*t*, and by (ii) the strict second law for light emitted with no preferred direction uses the Principle alone, through Theorems 8.1.9 and 8.6.1.

**8.7 Action: The McGucken Principle *dx*₄/*dt* = i*c* Reading of the Principle of Least Action.** **McGucken Principle anchor.** The McGucken Principle *dx*₄/*dt* = i*c* states that the fourth dimension is expanding at the velocity of light in a spherically symmetric manner from every spacetime event, as a McGucken Sphere whose wavelength sets the action *h*. For the action of a free particle the Principle predicts the following: the action of a worldline is its accumulated *x*₄-advance, *S* = −*mc* ∫ |*dx*₄| = −*mc*² ∫ *dτ*, and in units of the action ℏ it is the *x*₄-phase accumulated at the Compton frequency *ω*_C = *mc*²/ℏ of §8.3, *S*/ℏ = −*ω*_C *τ*. Proposition IV.1 of [61] proves that this functional is the only one satisfying five stated conditions, and Theorem VI.1 of [61] extends the uniqueness to four sectors.

**Proposition 8.7.1 (Uniqueness of the free-particle action; Proposition IV.1 of [61]).** Let *γ* be a timelike worldline in Minkowski spacetime and let *S*[*γ*] be a real scalar functional of *γ* satisfying: (a) Poincaré invariance — *S*[*γ*] is invariant under the full Poincaré group (Lorentz transformations plus translations) of spacetime, so that no spacetime point is preferred over any other; (b) reparametrization invariance — *S*[*γ*] depends on *γ* only through its image as a curve, not on the choice of parameter *λ* labeling points on *γ*; (c) locality — *S*[*γ*] is the integral along *γ* of a local functional of position and first derivatives, *S*[*γ*] = ∫ *F*(*x*, *ẋ*) *dλ*; (d) first-order derivatives only — *F* depends on *ẋ*^μ but not on second or higher derivatives; (e) dimensional consistency — *S* has units of action (energy × time). Under these five conditions, the unique (up to overall multiplicative constant and additive total-derivative terms) functional *S*[*γ*] is

*S*[*γ*] = −*mc* ∫ √(−*η*_μν *ẋ*^μ *ẋ*^ν) *dλ* = −*mc* ∫ |*dx*₄| = −*mc*² ∫ *dτ*,

with *m* a constant of dimension mass. No other functional of the worldline satisfies all five conditions.

*Proof (Proposition IV.1 of [61]).* The argument is a standard result in the calculus of variations, adapted here to the Lorentzian worldline setting. By condition (c), *S*[*γ*] = ∫ *F*(*x*, *ẋ*) *dλ* for some local *F*. By condition (b) (reparametrization invariance), *F* must be homogeneous of degree one in *ẋ*^μ: *F*(*x*, *αẋ*) = *αF*(*x*, *ẋ*) for *α* > 0. To see this, consider a reparametrization *λ* → *λ*′ with *dλ*′ = *α* *dλ* for constant *α* > 0 (local rescaling; the argument extends to general monotonic reparametrizations). Then the velocity in the new parameter is *dx*/*dλ*′ = *ẋ*/*α*, and the measure is *dλ* = *dλ*′/*α*. The integrand *F*(*x*, *ẋ*) *dλ* expressed in the new parameter becomes *F*(*x*, *α* *dx*/*dλ*′) *dλ*′/*α*. Reparametrization invariance requires this to equal *F*(*x*, *dx*/*dλ*′) *dλ*′, so *F* is homogeneous of degree one in its velocity argument. By condition (a) (Lorentz invariance), *F* must be a Lorentz scalar built from *ẋ*^μ and (possibly) *η*_μν. The most general such *F* that is homogeneous of degree one in *ẋ* is

*F*(*x*, *ẋ*) = *A*(*x*) √(−*η*_μν *ẋ*^μ *ẋ*^ν) + *B*_μ(*x*) *ẋ*^μ,

where *A*(*x*) is a Lorentz scalar and *B*_μ(*x*) is a Lorentz covector. The first term is homogeneous of degree one and Lorentz-invariant; the second term is also homogeneous of degree one and Lorentz-covariant when contracted with a covector. Exhaustiveness: any other degree-one scalar built from *ẋ* with *η*-contractions must be a power of *ẋ*·*ẋ*, and only the half-power is of degree one, giving the first term; degree-one scalars with external covector structure must contract *ẋ* against a rank-one object, giving the second term. The two-term form above is the most general allowed by (a)–(d).

By condition (d) (no higher-order derivatives) and condition (c) (locality), neither *A*(*x*) nor *B*_μ(*x*) can depend on *ẋ*^μ. For a free particle — by hypothesis, no external fields couple to the particle — the term *B*_μ(*x*) *ẋ*^μ must either vanish or reduce to a boundary term. The reason is that a nonzero field strength *F*_μν = ∂_μ *B*_ν − ∂_ν *B*_μ would produce, under variation of the action, an equation of motion containing the term *F*_μν *ẋ*^ν. This is the Lorentz force on a particle of unit charge in an external electromagnetic potential *B*_μ. The free-particle assumption therefore forces *F*_μν = 0, so the covector *B*_μ is closed. By the Poincaré lemma on the Minkowski manifold, a closed covector on a contractible domain is exact: *B*_μ = ∂_μ *φ* for some scalar function *φ*. The resulting contribution to *S* is ∫ ∂_μ *φ* *ẋ*^μ *dλ* = *φ*(end) − *φ*(start), a pure boundary term. Boundary terms do not affect the Euler–Lagrange equations and can be dropped from *S* without loss of generality. Therefore *F*(*x*, *ẋ*) = *A* √(−*η*_μν *ẋ*^μ *ẋ*^ν) with *A* a constant. (The *x*-independence of *A* follows from the translation-invariance part of condition (a): Poincaré invariance forbids *A* from depending on *x*^μ, since any *x*-dependent *A*(*x*) would distinguish some spacetime point as the origin of the function.) By condition (e) (dimensional consistency), *A* must have units of mass × velocity. Writing *A* = −*mc* (with the conventional sign chosen to make the Euler–Lagrange equations agree with the standard relativistic free-particle equations, and with *m* a mass parameter), we obtain *S*[*γ*] = −*mc* ∫ √(−*η*_μν *ẋ*^μ *ẋ*^ν) *dλ*, which is the claimed unique functional. The equivalent forms *S* = −*mc* ∫ |*dx*₄| and *S* = −*mc*² ∫ *dτ* follow from the interval *ds*² = *dx*₄² + *d***x**² of §9.1 and the definition of proper time *c*²*dτ*² = −*ds*². □

**The geodesic equation and the Newtonian limit [24, Part III].** Varying *S* = −*mc*² ∫ *dτ* with respect to the worldline *x*^μ(*τ*) with the endpoints held fixed, and using *c*²*dτ*² = −*η*_μν *dx*^μ *dx*^ν, gives *δS* = [−]*m* ∫ (*du*_μ/*dτ*) *δx*^μ *dτ*. Since *δx*^μ is arbitrary at interior points, *du*^μ/*dτ* = 0: the free worldline is a straight line of Minkowski spacetime. In coordinate time the Lagrangian is *L* = −*mc*² (1 − *v*²/*c*²)^(1/2) = −*mc*² + *mv*²/2 + ⋯, and with a potential *V*(**x**) the Euler–Lagrange equations give Newton’s second law.

**The Dirac matter sector: the McGucken Principle *dx*₄/*dt* = i*c* Reading of the Dirac Lagrangian.**

**McGucken Principle anchor.** The McGucken Principle *dx*₄/*dt* = i*c* states that the fourth dimension is expanding at the velocity of light in a spherically symmetric manner from every spacetime event, as a McGucken Sphere whose wavelength sets the action *h*. For a field *ψ* with the four components on which the matrices *γ*^μ of Theorem 8.5.3 act, the Principle predicts the following. The Lagrangian whose field equation is linear and of first order in the four coordinates, which is real, invariant under Lorentz transformations and parity, unchanged by the choice of the origin of *x*₄ (Proposition 9.3.2), and whose solutions carry the Compton frequency *ω*_C = *mc*²/ℏ of Theorem 8.3.1, is *ψ*†*γ*⁰(iℏ*γ*^μ∂_μ − *mc*)*ψ*, and there is no other. This is Proposition V.1 of [61]. The lemma on the sixteen products is Theorem 4.2 of [81]. The proofs are given here in full. Throughout, ∂₀ = *c*⁻¹∂ₜ, *η* = diag(−1, 1, 1, 1), the matrices *γ*^μ are those of Theorem 8.5.3(i), *ψ*† is the conjugate transpose of *ψ*, and a divergence is an expression ∂_μ*V*^μ.

**Lemma 8.7.2 (The sixteen products of the matrices *γ*^μ; Theorem 4.2 of [81]).** For a subset *S* of the indices 0, 1, 2, 3 let *γ*_S be the product of the *γ*^μ with *μ* in *S*, taken in increasing order of *μ*, with the empty product equal to 1. (i) The sixteen matrices *γ*_S are a basis of the complex 4 × 4 matrices. (ii) For *μ* ≠ *ν*, *γ*^μ*γ*^ν *γ*_S = ± *γ*_S *γ*^μ*γ*^ν, with the sign + exactly when *S* contains both of *μ*, *ν* or neither. (iii) The matrix *γ*⁵ = i*γ*⁰*γ*¹*γ*²*γ*³ satisfies (*γ*⁵)² = 1 and (*γ*⁵)† = *γ*⁵; it anticommutes with each *γ*^μ and commutes with each product *γ*^μ*γ*^ν; and *γ*⁰(*γ*^μ)†*γ*⁰ = *γ*^μ for each *μ*.

*Proof.* In the matrices of Theorem 8.5.3(i), *γ*⁰ is Hermitian with square 1 and each *γ*^j is anti-Hermitian with square −1, because the Pauli matrices are Hermitian. (iii) Moving one *γ*^μ through the product *γ*⁰*γ*¹*γ*²*γ*³ it passes three matrices with which it anticommutes and one, itself, with which it commutes, so it anticommutes with *γ*⁵; a product of two of them then commutes with *γ*⁵. Reversing the order of four mutually anticommuting factors takes six transpositions, so (*γ*⁰*γ*¹*γ*²*γ*³)² = (*γ*⁰)²(*γ*¹)²(*γ*²)²(*γ*³)² = −1 and (*γ*⁵)² = i²·(−1) = 1. The conjugate transpose of *γ*⁵ is −i(*γ*³)†(*γ*²)†(*γ*¹)†(*γ*⁰)† = i*γ*³*γ*²*γ*¹*γ*⁰ = i*γ*⁰*γ*¹*γ*²*γ*³. Finally *γ*⁰(*γ*⁰)†*γ*⁰ = *γ*⁰ and *γ*⁰(*γ*^j)†*γ*⁰ = −*γ*⁰*γ*^j*γ*⁰ = *γ*^j. (ii) A factor *γ*^ρ of *γ*_S with *ρ* different from *μ* and *ν* commutes with *γ*^μ*γ*^ν, since it anticommutes with each of the two. The factor *γ*^μ, if present, anticommutes with *γ*^μ*γ*^ν, since *γ*^μ*γ*^ν*γ*^μ = −*γ*^μ*γ*^μ*γ*^ν, and likewise *γ*^ν. The sign is therefore −1 raised to the number of the indices *μ*, *ν* that lie in *S*. (i) Each *γ*^μ is unitary, so each *γ*_S is unitary and the trace of (*γ*_S)†*γ*_S is 4. For *S* ≠ *S*′, the product (*γ*_S)†*γ*_S′ is, after the squares (*γ*^μ)² = ±1 are removed, ±*γ*_T with *T* the non-empty set of indices lying in exactly one of *S*, *S*′. For every non-empty *T* there is a *γ*^ν that anticommutes with *γ*_T: if *T* has one or three elements take *ν* outside *T*, and if *T* has two or four elements take *ν* in *T*; in both cases *γ*^ν passes an odd number of matrices with which it anticommutes. Then the trace of *γ*_T equals the trace of (*γ*^ν)⁻¹*γ*^ν*γ*_T = −(*γ*^ν)⁻¹*γ*_T*γ*^ν, which is minus the trace of *γ*_T; the trace is zero. So the trace of (*γ*_S)†*γ*_S′ is 4 for *S* = *S*′ and 0 otherwise. If a combination of the *γ*_S with coefficients *k*_S vanishes, multiplying by (*γ*_S′)† and taking the trace gives 4*k*_S′ = 0. The sixteen matrices are linearly independent in a space of dimension 16. □

**Lemma 8.7.3 (The Lorentz scalars and the Lorentz vectors among the 4 × 4 matrices).** Put *J*^μν = ½*γ*^μ*γ*^ν for *μ* ≠ *ν*. (i) *J*^μν*γ*^ρ − *γ*^ρ*J*^μν = *η*^μρ*γ*^ν − *η*^νρ*γ*^μ, and *γ*⁰(*J*^μν)†*γ*⁰ = −*J*^μν. (ii) A matrix *M* commutes with every *J*^μν if and only if *M* = *a* + *d* *γ*⁵ with complex numbers *a*, *d*. (iii) Four matrices *A*⁰, *A*¹, *A*², *A*³ satisfy *J*^μν*A*^ρ − *A*^ρ*J*^μν = *η*^μρ*A*^ν − *η*^νρ*A*^μ for all *μ* ≠ *ν* and all *ρ* if and only if *A*^ρ = *γ*^ρ(*b* + *e* *γ*⁵) with complex numbers *b*, *e*.

*Proof.* (i) By the relation *γ*^μ*γ*^ν + *γ*^ν*γ*^μ = −2*η*^μν of Theorem 8.5.3, *γ*^μ*γ*^ν*γ*^ρ − *γ*^ρ*γ*^μ*γ*^ν = *γ*^μ(*γ*^ν*γ*^ρ + *γ*^ρ*γ*^ν) − (*γ*^μ*γ*^ρ + *γ*^ρ*γ*^μ)*γ*^ν = −2*η*^νρ*γ*^μ + 2*η*^μρ*γ*^ν; halve it. By Lemma 8.7.2(iii), *γ*⁰(*γ*^μ*γ*^ν)†*γ*⁰ = *γ*⁰(*γ*^ν)†*γ*⁰ *γ*⁰(*γ*^μ)†*γ*⁰ = *γ*^ν*γ*^μ = −*γ*^μ*γ*^ν for *μ* ≠ *ν*. (ii) Expand *M* in the basis of Lemma 8.7.2(i) with coefficients *k*_S. By Lemma 8.7.2(ii), *γ*^μ*γ*^ν *M* − *M* *γ*^μ*γ*^ν is twice the sum of the terms *k*_S *γ*^μ*γ*^ν*γ*_S over the sets *S* containing exactly one of *μ*, *ν*, and the matrices *γ*^μ*γ*^ν*γ*_S are, up to sign, distinct basis matrices. So *M* commutes with *γ*^μ*γ*^ν exactly when *k*_S = 0 for every *S* containing exactly one of *μ*, *ν*. A set that contains both or neither of every pair of indices is the empty set or the full set, and *γ*_S for the full set is −i*γ*⁵. Conversely 1 and *γ*⁵ commute with every *J*^μν by Lemma 8.7.2(iii).

(iii) Let the *A*^ρ satisfy the relation. For *μ*, *ν* both different from *ρ* the right side is zero, so *A*^ρ commutes with *J*^μν. By the argument of (ii), the sets *S* occurring in *A*^ρ contain both or neither of every pair taken from the three indices other than *ρ*: they contain none of the three or all of the three. These are the empty set, the set {*ρ*}, the set of the three other indices, and the full set, and *γ*_S for the set of the three other indices is a multiple of *γ*^ρ*γ*⁵. Hence, with no sum over *ρ*, *A*^ρ = *a*_ρ + *b*_ρ *γ*^ρ + *e*_ρ *γ*^ρ*γ*⁵ + *d*_ρ *γ*⁵. Now take the relation with *ρ* = *μ*: its right side is *η*^μμ *A*^ν. On the left, *J*^μν commutes with 1 and *γ*⁵, and by (i) *J*^μν*γ*^μ − *γ*^μ*J*^μν = *η*^μμ*γ*^ν; multiplying on the right by *γ*⁵, which commutes with *J*^μν, gives *η*^μμ*γ*^ν*γ*⁵ for *γ*^μ*γ*⁵. The relation becomes *b*_μ *γ*^ν + *e*_μ *γ*^ν*γ*⁵ = *a*_ν + *b*_ν *γ*^ν + *e*_ν *γ*^ν*γ*⁵ + *d*_ν *γ*⁵, and the linear independence of Lemma 8.7.2(i) gives *a*_ν = *d*_ν = 0, *b*_ν = *b*_μ and *e*_ν = *e*_μ, for every pair *μ* ≠ *ν*. Conversely *γ*^ρ(*b* + *e* *γ*⁵) satisfies the relation by (i), since *γ*⁵ commutes with *J*^μν. □

**Theorem 8.7.4 (Uniqueness of the Dirac matter sector; Proposition V.1 of [61]).** Let *ψ* be a field with four complex components and let *L* be a Lagrangian density with the following properties. (a) Linearity, first order, and the origin of *x*₄: *L* is quadratic in the field, so that the field equation is linear; *L* is unchanged when *ψ* is replaced by exp(i*α*)*ψ* with *α* a real constant; *L* has no explicit dependence on the coordinates; and the field equation is of first order and contains the first derivatives. (b) Reality: the difference between *L* and its complex conjugate is a divergence. (c) Lorentz invariance: for every *μ* ≠ *ν* and real *s*, with *S* = exp(*s* *J*^μν) and Λ the matrix defined by *S*⁻¹*γ*^ρ*S* = Λ^ρ_σ *γ*^σ, the field *ψ*′(*x*) = *S* *ψ*(Λ⁻¹*x*) has *L*′(*x*) = *L*(Λ⁻¹*x*). (d) Parity: the field *ψ*′(*t*, **x**) = *γ*⁰*ψ*(*t*, −**x**) has *L*′(*t*, **x**) = *L*(*t*, −**x**). (e) Mass shell: every solution of the field equation satisfies the Klein–Gordon equation (□ − *μ*²)*ψ* = 0 of Theorem 8.3.3, with *μ* = *mc*/ℏ = *ω*_C/*c*. Then, up to a divergence,

*L* = *b* *ψ*†*γ*⁰(iℏ*γ*^μ∂_μ − *σ* *mc*)*ψ*,

with *b* a non-zero real constant and *σ* = 1 or *σ* = −1. The substitution of *γ*⁵*ψ* for *ψ* carries the Lagrangian with *σ* = −1 to the Lagrangian with *σ* = 1. For *σ* = 1 the field equation is the Dirac equation (iℏ*γ*^μ∂_μ − *mc*)*ψ* = 0 of Theorem 8.5.3(iii).

*Proof.* Step 1, the general form. A quadratic expression in the components of *ψ*, of *ψ*† and of their first derivatives that is unchanged by *ψ* → exp(i*α*)*ψ* contains one factor from *ψ*† and one from *ψ* in each term, because a term with two factors from *ψ* is multiplied by exp(2i*α*). It is therefore a sum of terms *ψ*†*Nψ*, *ψ*†*B*^μ∂_μ*ψ*, (∂_μ*ψ*†)*C*^μ*ψ* and (∂_μ*ψ*†)*D*^μν∂_ν*ψ* with matrices that are constant by the absence of explicit coordinates. A term with *D*^μν ≠ 0 gives second derivatives in the field equation, so *D*^μν = 0. Since (∂_μ*ψ*†)*C*^μ*ψ* = ∂_μ(*ψ*†*C*^μ*ψ*) − *ψ*†*C*^μ∂_μ*ψ*, the third kind reduces to the second up to a divergence. Writing the matrices as *γ*⁰ times others, which is possible because *γ*⁰ is invertible, *L* = *ψ*†*γ*⁰(iℏ*A*^μ∂_μ − *M*)*ψ* up to a divergence, with constant complex matrices *A*^μ and *M*.

Step 2, Lorentz invariance. By Lemma 8.7.3(i), *γ*⁰*S*†*γ*⁰ = exp(*s* *γ*⁰(*J*^μν)†*γ*⁰) = *S*⁻¹, so *S*†*γ*⁰ = *γ*⁰*S*⁻¹. The derivative of *S*⁻¹*γ*^ρ*S* with respect to *s* is *S*⁻¹(*γ*^ρ*J*^μν − *J*^μν*γ*^ρ)*S* = −*S*⁻¹(*η*^μρ*γ*^ν − *η*^νρ*γ*^μ)*S*, a linear differential equation with constant coefficients within the span of the *γ*^σ, so *S*⁻¹*γ*^ρ*S* = Λ^ρ_σ *γ*^σ with Λ = exp(−*sK*) and *K*^ρ_σ = *η*^μρ δ^ν_σ − *η*^νρ δ^μ_σ. With the first index lowered, *K* is antisymmetric, so Λ preserves *η* and is a Lorentz transformation of §8.1. With *y* = Λ⁻¹*x*, the derivative of *ψ*(Λ⁻¹*x*) with respect to *x*^ρ is (Λ⁻¹)^σ_ρ (∂_σ*ψ*)(*y*), so *L*′(*x*) = *ψ*†(*y*)*γ*⁰(iℏ *S*⁻¹*A*^ρ*S* (Λ⁻¹)^σ_ρ ∂_σ − *S*⁻¹*MS*)*ψ*(*y*). The values of *ψ* and of ∂_σ*ψ* at a point are arbitrary, so *L*′(*x*) = *L*(*y*) for all fields holds exactly when *S*⁻¹*MS* = *M* and *S*⁻¹*A*^ρ*S* = Λ^ρ_σ *A*^σ. Differentiating at *s* = 0 gives *MJ*^μν = *J*^μν*M* and *J*^μν*A*^ρ − *A*^ρ*J*^μν = *K*^ρ_σ *A*^σ = *η*^μρ*A*^ν − *η*^νρ*A*^μ. By Lemma 8.7.3, *M* = *a* + *d* *γ*⁵ and *A*^ρ = *γ*^ρ(*b* + *e* *γ*⁵).

Step 3, parity. For *ψ*′(*t*, **x**) = *γ*⁰*ψ*(*t*, −**x**), (*ψ*′)†*γ*⁰*Xψ*′ = *ψ*†*γ*⁰(*γ*⁰*Xγ*⁰)*ψ* for any matrix *X*, and a derivative ∂_j of *ψ*′ is −*γ*⁰(∂_j*ψ*) at the reflected point. Since *γ*⁵ anticommutes with *γ*⁰, *γ*⁰(*a* + *d* *γ*⁵)*γ*⁰ = *a* − *d* *γ*⁵, *γ*⁰*γ*⁰(*b* + *e* *γ*⁵)*γ*⁰ = *γ*⁰(*b* − *e* *γ*⁵), and *γ*⁰*γ*^j(*b* + *e* *γ*⁵)*γ*⁰ = −*γ*^j(*b* − *e* *γ*⁵), the last sign being cancelled by the sign of ∂_j. So *L*′(*t*, **x**) is the expression *L* with *e*, *d* replaced by −*e*, −*d*, at the point (*t*, −**x**). Property (d) holds for all fields exactly when *e* = 0 and *d* = 0. Hence *L* = *ψ*†*γ*⁰(iℏ *b* *γ*^μ∂_μ − *a*)*ψ*.

Step 4, reality. By Lemma 8.7.2(iii), (*γ*^μ)†*γ*⁰ = *γ*⁰*γ*^μ. The complex conjugate of *ψ*†*γ*⁰ iℏ*b* *γ*^μ∂_μ*ψ* is −iℏ*b*∗ (∂_μ*ψ*†)*γ*⁰*γ*^μ*ψ* = iℏ*b*∗ *ψ*†*γ*⁰*γ*^μ∂_μ*ψ* − iℏ*b*∗ ∂_μ(*ψ*†*γ*⁰*γ*^μ*ψ*), and the complex conjugate of *a* *ψ*†*γ*⁰*ψ* is *a*∗ *ψ*†*γ*⁰*ψ*. So the conjugate of *L* minus *L* is iℏ(*b*∗ − *b*) *ψ*†*γ*⁰*γ*^μ∂_μ*ψ* − (*a*∗ − *a*) *ψ*†*γ*⁰*ψ* plus a divergence. The integral of a divergence over spacetime is zero for a field that vanishes outside a bounded region. Take *ψ* = *f* *u* with *f* a real function vanishing outside a bounded region and *u* a constant column with *u*†*γ*⁰*u* ≠ 0: the first term integrates to a multiple of the integral of *f* ∂_μ*f* = ∂_μ(*f*²/2), which is zero, and the second to (*a*∗ − *a*) *u*†*γ*⁰*u* times the integral of *f*², so *a*∗ = *a*. Take next *ψ* = *f* exp(−i*ωt*) *u*: the first term integrates to iℏ(*b*∗ − *b*) (−i*ω*/*c*) *u*†*u* times the integral of *f*², so *b*∗ = *b*. Both constants are real.

Step 5, the field equation and the mass shell. The variation of the action with respect to *ψ*† gives (iℏ*b* *γ*^μ∂_μ − *a*)*ψ* = 0, and property (a) requires *b* ≠ 0. Applying iℏ*b* *γ*^μ∂_μ + *a* and repeating the computation of Theorem 8.5.3(ii), every solution satisfies (ℏ²*b*²□ − *a*²)*ψ* = 0. A non-zero solution exists: *ψ* = exp(−i*ωt*)*u* with *γ*⁰*u* = *u* and ℏ*bω*/*c* = *a*. By property (e) it also satisfies ℏ²*b*²(□ − *μ*²)*ψ* = 0; subtracting, (*a*² − ℏ²*b*²*μ*²)*ψ* = 0, so *a*² = *b*²*m*²*c*² and *a* = *σ* *b* *mc* with *σ* = ±1. This is the stated form. Step 6, the two signs. For *χ* = *γ*⁵*ψ*, *χ*†*γ*⁰ = *ψ*†*γ*⁵*γ*⁰ = −*ψ*†*γ*⁰*γ*⁵, so *χ*†*γ*⁰(iℏ*γ*^μ∂_μ + *mc*)*χ* = −*ψ*†*γ*⁰*γ*⁵(iℏ*γ*^μ∂_μ + *mc*)*γ*⁵*ψ* = −*ψ*†*γ*⁰(−iℏ*γ*^μ∂_μ + *mc*)*ψ* = *ψ*†*γ*⁰(iℏ*γ*^μ∂_μ − *mc*)*ψ*, because *γ*⁵*γ*^μ*γ*⁵ = −*γ*^μ and (*γ*⁵)² = 1. □

**The McGucken Principle *dx*₄/*dt* = i*c* reading of Theorem 8.7.4.** Each hypothesis is a property the Principle supplies. The first order of the field equation is the first order of *dx*₄/*dt* = i*c* and of its operator *D*_M (Theorem 8.5.1). The invariance under exp(i*α*) is the absence of an origin of *x*₄ (Proposition 9.3.2). The Lorentz transformations are those of §8.1, and *S* = exp(*s* *J*¹²) is the rotation *R*(*θ*) of Theorem 8.3.7 with *θ* = *s*. The mass term is the Compton frequency: *mc*/ℏ = *ω*_C/*c*, the rate of the *x*₄-phase of Theorem 8.3.1. The matrices *γ*^μ are those of Theorem 8.5.3, and the sign of their relation is the one fixed in the remark that follows that theorem. The Lagrangian of Dirac’s equation of 1928 is therefore the Lagrangian of the first-order field carried by the expansion of *x*₄.

**The Yang–Mills gauge sector: the McGucken Principle *dx*₄/*dt* = i*c* Reading of the Yang–Mills Lagrangian.**

**McGucken Principle anchor.** The McGucken Principle *dx*₄/*dt* = i*c* states that the fourth dimension is expanding at the velocity of light in a spherically symmetric manner from every spacetime event, as a McGucken Sphere whose wavelength sets the action *h*. The Principle fixes no origin of *x*₄ (Proposition 9.3.2). Theorem 123 of [82] states the requirement that follows when the origin may be chosen separately at each event: the physics is unchanged by *ψ* → exp(i*α*(*x*))*ψ* with *α* a function on spacetime. Theorem 8.5.4 and §9.4 show that this requirement introduces the connection *A*_μ in the covariant derivative. For the field *A*_μ itself the Principle then predicts the Lagrangian −¼ *F*^a_μν *F*_a^μν, and Theorem 8.7.8 proves that it is the only one under the stated conditions. This is Proposition VI.2 of [61]. The local invariance is a hypothesis of the theorem, named as hypothesis (a).

**Definition 8.7.5 (Gauge group, connection, gauge transformation, field strength).** Let *G* be U(1) or SU(*N*) with *N* ≥ 2, a group of unitary *n* × *n* matrices. Its Lie algebra is the real vector space of the matrices *X* with exp(*sX*) in *G* for every real *s*; these matrices are anti-Hermitian, and the Lie algebra contains *XY* − *YX* and *gXg*⁻¹ whenever it contains *X*, *Y* and *g* is in *G*. On it ⟨*X*, *Y*⟩ = −tr(*XY*) is a real inner product, positive because −tr(*X*²) = tr(*X*†*X*), and ⟨*gXg*⁻¹, *gYg*⁻¹⟩ = ⟨*X*, *Y*⟩. Let *T*_a be a basis with ⟨*T*_a, *T*_b⟩ = δ_ab and *T*_b*T*_c − *T*_c*T*_b = *f*_bc^a *T*_a. A connection is a field *A*_μ(*x*) = *A*^a_μ(*x*) *T*_a with values in the Lie algebra, and the covariant derivative of a field *ψ* with *n* components is *D*_μ*ψ* = ∂_μ*ψ* + *A*_μ*ψ*; for U(1) this is the derivative of Theorem 8.5.4 and §9.4. A gauge transformation by a smooth function *g*(*x*) with values in *G* replaces *ψ* by *gψ* and *A*_μ by *A*′_μ = *gA*_μ*g*⁻¹ − (∂_μ*g*)*g*⁻¹, which has values in the Lie algebra. The field strength is *F*_μν = ∂_μ*A*_ν − ∂_ν*A*_μ + *A*_μ*A*_ν − *A*_ν*A*_μ, with components *F*^a_μν = ∂_μ*A*^a_ν − ∂_ν*A*^a_μ + *f*_bc^a *A*^b_μ *A*^c_ν. The coupling constant of [61] is included in *A*_μ.

**Lemma 8.7.6 (Covariance of the field strength, and reduction of an invariant Lagrangian to it).** (i) *D*′_μ(*gψ*) = *g* *D*_μ*ψ*, where *D*′_μ = ∂_μ + *A*′_μ. (ii) *F*_μν*ψ* = *D*_μ*D*_ν*ψ* − *D*_ν*D*_μ*ψ*. (iii) *F*′_μν = *gF*_μν*g*⁻¹. (iv) Let *L* be a function of the values at a point of *A*_μ and of the first derivatives ∂_μ*A*_ν, and let *L* have the same value on *A*′ as on *A* for every gauge transformation. Then *L* = Φ(*F*), where Φ(*X*) is the value of *L* at *A*_μ = 0 and ∂_μ*A*_ν = ½*X*_μν, and Φ(*gFg*⁻¹) = Φ(*F*) for every *g* in *G*.

*Proof.* (i) ∂_μ(*gψ*) + *A*′_μ*gψ* = (∂_μ*g*)*ψ* + *g*∂_μ*ψ* + *gA*_μ*ψ* − (∂_μ*g*)*ψ* = *g*(∂_μ*ψ* + *A*_μ*ψ*). (ii) *D*_μ*D*_ν*ψ* = ∂_μ∂_ν*ψ* + (∂_μ*A*_ν)*ψ* + *A*_ν∂_μ*ψ* + *A*_μ∂_ν*ψ* + *A*_μ*A*_ν*ψ*. The first term and the sum of the third and fourth are symmetric in *μ*, *ν*; subtracting the same expression with *μ*, *ν* exchanged leaves *F*_μν*ψ*. (iii) By (ii) and (i) applied twice, *F*′_μν*gψ* = *D*′_μ*D*′_ν(*gψ*) − *D*′_ν*D*′_μ(*gψ*) = *g*(*D*_μ*D*_ν*ψ* − *D*_ν*D*_μ*ψ*) = *gF*_μν*ψ*. The value of *ψ* at a point is arbitrary, so *F*′_μν*g* = *gF*_μν.

(iv) Fix a point *x*₀ and write *y* = *x* − *x*₀, *a*_μ = *A*_μ(*x*₀) and *b*_μν = ½(∂_μ*A*_ν + ∂_ν*A*_μ)(*x*₀), which lie in the Lie algebra. Put *α*(*x*) = *a*_μ *y*^μ + ½ *b*_μν *y*^μ*y*^ν and *g*(*x*) = exp(*α*(*x*)), a smooth function with values in *G*. Since *g* = 1 + *α* + ½*α*² plus terms of the third order in *y*, at *x*₀ we have *g* = 1, ∂_μ*g* = *a*_μ, ∂_μ∂_ν*g* = *b*_μν + ½(*a*_μ*a*_ν + *a*_ν*a*_μ), and ∂_ν(*g*⁻¹) = −*a*_ν. Then *A*′_μ(*x*₀) = *A*_μ(*x*₀) − *a*_μ = 0. Differentiating *A*′_μ = *gA*_μ*g*⁻¹ − (∂_μ*g*)*g*⁻¹ and evaluating at *x*₀, ∂_ν*A*′_μ = *a*_ν*A*_μ + ∂_ν*A*_μ − *A*_μ*a*_ν − ∂_ν∂_μ*g* + *a*_μ*a*_ν. With *a*_μ = *A*_μ this is ∂_ν*A*_μ − *b*_μν − ½(*A*_μ*A*_ν − *A*_ν*A*_μ) = ½(∂_ν*A*_μ − ∂_μ*A*_ν) − ½(*A*_μ*A*_ν − *A*_ν*A*_μ) = ½*F*_νμ(*x*₀). So at *x*₀ the transformed connection has *A*′_μ = 0 and ∂_μ*A*′_ν = ½*F*_μν, and by the invariance of *L* its value on *A* at *x*₀ is Φ(*F*(*x*₀)). Every antisymmetric array *X*_μν of elements of the Lie algebra is the field strength at *x*₀ of the connection *A*_ν = ½*X*_μν *y*^μ. For a constant *g*, *A*′_μ = *gA*_μ*g*⁻¹ and *F*′ = *gFg*⁻¹ by (iii), so Φ(*gFg*⁻¹) = Φ(*F*). □

**Lemma 8.7.7 (The Lorentz-invariant forms of the field strength).** Put *E*^a_j = *F*^a_{0j} and *B*^a_j = ½ ε_jkl *F*^a_kl, and write *E*^a, *B*^a for the three-component vectors, with · the scalar product and × the vector product of such vectors. The position of the index *a* has no significance, since ⟨*T*_a, *T*_b⟩ = δ_ab. (i) *F*^a_μν *F*_b^μν = 2(*B*^a·*B*^b − *E*^a·*E*^b), and with ε^{0123} = 1, ε^{μνρσ} *F*^a_μν *F*^b_ρσ = 4(*E*^a·*B*^b + *E*^b·*B*^a). (ii) A rotation of the coordinates rotates every *E*^a and every *B*^a. The Lorentz transformation 1 + *sK*, with *K* the generator of the boost along the unit vector *n*, changes them to first order in *s* by *s* *n* × *B*^a and −*s* *n* × *E*^a. Parity changes *E*^a to −*E*^a and leaves *B*^a unchanged. (iii) Let *Q* be a real quadratic form in the components *E*^a_j, *B*^a_j that is invariant under the rotations and the boosts. Then *Q* = *P*_ab(*E*^a·*E*^b − *B*^a·*B*^b) + *R*_ab *E*^a·*B*^b with *P*_ab and *R*_ab real and symmetric. If *Q* is also invariant under parity, *R*_ab = 0. (iv) A linear form in the components that is invariant under the rotations is zero.

*Proof.* (i) With *η* = diag(−1, 1, 1, 1), *F*_μν*F*^μν = −2 *F*_{0j}*F*_{0j} + *F*_jk*F*_jk, and *F*_jk = ε_jkl *B*_l gives *F*^a_jk*F*^b_jk = 2 *B*^a·*B*^b. In ε^{μνρσ}*F*^a_μν*F*^b_ρσ the index 0 is carried by the first or the second factor; each case contributes 2·*F*_{0j} ε_jkl *F*_kl = 4 *E*·*B* of the corresponding pair. (ii) *F*_μν transforms as a covariant tensor. For a rotation the components *F*_{0j} form a vector and *F*_jk an antisymmetric tensor, whose dual *B* is a vector under rotations of determinant 1. For the boost along the first axis, *K* has the entries *K*^0_1 = *K*^1_0 = 1 and the change of *F* is −*s*(*K*ᵀ*F* + *FK*), which gives the changes *s*(0, −*B*₃, *B*₂) of *E* and *s*(0, *E*₃, −*E*₂) of *B*; these are *s* *n* × *B* and −*s* *n* × *E* for *n* = (1, 0, 0), and the other directions follow by a rotation. Under **x** → −**x**, *F*_{0j} changes sign and *F*_jk does not.

(iii) *Q* is a sum of terms *X*ᵀ*CY* with *X*, *Y* running over the vectors *E*^a, *B*^a and *C* a real 3 × 3 matrix for each pair, symmetric when *X* = *Y*. Invariance under every rotation ρ requires ρᵀ*C*ρ = *C*, that is, *C* commutes with ρ. Commuting with the rotation by π about each coordinate axis makes the off-diagonal entries of *C* zero, and commuting with the rotations by π/2 that exchange two axes makes the diagonal entries equal. So each *C* is a multiple of the identity and *Q* = *P*_ab *E*^a·*E*^b + *R*_ab *E*^a·*B*^b + *P*′_ab *B*^a·*B*^b with *P*, *P*′ symmetric. Under the boost, using *B*^a·(*n* × *E*^b) = −*E*^b·(*n* × *B*^a) and (*n* × *B*^a)·*B*^b = *n*·(*B*^a × *B*^b), the change of *Q* divided by *s* is 2(*P*_ab + *P*′_ab) *E*^a·(*n* × *B*^b) + *R*_ab *n*·(*B*^a × *B*^b + *E*^a × *E*^b). The cross products are antisymmetric in *a*, *b*. Setting every *B* to zero, the antisymmetric part of *R*_ab must vanish; then, choosing *E*^a and *B*^b along the first and second axes and *n* along the third, *P*_ab + *P*′_ab = 0. Under parity *E*^a·*B*^b changes sign and the other two products do not. (iv) A linear form is a sum of scalar products of fixed vectors with the *E*^a and *B*^a, and a vector fixed by every rotation is zero. □

**Theorem 8.7.8 (Uniqueness of the Yang–Mills gauge sector; Proposition VI.2 of [61]).** Let *G* be U(1) or SU(*N*), and let *L* be a real function of the values at a point of the connection *A*_μ and of its first derivatives, with the following properties. (a) Local gauge invariance: *L* has the same value on *A*′ as on *A* for every gauge transformation. (b) Lorentz invariance. (c) *L* contains no derivative of *A*_μ above the first. (d) *L* is a polynomial of degree at most 2 in the field strength; in units with ℏ = *c* = 1 the field strength has the dimension of the square of a mass, and this is the condition that *L* have dimension at most the fourth power of a mass. (e) Parity invariance. Then *L* = *L*₀ + *α* *F*^a_μν *F*_a^μν with real constants *L*₀ and *α*. For *α* = −¼ this is the Yang–Mills Lagrangian −¼ *F*^a_μν *F*_a^μν, which for U(1) is Maxwell’s Lagrangian ½(*E*·*E* − *B*·*B*). Its field equation is ∂_μ*F*^μν + *A*_μ*F*^μν − *F*^μν*A*_μ = 0.

*Proof.* By (a), (c) and Lemma 8.7.6(iv), *L* = Φ(*F*) with Φ(*gFg*⁻¹) = Φ(*F*). By (d), Φ = Φ₀ + Φ₁ + Φ₂ with Φ_k homogeneous of degree *k* in the components *F*^a_μν, and since rotations, boosts, parity and *F* → *gFg*⁻¹ act linearly on the components, each Φ_k has the invariances of Φ. Φ₀ is a constant *L*₀. Φ₁ = 0 by Lemma 8.7.7(iv). By Lemma 8.7.7(iii) and (e), Φ₂ = *P*_ab(*E*^a·*E*^b − *B*^a·*B*^b), which by Lemma 8.7.7(i) is −½ *P*_ab *F*^a_μν *F*_b^μν. It remains to determine the symmetric form *P*(*X*, *Y*) = *P*_ab *x*^a *y*^b on the Lie algebra, where *X* = *x*^a*T*_a and *Y* = *y*^a*T*_a. For a field strength whose only non-zero component is *F*_{01} = *X*, Φ₂ = *P*(*X*, *X*), so *P*(*gXg*⁻¹, *gXg*⁻¹) = *P*(*X*, *X*) for every *g* in *G*, and by the identity 4*P*(*X*, *Y*) = *P*(*X* + *Y*, *X* + *Y*) − *P*(*X* − *Y*, *X* − *Y*) the same holds for *P*(*X*, *Y*). Write *P*(*X*, *Y*) = ⟨*ΘX*, *Y*⟩ with Θ a linear map that is symmetric for the inner product of Definition 8.7.5. Both forms are invariant, so Θ commutes with every map *X* → *gXg*⁻¹. Θ has a real eigenvalue *λ*, and its eigenspace *V* is carried into itself by every *X* → *gXg*⁻¹; taking *g* = exp(*sZ*) and differentiating at *s* = 0, *ZX* − *XZ* lies in *V* for every *X* in *V* and every *Z* in the Lie algebra. For U(1) the Lie algebra has dimension 1 and *V* is all of it. For SU(*N*) the Lie algebra is simple: a non-zero subspace *V* with this property is the whole Lie algebra. In both cases Θ = *λ* and *P*_ab = *λ* δ_ab, so Φ₂ = −(*λ*/2) *F*^a_μν *F*_a^μν, which is the stated form with *α* = −*λ*/2.

The field equation. By Definition 8.7.5, *F*^a_μν *F*_a^μν = −tr(*F*_μν*F*^μν). A variation δ*A*_ν changes *F*_μν by ∇_μ(δ*A*_ν) − ∇_ν(δ*A*_μ), where ∇_μ*X* = ∂_μ*X* + *A*_μ*X* − *XA*_μ. So the variation of tr(*F*_μν*F*^μν) is 4 tr(*F*^μν∇_μ(δ*A*_ν)). Since the trace of a product is unchanged by a cyclic permutation, tr(*F*^μν(*A*_μδ*A*_ν − δ*A*_ν*A*_μ)) = −tr((*A*_μ*F*^μν − *F*^μν*A*_μ)δ*A*_ν), hence tr(*F*^μν∇_μ(δ*A*_ν)) = ∂_μ tr(*F*^μνδ*A*_ν) − tr((∇_μ*F*^μν)δ*A*_ν). The divergence does not contribute to the variation of the action for δ*A*_ν vanishing outside a bounded region, so the action is stationary exactly when ⟨∇_μ*F*^μν, δ*A*_ν⟩ integrates to zero for every such δ*A*_ν with values in the Lie algebra. ∇_μ*F*^μν has values in the Lie algebra and the inner product is positive, so ∇_μ*F*^μν = 0. For U(1) the matrices commute and the equation is ∂_μ*F*^μν = 0, Maxwell’s equations without sources. □

**The McGucken Principle *dx*₄/*dt* = i*c* reading of Theorem 8.7.8.** The connection *A*_μ records how the origin of the *x*₄-phase at one event is compared with the origin at a neighbouring event, and the field strength is the commutator of two covariant derivatives (Lemma 8.7.6(ii)), the gauge-covariant form of the operator *D*_M of Theorem 8.5.4. Lemma 8.7.6(iv) shows that local invariance leaves the field strength as the only quantity on which a Lagrangian of *A*_μ can depend, and the Lorentz transformations of §8.1 with parity leave the one combination *E*^a·*E*^a − *B*^a·*B*^a. Three inputs are hypotheses of the theorem and are stated as such: the local form of the invariance, hypothesis (a), whose ground is Proposition 9.3.2 and Theorem 123 of [82]; the degree condition (d); and parity invariance (e) of the term containing *A*_μ alone. The simplicity of the Lie algebra of SU(*N*) is a theorem of the theory of Lie algebras and is used as an external result.

**Theorem 8.7.9 (Four-fold uniqueness of the McGucken Lagrangian; Theorem VI.1 of [61]).** The McGucken Lagrangian, the sum of the free-particle kinetic sector −*mc* √(−∂_μ *x*₄ ∂^μ *x*₄), the Dirac matter sector, the Yang–Mills gauge sector −¼ *F*_μν *F*^μν and the Einstein–Hilbert gravitational sector (*c*⁴/16*πG*) *R*, is the unique Lorentz-invariant, reparametrization-invariant, first-order local Lagrangian consistent with the McGucken Principle *dx*₄/*dt* = i*c*. Each sector is forced by a specific uniqueness subtheorem: (a) the free-particle kinetic term by Proposition IV.1 of [61] (Proposition 8.7.1 above); (b) the Dirac matter sector by Theorem 8.7.4 above, which is Proposition V.1 of [61] (the unique first-order Lagrangian on Clifford-algebra fields); (c) the Yang–Mills gauge sector by Theorem 8.7.8 above, which is Proposition VI.2 of [61] (the unique Lagrangian consistent with local *x*₄-phase invariance for a given compact Lie group), with the specific Standard Model gauge group U(1) × SU(2) × SU(3) an empirical input in [61], and the subject of the derivation in [82]; (d) the Einstein–Hilbert gravitational sector by Proposition VI.3 of [61] (Lovelock’s theorem). Each uniqueness subtheorem reduces to the McGucken Principle combined with a specific minimal consistency requirement — Lorentz invariance for (a) and (b), local gauge invariance for (c), diffeomorphism invariance for (d).

**The McGucken Principle *dx*₄/*dt* = i*c* reading of Hilbert’s 1915 action.** Hilbert’s variational principle of §3, *S* = ∫ (*R* + *L*_matter) √−*g* *d*⁴*x*, took the spacetime arena and the form of the Lagrangian as given. In the McGucken *dx*₄/*dt* = i*c* framework the arena is generated by Theorem 4.1, the gravitational sector is sector (d) of Theorem 8.7.9 with Lovelock’s hypotheses derived in §9.2, the gauge sector is sector (c) with the connection of §9.4, and the free-particle sector is Proposition 8.7.1, the accumulated *x*₄-advance of the worldline.

## 9. Explicit Derivations: Lorentzian Signature, Lovelock Closure, the Born Rule, the Gauge Connection, and the Strict Second Law

The McGucken Principle asserts that the fourth dimension *x*₄ is expanding at the velocity of light: *dx*₄/*dt* = i*c*, with *x*₄ = i*ct*, as a spherically symmetric McGucken Sphere whose wavelength sets the action *h* and whose volume defines a nonlocality.

The derivation lattice of §8 sketched the chains by which the canonical content of twentieth-century physics descends from the McGucken Principle. To meet Hilbert’s requirement that “the correctness of the solution” be establishable “by means of a finite number of steps,” we now exhibit five of the most foundationally sensitive derivations in full. The Lorentzian signature is what distinguishes spacetime from a Riemannian 4-manifold; the Lovelock closure is what fixes the Einstein tensor uniquely; the Born rule is what identifies |*ψ*|² as a probability; §9.4 and §9.5 add the gauge connection and the strict second law with the Bekenstein–Hawking entropy. Each is derived from *dx*₄/*dt* = i*c* below in a self-contained calculation.

## 9.1 Lorentzian Signature from the Imaginary Unit

**Claim.** The metric induced by the McGucken Principle on **R**⁴ with coordinates (*t*, **x**) has signature (−, +, +, +).

**Derivation.** Take the Euclidean line element on the four coordinates (*x*₄, *x*¹, *x*², *x*³):

*dσ*² = *dx*₄² + (*dx*¹)² + (*dx*²)² + (*dx*³)².

This is unambiguously positive-definite: it is the standard inner product on **R**⁴. Now apply the McGucken Principle. Differentiating *x*₄ = i*ct* gives *dx*₄ = i*c* *dt*, and therefore *dx*₄² = (i*c*)²*dt*² = −*c*²*dt*². Substituting:

*ds*² = −*c*²*dt*² + *d***x**².

This is the Minkowski line element with signature (−, +, +, +). The negative sign in front of *c*²*dt*² is not a separately postulated convention; it is a calculated consequence of *i*² = −1 applied to the rate *dx*₄/*dt* = i*c*. The light cone, the causal partial order, and the distinction between timelike, null, and spacelike vectors all follow from this single sign. □

**Remark.** The same derivation, with *i* replaced by 1, would have produced a Riemannian (Euclidean) 4-manifold with no light cones. This is what Wick rotation is: the formal substitution *t* → −*iτ* that converts a Minkowskian path-integral into a Euclidean one. From the McGucken viewpoint Wick rotation is not a trick but the inverse of the McGucken substitution: *x*₄ = i*ct* is what gives ordinary spacetime its Lorentzian character; un-doing the *i* returns it to Euclidean form. This signature derivation is part of the original Light–Time–Dimension framework [28] and is used in the GR derivation chain [56, 71] and the GR–QM unification [57]. It is Lemma 1 of [59], where the four-coordinate inner product *dx*₁² + *dx*₂² + *dx*₃² + *dx*₄² is the labelled Hypothesis A3 and the signature (3,1) of the resulting form follows by Sylvester’s law of inertia.

## 9.2 Lovelock’s Theorem and the Closure of the Field Equations

**Theorem (Lovelock 1971)** [14]. Let *M* be a 4-dimensional smooth Lorentzian manifold and let *A*_μν(*g*) be a symmetric (0,2)-tensor that is (i) constructed locally from the metric *g*_μν and its first two derivatives, (ii) divergence-free: ∇^μ*A*_μν = 0. Then *A*_μν = *α* *G*_μν + *β* *g*_μν for some constants *α*, *β*, where *G*_μν = *R*_μν − ½ *g*_μν*R* is the Einstein tensor.

**The McGucken Principle *dx*₄/*dt* = i*c* Reading of the Uniqueness of the Einstein Tensor: Proof for Tensors Linear in the Second Derivatives of the Metric.** **McGucken Principle anchor.** The McGucken Principle *dx*₄/*dt* = i*c* states that the fourth dimension is expanding at the velocity of light in a spherically symmetric manner from every spacetime event, as a McGucken Sphere whose wavelength sets the action *h*. For the field equations the Principle supplies the four-dimensional Lorentzian metric (§9.1) and, through the McGucken-Invariance Lemma below, the vanishing divergence of the matter tensor. The theorem that turns these into the Einstein tensor is proved here in full under the hypothesis that the tensor is linear in the second derivatives of the metric; this form is due to Vermeil [84], Cartan [85] and Weyl. Lovelock’s theorem [14], stated above, removes that hypothesis in four dimensions and is used as an external theorem. Throughout, *η* = diag(−1, 1, 1, 1), indices are raised with the inverse metric, and *G*_μν = *R*_μν − ½*g*_μν*R*.

**Lemma 9.2.1 (Coordinates adapted to a point; the curvature there; the contracted Bianchi identity).** Let *g*_μν be a smooth Lorentzian metric near a point *x*₀ of a four-dimensional manifold. (i) There are coordinates *y* with *y*(*x*₀) = 0 in which *g*_μν(0) = *η*_μν and ∂_ρ*g*_μν(0) = 0. Call them adapted to *x*₀, and write *h*_{μνρσ} = ∂_ρ∂_σ*g*_μν(0), the last two indices being those of the derivatives. (ii) In adapted coordinates the connection coefficients vanish at 0, and there *R*_μν = ½*η*^ρσ(*h*_{ρμνσ} + *h*_{ρνμσ} − *h*_{ρσμν} − *h*_{μνρσ}) and *R* = *η*^μν*η*^ρσ(*h*_{μρνσ} − *h*_{μνρσ}). (iii) If *c*^μ_{αβγ} are constants symmetric in *α*, *β*, *γ*, the coordinates *z*^μ = *y*^μ + (1/6)*c*^μ_{αβγ} *y*^α*y*^β*y*^γ are adapted to *x*₀, and in them *h*_{μνρσ} is replaced by *h*_{μνρσ} − *c*_{μνρσ} − *c*_{νμρσ}, where *c*_{μνρσ} = *η*_μλ*c*^λ_{νρσ}. (iv) For every metric, ∇^μ*R*_μν = ½∂_ν*R*, that is, ∇^μ*G*_μν = 0.

*Proof.* (i) The matrix *g*_μν(*x*₀) is symmetric with one negative and three positive eigenvalues, so by Sylvester’s law of inertia a linear change of coordinates brings it to *η*_μν. Let Γ^μ_αβ be the connection coefficients at the point in these coordinates and put *y*′^μ = *y*^μ + ½Γ^μ_αβ*y*^α*y*^β. The Jacobian matrix of this change is the identity at 0, so *g*(0) is unchanged, and the transformation law of the connection coefficients, new = transformed old minus the second derivatives ∂²*y*′^μ/∂*y*^α∂*y*^β, gives the new coefficients Γ^μ_αβ − Γ^μ_αβ = 0 at 0. Since ∂_ρ*g*_μν = *g*_μλ Γ^λ_νρ + *g*_νλ Γ^λ_μρ, the first derivatives vanish at 0. (ii) With the first index lowered, Γ_μνσ = ½(∂_σ*g*_μν + ∂_ν*g*_μσ − ∂_μ*g*_νσ), and Γ(0) = 0, so the curvature tensor at 0 is *R*_{μνρσ} = ∂_ρ Γ_μνσ − ∂_σ Γ_μνρ = ½(*h*_{μσνρ} + *h*_{νρμσ} − *h*_{μρνσ} − *h*_{νσμρ}). Contracting *μ* with *ρ* by *η* gives *R*_νσ, and contracting again gives *R*. (iii) The inverse change is *y*^μ = *z*^μ − (1/6)*c*^μ_{αβγ}*z*^α*z*^β*z*^γ plus terms of the fifth order, so ∂*y*^α/∂*z*^μ = δ^α_μ − ½*c*^α_{μρσ}*z*^ρ*z*^σ plus terms of the fourth order. The metric in the coordinates *z* is (∂*y*^α/∂*z*^μ)(∂*y*^β/∂*z*^ν)*g*_αβ = *g*_μν − ½*c*_{νμρσ}*z*^ρ*z*^σ − ½*c*_{μνρσ}*z*^ρ*z*^σ plus terms of the third order. Its value and first derivatives at 0 are unchanged and its second derivatives change as stated. (iv) In adapted coordinates the products of connection coefficients in the curvature tensor have vanishing first derivatives at 0, so there ∇_λ*R*^μ_{νρσ} = ∂_λ∂_ρ Γ^μ_νσ − ∂_λ∂_σ Γ^μ_νρ. The sum of this expression over the three cyclic orders of *λ*, *ρ*, *σ* is zero term by term. So ∇_λ*R*^μ_{νρσ} + ∇_ρ*R*^μ_{νσλ} + ∇_σ*R*^μ_{νλρ} = 0 at *x*₀, and, being an equation between tensors, in all coordinates at every point. Contracting *μ* with *ρ* gives ∇_λ*R*_νσ + ∇_μ*R*^μ_{νσλ} − ∇_σ*R*_νλ = 0; contracting *ν* with *λ* by the inverse metric and using the antisymmetry of the curvature tensor in each pair of indices gives 2∇^μ*R*_μσ − ∂_σ*R* = 0. □

**Theorem 9.2.2 (The Einstein tensor is the only divergence-free tensor linear in the second derivatives; Vermeil 1917, Cartan 1922, Weyl 1922).** Let *A*_μν be a symmetric tensor field that, for every Lorentzian metric on a four-dimensional manifold and in every coordinate system, is given by one fixed function of the values at the point of *g*_αβ, ∂_ρ*g*_αβ and ∂_ρ∂_σ*g*_αβ, this function being the sum of a part linear in the second derivatives and a part free of them. If ∇^μ*A*_μν = 0 for every metric, then *A*_μν = *α* *G*_μν + *β* *g*_μν with constants *α*, *β*.

*Proof.* Step 1. Fix a metric and a point *x*₀ and use adapted coordinates (Lemma 9.2.1(i)). There *A*_μν = *L*_μν^{αβρσ} *h*_{αβρσ} + *N*_μν, where the numbers *L* and *N* are the values of the fixed function at *g* = *η*, ∂*g* = 0; they do not depend on the metric or the point. Step 2. A linear change of coordinates by a matrix Λ that preserves *η* keeps the coordinates adapted, transforms *h*_{αβρσ} as a tensor of rank four and *A*_μν as a tensor of rank two, and leaves *L*, *N* the same numbers. Hence *L* and *N* are tensors invariant under the full group of matrices preserving *η*. By the first main theorem of the theory of invariants for the orthogonal group of a non-degenerate quadratic form (Weyl [83], Chapter II), every such invariant tensor is a linear combination of products of the *η*^μν. So *N*_μν = *β* *η*_μν, and, taking into account the symmetry of *h*_{αβρσ} in *α*, *β* and in *ρ*, *σ* and of *A*_μν in *μ*, *ν*, the fifteen ways of pairing the six indices give five expressions: *T*₁ = *η*_μν*η*^αβ*η*^ρσ*h*_{αβρσ}, *T*₂ = *η*_μν*η*^αρ*η*^βσ*h*_{αβρσ}, *T*₃ = *η*^ρσ*h*_{μνρσ}, *T*₄ = *η*^αβ*h*_{αβμν}, *T*₅ = *η*^ρσ(*h*_{ρμνσ} + *h*_{ρνμσ}). Therefore *A*_μν = *a*₁*T*₁ + *a*₂*T*₂ + *a*₃*T*₃ + *a*₄*T*₄ + *a*₅*T*₅ + *β* *η*_μν with constants *a*₁, …, *a*₅.

Step 3. The change of coordinates of Lemma 9.2.1(iii) has Jacobian matrix the identity at *x*₀, so it leaves the components *A*_μν(*x*₀) unchanged while it changes *h*. Take *c*_{μνρσ} = *p*_μ*q*_ν*q*_ρ*q*_σ with vectors *p*, *q*, and write *p*·*q* and *q*·*q* for the products formed with *η*. The change of *h*_{μνρσ} is −(*p*_μ*q*_ν + *p*_ν*q*_μ)*q*_ρ*q*_σ, and the changes of the five expressions are: of *T*₁ and of *T*₂, −2(*p*·*q*)(*q*·*q*)*η*_μν; of *T*₃, −(*q*·*q*)(*p*_μ*q*_ν + *p*_ν*q*_μ); of *T*₄, −2(*p*·*q*)*q*_μ*q*_ν; of *T*₅, −(*q*·*q*)(*p*_μ*q*_ν + *p*_ν*q*_μ) − 2(*p*·*q*)*q*_μ*q*_ν. The change of *A*_μν, which must vanish, is −2(*a*₁ + *a*₂)(*p*·*q*)(*q*·*q*)*η*_μν − (*a*₃ + *a*₅)(*q*·*q*)(*p*_μ*q*_ν + *p*_ν*q*_μ) − 2(*a*₄ + *a*₅)(*p*·*q*)*q*_μ*q*_ν. With *q* the unit vector of the first space axis and *p* that of the second, *p*·*q* = 0 and *q*·*q* = 1, so *a*₃ + *a*₅ = 0. With *q* the sum of the unit vectors of the time axis and the first space axis, *q*·*q* = 0, and with *p* the unit vector of the first space axis, *p*·*q* = 1, so *a*₄ + *a*₅ = 0. With *p* = *q* the unit vector of the first space axis, the remaining term gives *a*₁ + *a*₂ = 0.

Step 4. Hence *A*_μν = *a*₅(*T*₅ − *T*₃ − *T*₄) + *a*₂(*T*₂ − *T*₁) + *β* *η*_μν, which by Lemma 9.2.1(ii) is 2*a*₅ *R*_μν + *a*₂ *R* *g*_μν + *β* *g*_μν at *x*₀. Both sides are tensors, so the equation *A*_μν = *α* *R*_μν + *γ* *R* *g*_μν + *β* *g*_μν, with *α* = 2*a*₅ and *γ* = *a*₂, holds in all coordinates, at every point and for every metric, with the same constants. Step 5. By Lemma 9.2.1(iv) and ∇_λ*g*_μν = 0, ∇^μ*A*_μν = (*α*/2 + *γ*) ∂_ν*R*. This vanishes for every metric. The metric of Theorem 8.2.7 with *k* = 0 and *a*(*t*) = *t*^(2/3) has *R* = 4/(3*c*²*t*²), which is not constant, so *γ* = −*α*/2 and *A*_μν = *α* *G*_μν + *β* *g*_μν. □

The hypotheses of Theorem 9.2.2 are those of Lovelock’s theorem together with linearity in the second derivatives. For the field equations of §8.2 either theorem gives the same conclusion: the tensor equated to the matter tensor is *α* *G*_μν + *β* *g*_μν, with *β*/*α* = Λ and the constant of proportionality 8π*G*/*c*⁴ fixed by the Newtonian limit.

**Closure of GR from McGucken.** What Lovelock requires is exactly what the McGucken Principle supplies. (i) The 4-dimensionality is manifest in the four-coordinate structure (*t*, **x**) of the principle. (ii) The Lorentzian signature is calculated in §9.1. (iii) Diffeomorphism invariance follows from the McGucken-Invariance Lemma (§8.2, proved below): the Lemma selects the foliation-preserving subgroup Diff_McG(*M_G*) as the off-shell symmetry of the matter action, and the full Diff(*M_G*), with ∇_μ*T*^μν = 0 in all four directions, is recovered on shell (Theorem 9 of [71], reproduced below). (iv) The geodesic action *S* = −*mc* ∫ √−*g*_μν·ẋ^μẋ^ν *dλ* is the variational extremum of proper-time arc length on *Σ_M*. Lovelock’s theorem then closes the deduction: the unique field equation derivable from the McGucken hypersurface and divergence-freeness in 4D is *G*_μν + Λ*g*_μν = (8π*G*/*c*⁴)*T*_μν, with the Newtonian limit fixing the constant 8π*G*/*c*⁴ and observation bounding Λ. The full Einstein-equation derivation chain—Lovelock-uniqueness applied on the McGucken hypersurface, with Schwarzschild, FLRW, light-bending, perihelion precession, and gravitational-wave polarizations as downstream theorems—is presented in McGucken [56, 71], with the action and Lagrangian formulation in [61] and the joint GR–QM unification in [57].

**The McGucken Principle *dx*₄/*dt* = i*c* Reading of Lovelock’s Hypotheses: The McGucken-Invariance Lemma and the Factorisation of Diff(*M*).**

**McGucken Principle anchor.** The McGucken Principle *dx*₄/*dt* = i*c* states that the fourth dimension is expanding at the velocity of light in a spherically symmetric manner from every spacetime event, as a McGucken Sphere whose wavelength sets the action *h*. For Lovelock’s hypotheses it predicts two things: the rate of *x*₄-advance is the same at every event whatever the metric there, and the diffeomorphisms that preserve that rate off shell are those that preserve the foliation by spatial slices. Both are theorems of [71]. Their statements and proofs are reproduced here from [71, §1.1, §1.4, §1.5.1]. In them the McGucken Space *M_G* denotes the spacetime of expanding McGucken Spheres at every point, foliated by the spatial slices Σ_*t*.

**Component (i) of the McGucken Principle *dx*₄/*dt* = i*c* (Invariance)** [71, §1.1]. The rate *dx*₄/*dt* = i*c* is the same at every spacetime event *p* ∈ *M_G* and is unaffected by the presence of mass, energy, or curvature in the three spatial dimensions. Formally, for any two events *p*, *q* ∈ *M_G*, the differential rate *dx*₄/*dt*|_*p* equals *dx*₄/*dt*|_*q* identically.

**Lemma (McGucken-Invariance Lemma; Lemma 6 and Theorem GR T2 of [71]).** Let *g*_μν(*x*) be the spacetime metric on the McGucken Space *M_G*. Then

∂(*dx*₄/*dt*)/∂*g*_μν = 0 for all μ, ν ∈ {0, 1, 2, 3}.

That is, the *x*₄-expansion rate is independent of the metric components: it is an input to the geometry, not a derived quantity.

*Proof.* By the McGucken Principle *dx*₄/*dt* = i*c*, component (i), the rate *dx*₄/*dt*|_*p* = i*c* for every *p* ∈ *M_G*, regardless of the local value of *g*_μν(*p*). Suppose, for contradiction, that ∂(*dx*₄/*dt*)/∂*g*_μν ≠ 0 at some event *p*. Then there exist two events *p*, *q* ∈ *M_G* with *g*_μν(*p*) ≠ *g*_μν(*q*) such that *dx*₄/*dt*|_*p* ≠ *dx*₄/*dt*|_*q*, contradicting the McGucken Principle *dx*₄/*dt* = i*c*, component (i). Hence the partial derivative vanishes identically. □

**Theorem (Factorisation of Diff(*M*) via McGucken; Theorem 9 of [71]).** Hilbert’s diffeomorphism invariance of the gravitational action is factored by *dx*₄/*dt* = i*c* into two physically distinct components: (a) A kinematic symmetry: Diff_McG(*M_G*)-invariance of the matter action, where Diff_McG(*M_G*) is the foliation-preserving subgroup of Diff(*M_G*) generated by vector fields *ξ*^μ = (*ξ*⁰(*x*⁰), *ξ*^i(*x*⁰, *x*^j)) with *ξ*⁰ depending only on the time coordinate *x*⁰. (b) A constitutive identity: *u*^μ*u*_μ = −*c*² enforced pointwise on every massive worldline. Off shell, only Diff_McG(*M_G*) acts as a symmetry. On shell, given the matter equations of motion, the full Diff(*M_G*) is recovered, and the Einstein field equations *G*_μν + Λ*g*_μν = (8*πG*/*c*⁴) *T*_μν follow from Lovelock uniqueness plus Newtonian matching.

*Proof.* **Step 1 (the subgroup Diff_McG).** The McGucken Principle *dx*₄/*dt* = i*c* fixes the rate of *x*₄-advance to i*c* at every point of the McGucken Space *M_G*. Under a diffeomorphism *φ*: *M_G* → *M_G* generated by a vector field *ξ*^μ, the McGucken Principle *dx*₄/*dt* = i*c* requires *dx*₄/*dt* = i*c* to be invariant. The constraint is that *ξ*⁰ cannot depend on the spatial coordinates *x*^j in a way that varies the rate at different points of the same spatial slice; otherwise the McGucken Principle *dx*₄/*dt* = i*c* would be broken because *dx*₄/*dt* would acquire spatial dependence. Hence ∂*ξ*⁰/∂*x*^j = 0 for *j* = 1, 2, 3, i.e., *ξ*⁰ = *ξ*⁰(*x*⁰) only. The spatial components *ξ*^j are unconstrained because the spatial sector is deformable. The vector field is therefore of the form *ξ*^μ = (*ξ*⁰(*x*⁰), *ξ*^j(*x*⁰, *x*^k)), which defines Diff_McG(*M_G*).

**Lemma 9.1 of [71].** Diff_McG(*M_G*) is a strict subgroup of Diff(*M_G*). *Proof.* The general vector field on the McGucken Space *M_G* has *ξ*⁰ = *ξ*⁰(*x*⁰, *x*^i) depending on all coordinates. Restricting *ξ*⁰ to depend only on *x*⁰ yields a strict subgroup, since vector fields with ∂*ξ*⁰/∂*x*^j ≠ 0 are not in the McGucken subgroup. □

**Step 2 (the constitutive identity).** By the McGucken Principle *dx*₄/*dt* = i*c*, every massive particle’s worldline *x*^μ(*τ*) on the McGucken Space *M_G* has *dx*₄/*dt* = i*c*. Parametrising by proper time *τ* via *dτ*² = −*c*⁻² *g*_μν *dx*^μ *dx*^ν, the four-velocity is *u*^μ = *dx*^μ/*dτ*. The four-velocity budget identity

*u*^μ*u*_μ = *g*_μν *u*^μ *u*^ν = −*c*²

follows directly from the definition of *τ*: indeed *g*_μν *u*^μ *u*^ν = (*dx*^μ *dx*_μ)/*dτ*² = −*c*². The stress-energy tensor of a perfect fluid of such particles is

*T*^μν = (*ρc*² + *p*) *u*^μ*u*^ν/*c*² + *p* *g*^μν, (T)

with the constraint *u*^μ*u*_μ = −*c*² enforced pointwise.

**Step 3 (Noether’s second theorem applied to Diff_McG).** Apply Noether’s second theorem to the matter action *S_M*[*φ*, *g*_μν] invariant under Diff_McG(*M_G*). Noether’s second theorem is here the standard variational result applied to framework-derived inputs: the McGucken Lagrangian, the Diff_McG-symmetry, and the +i*c* foliation all descend from *dx*₄/*dt* = i*c* (precise status in Remark 10.3.1.1 of [71]). The variational theorem itself is general calculus of variations, a tool, not re-derived from the Principle; what the Principle supplies are its inputs. Therefore the Algebraic Channel rests on no physical input external to *dx*₄/*dt* = i*c* for this step.

The conserved currents associated with Diff_McG-invariance are weaker than those of full Diff(*M_G*) because the symmetry group is smaller. Specifically: (S) spatial diffeomorphism invariance (*ξ*^j unconstrained, *ξ*⁰ = 0) yields the spatial conservation law ∇_i*T*^iν = 0 on each spatial slice Σ_*t*; (T-int) time-reparametrisation invariance (*ξ*⁰ = *ξ*⁰(*x*⁰), *ξ*^j = 0) yields the integrated time-conservation (*d*/*dt*) ∫_{Σ_t} *T*^0ν √*h* *d*³*x* = 0. The local conservation ∇_μ*T*^μν = 0 in all four directions is not given by Diff_McG alone because Diff_McG is a strict subgroup (Lemma 9.1).

**Step 4 (on-shell enhancement).** Claim: given the matter equations of motion (relativistic energy equation and Euler equation) together with the constitutive identity (Step 2), the local conservation ∇_μ*T*^μν = 0 holds in all four directions.

*Proof of the on-shell enhancement.* The perfect-fluid stress-energy tensor (T) gives, upon differentiation,

∇_μ*T*^μν = ∇_μ[(*ρc*² + *p*) *u*^μ/*c*²] *u*^ν + (*ρc*² + *p*) (*u*^μ/*c*²) ∇_μ*u*^ν + *g*^μν ∂_μ*p*. (†)

*Timelike projection.* Contract (†) with *u*_ν and apply the constitutive identity *u*_ν*u*^ν = −*c*² together with its consequence *u*_ν∇_μ*u*^ν = 0 (from differentiating *u*_ν*u*^ν = −*c*², a constant on the constraint surface):

*u*_ν∇_μ*T*^μν = −*c*² ∇_μ[(*ρc*² + *p*) *u*^μ/*c*²] + 0 + *u*^μ∂_μ*p*.

Expanding the first term:

−*c*² ∇_μ[(*ρc*² + *p*) *u*^μ/*c*²] = −∇_μ[(*ρc*² + *p*) *u*^μ] = −(*ρc*² + *p*) ∇_μ*u*^μ − *c*² *u*^μ∂_μ*ρ* − *u*^μ∂_μ*p*.

Combining:

*u*_ν∇_μ*T*^μν = −(*ρc*² + *p*) ∇_μ*u*^μ − *c*² *u*^μ∂_μ*ρ*.

Setting this to zero gives the relativistic energy equation (the perfect-fluid matter equation of motion for energy conservation along the flow):

*u*^μ∂_μ*ρ* + (*ρ* + *p*/*c*²) ∇_μ*u*^μ = 0. (Energy-EOM)

The timelike projection *u*_ν∇_μ*T*^μν = 0 is therefore equivalent to the perfect-fluid energy equation, which is one of the matter equations of motion.

*Spatial projection.* Project (†) orthogonal to *u*^ν using the spatial projector *h*^ν_λ = δ^ν_λ + *u*^ν*u*_λ/*c*² (with *h*^ν_λ *u*^λ = 0 and *h*^ν_λ *h*^λ_σ = *h*^ν_σ):

*h*^ν_λ ∇_μ*T*^μλ = (*ρc*² + *p*) (*u*^μ/*c*²) (*h*^ν_λ ∇_μ*u*^λ) + *h*^νμ ∂_μ*p*,

where the first term of (†) drops out under the spatial projection because *h*^ν_λ *u*^λ = 0, and the third term contracts to *h*^νμ∂_μ*p* via *h*^ν_λ *g*^λμ = *h*^νμ. Setting this to zero gives the relativistic Euler equation (the perfect-fluid matter equation of motion for spatial momentum):

(*ρc*² + *p*) [*c*⁻²] *u*^μ∇_μ*u*^ν = −*h*^νμ ∂_μ*p*, (Euler-EOM)

where the projection *h*^ν_λ ∇_μ*u*^λ ≡ ∇_μ*u*^ν on the spatial subspace (using *u*_λ∇_μ*u*^λ = 0) eliminates the redundant temporal component. The spatial projection *h*^ν_λ ∇_μ*T*^μλ = 0 is therefore equivalent to the relativistic Euler equation.

*Span and conclusion.* The timelike projector −*u*^ν*u*_λ/*c*² and the spatial projector *h*^ν_λ = δ^ν_λ + *u*^ν*u*_λ/*c*² together span the full tangent space at every event:

−*u*^ν*u*_λ/*c*² + *h*^ν_λ = −*u*^ν*u*_λ/*c*² + δ^ν_λ + *u*^ν*u*_λ/*c*² = δ^ν_λ.

Hence the vanishing of ∇_μ*T*^μν on both subspaces implies ∇_μ*T*^μν = 0 identically. On shell, given the relativistic energy equation (Energy-EOM) and the relativistic Euler equation (Euler-EOM) as the perfect-fluid matter equations of motion, the local conservation ∇_μ*T*^μν = 0 holds in all four directions. The matter action becomes invariant under arbitrary diffeomorphisms acting consistently on metric and matter, recovering the full Diff(*M_G*) on shell. □ (end of Step 4)

**Step 5 (Einstein field equations from Lovelock).** With ∇_μ*T*^μν = 0 established, the geometric side of the field equations is forced by Lovelock’s theorem [14]: in four dimensions, the unique divergence-free symmetric rank-2 tensor built from the metric *g*_μν and its first two derivatives, linear in second derivatives, is

*L*_μν = *α* *G*_μν + *β* *g*_μν, (Lovelock)

where *G*_μν = *R*_μν − ½ *R* *g*_μν is the Einstein tensor and *α*, *β* are constants. The priority of Lovelock as a theorem of *dx*₄/*dt* = i*c* operates through the McGucken-Invariance Lemma: the McGucken-Invariance Lemma restricts the allowed geometric structure to one in which *g*_μν is the deformable-sector variable (spatial sector) coupled to matter through the universal Sphere geometry; the Lovelock uniqueness then applies to the divergence-free combinations on the four-dimensional manifold the McGucken Space *M_G*. Setting *L*_μν = *κ* *T*_μν with *κ* = 8*πG*/*c*⁴ and *β* = Λ gives

*G*_μν + Λ *g*_μν = (8*πG*/*c*⁴) *T*_μν. (Einstein field equations)

They are GR T11 of the 24-theorem gravitational chain of [71].

**Step 6 (Newtonian matching fixes *κ*).** In the weak-field static limit *g*₀₀ = −(1 + 2Φ/*c*²), *g*_ij = δ_ij, the dominant component of the Einstein field equations at 00 reduces to Poisson’s equation ∇²Φ = 4*πGρ*. The coupling constant *κ* = 8*πG*/*c*⁴ is forced by this matching. □ (end of the Theorem)

**The McGucken Principle *dx*₄/*dt* = i*c* reading.** Lovelock’s uniqueness theorem enters at Step 5 as an external mathematical result. Each of its hypotheses is supplied by a theorem of *dx*₄/*dt* = i*c*: the four dimensions and the Lorentzian signature by §9.1 (Lemma 1 of [59]); the off-shell symmetry Diff_McG(*M_G*) by the McGucken-Invariance Lemma (GR T2 of [71]); the divergence-free source ∇_μ*T*^μν = 0 by the on-shell enhancement of Step 4. This is the *dx*₄/*dt* = i*c* Algebraic Channel derivation of the Einstein field equations (GR T11 of [71]).

## 9.3 The Born Rule via Symmetry of the Spherical Measure

**Claim.** The probability assigned to outcome corresponding to state |φ⟩, given system in |ψ⟩, is P(φ|ψ) = |⟨φ|ψ⟩|².

**Derivation.** The McGucken Sphere *S_M*(τ) of radius *cτ* carries the unique rotation-invariant (Hausdorff) probability measure μ_SM (Lemma 7.1.5 of [73], reproduced below). By the co-generation of the McGucken Operator *D_M* with the McGucken Sphere *S_M* (Theorem 4.1) and the wave equation □ψ = 0 derived in §8.3, every physically realizable amplitude ψ is a complex-valued function on the McGucken Sphere *S_M*. The space of square-integrable amplitudes is *L*²(*S_M*, μ_SM) with inner product ⟨φ|ψ⟩ = ∫ φ^∗ψ *dμ*_SM; this is the spherical specialization of the complex Hilbert space derived in §8.4.

We now invoke **Gleason’s theorem** (1957) [18]: any σ-additive probability measure ν on the lattice of closed subspaces of a separable complex Hilbert space of dimension ≥ 3 is of the form ν(*P*) = tr(ρ*P*) for a unique density operator ρ. A system prepared in |*ψ*⟩ has ν(*P*_ψ) = 1, which forces ρ = |*ψ*⟩⟨*ψ*|, and this gives ν(*P*_φ) = |⟨*φ*|*ψ*⟩|², the Born rule.

**What the McGucken Principle supplies.** Gleason’s theorem requires (a) a complex Hilbert space of dimension ≥ 3 and (b) the requirement that probability is σ-additive on closed subspaces. (a) is supplied by §8.4 (the McGucken Sphere yields infinite dimension via spherical harmonics). (b) is supplied by Theorem 7.2 of [73], reproduced below, in its projective form: the density |*ψ*|² assigns to the closed subspace with orthogonal projection *Π* the probability ⟨*ψ*|*Πψ*⟩, and for mutually orthogonal projections *Π*_k the sum Σ_k *Π*_k converges strongly to the projection onto the closed span, so the assignment is σ-additive. The Born rule is therefore not a postulate of quantum mechanics in the McGucken *dx*₄/*dt* = i*c* framework but a theorem: the unique phase-invariant quadratic probability measure on the sphere of complex amplitudes generated by the McGucken Principle. □ The full derivation, including the sphere–amplituhedron correspondence and the symmetry argument that fixes the unique measure, is presented in McGucken [58, 59, 60, 72, 73].

**The McGucken Principle *dx*₄/*dt* = i*c* Reading of the Born Rule: The Unique Invariant Measure on the McGucken Sphere and the Uniqueness of *P* = |*ψ*|².**

**McGucken Principle anchor.** The McGucken Principle *dx*₄/*dt* = i*c* states that the fourth dimension is expanding at the velocity of light in a spherically symmetric manner from every spacetime event, as a McGucken Sphere whose wavelength sets the action *h*. For probability it predicts two things: the isotropy of the expansion fixes one and only one rotation-invariant probability measure on the Sphere, and the density that the expansion of *x*₄ conserves is |*ψ*|² and no other function of the modulus. Both are theorems of [73]. Their statements and proofs are reproduced here from [73, §7.3–§7.5]. In [73] the McGucken Sphere of an event *p* is written Σ₊(*p*); it is the McGucken Sphere, written *S_M*(*τ*) in the present paper.

**The McGucken wavefunction (Definition 2.6 of [73]).** For a system whose *x*₄-advance propagates from an event *E*, the McGucken wavefunction evaluated at spatial point **x** ∈ ℝ³ and parameter time *t* is the ℂ-valued field *ψ*(**x**, *t*) := Σ_γ exp(i*S*[*γ*]/ℏ), the sum running over paths *γ* from *E* to (**x**, *t*).

**The four requirements (R1)–(R4) of [73, §7.3].** The McGucken Principle *dx*₄/*dt* = i*c* predicts that a probability density *P* on ℝ³ derived from the McGucken wavefunction *ψ* satisfies four requirements, each supplied by a property of the Sphere, and that these four fix *P* = |*ψ*|² with the quadratic form as the conclusion rather than an input.

**(R1) Reality.** *P* is real-valued. (A probability density counts detection events; it is real by physical interpretation.)

**(R2) Non-negativity.** *P* ≥ 0. (Detection counts are non-negative; the meeting of the emitter’s and the absorber’s McGucken Spheres at the detection event has the non-negative rate *κ*|*ψ*|², Theorem 9.3.5 below, which is Theorem 7.7(iv), (v) of [73].)

**(R3) Phase invariance under global *x*₄-shift.** *P*(*e*^iα *ψ*) = *P*(*ψ*) for all real α. (A global phase is a shift of the *x*₄-origin, and the McGucken Principle *dx*₄/*dt* = i*c* fixes no origin of *x*₄: Proposition 9.3.2 below.)

**(R4) Locality and conservation under *x*₄’s expansion.** (a) The density at an event *B* is a function of the wavefront at *B*; with (R3) this means *P*(**x**) = *F*(|*ψ*(**x**)|). This is Theorem 9.3.3 below (Theorem 7.4 of [73]): the density at *B* is the overlap at *B* of the forward and conjugate *x*₄-expansions. (b) The total ∫ *P* *d*³*x* is the same at every time. The expansion of *x*₄ carries the wavefront forward by the propagation *W*(*t*) = exp(−i*t*Ω), where Ω = *c*(−∇² + *μ*²)^(1/2) with *μ* = *mc*/ℏ is the positive-energy square root of the Klein–Gordon operator, the Klein–Gordon equation (□ − *m*²*c*²/ℏ²)*ψ* = 0 being the differential expression of the McGucken-Sphere wavefront (Theorem 8.3.3; Theorem 9.0 of [72] and theorem QM T8 of [58]), and the McGucken Sphere’s weight, in standard terms the total probability it carries, is conserved along its expansion (Lemma 9.3.6(ii) below; Lemma 6.6C, step 2, of [58]), so a detection is certain at every time. In the non-relativistic regime *W*(*t*) reduces to the Schrödinger evolution of Theorem 8.3.4 (Theorem 9.2 of [72]).

Requirement (R4)(b) is the McGucken Sphere’s conservation law, Lemma 9.3.6 below (Lemma 6.6C of [58]), applied to the wavefront: the weight the source deposits on its McGucken Sphere is neither created nor destroyed as *x*₄ advances. No bilinearity in (*ψ*, *ψ*∗) is required; Lemma 7.1 derives it.

Step 2 of Lemma 6.6C of [58] reads: “By Theorem 6.6B Step 2 the wavefront carries the SO(3)-invariant measure of total mass 1, and by C1 the rate of advance is the same at every event, so no weight is created or destroyed as the wavefront expands.” Requirement (R4)(b) uses this step alone. The quadratic form of the density enters Lemma 6.6C only at its Step 3, which is not used here, so Lemma 7.1 below derives the quadratic form without assuming it.

**Lemma (Quadratic form from conservation under *x*₄’s expansion; Lemma 7.1 of [73]).** Let *F* be continuous on [0, ∞) and twice continuously differentiable on (0, ∞), with *F*(0) = 0, *F*′ bounded on bounded sets, and *F*(*s*) and *sF*′(*s*) both of order *s*^δ as *s* → 0 for some δ > 0. If ∫ *F*(|*ψ*(**x**, *t*)|) *d*³*x* is independent of *t* for every wavefront *ψ*(*t*) = *W*(*t*)*f* with *f* a Schwartz function, then *F*(*s*) = *κ* *s*² for a constant *κ*.

*Notation for the proof.* *R̂* is the operator that multiplies the Fourier transform by *r*(*k*) = *ω*_C + *c*|*k*|²/2*μ* − *ω*(*k*), where *ω*(*k*) = *c*(|*k*|² + *μ*²)^(1/2) and *ω*_C = *cμ* is the Compton frequency. For *x* ≥ 0, 0 ≤ 1 + *x*/2 − (1 + *x*)^(1/2) ≤ *x*²/8, which gives 0 ≤ *r*(*k*) ≤ *c*|*k*|⁴/(8*μ*³).

*Proof.* Take a profile *f* = *g* *e*^iθ with *g* > 0 a Gaussian and *θ* a real smooth function with bounded derivatives, and its dilations *f*_λ(*x*) = *f*(*x*/*λ*), *λ* ≥ 1.

*Step 1, the derivative.* Since ∂*ψ*/∂*t* = −iΩ*ψ* and |*f*| > 0, the derivative of |*ψ*| at *t* = 0 is Im(*e*^−iθ Ω*f*), and the difference quotients of *F*(|*ψ*|) are dominated by an integrable function, because *F*′ is bounded on the range of |*ψ*| and *ψ*, Ω*ψ* are Schwartz functions uniformly for *t* in a bounded interval. Hence

(*d*/*dt*) ∫ *F*(|*ψ*|) *d*³*x* = ∫ *F*′(*g*) Im(*e*^−iθ Ω*f*) *d*³*x*, *t* = 0.

*Step 2, splitting Ω.* By the bound just stated, Ω = *ω*_C + (*c*/2*μ*)(−∇²) − *R̂*, where *R̂* multiplies the Fourier transform by *r*(*k*) with 0 ≤ *r*(*k*) ≤ *c*|*k*|⁴/(8*μ*³). The term *ω*_C contributes Im(*e*^−iθ *ω*_C *f*) = 0.

*Step 3, the main term.* Im(*e*^−iθ (−∇²*f*)) = −(1/*g*) ∇·(*g*²∇*θ*). Integrating by parts twice, with *H*(*s*) = *sF*′(*s*) − 2*F*(*s*), so that *H*′(*s*) = *sF*″(*s*) − *F*′(*s*), and with no boundary terms because *H*(*g*) and *gF*′(*g*) are of order *g*^δ and *g* is a Gaussian,

∫ (*F*′(*g*)/*g*) (−∇·(*g*²∇*θ*)) *d*³*x* = ∫ ∇(*H*(*g*))·∇*θ* *d*³*x* = −∫ *H*(*g*) ∇²*θ* *d*³*x*.

*Step 4, scaling.* For *f*_λ the main term equals −(*c*/2*μ*) *λ* ∫ *H*(*g*)∇²*θ* *d*³*x*, since the volume element contributes *λ*³ and the Laplacian *λ*⁻². The remainder is bounded by sup|*F*′(*g*)| · ‖(1 + |*x*|²/*λ*²)⁻¹‖₂ · ‖(1 + |*x*|²/*λ*²)*R̂f*_λ‖₂ ≤ *C* *λ*^(3/2) · *λ*^(3/2 − 4) = *C* *λ*⁻¹, because *λ*⁴ *r*(*k*/*λ*) and its first two derivatives are bounded by polynomials in |*k*| uniformly in *λ* ≥ 1.

*Step 5, conclusion.* Conservation makes the derivative vanish for every *λ*. Dividing by *λ* and letting *λ* → ∞ gives ∫ *H*(*g*)∇²*θ* *d*³*x* = 0 for every admissible *θ*, in particular for every smooth *θ* of compact support. So *H*(*g*(*x*)) is a continuous weakly harmonic function on ℝ³ that tends to *H*(0) = 0 at infinity, and it vanishes identically by the maximum principle. A Gaussian of height *a* takes every value in (0, *a*], so *H*(*s*) = 0 for all *s* > 0. Then *sF*′(*s*) = 2*F*(*s*), whose solutions are *F*(*s*) = *κ* *s*². □

For the Schrödinger propagation of Theorem 9.2 of [72], Ω is replaced by −(ℏ/2*m*)∇² up to the rest-energy constant, Steps 2 and 4 are void, and Step 3 alone gives ∫ *H*(*g*)∇²*θ* *d*³*x* = 0.

**McGucken Principle anchor.** The exponent 2 is the exponent that *x*₄’s expansion conserves. The rank-2 character of the interval (i*ct*)² = −*c*²*t*² enters through the Klein–Gordon equation, which is second order because the interval is quadratic, and the lemma shows that its propagation conserves the quadratic density and no other function of the modulus. The conservation itself is the Sphere’s: Lemma 6.6C of [58], the weight of the Sphere conserved along its advance [73, §7.4].

**Lemma (Uniqueness of the SO(3)-invariant probability measure on the McGucken Sphere; Lemma 7.1.5 of [73]).** The McGucken Sphere Σ₊(*p*) at parameter time *t* is a round 2-sphere *S*² of radius *ct*, carrying the transitive action of the rotation group SO(3) fixed by the isotropy of the *x*₄-expansion from *p*. Then there exists a unique SO(3)-invariant Borel probability measure on the Sphere, namely the normalized round measure *dμ*(*θ*, *φ*) = sin*θ* *dθ* *dφ*/(4π), and every SO(3)-invariant probability density on the Sphere is this *dμ*. This is the measure that supplies the Born-rule probability over detection directions.

*Proof.* The Sphere is a homogeneous space for SO(3): the isotropy of the expansion makes the action transitive, and the stabilizer of any direction is the subgroup SO(2) of rotations about that axis, so Σ₊(*p*) ≅ SO(3)/SO(2) = *S*². SO(3) is compact; by Haar’s theorem (1933) [32] every compact group carries a unique normalized invariant measure, which pushes forward to a unique invariant probability measure on any homogeneous space. Existence: *dμ* = sin*θ* *dθ* *dφ*/(4π) is invariant because rotations are isometries of the round metric and sin*θ* *dθ* *dφ* is its area element. Uniqueness: if *ν* is any SO(3)-invariant probability measure, then for continuous *f*, invariance plus transitivity give ∫ *f* *dν* = ∫ *f* *dμ* (both equal the round average of *f*); since this holds for all continuous *f*, *ν* = *μ* by the Riesz representation theorem. □

**The McGucken Principle *dx*₄/*dt* = i*c* Reading of the Requirements (R1)–(R4): the Conjugate Expansion, the Overlap at the Detection Event, the Meeting of Two McGucken Spheres, and the Inverse-Square Law of the McGucken Sphere.**

**McGucken Principle anchor.** The McGucken Principle *dx*₄/*dt* = i*c* states that the fourth dimension is expanding at the velocity of light in a spherically symmetric manner from every spacetime event, as a McGucken Sphere whose wavelength sets the action *h*. For the four requirements it predicts the following. (R3) holds because the Principle fixes no origin of *x*₄ (Proposition 9.3.2). (R1), (R2) and (R4)(a) hold because the density at an event is the overlap, at that event, of the expansion with its conjugate reading (Theorem 9.3.3, Lemma 9.3.4, Theorem 9.3.5). (R4)(b) holds because each McGucken Sphere carries the total weight 1 at every radius (Lemma 9.3.6). The statements are Definition 7.3, Theorem 7.4, Lemma 7.7.5 and Theorem 7.7(ii), (iv), (v) of [73] and Lemma 6.6C of [58]. The proofs are given here in full.

**Definition 9.3.1 (Conjugate expansion; Definition 7.3 of [73]).** The conjugate of the McGucken expansion *x*₄ = i*ct* is the expansion obtained by complex-conjugating both sides: *x*₄∗ = (i*ct*)∗ = −i*ct*. Complex conjugation reverses the orientation of the perpendicular *x*₄-axis. The conjugate is the same expansion read in the opposite orientation; it has no independent physical existence. For a wavefunction *ψ* = Σ_γ exp(i*S*[*γ*]/ℏ) the conjugate wavefunction is *ψ*∗ = Σ_γ exp(−i*S*[*γ*]/ℏ): the same paths with the opposite phase.

**Proposition 9.3.2 (The McGucken Principle *dx*₄/*dt* = i*c* fixes no origin of *x*₄; the ground of (R3)).** (i) If *x*₄(*t*) satisfies *dx*₄/*dt* = i*c*, then *x*₄(*t*) + *a* satisfies it for every constant *a*. (ii) The amplitude of a body at rest, *ψ* = exp(−i*ω*_C *t*) of Theorem 8.3.1, is on *x*₄ = i*ct* the function exp(−*μ* *x*₄) with *μ* = *mc*/ℏ. Replacing *x*₄ by *x*₄ + i*cτ*₀ with *τ*₀ real multiplies it by exp(−i*ω*_C *τ*₀), and every number exp(i*α*) with *α* real is obtained, for *τ*₀ = −*α*/*ω*_C. (iii) If the amplitude at the emission event is multiplied by exp(i*α*), then the wavefunction is multiplied by exp(i*α*) at every later event. Consequently *ψ* and exp(i*α*)*ψ* are the wavefunctions of one emission referred to two origins of *x*₄, and a density determined by the Principle has the same value on both. This is (R3).

*Proof.* (i) *d*(*x*₄ + *a*)/*dt* = *dx*₄/*dt*. (ii) On *x*₄ = i*ct*, *t* = *x*₄/(i*c*), so −i*ω*_C *t* = −*ω*_C *x*₄/*c* = −*μ* *x*₄, because *ω*_C/*c* = *mc*/ℏ. Then exp(−*μ*(*x*₄ + i*cτ*₀)) = exp(−i*μc* *τ*₀) exp(−*μ* *x*₄) and *μc* = *ω*_C. (iii) The wavefunction at a later event is obtained from the amplitude at emission by the superposition of Theorem 8.3.3(ii) or by the integral of Theorem 8.3.8(ii). Both are linear in the amplitude at emission, so a constant factor passes through them. □

**Theorem 9.3.3 (The density is the overlap of the forward and the conjugate expansion; Theorem 7.4 of [73]).** (i) Let *γ*₁, …, *γ*_N be paths from an event *A* to an event *B* with real actions *S*₁, …, *S*_N, and let *ψ*(*B*) = Σ_j exp(i*S*_j/ℏ). Then the product of the conjugate and the forward wavefunction at *B* is

*ψ*∗(*B*) *ψ*(*B*) = Σ_j Σ_k exp(i(*S*_j − *S*_k)/ℏ) = *N* + 2 Σ_(jS_j − *S*_k)/ℏ),

a real number with 0 ≤ *ψ*∗*ψ* ≤ *N*². The *N* terms with *j* = *k* each equal 1, one for each path. The terms with *j* ≠ *k* are the interference terms, each fixed by the difference of the actions of two paths. (ii) For the free evolution of Theorem 8.3.8, with *ψ*(*x*, *t*) = ∫ *K*_t(*x* − *y*) *φ*₀(*y*) *d*³*y* and *S*(*x*, *y*) = *m*|*x* − *y*|²/(2*t*) the action of the straight path from *y* to *x*,

*ψ*∗(*x*, *t*) *ψ*(*x*, *t*) = (*m*/(2πℏ*t*))³ ∫∫ exp(i(*S*(*x*, *y*) − *S*(*x*, *y*′))/ℏ) *φ*₀∗(*y*′) *φ*₀(*y*) *d*³*y* *d*³*y*′.

(iii) In both cases *ψ*∗*ψ* at the event is |*ψ*|² there: a real, non-negative function of the value of the wavefunction at that event and of nothing else.

*Proof.* (i) Multiply the two sums: *ψ*∗*ψ* = (Σ_k exp(−i*S*_k/ℏ)) (Σ_j exp(i*S*_j/ℏ)) = Σ_j Σ_k exp(i(*S*_j − *S*_k)/ℏ). The terms with *j* = *k* are exp(0) = 1 and there are *N* of them. The terms (*j*, *k*) and (*k*, *j*) with *j* < *k* are complex conjugates and add to 2 cos((*S*_j − *S*_k)/ℏ). Since *ψ*∗ is the complex conjugate of *ψ*, *ψ*∗*ψ* = |*ψ*|² ≥ 0, and |*ψ*| ≤ *N* because each term has modulus 1. (ii) By Theorem 8.3.8(i), *K*_t(*x* − *y*) = (*m*/(2πiℏ*t*))^(3/2) exp(i*S*(*x*, *y*)/ℏ), and |(*m*/(2πiℏ*t*))^(3/2)|² = (*m*/(2πℏ*t*))³. The conjugate of the integral for *ψ* is the integral of the conjugate integrand, with integration variable *y*′, and the product of the two integrals is the double integral because *φ*₀ is a Schwartz function and the exponentials have modulus 1, so the double integral converges absolutely. (iii) is the identity *z*∗*z* = |*z*|² for a complex number *z*. □

**The McGucken Principle *dx*₄/*dt* = i*c* reading of Theorem 9.3.3.** The forward wavefunction carries the phase exp(i*S*/ℏ) of the action clause for *x*₄ = i*ct*, and the conjugate wavefunction carries the phase of the same paths for *x*₄∗ = −i*ct* (Definition 9.3.1). The product at *B* is the overlap at *B* of the two readings of one expansion. Its value depends on the wavefront at *B* alone, which is (R4)(a), and it is real and non-negative, which is (R1) and (R2). That no other pairing of the two readings is available is the next lemma.

**Lemma 9.3.4 (Coincidence of two McGucken Spheres: the product and the conjugation; Lemma 7.7.5 of [73]).** Let two McGucken Spheres, of a source *A* and a source *B*, arrive at the spatial point *P* at the time *t*, with amplitudes *ψ*_A(*P*) and *ψ*_B(*P*). (a) Product. Let *ψ*_A(*P*) = Σ_j exp(i*S*_j/ℏ) over the paths of *A* and *ψ*_B(*P*) = Σ_k exp(i*T*_k/ℏ) over the paths of *B*, and let the action of a pair of paths, one of each source, be the sum *S*_j + *T*_k. Then the amplitude of the pair of arrivals, summed over the pairs of paths, is the product *ψ*_A(*P*) *ψ*_B(*P*). (b) Conjugation. Let *V* be a real vector space and *β* a real symmetric bilinear form on *V* that is not identically zero. On the complexification, whose elements are *z* = *u* + i*v* with *u*, *v* in *V*, *β* has exactly one extension *β*_bil that is complex-linear in both arguments and exactly one extension *β*_ses that is conjugate-linear in the first argument and complex-linear in the second. The diagonal value *β*_bil(*z*, *z*) has a non-zero imaginary part for some *z*. The diagonal value *β*_ses(*z*, *z*) = *β*(*u*, *u*) + *β*(*v*, *v*) is real for every *z*. (c) Uniqueness. Let *R*(*z*, *w*) be a complex-valued function of two complex numbers that is additive and real-homogeneous in *z*, complex-linear in *w*, unchanged when *z* and *w* are multiplied by the same exp(i*α*) (the requirement (R3)), and real for *z* = *w* (the requirement (R1)). Then *R*(*z*, *w*) = *κ* *z*∗*w* with *κ* a real constant, and *R*(*z*, *z*) ≥ 0 for every *z* (the requirement (R2)) exactly when *κ* ≥ 0.

*Proof.* (a) By exp(*a* + *b*) = exp(*a*) exp(*b*) and the distributive law, the double sum Σ_j Σ_k exp(i(*S*_j + *T*_k)/ℏ) equals Σ_j Σ_k exp(i*S*_j/ℏ) exp(i*T*_k/ℏ), which is the product of the sum Σ_j exp(i*S*_j/ℏ) and the sum Σ_k exp(i*T*_k/ℏ). The sum of the actions is the action of two worldlines without interaction: the functional of Proposition 8.7.1 is an integral along each worldline, and by the action clause each action is ℏ times the phase of the advance of *x*₄ along its path, so the phases of two separate advances add. The value of the pair amplitude is therefore the product, and the other symmetric combinations of two amplitudes differ from it: for *ψ*_A = *ψ*_B = i the product is −1, the sum is 2i, and the maximum, the minimum, the arithmetic mean, the geometric mean and the harmonic mean of the moduli are each 1.

(b) A form that is complex-linear in both arguments and equals *β* on *V* must satisfy *β*_bil(*u* + i*v*, *u*′ + i*v*′) = *β*(*u*, *u*′) − *β*(*v*, *v*′) + i(*β*(*u*, *v*′) + *β*(*v*, *u*′)), by expanding in both arguments with i·i = −1, and this formula defines such a form. A form that is conjugate-linear in the first argument, complex-linear in the second and equal to *β* on *V* must satisfy *β*_ses(*u* + i*v*, *u*′ + i*v*′) = *β*(*u*, *u*′) + *β*(*v*, *v*′) + i(*β*(*u*, *v*′) − *β*(*v*, *u*′)), by expanding with (−i)·i = 1, and this formula defines such a form. Put *z* = *w* and use *β*(*u*, *v*) = *β*(*v*, *u*): *β*_bil(*z*, *z*) = *β*(*u*, *u*) − *β*(*v*, *v*) + 2i *β*(*u*, *v*) and *β*_ses(*z*, *z*) = *β*(*u*, *u*) + *β*(*v*, *v*). Since *β* is symmetric and not identically zero, the identity 4*β*(*u*, *u*′) = *β*(*u* + *u*′, *u* + *u*′) − *β*(*u* − *u*′, *u* − *u*′) gives a vector *u*₀ with *β*(*u*₀, *u*₀) ≠ 0, and for *z* = *u*₀ + i*u*₀ the imaginary part of *β*_bil(*z*, *z*) is 2*β*(*u*₀, *u*₀) ≠ 0. For the values of amplitudes at a point, *V* is the real line and *β*(*u*, *u*′) = *uu*′; then *β*_bil(*z*, *w*) = *zw* and *β*_ses(*z*, *w*) = *z*∗*w*, and for *z* = i the first gives (i)² = −1 while the second gives (−i)·i = 1 = |i|².
