(c) For fixed *z*, *R*(*z*, *w*) = *L*(*z*) *w* with *L*(*z*) = *R*(*z*, 1), because *R* is complex-linear in *w*. *L* is additive and real-homogeneous, so with *z* = *x* + i*y*, *L*(*z*) = *x* *L*(1) + *y* *L*(i) = *a* *z* + *b* *z*∗, where *a* = (*L*(1) − i*L*(i))/2 and *b* = (*L*(1) + i*L*(i))/2. Hence *R*(*z*, *w*) = *a* *zw* + *b* *z*∗*w*. Multiplying *z* and *w* by exp(i*α*) multiplies *zw* by exp(2i*α*) and leaves *z*∗*w* unchanged, so the invariance, taken at *z* = *w* = 1 and *α* = π/2, gives −*a* + *b* = *a* + *b*, that is *a* = 0. Then *R*(*z*, *z*) = *b* |*z*|², which is real at *z* = 1 only if *b* is real. With *κ* = *b*, *R*(*z*, *w*) = *κ* *z*∗*w*, and *κ*|*z*|² ≥ 0 for every *z* exactly when *κ* ≥ 0. □

**The McGucken Principle *dx*₄/*dt* = i*c* reading of Lemma 9.3.4.** The symmetric real form of part (b) is the one the Principle supplies: (i*c*)² = −*c*² gives the interval and its symmetric metric *η*_μν (§8.1), and the factor i of *x*₄ = i*ct* makes the amplitudes complex (Theorem 8.3.1). Part (c) shows that the requirements (R1) and (R3), with linearity in the arriving amplitude, leave the pairing *z*∗*w* and no other, so the conjugation in the Born density is the conjugate reading of Definition 9.3.1 and is obtained without a separate postulate of time reversal.

**Theorem 9.3.5 (The rate at the meeting of the emitter’s and the absorber’s McGucken Spheres; Theorem 7.7(ii), (iv), (v) of [73]).** (i) Conjugation and the direction of the expansion. If *φ*(*x*, *t*) satisfies the Schrödinger equation iℏ ∂ₜ*φ* = −(ℏ²/2*m*) ∇²*φ* + *Vφ* with real *V*, then *φ*∗(*x*, *t*) satisfies the equation with i replaced by −i, which is the equation of the conjugate expansion *x*₄∗ = −i*ct*, and *φ*∗(*x*, −*t*) satisfies the original equation. For *V* = 0 and *r* > 0, the outgoing spherical wave exp(i(*kr* − *ωt*))/*r* with ℏ*ω* = ℏ²*k*²/2*m* is a solution, and the operation *φ*(*x*, *t*) → *φ*∗(*x*, −*t*) carries it to exp(−i(*kr* + *ωt*))/*r*, the spherical wave of the same frequency converging on the centre. (ii) Rate. The rate of the coincidence at *P* of an arriving amplitude *ψ*_A(*P*) with an absorbing amplitude *ψ*_B(*P*), being a pairing that satisfies the hypotheses of Lemma 9.3.4(c), is *κ* *ψ*_B∗(*P*) *ψ*_A(*P*) with *κ* > 0. (iii) Detection. For an absorber at *P* that takes up the wavefront arriving there, the absorbing amplitude is the arriving amplitude *ψ*(*P*) in its conjugate reading, and the rate is *κ* *ψ*∗(*P*) *ψ*(*P*) = *κ* |*ψ*(*P*)|².

*Proof.* (i) Take the complex conjugate of the equation; *V*, *m*, ℏ and the operators ∂ₜ and ∇² are real, so −iℏ ∂ₜ*φ*∗ = −(ℏ²/2*m*) ∇²*φ*∗ + *Vφ*∗. Put *χ*(*x*, *t*) = *φ*∗(*x*, −*t*); then ∂ₜ*χ*(*x*, *t*) = −(∂ₜ*φ*∗)(*x*, −*t*), and the last equation becomes iℏ ∂ₜ*χ* = −(ℏ²/2*m*) ∇²*χ* + *Vχ*. For a function of *r* alone, ∇²*g* = (1/*r*) ∂²(*rg*)/∂*r*², so ∇²(exp(i*kr*)/*r*) = −*k*² exp(i*kr*)/*r* for *r* > 0, and iℏ ∂ₜ gives ℏ*ω*; the two sides agree when ℏ*ω* = ℏ²*k*²/2*m*. The conjugate of exp(i(*kr* + *ωt*))/*r*, which is the wave at −*t*, is exp(−i(*kr* + *ωt*))/*r*; its surfaces of constant phase are *kr* + *ωt* = constant, on which *r* decreases at the rate *ω*/*k* as *t* increases. (ii) is Lemma 9.3.4(c), with *κ* > 0 because a rate that is not identically zero is positive somewhere. (iii) By Definition 9.3.1 the conjugate reading is of the same expansion, so both arguments of the pairing are the one number *ψ*(*P*), and *R*(*ψ*(*P*), *ψ*(*P*)) = *κ* |*ψ*(*P*)|². □

**The McGucken Principle *dx*₄/*dt* = i*c* reading of Theorem 9.3.5.** The McGucken Sphere of the emitter arrives at *P* as an outgoing wavefront. The conjugate reading *x*₄∗ = −i*ct* of that wavefront is, by part (i), the wavefront converging on *P*. A detection is the meeting at *P* of the two, and its rate is |*ψ*(*P*)|² up to the constant *κ* fixed by normalization. Requirements (R1) and (R2) are therefore properties of this rate, and Theorem 7.2 below fixes the density from (R1)–(R4).

**Lemma 9.3.6 (Inverse-square law of the McGucken Sphere and the exponent 2; Lemma 6.6C of [58]).** Write *r* = |*x*| and let *ψ* be a solution of the wave equation □*ψ* = 0 of the McGucken Sphere’s wavefront (Theorem 8.3.3 with *μ* = 0) that depends on *r* and *t* alone. (i) For *r* > 0, *ψ* = (*f*(*r* − *ct*) + *g*(*r* + *ct*))/*r* with *f*, *g* twice continuously differentiable functions of one variable. The outgoing wavefront is *f*(*r* − *ct*)/*r*: its amplitude at the radius *r* = *c*(*t* − *t*_p) from its source event *p* falls as 1/*r*. (ii) The McGucken Sphere of radius *r* has area 4π*r*², and its rotation-invariant probability measure (Lemma 7.1.5) has the weight 1/(4π*r*²) per unit area, the same total weight 1 at every radius. (iii) Let *F* be a continuous function on [0, ∞). If ∫ *F*(|*ψ*|) *d*³*x* has the same value at every *t* > 0 for every outgoing wavefront *ψ* = *f*(*r* − *ct*)/*r* with *f* twice continuously differentiable and zero outside a bounded interval [0, *ε*], then *F*(*s*) = *κ* *s*² for a constant *κ*. (iv) With the density *κ*|*ψ*|², a source at distance *r* from an event *E* deposits there a density proportional to 1/*r*², and the sources in the shell between *r* and *r* + *dr* about *E*, of volume 4π*r*² *dr*, deposit together an amount proportional to *dr*, which is finite on every bounded range of distances.

*Proof.* (i) For a function of *r* and *t*, ∇²*ψ* = (1/*r*) ∂²(*rψ*)/∂*r*², so *u* = *rψ* satisfies ∂²*u*/∂*r*² − *c*⁻² ∂²*u*/∂*t*² = 0. In the variables *ξ* = *r* − *ct* and *η* = *r* + *ct* this equation is 4 ∂²*u*/∂*ξ*∂*η* = 0, whose solutions are *u* = *f*(*ξ*) + *g*(*η*). The term *f*(*r* − *ct*) keeps its value along *r* = *ct* + constant, so it moves outward at the rate *c*, and |*ψ*| = |*f*|/*r*. (ii) The area of a round sphere of radius *r* is 4π*r*². By Lemma 7.1.5 the invariant probability measure is the normalized area measure, whose weight per unit area is 1/(4π*r*²) and whose total is 1.

(iii) Fix *a* > 0 and *ε* > 0. Choose twice continuously differentiable functions *f*_n with 0 ≤ *f*_n ≤ *a*, zero outside [0, *ε*], and *f*_n(*u*) → *a* for every *u* strictly between 0 and *ε*. Where *f*_n(*r* − *ct*) is different from zero the radius is *r* = *ct* + *u* with *u* ≥ 0, hence *r* ≥ *ct* > 0, so each *f*_n(*r* − *ct*)/*r* is a solution for *t* > 0 by (i), and in polar coordinates ∫ *F*(|*ψ*|) *d*³*x* = 4π ∫ *F*(*f*_n(*r* − *ct*)/*r*) *r*² *dr*, the integral running over *ct* ≤ *r* ≤ *ct* + *ε*. *F* is continuous, hence bounded on [0, *a*/(*ct*)], so by dominated convergence the limit as *n* → ∞ is 4π ∫ *G*(*r*) *dr* over *ct* ≤ *r* ≤ *ct* + *ε*, with *G*(*r*) = *F*(*a*/*r*) *r*², and this limit has the same value at every *t* > 0 because each term of the sequence has. Take two times *t* and *t*′. The two integrals of *G*, over [*ct*, *ct* + *ε*] and over [*ct*′, *ct*′ + *ε*], are equal for every *ε* > 0; *G* is continuous, so differentiating with respect to *ε* gives *G*(*ct* + *ε*) = *G*(*ct*′ + *ε*). Any two radii 0 < *r*₁ < *r*₂ are of this form, with *ε* = *r*₁/2, *ct* = *r*₁/2 and *ct*′ = *r*₂ − *r*₁/2. So *G* is a constant *C*(*a*) on (0, ∞): *F*(*a*/*r*) = *C*(*a*)/*r*². Every *s* > 0 is *a*/*r* with *r* = *a*/*s*, so *F*(*s*) = *C*(*a*) *s*²/*a*² for all *s* > 0 and every *a* > 0; with *a* = 1, *F*(*s*) = *C*(1) *s*², and *F*(0) = 0 by continuity. (iv) By (i) the amplitude at distance *r* from the source is proportional to 1/*r*, so *κ*|*ψ*|² is proportional to 1/*r*²; multiplied by the volume 4π*r*² *dr* of the shell this is proportional to *dr*. □

**The McGucken Principle *dx*₄/*dt* = i*c* reading of Lemma 9.3.6.** The expansion of *x*₄ is spherically symmetric, so the McGucken Sphere has area 4π*r*² and its amplitude falls as 1/*r*; the expansion has the same rate *c* at every event, so the weight of the McGucken Sphere stays 1 as it advances. Part (iii) shows that these two facts fix the exponent: a density that is a function of the modulus of the amplitude, and whose total over the McGucken Sphere is the same at every radius, is the square of the modulus. This is the inverse-square law, and it is the requirement (R4)(b) for the wavefront of the McGucken Sphere itself. Lemma 6.6C of [58] states the same relation between the 1/*r* amplitude and the 1/*r*² density. Lemma 7.1 above proves (R4)(b) ⇒ *F*(*s*) = *κ* *s*² for the Klein–Gordon propagation of a body of rest mass *m*; for that propagation ∫ |*ψ*|² *d*³*x* is conserved because *W*(*t*) multiplies the Fourier transform by exp(−i*ω*(*k*)*t*), a number of modulus 1, and Plancherel’s identity applies as in the proof of Theorem 8.4.1(i).

**Theorem (Born rule from *dx*₄/*dt* = i*c*; Theorem 7.2 of [73]).** Let *ψ*: ℝ³ → ℂ be the McGucken wavefunction. The unique density *P*: ℝ³ → ℝ_{≥0} satisfying (R1), (R2), (R3), (R4) is

*P*(**x**) = |*ψ*(**x**)|² / ∫ |*ψ*|² *d*³*x*.

*Proof.* By (R3) and (R4)(a), *P* = *F*(|*ψ*|). By (R4)(b) and Lemma 7.1, *F*(*s*) = *κ* *s*². (R1) and (R2) hold for *κ* > 0, and normalization fixes *κ*. □

**The McGucken Principle *dx*₄/*dt* = i*c* reading of Gleason’s theorem.** Gleason’s theorem (1957) [18] is an external mathematical result: on a separable complex Hilbert space of dimension ≥ 3 every σ-additive probability measure on closed subspaces has the trace form. In the McGucken *dx*₄/*dt* = i*c* framework its two hypotheses are supplied by theorems: the Hilbert space by Theorem 6.1 of [72] (§8.4), and the σ-additive assignment by Theorem 7.2 of [73] above. The Born rule itself is Theorem 7.2, proved from (R1)–(R4) with the quadratic form derived in Lemma 7.1 and without Gleason’s theorem; Gleason’s theorem adds that no other σ-additive assignment exists on the derived Hilbert space. Lemma 7.1 is the Conservation Route to the exponent 2, and Lemma 7.1.5 is the route through the symmetry group of the McGucken Sphere [73, §7.4–§7.5].

## 9.4 Gauge Connection from Covariantization of the McGucken Operator *D_M*

**Claim.** The gauge-covariant derivative ∇_μ = ∂_μ + i*qA*_μ/ℏ arises uniquely from local *U*(1) covariantization of the McGucken Operator *D_M*, and the Yang–Mills connection arises by the same construction with SU(*N*) in place of U(1).

**Derivation.** Under a global phase rotation ψ → *e*^iαψ (with α constant), the McGucken operator *D_M* commutes with the transformation: *D_M*(*e*^iαψ) = *e*^iα(*D_M*ψ). Promote α to a function α(*x*): now *D_M*(*e*^iα(x)ψ) = *e*^iα(*D_M* + i(*D_M*α))ψ, and the operator no longer commutes. To restore covariance we introduce a 1-form field *A*_μ transforming as *A*_μ → *A*_μ − (ℏ/*q*)∂_μα and replace ∂_μ by ∇_μ = ∂_μ + i*qA*_μ/ℏ. This is the unique minimal coupling consistent with local *U*(1) gauge invariance and reproducing the Maxwell equations (via *F*_μν = ∂_μ*A*_ν − ∂_ν*A*_μ) when the action − (1/4) *F*_μν*F*^μν is added. Replacing *U*(1) by *SU*(*N*) yields the non-abelian *A*_μ = *A*_μ^a*T*^a and the corresponding Yang–Mills field strength *F*_μν^a = ∂_[μ*A*_ν]^a + *gf*^abc*A*_μ^b*A*_ν^c. □ The covariantization of the McGucken Operator *D_M* as the source of the gauge connection, and the associated Lagrangian, are developed in McGucken [59, 61]. The covariance of the field strength is proved in Lemma 8.7.6, and the uniqueness of the Lagrangian −¼ *F*^a_μν *F*_a^μν of the connection in Theorem 8.7.8.

## 9.5 The Strict Second Law and Bekenstein–Hawking Entropy

We give two explicit derivations: the strict second law *dS*/*dt* = (3/2)*k*_*B*/*t* from *D_M*-driven Brownian motion, and the Bekenstein–Hawking entropy *S*_BH = *k*_*B* *A*/(4*ℓ*_*P*²) from the McGucken Wick rotation.

**Claim 1 (Strict second law).** For an ensemble of test particles subject only to *D_M*-driven isotropic spatial displacement, the Boltzmann–Gibbs entropy satisfies *dS*/*dt* = (3/2)*k*_*B*/*t* > 0 for all *t* > 0.

**Derivation.** By Theorem 5 of McGucken (2026e), the spatial projection of the *x*₄-driven displacement at any instant is isotropic on the spherical wavefront. By Theorem 6, iterated isotropic displacements give Wiener-process motion: Var(**r**(*t*)) = 6*Dt*, with the diffusion constant *D* fixed by the Compton-coupling formula of Theorem 14. The probability density of the test ensemble is therefore the Gaussian fundamental solution of the heat equation:

*ρ*(**r**, *t*) = (4*πDt*)^−3/2 exp(−|**r**|²/(4*Dt*)).

Substitute into the Boltzmann–Gibbs entropy:

*S*(*t*) = −*k*_*B* ∫ *ρ*(**r**, *t*) ln *ρ*(**r**, *t*) *d*³*r*.

Using ln *ρ* = −(3/2) ln(4*πDt*) − |**r**|²/(4*Dt*), the integral splits into the normalization piece and the second-moment piece. The first gives (3/2)*k*_*B* ln(4*πDt*); the second uses ∫ |**r**|²*ρ* *d*³*r* = 6*Dt* and yields (3/2)*k*_*B*. Adding:

*S*(*t*) = (3/2)*k*_*B*[1 + ln(4*πDt*)].

Differentiating with respect to *t*:

*dS*/*dt* = (3/2)*k*_*B*(1/*t*) > 0.

The inequality is strict for every finite *t* > 0, with no appeal to the Past Hypothesis, no ergodic hypothesis, and no coarse-graining. The second law is the rate at which *D_M*-driven Gaussian spreading on the McGucken Sphere *S_M*(τ) widens the spatial-projection support. □

**Remark on Loschmidt.** The Loschmidt reversibility paradox (1876) observes that any Hamiltonian dynamics is time-reversal symmetric, hence cannot derive a strict *dS*/*dt* > 0. The McGucken resolution (Theorem 12) is that the spatial Hamiltonian channel (the Algebraic Channel) is indeed reversible, but it is not the only channel: the geometric expansion *R*(*t*) = *ct* on the McGucken Sphere *S_M*(τ) (the Geometric Channel) is monotone in *x*₄ by definition of the McGucken Principle. Boltzmann’s H-theorem (1872) and the Loschmidt objection both concern the Algebraic Channel alone; the Geometric Channel supplies the geometric monotonicity they were missing. The strict inequality of Claim 1 is a Geometric Channel identity, not a Algebraic Channel theorem.

**Claim 2 (Bekenstein–Hawking entropy [36, 37]).** A Killing horizon of area *A* in the McGucken hypersurface carries entropy *S*_BH = *k*_*B* *A*/(4*ℓ*_*P*²), where *ℓ*_*P* = (ℏ*G*/*c*³)^1/2 is the Planck length.

**Derivation.** The argument has six steps.

**Step 1 (Horizon as *x*₄-stationary surface).** A Killing horizon is the locus where a timelike Killing vector becomes null. In McGucken-coordinates this is the locus where the *x*₄-component of motion vanishes for stationary observers; the horizon is *x*₄-stationary in the instantaneous co-moving frame.

**Step 2 (McGucken Wick rotation).** Define *τ*_E = *x*₄/*c* = i*t*. Then *dx*₄/*dτ*_E = *c*: read in *τ*_E, the law carries no i. In the variables (*t*, *τ*_E) the McGucken Operator is *D_M* = ∂ₜ + i*c*∂₄ = ∂ₜ + i∂_τE = 2∂/∂*z̄* with *z* = *t* + i*τ*_E, the Cauchy–Riemann operator, which is elliptic; the derivative identity ∂_τ = −i∂ₜ is Theorem 10.1 of [65], where *τ*_E is written *τ*. The Lorentzian metric becomes the Euclidean Riemannian metric *dσ*² = *c*²*dτ*_E² + *d***x**².

**Step 3 (Euclidean cigar).** Near the horizon, the Euclideanized metric is *dσ*² = *κ*²*ξ*²*dθ*² + *dξ*² + *dA*, with *ξ* the proper distance to the horizon and *κ* the surface gravity. Regularity at *ξ* = 0 (no conical singularity) requires *θ* to be periodic with period 2*π*/*κ*; the Euclideanized time circle is the cigar.

**Step 4 (Hawking temperature).** The Matsubara identification *β* = 1/(*k*_*B* *T*) = 2*π*/*κ* in natural units, with the ℏ and *c* restored from *τ*_E = *x*₄/*c*:

*T*_H = ℏ*κ*/(2*πck*_*B*).

This is Theorem 16; it falls out of the Wick rotation without a separate quantization of the matter field.

**Step 5 (Mode counting at the Planck density).** On the Euclidean horizon the *x*₄-stationary modes are quantized at one mode per Planck area: *N*(*A*) = *A*/*ℓ*_*P*². By the Planck-scale quantization of *x*₄-oscillation, the smallest independent mode of *x*₄-oscillation has wavelength *ℓ*_*P* = (ℏ*G*/*c*³)^(1/2); on the two-dimensional horizon the minimum cross-sectional area per mode is *ℓ*_*P*², and for a black hole, the maximum-entropy configuration of given mass, charge and angular momentum, the count is saturated ([62], Theorem 15, Step B3). Each mode contributes *k*_*B* ln 2 in the naive Bekenstein argument [36], but the precise normalization is fixed by Step 6.

**Step 6 (First-law normalization).** The thermodynamic identity *dE* = *T*_H *dS*_BH at the horizon, combined with the first law of black-hole mechanics *dE* = (*c*²*κ*/8*πG*) *dA* [70] (for a Schwarzschild black hole *E* = *Mc*², *κ* = *c*⁴/(4*GM*), *A* = 16*πG*²*M*²/*c*⁴, so that (*c*²*κ*/8*πG*) *dA* = *c*² *dM*), fixes the proportionality constant:

*dS*_BH/*dA* = (*c*²*κ*/8*πG*) / (ℏ*κ*/2*πck*_*B*) = *k*_*B* *c*³/(4ℏ*G*) = *k*_*B*/(4*ℓ*_*P*²),

yielding

*S*_BH = *k*_*B* *A*/(4*ℓ*_*P*²).

The factor of 1/4 is fixed by the Wick-rotation period of the Euclidean cigar; no additional axiom is required. The generalized second law *dS*ₜₒₜₐₗ/*dt* ≥ 0 (Theorem 17) now follows by adding Claim 1 (matter entropy) to the area increase theorem of Hawking (1971) [69], which holds, under the null energy condition and cosmic censorship, on the Lorentzian causal structure derived in §9.1. With Hawking radiation, where the horizon area decreases, the generalized second law is Theorem 17 of [62], stated as the non-decrease of the total *x*₄-flux through closed hypersurfaces. □

**Remark.** In the standard derivation of Bekenstein–Hawking the Wick rotation *t* → −i*τ*_E is performed by hand as a calculational device. In the McGucken framework it is part of the law: the i is already in the law *dx*₄/*dt* = i*c*, and the rotation *τ*_E = *x*₄/*c* simply reads the law in its Euclidean chart. The Hawking temperature, Bekenstein area-entropy, and generalized second law are theorems about the inverse of the same i that produced the Lorentzian signature in §9.1 and the complex Hilbert space in §8.4.

## 9.6 The McGucken Principle *dx*₄/*dt* = i*c* Reading of the Operator Non-Identity and Maximality Theorems, the Category of the Source-Pair, the Symmetry Theorems, Twistor Space, the Unit i, and the Spectral Triples of Connes

The McGucken Principle asserts that the fourth dimension *x*₄ is expanding at the velocity of light: *dx*₄/*dt* = i*c*, with *x*₄ = i*ct*, as a spherically symmetric McGucken Sphere whose wavelength sets the action *h* and whose volume defines a nonlocality.

**McGucken Principle anchor.** The McGucken Principle *dx*₄/*dt* = i*c* states that the fourth dimension is expanding at the velocity of light in a spherically symmetric manner from every spacetime event, as a McGucken Sphere whose wavelength sets the action *h*. This section proves, inside the paper, six groups of results that the companion papers [59, 60, 65, 66, 92, 93] state about the McGucken Operator *D_M* = ∂ₜ + i*c*∂₄ and the McGucken Space *M_G*: the non-identity and maximality of the McGucken Operator; the category of the source-pair with its functors; the symmetry results of Noether, Wigner and of the CPT theorem, with the exact standing of the gauge group, of supersymmetry and of the string dualities; twistor space and the kinematic data of the amplituhedron from the McGucken Sphere; the origin of the unit i in the formulas of quantum theory; and the relation to the spectral triples of Connes. Each result is stated with its hypotheses and proved step by step. Where a statement of a companion paper rests on a theorem that is not proved here, the theorem is named, attributed and marked as external, and the part proved here is stated separately.

**9.6.1 The McGucken Principle *dx*₄/*dt* = i*c* Reading of the Operator Non-Identity and Maximality Theorems.** **McGucken Principle anchor.** The McGucken Principle *dx*₄/*dt* = i*c* states that the fourth dimension is expanding at the velocity of light in a spherically symmetric manner from every spacetime event, as a McGucken Sphere whose wavelength sets the action *h*. The Principle makes *x*₄ a coordinate that advances, and the McGucken Operator *D_M* differentiates along that advance. The operators of the earlier literature act on functions of the event (*t*, **x**) alone. The commutator with *x*₄ separates the two classes.

**Lemma 9.6.1 (Commutators of the McGucken Operator).** Let 𝒦 be the space of the functions Ψ(*t*, **x**, *x*₄) of Definition 6.3, with values in ℂ or in ℂ^N, differentiable in *t* and **x** and holomorphic in *x*₄. Write *x*₄ also for the operator of multiplication by *x*₄. Call a linear operator *T* on 𝒦 an operator of spacetime if there is a linear operator *T*₀ on functions of (*t*, **x**) with (*T*Ψ)(·, ·, *x*₄) = *T*₀(Ψ(·, ·, *x*₄)) for every value of *x*₄. Then (i) [*D_M*, *x*₄] = i*c*; (ii) [*D_M*, *t*] = 1; (iii) every operator of spacetime commutes with *x*₄; (iv) the operators on 𝒦 that commute with *x*₄ are closed under sums, products and multiplication by numbers; (v) with (*R*Ψ)(*t*, **x**, *x*₄) = Ψ(*t*, **x**, −*x*₄), *R* *D_M* *R*⁻¹ = ∂ₜ − i*c*∂₄.

*Proof.* (i) *D_M*(*x*₄Ψ) = *x*₄∂ₜΨ + i*c*(Ψ + *x*₄∂₄Ψ) = *x*₄*D_M*Ψ + i*c*Ψ. (ii) *D_M*(*t*Ψ) = Ψ + *t*∂ₜΨ + i*c* *t*∂₄Ψ = *t* *D_M*Ψ + Ψ. (iii) For a fixed value of *x*₄ the function (*x*₄Ψ)(·, ·, *x*₄) is the number *x*₄ times Ψ(·, ·, *x*₄), and *T*₀ is linear, so *T*(*x*₄Ψ) = *x*₄ *T*Ψ. (iv) If *A* and *B* commute with *x*₄, then (*A* + *B*)*x*₄ = *x*₄(*A* + *B*), *AB* *x*₄ = *A* *x*₄ *B* = *x*₄ *AB*, and (λ*A*)*x*₄ = *x*₄(λ*A*). (v) *R* maps 𝒦 onto itself, *R*² = 1, *R* commutes with ∂ₜ, and ∂₄(*R*Ψ) = −*R*(∂₄Ψ), so *R*∂₄*R*⁻¹ = −∂₄. □

**Theorem 9.6.2 (Non-identity of the McGucken Operator; Theorems 34.4–34.9 of [65] in the form proved here).** (a) The McGucken Operator *D_M* is different from every operator of spacetime, and from every sum of products of operators of spacetime. In particular it is different from the Hamiltonian *Ĥ* = iℏ∂ₜ and from every Schrödinger operator iℏ∂ₜ + (ℏ²/2*m*)∇² − *V*(*t*, **x**) of Theorem 8.3.4; from the momentum operator −iℏ∇ and the position operators of Theorem 8.3.5; from the d’Alembertian □ on functions of (*t*, **x**); from the Dirac operator iℏγ^μ∂_μ + *mc* of Theorem 8.5.3; from every gauge-covariant derivative ∂_μ + *A*_μ(*t*, **x**); and from the operators *D* and π(*a*) of every spectral triple (𝒜, ℋ, *D*) whose Hilbert space consists of functions or spinor fields on spacetime, extended to 𝒦 with *x*₄ as a parameter. (b) the McGucken Operator *D_M* is different from the Dirac operator *D_E* = −iΣ_μ γ_E^μ∂_μ of the Euclidean four-coordinate space of Theorem 9.6.20: for Ψ = *x*₁², *D_M*²Ψ = 0 and *D_E*²Ψ = −2. (c) the McGucken Operator *D_M* is different from the Wick substitution (*W*Ψ)(τ, **x**) = Ψ(−iτ, **x**): *W* satisfies *W*(ΨΦ) = *W*(Ψ)*W*(Φ), the McGucken Operator *D_M* satisfies *D_M*(ΨΦ) = Ψ*D_M*Φ + Φ*D_M*Ψ, and a linear map with both properties is zero. (d) The Wheeler–DeWitt operator acts on functionals of the metrics of a three-dimensional space; its domain has no function of (*t*, **x**, *x*₄) in it, so it and the McGucken Operator *D_M* are operators on different spaces.

*Proof.* (a) By Lemma 9.6.1(iii) and (iv) every operator of spacetime, and every sum of products of such operators, commutes with *x*₄. By Lemma 9.6.1(i) the commutator of the McGucken Operator *D_M* with *x*₄ is i*c*, and *c* > 0. Each operator in the list acts on the variables (*t*, **x**) and on the components of Ψ alone, with *x*₄ as a parameter, so each is an operator of spacetime. (b) Ψ = *x*₁² is independent of *t* and *x*₄, so *D_M*Ψ = 0, and *D_E*² = −(∂₁² + ∂₂² + ∂₃² + ∂₄²) by Theorem 9.6.20(i), which gives −2. (c) If a linear map *L* satisfies both rules, then *L*(1) = *L*(1·1) = *L*(1)² by the first and *L*(1) = 2*L*(1) by the second, so *L*(1) = 0, and then *L*(Ψ) = *L*(1·Ψ) = *L*(1)*L*(Ψ) = 0 for every Ψ. The McGucken Operator *D_M* is different from zero, since *D_M* *t* = 1. (d) is the comparison of the two domains. □

**Theorem 9.6.3 (Maximality of the McGucken Operator; Section 33.10 of [65] in the form proved here).** (i) Generation. The Hamiltonian, the d’Alembertian, the Dirac operator and the gauge-covariant operator are obtained from the McGucken Operator *D_M*: iℏ*D_M* = *Ĥ* − i*c* *p̂*₄ (Theorem 8.5.1); the four-coordinate Laplacian restricted to the constraint surface *x*₄ = i*ct* is □ (Theorem 8.5.2); □ − μ² is the product of the two Dirac operators (Theorem 8.5.3); and *D_M* + *A_M* is the gauge-covariant operator (Theorem 8.5.4). (ii) No return. No sum of products of the operators of spacetime of Theorem 9.6.2(a) equals the McGucken Operator *D_M*. (iii) Orientation. Let *E_R* be the set of the operators on 𝒦 that commute with the reflection *R* of Lemma 9.6.1(v); it contains ∂ₜ, ∇, ∂₄², the four-coordinate Laplacian ∇² + ∂₄², and the multiplications by functions of (*t*, **x**) and of *x*₄². *E_R* is closed under sums and products, and the McGucken Operator *D_M* does not belong to *E_R*.

*Proof.* (i) is the statement of the four theorems cited. (ii) is Theorem 9.6.2(a). (iii) ∂ₜ, ∇ and the multiplications by functions of (*t*, **x**) commute with *R*; ∂₄² = (−∂₄)² and (−*x*₄)² = *x*₄² give the same for ∂₄² and for functions of *x*₄². If *A*, *B* commute with *R* so do *A* + *B* and *AB*. By Lemma 9.6.1(v), *R* *D_M* *R*⁻¹ − *D_M* = −2i*c*∂₄, which is different from zero on Ψ = *x*₄. □

**The McGucken Principle *dx*₄/*dt* = i*c* reading.** The commutator [*D_M*, *x*₄] = i*c* is the McGucken Principle *dx*₄/*dt* = i*c* in operator form: the McGucken Operator *D_M* advances *x*₄ at the rate i*c* per unit of *t*. An operator of spacetime leaves *x*₄ where it is. Part (iii) states the same for the sign: the operators that are even under *x*₄ → −*x*₄ cannot produce the operator that carries the orientation +i*c* of the expansion. The sentence of [65] that no operator of the earlier literature coincides with the McGucken Operator is a statement about the literature; what is proved here is the list (a)–(d) of Theorem 9.6.2 and parts (ii), (iii) of Theorem 9.6.3. One identity belongs with that list. As a differential expression in two real variables *t* and *s* = *x*₄/*c*, ∂ₜ + i*c*∂₄ = ∂ₜ + i∂_s = 2∂/∂*z*∗ with *z* = *t* + i*s*, the Cauchy–Riemann operator in the notation of Wirtinger [103], and *z* = (i/*c*)Φ_M. The McGucken Principle supplies what the expression by itself lacks: the variable *x*₄ is the fourth dimension of the physical world, the surface Φ_M = 0 is the set of events, and the three spatial dimensions with the McGucken Sphere at each event complete the McGucken Space.

**9.6.2 The McGucken Principle *dx*₄/*dt* = i*c* Reading of the Category of the Source-Pair: Selection of the Rate i*c*, the Obstruction Theorem, the Six Functors and the Initial Object.** **McGucken Principle anchor.** The McGucken Principle *dx*₄/*dt* = i*c* states that the fourth dimension is expanding at the velocity of light in a spherically symmetric manner from every spacetime event, as a McGucken Sphere whose wavelength sets the action *h*. The source-pair theorems of §6 (Theorems 6.12–6.23) show that the McGucken Operator and the McGucken Space each contain and each generate the other. This subsection determines which part of that reciprocity belongs to every law of constant rate and which part belongs to the rate i*c* alone, and it gives the category of the source-pair with exact definitions.

**Proposition 9.6.4 (The reciprocal properties for a law of constant rate).** For a complex number *k* ≠ 0 let a coordinate *y* obey *dy*/*dt* = *k*, and put Φ_k = *y* − *kt* and *D_k* = ∂ₜ + *k*∂_y. Then (a) *D_k*Φ_k = 0; (b) a function Ψ(*t*, *y*), differentiable in *t* and holomorphic in *y*, satisfies *D_k*Ψ = 0 exactly when it is a function of *y* − *kt* alone; (c) *D_k* is the only operator ∂ₜ + *b*∂_y with constant *b* and (∂ₜ + *b*∂_y)Φ_k = 0, and Φ_k is the only function *y* − *bt* with *D_k*(*y* − *bt*) = 0. Consequently Lemmas 6.9–6.11, Theorems 6.12–6.16 and 6.18, Steps 1–3 of Theorem 6.17, and the parts of Theorems 6.19–6.23 that do not involve Step 4 of Theorem 6.17, hold for the pair (Φ_k, *D_k*) with *k* in place of i*c*. For *k* = i*c* and *y* = *x*₄ the pair is the constraint function Φ_M together with the McGucken Operator *D_M*.

*Proof.* (a) ∂ₜΦ_k = −*k* and ∂_y Φ_k = 1. (b) Put *u* = *y* − *kt* and Ψ(*t*, *y*) = *F*(*t*, *u*). Then ∂ₜΨ + *k*∂_y Ψ = ∂ₜ*F* − *k*∂_u*F* + *k*∂_u*F* = ∂ₜ*F*, which vanishes exactly when *F* is independent of *t*. (c) (∂ₜ + *b*∂_y)(*y* − *kt*) = *b* − *k* and *D_k*(*y* − *bt*) = *k* − *b*. The proofs of the results listed use (a), (b), (c) and the chain rule *d*Ψ/*dt* = ∂ₜΨ + (*dy*/*dt*)∂_y Ψ, and no other property of the number i*c*. Step 4 of Theorem 6.17 uses (i*c*)² = −*c*²; it is the subject of the next theorem. □

**Theorem 9.6.5 (The rate i*c* is selected by the Lorentzian interval and by propagation at a finite speed).** In the situation of Proposition 9.6.4 let the four coordinates (*x*₁, *x*₂, *x*₃, *y*) carry the form *d*ℓ² = *dx*₁² + *dx*₂² + *dx*₃² + *dy*². (i) On the surface *y* = *kt* the form is *d***x**² + *k*²*dt*². (ii) It is real for all real displacements exactly when *k*² is real. It has one negative and three positive squares exactly when *k*² < 0, that is, *k* = ±iκ with κ > 0; it is positive definite exactly when *k* is real. (iii) For *G*(**x**, *y*) holomorphic in *y* and *g*(*t*, **x**) = *G*(**x**, *kt*), (∂_y²*G* + ∇²*G*)(**x**, *kt*) = ∇²*g* + *k*⁻²∂ₜ²*g*. For *k* = ±iκ this is the wave operator ∇² − κ⁻²∂ₜ² with the speed κ; for real *k* it is the Laplace operator in the four real variables (**x**, *kt*). (iv) With κ = *c*, the invariant speed, the two laws are *dy*/*dt* = ±i*c*. The reflection *y* → −*y* exchanges them, and the McGucken Principle fixes the sign +, the direction in which *x*₄ advances.

*Proof.* (i) *dy* = *k* *dt*. (ii) The coefficient of *dt*² is *k*²; the three coefficients of *dx_j*² are 1. (iii) ∂ₜ*g* = *k* ∂_y*G* and ∂ₜ²*g* = *k*² ∂_y²*G* at *y* = *kt*, by the chain rule, as in Theorem 8.5.2. (iv) Under *y* → −*y* the law *dy*/*dt* = *k* becomes *dy*/*dt* = −*k*. □

**Proposition 9.6.6 (The single-relation obstruction; the earlier arena–operator pairs. Theorems 6.11 and 6.12 of [66] in the form proved here).** (i) Obstruction. Let *X* be the data of an arena, and suppose that two different operators *L*₁ ≠ *L*₂ are each compatible with the same data *X*. Then no procedure whose only input is *X* produces both, so at least one of the pairs (*X*, *L*₁), (*X*, *L*₂) fails generation of the operator by the arena (Definition 6.8). (ii) The Hilbert space *L*²(ℝ³) carries the Hamiltonians −(ℏ²/2*m*)∇² and −(ℏ²/2*m*)∇² + ½*m*ω²|**x**|². (iii) If (𝒜, ℋ, *D*) is a spectral triple, so is (𝒜, ℋ, 2*D*), with the same algebra and Hilbert space and with every distance of Connes halved. (iv) A smooth manifold without metric carries the Laplace operators of the metrics *g* and 2*g*, which differ by the factor ½. (v) A Riemannian manifold (*M*, *g*) and its Laplace operator Δ_g determine each other: Δ_g = *g*^ij∇_i∇_j is built from *g*, and *g*^ij∂_i*f*∂_j*f* = ½(Δ_g(*f*²) − 2*f*Δ_g*f*) for every smooth *f*. (vi) The complex plane with the operator ∂/∂*z*∗ is the pair (Φ_k, *D_k*) of Proposition 9.6.4 with *k* = i up to the constant factors stated after Theorem 9.6.3, and has the properties (a)–(c) of that proposition.

*Proof.* (i) A procedure in the sense of Definition 6.8 returns one output for one input. (ii) Both are self-adjoint operators on domains in *L*²(ℝ³), and they differ on every function that is not zero almost everywhere. (iii) 2*D* is self-adjoint with the resolvent of *D* rescaled, and [2*D*, *a*] = 2[*D*, *a*] is bounded when [*D*, *a*] is; the condition ‖[2*D*, *f*]‖ ≤ 1 is ‖[*D*, *f*]‖ ≤ ½, so the supremum of |*f*(*p*) − *f*(*q*)| in Theorem 9.6.20(iii) is halved. (iv) The inverse of the metric 2*g* is ½*g*^ij, and the connection coefficients of *g* and 2*g* agree. (v) By the product rule for ∇, Δ_g(*f*²) = 2*f*Δ_g*f* + 2*g*^ij∂_i*f*∂_j*f*; the values of *g*^ij∂_i*f*∂_j*f* for all *f* determine *g*^ij at each point. (vi) is Proposition 9.6.4. □

**The McGucken Principle *dx*₄/*dt* = i*c* reading.** Proposition 9.6.4 and Theorem 9.6.5 separate two things. Mutual containment and reciprocal generation hold for the transport operator of every law of constant rate, and Proposition 9.6.6(v), (vi) records two earlier pairs, the Riemannian manifold with its Laplace operator and the complex plane with the Cauchy–Riemann operator, in which the arena and the operator also determine each other. What the McGucken Principle *dx*₄/*dt* = i*c* adds, and what the earlier pairs do not have, is stated in Theorem 9.6.5 and in Definitions 6.4–6.5: a law of advance in the physical time *t*; the rate i*c*, which among the constant rates is the one that yields the Lorentzian interval and the wave operator with the invariant speed *c*; the orientation +i*c*; and the McGucken Sphere at every event. The statement of [66], Theorem 6.11, that the McGucken source-pair is the first pair for which the three source-pair theorems hold, is to be read with these qualifications, and its remark that the Riemannian pair fails by the isospectral manifolds answering the question of Kac is to be replaced by Proposition 9.6.6(v): isospectral manifolds share the spectrum of the Laplace operator, while the operator itself determines the metric.

**Definition 9.6.7 (The category of the McGucken source-pair).** Let ISO⁺(1,3) denote, as in Lemma 6.26, the group of the maps *x* → Λ*x* + *a* of the events *x* = (*ct*, **x**), with *a* a translation and Λ a Lorentz transformation preserving the direction of time and the orientation (Definition 5.1(c), Lemma 6.26). **McG** is the category with one object, the McGucken source-pair (McGucken Space *M_G*, McGucken Operator *D_M*), whose morphisms are the elements of ISO⁺(1,3) with the composition of maps. Each element of ISO⁺(1,3) preserves the interval *d***x**² − *c*²*dt*² that Step 4 of Theorem 6.17 obtains from the source-pair, and preserves the direction of the advance of *x*₄ = i*ct*.

**Theorem 9.6.8 (The six functors of the source-pair; Theorems 7.10–7.18 of [66] in the form proved here).** Each of the following assigns to the object of **McG** an object of the category named and to each *g* = (*a*, Λ) in ISO⁺(1,3) an isomorphism *F*(*g*) of that object, with *F*(1) = 1 and *F*(*gh*) = *F*(*g*)*F*(*h*); each is therefore a functor. (1) *F*_(spacetime), to the Lorentzian manifolds with their isometries preserving the direction of time: the object ℝ⁴ with the interval of Lemma 6.25, and *F*(*g*) = *g*. (2) *F*_(Hilbert), to the Hilbert spaces with unitary maps: the Fock space ℱ of Definition 5.1 and *F*(*g*) = *V*(*a*, Λ). (3) *F*_(Clifford), to the algebras with their isomorphisms: the algebra *Cl*, the quotient of the tensor algebra of ℝ⁴ by the two-sided ideal generated by the elements *v* ⊗ *v* + η(*v*, *v*)·1, and *F*(*g*) the automorphism induced by *v* → Λ*v*; the matrices of Theorem 8.5.3 represent *Cl* by *v* → *v*_μ γ^μ. (4) *F*_(gauge), for each group *G* of Definition 8.7.5, to the principal *G*-bundles with their isomorphisms: the product bundle ℝ⁴ × *G* and *F*(*g*)(*x*, *u*) = (*gx*, *u*). (5) *F*_(algebra), to the C∗-algebras with their ∗-isomorphisms: the algebra of the bounded operators on ℱ and *F*(*g*)(*B*) = *V*(*g*)*B* *V*(*g*)⁻¹. (6) *F*_(Klein), to the sets with a transitive action of a group, where a morphism is a pair of an isomorphism φ of the groups and a bijection *f* of the sets with *f*(*h*·*x*) = φ(*h*)·*f*(*x*): the set ℝ⁴ with the action of ISO⁺(1,3), which is the Klein pair (ISO⁺(1,3), SO⁺(1,3)) of Theorems 6.28 and 6.30, and *F*(*g*) = (*h* → *ghg*⁻¹, *x* → *gx*).

*Proof.* (1) Each *g* is an isometry by Lemma 6.26, and the composition in **McG** is the composition of maps. (2) *V* is a unitary representation of ISO⁺(1,3) by Theorem 5.4 (W1). (3) The linear map Λ extends to an automorphism of the tensor algebra, multiplicative by construction; it maps *v* ⊗ *v* + η(*v*, *v*) to Λ*v* ⊗ Λ*v* + η(Λ*v*, Λ*v*), since η(Λ*v*, Λ*v*) = η(*v*, *v*), so it maps the ideal onto itself and induces an automorphism of the quotient; the extension of ΛΛ′ is the product of the extensions. The relation (*v*_μ γ^μ)² = −η(*v*, *v*) of Theorem 8.5.3 shows that *v* → *v*_μ γ^μ respects the ideal. (4) *F*(*g*) is a bijection of ℝ⁴ × *G* that covers *g* and commutes with the right multiplication of *G* on the second factor. (5) Conjugation by a unitary operator is a ∗-isomorphism, and *V*(*gh*) = *V*(*g*)*V*(*h*). (6) The translations act transitively, the subgroup fixing the origin is the group of the Λ, and *g*(*h*·*x*) = (*ghg*⁻¹)·(*gx*); both components are multiplicative in *g*. □

**Theorem 9.6.9 (The initial-object statement; Theorem 7.21 of [66] in the form proved here).** Let *S* be a set of mathematical structures that contains the McGucken source-pair, and write *X* ≤ *Y* when *Y* is generated from *X* in the sense of Definition 6.8. Regard (*S*, ≤) as a category with one morphism from *X* to *Y* when *X* ≤ *Y* and none otherwise. (i) The source-pair is an initial object of this category exactly when every member of *S* is generated from it. (ii) This holds for the set *S* consisting of the source-pair together with the Lorentzian spacetime (Lemma 6.25), the Klein pair of spacetime (Theorem 6.30), the Hilbert space of states (Theorem 8.4.3), the Fock space with its free fields (Definition 5.1, Theorems 5.4 and 5.7), the operators of Theorems 8.5.1–8.5.4, and the Klein pairs of Theorems 6.33, 6.34, 6.36–6.39 and 6.41–6.43. (iii) In the category **McG** of Definition 9.6.7 the single object has the automorphism group ISO⁺(1,3), which has more than one element, so it is not an initial object of **McG**.

*Proof.* (i) An initial object is one with exactly one morphism to each object; in a category with at most one morphism between two objects this is the existence of the morphism, that is, *X*₀ ≤ *Y* for every *Y*. (ii) Each theorem cited constructs the structure named from the source-pair by an explicit procedure. (iii) An initial object has the identity as its only morphism to itself. □

**The McGucken Principle *dx*₄/*dt* = i*c* reading.** Theorems 9.6.8 and 9.6.9 give the two senses in which the source-pair of the McGucken Principle *dx*₄/*dt* = i*c* stands at the base of the categories of mathematical physics: as the object whose automorphisms act, functorially, on spacetime, on the space of states, on the Clifford algebra, on the gauge bundles, on the algebra of operators and on the Klein pair; and as the least element of the order of generation among the structures constructed in this paper. Three corrections are owed in [66], §7: the morphisms defined there by the conditions (C1), (C2) include maps that are not isometries, for example (*t*, **x**, *x*₄) → (*t*, 2**x**, *x*₄), so the functor to the Lorentzian manifolds requires the morphisms of Definition 9.6.7; the uniqueness of the morphism in its Theorem 7.21 holds in the sense of part (i) above; and part (iii) above shows that no category in which the Poincaré group acts on the source-pair has it as an initial object.

**9.6.3 The McGucken Principle *dx*₄/*dt* = i*c* Reading of Noether’s Theorem, the Classification of Wigner, the CPT Theorem, the Gauge Group, Supersymmetry and the String Dualities.** **McGucken Principle anchor.** The McGucken Principle *dx*₄/*dt* = i*c* states that the fourth dimension is expanding at the velocity of light in a spherically symmetric manner from every spacetime event, as a McGucken Sphere whose wavelength sets the action *h*. The Principle yields the group ISO⁺(1,3) of Definition 9.6.7 (Theorems 6.28, 6.30) and the Lagrangian of Theorem 8.7.9. The symmetry results of [59] are consequences of these two together with the theorems proved or named below.

**Theorem 9.6.10 (Noether’s first theorem [94]; Theorem 30 of [59]).** Let ℒ(φ, ∂φ) be a twice differentiable function of fields φ^a(*x*) on ℝ⁴ and of their first derivatives, π_a^μ = ∂ℒ/∂(∂_μ φ^a), and *E_a* = ∂ℒ/∂φ^a − ∂_μ π_a^μ, so that the field equations of §8.7 are *E_a* = 0. Suppose that for functions Δ^a of the fields, of their derivatives and of *x* the first-order change of ℒ under φ^a → φ^a + εΔ^a is ε∂_μ*K*^μ for every field configuration, with *K*^μ a function of the fields, their derivatives and *x*. Then *j*^μ = π_a^μ Δ^a − *K*^μ satisfies ∂_μ*j*^μ = −*E_a*Δ^a for every field configuration, and ∂_μ*j*^μ = 0 for every solution of the field equations. If the fields vanish at spatial infinity fast enough for the integral to exist and for the surface term to vanish, *Q* = ∫ *j*⁰ *d*³*x* is independent of *t*.

*Proof.* The first-order change of ℒ is (∂ℒ/∂φ^a)Δ^a + π_a^μ∂_μ Δ^a = *E_a*Δ^a + ∂_μ(π_a^μ Δ^a), by the product rule. Setting it equal to ∂_μ*K*^μ gives ∂_μ(π_a^μ Δ^a − *K*^μ) = −*E_a*Δ^a. For a solution the right side is zero; then *dQ*/*dt* = *c*∫ ∂₀*j*⁰ *d*³*x* = −*c*∫ ∂_k*j*^k *d*³*x*, a surface integral at infinity. □

**Corollary 9.6.11 (The conserved quantities of the group of the McGucken Principle).** Let ℒ have no explicit dependence on *x*. (i) Translations: Δ^a = *a*^ν∂_ν φ^a gives *K*^μ = *a*^μℒ and *j*^μ = *a*^ν*T*^μ_ν with *T*^μ_ν = π_a^μ∂_ν φ^a − δ^μ_νℒ; the four conserved quantities are the energy and the three components of the momentum. (ii) Lorentz transformations, for scalar fields and a Lagrangian that is a Lorentz scalar: Δ^a = ω^ν_ρ*x*^ρ∂_ν φ^a with ω_νρ = −ω_ρν gives *K*^μ = ω^μ_ρ*x*^ρℒ and *j*^μ = ω^ν_ρ*x*^ρ*T*^μ_ν; the six conserved quantities are the three components of the angular momentum and the three of the motion of the center of energy. For fields with components transforming by matrices *S*^ρσ the change Δ^a gains the term ½ω_ρσ(*S*^ρσ φ)^a and the current the corresponding term; the proof is the same. (iii) Phase: for a complex field with ℒ unchanged under φ → exp(iα)φ, Δφ = iφ, Δφ∗ = −iφ∗ and *K*^μ = 0 give *j*^μ = i(π^μ φ − (π^μ)∗φ∗), where π^μ = ∂ℒ/∂(∂_μ φ) and ℒ is real; the conserved quantity is the charge.

*Proof.* (i) ℒ(*x* + ε*a*) − ℒ(*x*) = ε*a*^ν∂_ν ℒ = ε∂_μ(*a*^μℒ) to first order. (ii) The change of a scalar ℒ is ω^ν_ρ*x*^ρ∂_ν ℒ = ∂_ν(ω^ν_ρ*x*^ρℒ), because ∂_ν(ω^ν_ρ*x*^ρ) = ω^ν_ν = 0. (iii) ℒ is unchanged, so *K*^μ = 0. Theorem 9.6.10 applies in each case. □

**The McGucken Principle *dx*₄/*dt* = i*c* reading.** The ten conserved quantities of (i) and (ii) belong to the ten parameters of the group ISO⁺(1,3), which Theorems 6.28 and 6.30 obtain from the McGucken Principle *dx*₄/*dt* = i*c*; the charge of (iii) belongs to the phase of Theorem 8.5.4, the phase of the advance along *x*₄. The Lagrangian of Theorem 8.7.9 is invariant under all of them. Noether’s theorem itself is the variational identity proved above and holds for every Lagrangian; the Principle supplies the group and the Lagrangian to which it is applied.

**Theorem 9.6.12 (The orbits and the stabilizers in the classification of Wigner [95]; Theorem 33 of [59]).** Let *U*(*a*, *A*) be a strongly continuous unitary representation of the group of pairs (*a*, *A*), *a* a translation and *A* in SL(2, ℂ), acting on events by *x* → Λ(*A*)*x* + *a* (Theorem 6.38), and let it be irreducible. Let *E* be the spectral measure of the translations, *U*(*a*, 1) = ∫ exp(−i*p*·*a*/ℏ) *dE*(*p*). (i) *U*(0, *A*)*E*(*S*)*U*(0, *A*)⁻¹ = *E*(Λ(*A*)*S*) for every Borel set *S*. (ii) *E* is concentrated on one orbit of the Lorentz group in the space of the *p*: the mass shell η(*p*, *p*) = −*m*²*c*², *p*⁰ > 0, with *m* > 0; its mirror image with *p*⁰ < 0; the forward or the backward null cone without the origin; the origin; or a hyperboloid η(*p*, *p*) = λ > 0. (iii) The subgroup of SL(2, ℂ) fixing the point (*mc*, 0, 0, 0) of the mass shell is SU(2). (iv) The subgroup fixing the null vector (1, 0, 0, 1) consists of the matrices with the rows (exp(iθ/2), *b*) and (0, exp(−iθ/2)), θ real, *b* complex; it covers twice the group of the rotations and translations of a plane.

*Proof.* (i) *U*(0, *A*)*U*(*a*, 1)*U*(0, *A*)⁻¹ = *U*(Λ*a*, 1) by the group law, and *p*·Λ*a* = (Λ⁻¹*p*)·*a*. So the measure *S* → *U*(0, *A*)*E*(*S*)*U*(0, *A*)⁻¹ and the measure *S* → *E*(Λ*S*) are both spectral measures of the unitary group *a* → *U*(Λ*a*, 1), and the spectral measure of a unitary representation of ℝ⁴ is unique (Stone’s theorem in four variables). (ii) If *S* is invariant under the Lorentz group, *E*(*S*) commutes with every *U*(0, *A*) by (i) and with every *U*(*a*, 1); its range is an invariant closed subspace, so *E*(*S*) is 0 or 1. Apply this to the sets η(*p*, *p*) ≤ λ: the projections increase from 0 to 1 with λ and are continuous from the right, so there is one value λ₀ with *E*(η(*p*, *p*) = λ₀) = 1. For λ₀ < 0 the two sheets *p*⁰ > 0 and *p*⁰ < 0 are invariant sets; for λ₀ = 0 the forward cone, the backward cone and the origin are. The group acts transitively on each set named: on a sheet with λ₀ < 0 a boost along **p** with the velocity *c*|**p**|/|*p*⁰| brings **p** to zero; on a null cone a rotation brings **p** to the third axis and the boost of Theorem 6.38(iii) along it multiplies *p* by exp(±*s*); for λ₀ > 0 a boost along **p** with the velocity *c* *p*⁰/|**p**| brings *p*⁰ to zero and a rotation brings **p** to a fixed direction. (iii) *M*(*p*) = *mc*·1 for *p* = (*mc*, 0, 0, 0), and *A* *M*(*p*) *A*† = *M*(*p*) is *AA*† = 1. (iv) *M*(*p*) for *p* = (1, 0, 0, 1) is the matrix with the single non-zero entry 2 in the first place of the diagonal. With the first column of *A* equal to (α, γ), *A* *M*(*p*) *A*† has the entries 2|α|², 2αγ∗, 2γα∗, 2|γ|², so the condition is γ = 0 and |α| = 1, and det *A* = 1 makes the second diagonal entry 1/α. The matrices with θ = 0 form a group isomorphic to the translations of a plane, on which the diagonal matrices act by *b* → exp(iθ)*b*, a rotation by θ; θ and θ + 2π give the same rotation. □

**The McGucken Principle *dx*₄/*dt* = i*c* reading.** For *m* > 0 the representative point (*mc*, 0, 0, 0) is the particle at rest in space, all of whose motion is the advance along *x*₄ at the rate *c* (Theorem 8.1.6); the stabilizer SU(2) is the group of Theorem 8.3.7 and its representations carry the spin. For *m* = 0 the representative is the photon, which is at rest in *x*₄ and moves on the McGucken Sphere; the rotations about its direction of motion carry the helicity. The remaining step of the classification, that every irreducible representation with the spectral measure on a given orbit is induced from an irreducible representation of the stabilizer, is the theorem of Wigner [95] and Mackey [96] and is used as an external theorem; it gives the labels mass and spin *s* = 0, ½, 1, … for *m* > 0 and the helicity for *m* = 0. The representations of mass *m* > 0 and spin 0 and ½ are constructed in this paper (Theorems 5.4 and 5.7).

**Theorem 9.6.13 (The total reflection is a rotation of the four-coordinate carrier; the CPT operator of the free field. Theorem 45 of [59]).** (i) For real θ let *R*(θ) be the rotation by θ in the (*x*₁, *x*₂)-plane together with the rotation by θ in the (*x*₃, *x*₄)-plane of the carrier of Definition 6.1. *R*(θ) preserves *x*₁² + *x*₂² + *x*₃² + *x*₄², *R*(0) = 1 and *R*(π) = −1. On the constraint surface *x*₄ = i*ct* the second rotation is *x*₃′ = *x*₃ cos θ − i*ct* sin θ, *t*′ = *t* cos θ − i(*x*₃/*c*) sin θ, which is real exactly when sin θ = 0. (ii) The map *x* → −*x* of the events does not belong to the group SO⁺(1,3) of Lemma 6.35. (iii) On the Fock space of Definition 5.1 let (ΘΨ)_n(*k*₁, …, *k_n*) = Ψ_n(*k*₁, …, *k_n*)∗. Then Θ is antiunitary, Θ² = 1, ΘΩ = Ω, Θ*V*(*a*, Λ)Θ = *V*(−*a*, Λ), and ΘΦ(*f*)Θ = Φ(*f_r*) with *f_r*(*x*) = *f*(−*x*)∗.

*Proof.* (i) A rotation of a coordinate plane preserves the sum of the two squares; at θ = π each of the two rotations is −1 on its plane. Substituting *x*₄ = i*ct* and *x*₄′ = i*ct*′ in *x*₃′ = *x*₃ cos θ − *x*₄ sin θ, *x*₄′ = *x*₃ sin θ + *x*₄ cos θ gives the two formulas. (ii) Its matrix has Λ⁰₀ = −1. (iii) Complex conjugation preserves the norms and conjugates the inner products. From Definition 5.1(e), Θ*A*(*g*)Θ = *A*(*g*∗) and Θ*A*†(*g*)Θ = *A*†(*g*∗). From Definition 5.1(f), (*Ef*)(*k*)∗ = ∫ *f*(*x*)∗ exp(i*k*·*x*) *d*⁴*x* = ∫ *f*(−*x*)∗ exp(−i*k*·*x*) *d*⁴*x* = (*Ef_r*)(*k*), and in the same way (*E*(*f*∗))∗ = *E*((*f_r*)∗). Therefore ΘΦ(*f*)Θ = *A*(*E*((*f_r*)∗)) + *A*†(*Ef_r*) = Φ(*f_r*). From Definition 5.1(c), Θ*V*₁(*a*, Λ)Θ multiplies φ(Λ⁻¹*k*) by exp(i*k*·*a*), which is *V*₁(−*a*, Λ). □

**The McGucken Principle *dx*₄/*dt* = i*c* reading.** Part (i) is where the McGucken Principle *dx*₄/*dt* = i*c* enters the CPT theorem. In real spacetime the total reflection cannot be reached from the identity (part (ii)). In the four coordinates (*x*₁, *x*₂, *x*₃, *x*₄), with *x*₄ the fourth dimension, it is the end of a path of rotations, and on the constraint surface *x*₄ = i*ct* that path is the path of complex Lorentz transformations on which the proof of Jost [97] rests. Part (iii) exhibits the antiunitary operator for the free neutral scalar field, for which charge conjugation is the identity. The CPT theorem for every field theory satisfying the Wightman properties (W1)–(W6) is the theorem of Jost [97], given in [10], and is used as an external theorem; Lemma 47 of [59] states the same condition.

**Proposition 9.6.14 (The form of a supersymmetry algebra over the spacetime of the Principle; Theorem 51 of [59]).** Let *Q*₁, *Q*₂ be operators on a Hilbert space with a common dense domain mapped into itself by them and by their adjoints, and let *U*(*A*) be a unitary representation of SL(2, ℂ) with *U*(*A*)*Q*_α*U*(*A*)⁻¹ = Σ_β (*A*⁻¹)_αβ*Q*_β. For a vector ψ of the domain let *N*(ψ) be the 2 × 2 matrix with the entries ⟨ψ, (*Q*_α*Q*_β† + *Q*_β†*Q*_α)ψ⟩. Then *N*(ψ) is Hermitian and positive semidefinite, so *N*(ψ) = *M*(*v*) for exactly one real four-vector *v* = *v*(ψ), with *v*⁰ ≥ |**v**|; and *N*(*U*(*A*)⁻¹ψ) = *A*⁻¹*N*(ψ)(*A*⁻¹)†, so *v* transforms as a four-vector under Λ(*A*⁻¹).

*Proof.* The adjoint of *Q*_α*Q*_β† + *Q*_β†*Q*_α is *Q*_β*Q*_α† + *Q*_α†*Q*_β, so *N* is Hermitian. For complex *z*₁, *z*₂ put *q* = Σ_α *z*_α*Q*_α; then Σ *z*_α*z*_β∗*N*_αβ(ψ) = ‖*q*†ψ‖² + ‖*q*ψ‖² ≥ 0. A Hermitian 2 × 2 matrix is *M*(*v*) for one *v* (Theorem 6.38); it is positive semidefinite exactly when its trace 2*v*⁰ and its determinant (*v*⁰)² − |**v**|² are non-negative. The transformation rule follows by applying the rule for *U*(*A*)*Q*_α*U*(*A*)⁻¹ to *Q*_α and its adjoint to *Q*_β†; then *M*(*v*) is replaced by *A*⁻¹*M*(*v*)(*A*⁻¹)† = *M*(Λ(*A*⁻¹)*v*). □

**Remark 9.6.15 (The exact standing of the gauge group, of supersymmetry and of the string dualities).** (a) Gauge group. Proved in this paper: the phase covariance of the McGucken Operator (Theorem 8.5.4) and, for *G* = U(1) or SU(*N*), the uniqueness of the Yang–Mills Lagrangian (Theorem 8.7.8). The choice of the group U(1) × SU(2) × SU(3) is taken from experiment in [59], whose Level 4 statement reads: “The Standard Model gauge group SU(3) × SU(2) × U(1) is the empirical content”. A derivation of the group itself is the subject of [82]; it has not been checked for this paper and no result here depends on it. (b) Supersymmetry. Proposition 9.6.14 shows that the anticommutators of spinor charges form a four-vector in the closed forward cone. That this four-vector is twice the energy–momentum, and that the resulting algebra is the only graded extension of the algebra of ISO⁺(1,3) under the hypotheses of the theorem, is the theorem of Haag, Łopuszański and Sohnius [98], used in [59] as an external theorem. Whether such charges exist in nature is open; Lemma 54 of [59] states that its Theorem 51 does not assert that supersymmetry is realised in nature. (c) String dualities. Theorem 55 of [59] is conditional on its Hypotheses A1–A22, and its Proposition 57′ A states that the carriers of the five dualities are postulated. No statement about the string dualities is proved in this paper, and none is used.

**9.6.4 The McGucken Principle *dx*₄/*dt* = i*c* Reading of Twistor Space and of the Kinematic Data of the Amplituhedron.** **McGucken Principle anchor.** The McGucken Principle *dx*₄/*dt* = i*c* states that the fourth dimension is expanding at the velocity of light in a spherically symmetric manner from every spacetime event, as a McGucken Sphere whose wavelength sets the action *h*. The McGucken Sphere of an event *x* is the set of the points reached from *x* at the velocity *c*; each of its points moves along a light ray through *x*. The light rays of spacetime are therefore the worldlines of the points of the McGucken Spheres. The next theorem shows that these rays are the points of a real hypersurface of the complex projective space ℂP³, the projective twistor space of Penrose [99], and that the McGucken Sphere of each event is a projective line in it.

**Theorem 9.6.16 (Twistor space from the McGucken Sphere; Theorem 13.1 of [60] in the form proved here).** For an event *x* let *M*(*x*) = *x*⁰ + *x*¹σ₁ + *x*²σ₂ + *x*³σ₃ be the Hermitian matrix of Theorem 6.38, with *x*⁰ = *ct*. For a point *Z* = (ω, π) of ℂ⁴, ω and π in ℂ², put *s*(*Z*) = π†ω + ω†π. (i) i*M*(*x*) = *x*₄ + i(*x*¹σ₁ + *x*²σ₂ + *x*³σ₃) with *x*₄ = i*ct*. (ii) For every event *x* and every π ≠ 0 the point (i*M*(*x*)π, π) satisfies *s* = 0. (iii) For π ≠ 0 let ρ be a non-zero vector with π†ρ = 0 and let *n*(π) be the vector with *M*(*n*(π)) = ρρ†. It is a null vector pointing to the future, and its direction depends only on the ray of π. (iv) Two pairs (*x*, π), (*y*, π′) give the same point of ℂP³ exactly when π′ is a multiple of π and *y* − *x* is a real multiple of *n*(π). (v) Every point (ω, π) with π ≠ 0 and *s* = 0 is (i*M*(*x*)π, π) for some event *x*. (vi) Consequently the map that sends the light ray through *x* with the direction *n*(π) to the point [i*M*(*x*)π, π] is a bijection of the set of the light rays of spacetime onto the set *PN* of the points of ℂP³ with *s* = 0 and π ≠ 0, a real hypersurface of dimension 5. The rays of the McGucken Sphere of the event *x* form the projective line *L_x* of the points [i*M*(*x*)π, π], π ≠ 0, a copy of ℂP¹, the sphere *S*². (vii) *L_x* and *L_y* have a common point exactly when *y* − *x* is null or zero, that is, when one of the two events lies on a McGucken Sphere of the other or the events coincide. (viii) The form *s* has two positive and two negative squares.

*Proof.* (i) i*x*⁰ = i*ct* = *x*₄. (ii) With ω = i*M*π, π†ω + ω†π = iπ†*M*π − iπ†*M*†π = 0, since *M*† = *M*. (iii) ρρ† is Hermitian with determinant 0 and positive trace, so by det *M*(*n*) = −η(*n*, *n*) the vector *n* is null with *n*⁰ > 0. The vectors orthogonal to π form a line, so ρ is determined up to a factor λ, which multiplies *n* by |λ|²; replacing π by a multiple leaves the line unchanged. (iv) The two points of ℂ⁴ are proportional exactly when π′ = λπ and i*M*(*y*)λπ = λ i*M*(*x*)π, that is, *M*(*y* − *x*)π = 0. A Hermitian 2 × 2 matrix *H* with *H*π = 0 maps ρ to a vector orthogonal to π, hence to a multiple of ρ, with a real factor; so *H* is a real multiple of ρρ†. (v) Put *w* = −iω. The condition *s* = 0 states that π†*w* is real. The matrix *H* = (*w*π† + π*w*†)/(π†π) − (π†*w*)ππ†/(π†π)² is Hermitian, and *H*π = *w* + π(*w*†π)/(π†π) − (π†*w*)π/(π†π) = *w*, because *w*†π = (π†*w*)∗ = π†*w*. With *H* = *M*(*x*), i*M*(*x*)π = ω. (vi) follows from (ii), (iv) and (v): the events *x* + *s* *n*(π), *s* real, are the light ray through *x* with the direction *n*(π), and by (iii) every future null direction occurs. The equation *s* = 0 is one real equation on the six real dimensions of ℂP³. (vii) A common point exists exactly when (*M*(*x*) − *M*(*y*))π = 0 for some π ≠ 0, that is, det *M*(*x* − *y*) = 0. (viii) With *u* = (ω + π)/√2 and *v* = (ω − π)/√2, *s* = *u*†*u* − *v*†*v*. □

**The McGucken Principle *dx*₄/*dt* = i*c* reading.** In Theorem 9.6.16 the complex structure of twistor space comes from the unit i of *x*₄ = i*ct*: by (i) the incidence matrix i*M*(*x*) is the fourth coordinate *x*₄ of the McGucken Principle *dx*₄/*dt* = i*c* together with i times the three spatial coordinates, and by (ii) it is this i that places the rays on the hypersurface *s* = 0. Twistor theory takes ℂP³ and the incidence relation ω = i*M*(*x*)π as its starting point (Penrose [99]). The Principle gives them a physical origin: the points of *PN* are the worldlines of the points of the expanding McGucken Spheres, and the line *L_x* is the McGucken Sphere of the event *x*. The group preserving the form *s* is SU(2,2), which acts on the rays through the conformal group of Theorem 6.41; that statement is used here as a remark and is not needed for (i)–(viii).

**Proposition 9.6.17 (The kinematic data of the amplituhedron are meeting points of lines of McGucken Spheres; Theorem 9.2 of [60] in the part proved here).** Let *p*₁, …, *p_n* be non-zero null vectors with *p*₁ + ⋯ + *p_n* = 0, the energy–momenta of *n* massless particles, each at rest in *x*₄. Choose an event *y*₁ and put *y*_(j+1) = *y_j* − *p_j*. Then *y*_(n+1) = *y*₁; consecutive events *y_j*, *y*_(j+1) are null separated; and the lines *L*_(y_j) and *L*_(y_(j+1)) of Theorem 9.6.16 meet in exactly one point *Z_j* of ℂP³, so that *L*_(y_j) is the line through *Z*_(j−1) and *Z_j* when these two points differ.

*Proof.* The sum of the *p_j* is zero, which gives *y*_(n+1) = *y*₁. *y_j* − *y*_(j+1) = *p_j* is null, so by Theorem 9.6.16(vii) the two lines meet; the common points are the [i*M*(*y_j*)π, π] with *M*(*p_j*)π = 0, and the Hermitian matrix *M*(*p_j*) has determinant 0 and is not zero, so its null space is one line. Two different points of a projective line determine it, and *Z*_(j−1), *Z_j* lie on *L*_(y_j). □

**The McGucken Principle *dx*₄/*dt* = i*c* reading.** The points *Z*₁, …, *Z_n* are the momentum twistors of Hodges [100]. The amplituhedron of Arkani-Hamed and Trnka [101] is defined from them: for real data, the *Z_j* with *k* further components adjoined, whose ordered maximal minors are positive, it is the set of the *Y* = *C*·*Z* with *C* a *k* × *n* matrix of non-negative ordered maximal minors, and its canonical form is identified in [101] with the tree amplitudes and the loop integrands of the planar maximally supersymmetric Yang–Mills theory. Proposition 9.6.17 proves the part of Theorem 9.2 of [60] that concerns the data: each *Z_j* is the common ray of two McGucken Spheres whose centers are separated by the momentum of one massless particle. The further statement of [60], that the canonical form is the sum over the chains of intersecting McGucken Spheres and that the positivity is the orientation +i*c*, is not proved in this paper; [60] states of the explicit equivalence that “the calculation has not been written”. It is recorded here as an open problem of the McGucken *dx*₄/*dt* = i*c* framework.

**9.6.5 The McGucken Principle *dx*₄/*dt* = i*c* Reading of the Unit i in the Formulas of Quantum Theory.** **McGucken Principle anchor.** The McGucken Principle *dx*₄/*dt* = i*c* states that the fourth dimension is expanding at the velocity of light in a spherically symmetric manner from every spacetime event, as a McGucken Sphere whose wavelength sets the action *h*. The Principle contains the unit i once, in the rate i*c*, where it marks the perpendicularity of *x*₄ to the three spatial dimensions. The companion paper [92] derives the appearances of i in quantum theory from this one occurrence in thirty-four theorems. The mechanism and the cases proved in this paper follow.

**Theorem 9.6.18 (The three substitutions that carry the unit i; Theorems 1, 6 and 9 and Lemma 14 of [92]).** (i) If *G*(**x**, *x*₄) is holomorphic in *x*₄ and *g*(*t*, **x**) = *G*(**x**, i*ct*), then (∂₄*G*)(**x**, i*ct*) = (i*c*)⁻¹∂ₜ*g* = −(i/*c*)∂ₜ*g*: on the constraint surface every derivative along *x*₄ is −i/*c* times the derivative in *t*. (ii) With τ = *x*₄/*c*, the relation *x*₄ = i*ct* is *t* = −iτ. (iii) For the action *S* = ∫ (½*m*(*d***x**/*dt*)² − *V*(**x**)) *dt* of §8.7 the substitution *t* = −iτ gives i*S* = −*S_E* with *S_E* = ∫ (½*m*(*d***x**/*d*τ)² + *V*(**x**)) *d*τ, so that exp(i*S*/ℏ) = exp(−*S_E*/ℏ).

*Proof.* (i) The chain rule gives ∂ₜ*g* = i*c*(∂₄*G*). (ii) *t* = *x*₄/(i*c*) = −i*x*₄/*c*. (iii) *dt* = −i *d*τ and *d***x**/*dt* = i *d***x**/*d*τ, so (*d***x**/*dt*)² = −(*d***x**/*d*τ)² and *S* = ∫ (−½*m*(*d***x**/*d*τ)² − *V*)(−i) *d*τ = i*S_E*. □

**Proposition 9.6.19 (The term +iε; Theorem 12 of [92]).** Let *H* be a self-adjoint operator on a finite-dimensional Hilbert space, *E* real and ε > 0. Then (i) exp(−i*H* *t*(1 − iε)/ℏ) = exp(−i*Ht*/ℏ) exp(−ε*Ht*/ℏ), and for *H* ≥ 0 and *t* ≥ 0 its norm is at most 1; (ii) the integral of exp(i(*E* − *H* + iε)*t*/ℏ) over *t* from 0 to ∞ equals iℏ(*E* − *H* + iε)⁻¹. The time *t*(1 − iε) is the time *t* displaced by ε*t* in the direction −i, the direction of τ in *t* = −iτ of Theorem 9.6.18(ii).

*Proof.* (i) The two exponents commute and add. (ii) On an eigenvector of *H* with the eigenvalue *h* the integrand is exp(i(*E* − *h*)*t*/ℏ) exp(−ε*t*/ℏ), and its integral is ℏ/(ε − i(*E* − *h*)) = iℏ/(*E* − *h* + iε). □

**The appearances of i and the place of each in this paper.** In the table the first column gives the formula, the second the theorem of this paper in which the unit i of the formula is obtained from the McGucken Principle *dx*₄/*dt* = i*c*, and the third the theorem of [92]. The twelve insertions are those listed in Theorem 16 of [92].
