**The McGucken Principle *dx*₄/*dt* = i*c* reading.** Every row of the table that displays the unit i and has an entry in the second column is an instance of Theorem 9.6.18: the unit i of the formula is the unit i of *x*₄ = i*ct*, carried by a derivative along *x*₄ (part (i)), by the substitution *t* = −iτ (part (ii)), or by both in the action (part (iii)). The Hawking temperature and the Born rule are listed because [92] counts them among its theorems. The count thirty-four in the title of [92] is the count of its theorems. Seventeen rows of the table are proved in this paper; the rows of the last line are results of [92] that this paper does not reproduce and does not use.

**9.6.6 The McGucken Principle *dx*₄/*dt* = i*c* Reading of the Spectral Triples of Connes.** **McGucken Principle anchor.** The McGucken Principle *dx*₄/*dt* = i*c* states that the fourth dimension is expanding at the velocity of light in a spherically symmetric manner from every spacetime event, as a McGucken Sphere whose wavelength sets the action *h*. A spectral triple (𝒜, ℋ, *D*) in the sense of Connes [22] is an algebra 𝒜 with an involution, represented by bounded operators on a Hilbert space ℋ, together with a self-adjoint operator *D* with compact resolvent such that [*D*, *a*] is bounded for every *a* in 𝒜. The four-coordinate carrier of the Principle, taken with *x*₄ real, carries the Dirac operator of such a triple, and on the constraint surface *x*₄ = i*ct* that operator becomes the Dirac operator of Theorem 8.5.3.

**Theorem 9.6.20 (The Dirac operator of the four-coordinate carrier, the distance of Connes, and the restriction to the constraint surface; Definition 4.1, Lemma 3.11 and Theorem B of [93] in the form proved here).** With the matrices of Theorem 8.5.3(i) put γ_E^j = iγ^j for *j* = 1, 2, 3 and γ_E⁴ = −γ⁰, and on the functions of the real variables (*x*₁, *x*₂, *x*₃, *x*₄) with values in ℂ⁴ let *D_E* = −iΣ_μ γ_E^μ∂_μ, the sum over μ = 1, …, 4. (i) The γ_E^μ are Hermitian, γ_E^μ γ_E^ν + γ_E^ν γ_E^μ = 2δ^μν, and *D_E*² = −(∂₁² + ∂₂² + ∂₃² + ∂₄²). (ii) For a smooth real function *f*, [*D_E*, *f*] is the multiplication by the matrix −iΣ_μ γ_E^μ∂_μ*f*, whose norm at each point is the Euclidean length |∇*f*| of the gradient. (iii) For two points *p*, *q* the supremum of |*f*(*p*) − *f*(*q*)| over the smooth real functions *f* with ‖[*D_E*, *f*]‖ ≤ 1 is the Euclidean distance |*p* − *q*|. (iv) *D_E* is symmetric on the smooth functions of compact support with the inner product ∫ ψ†φ *d*⁴*x*. (v) *D_E* = Σ_j γ^j∂_j + iγ⁰∂₄; for Ψ holomorphic in *x*₄ and ψ(*t*, **x**) = Ψ(**x**, i*ct*), (*D_E*Ψ)(**x**, i*ct*) = γ^μ∂_μ ψ, so that iℏ*D_E* + *mc* restricts to the Dirac operator iℏγ^μ∂_μ + *mc* of Theorem 8.5.3, and *D_E*² restricts to −□.

*Proof.* (i) γ⁰ is Hermitian with square 1 and each γ^j is anti-Hermitian with square −1 (Lemma 8.7.2), so the γ_E^μ are Hermitian with square 1; they anticommute in pairs because the γ^μ do. Then *D_E*² = −Σ_μ Σ_ν γ_E^μ γ_E^ν∂_μ∂_ν = −Σ_μ ∂_μ², the mixed terms cancelling in pairs. (ii) By the product rule *D_E*(*f*ψ) = *f* *D_E*ψ − i(Σ_μ γ_E^μ∂_μ*f*)ψ. For real numbers *v*_μ the matrix Σ_μ *v*_μ γ_E^μ is Hermitian with the square |*v*|², so its norm is |*v*|. (iii) If |∇*f*| ≤ 1 everywhere, then |*f*(*p*) − *f*(*q*)| ≤ |*p* − *q*| by the mean value theorem along the segment from *q* to *p*. The linear function *f*(*x*) = *u*·(*x* − *q*), with *u* the unit vector along *p* − *q*, has |∇*f*| = 1 and *f*(*p*) − *f*(*q*) = |*p* − *q*|. (iv) Integration by parts moves ∂_μ to the other factor with a change of sign, the conjugation changes the sign of −i, and the γ_E^μ are Hermitian. (v) −iγ_E^j = γ^j and −iγ_E⁴ = iγ⁰. By Theorem 9.6.18(i), (∂₄Ψ)(**x**, i*ct*) = −(i/*c*)∂ₜψ, so the last term becomes γ⁰*c*⁻¹∂ₜψ = γ⁰∂₀ψ. The statement on the square is Theorem 8.5.2. □

**The McGucken Principle *dx*₄/*dt* = i*c* reading.** Theorem 9.6.20 is the relation of the McGucken Principle *dx*₄/*dt* = i*c* to the geometry of Connes that is proved in this paper. The triple of Connes takes the algebra, the Hilbert space and the operator as three data (Proposition 9.6.6(iii)); the Principle yields the operator *D_E* from the four coordinates and the Dirac operator of physics from *D_E* by the one substitution *x*₄ = i*ct*, and the distance of Connes computed from *D_E* is the distance of the four-coordinate form of Lemma 6.25. Three limits of the theorem are stated. The resolvent of *D_E* on the whole of ℝ⁴ is not compact, so the axioms of [22] apply after restriction to a compact manifold such as a torus. On the constraint surface the form is Lorentzian and iℏγ^μ∂_μ is not symmetric for the positive inner product of part (iv), so the axioms, which are Riemannian, apply to the real section and reach the Lorentzian operator through part (v). The verification of the seven axioms, the reconstruction theorem of Connes [102] and the correspondence of the spectral action with the Lagrangian of Theorem 8.7.9 are results of [93] that rest on external theorems of spectral geometry; [93] grades them accordingly and takes the finite algebra of the gauge group as empirical input. They are not used in this paper. The McGucken Operator *D_M* and the operator *D_E* are different operators (Theorem 9.6.2(b)): the McGucken Operator *D_M* is the transport along the expansion of *x*₄, of the first order in *t*, and *D_E* is a square root of minus the four-coordinate Laplacian.

**Summary of §9.6 by item.** The McGucken Principle *dx*₄/*dt* = i*c* yields: the commutator [*D_M*, *x*₄] = i*c* that separates the McGucken Operator from the operators of spacetime (Theorems 9.6.2, 9.6.3); the rate i*c* as the one constant rate with the Lorentzian interval and the wave operator (Theorem 9.6.5); the six functors of the source-pair and its place as the least element of the order of generation (Theorems 9.6.8, 9.6.9); the group and the Lagrangian to which Noether’s theorem is applied (Theorem 9.6.10, Corollary 9.6.11); the orbits and stabilizers of the classification of Wigner, with the particle at rest advancing along *x*₄ and the photon at rest in *x*₄ (Theorem 9.6.12); the path of rotations of the four coordinates that ends in the total reflection of the CPT theorem (Theorem 9.6.13); the light rays of the McGucken Spheres as the points of twistor space and the momentum twistors as their meeting points (Theorem 9.6.16, Proposition 9.6.17); the unit i of seventeen formulas of quantum theory (Theorem 9.6.18 and the table); and the Dirac operator of Connes on the four-coordinate carrier with its restriction to the Dirac operator of physics (Theorem 9.6.20). External theorems used with attribution: Wigner [95] and Mackey [96], Jost [97], Haag, Łopuszański and Sohnius [98], Arkani-Hamed and Trnka [101], Connes [22, 102].

## 10. Why the McGucken Principle Completes the Hilbert Programme

The McGucken Principle asserts that the fourth dimension *x*₄ is expanding at the velocity of light: *dx*₄/*dt* = i*c*, with *x*₄ = i*ct*, as a spherically symmetric McGucken Sphere whose wavelength sets the action *h* and whose volume defines a nonlocality.

The completion of Hilbert’s Sixth Problem requires three things. Each prior programme supplied at most two. The McGucken Principle supplies all three.

**(R1) A minimal axiomatic base.** Hilbert demanded “a small number of axioms.” The McGucken Principle is one equation:  
*dx*₄/*dt* = i*c*. The constraint *C_M* is its integrated form. The McGucken Operator *D_M* is its chain-rule tangent vector. The McGucken Sphere *S_M* is its solution surface. These are not independent axioms; they are the law in three different presentations. By any reasonable count, the framework’s axiomatic input is *one* primitive law—the floor of what Hilbert could have asked.

**(R2) Deductive completeness over physical phenomena.** Hilbert demanded that the axioms include “as large a class as possible of physical phenomena.” The McGucken Principle generates in parallel theorem-chains: special relativity (the master equation *u^μu_μ* = −*c*² and its consequences), general relativity (the Einstein field equations via the invariance lemma and Lovelock closure), all of canonical non-relativistic and relativistic quantum mechanics (Schrödinger, Klein–Gordon, Dirac, the canonical commutator, Born rule, uncertainty, Tsirelson 2√2, path integral, gauge structure, Pauli exclusion, antimatter), the canonical solutions of GR (Schwarzschild, redshift, light bending, perihelion, gravitational waves, FLRW, black-hole thermodynamics through the generalized second law), and a quantitative cosmology with zero dark parameters. This is the full canonical content of twentieth-century theoretical physics, presented as theorems of one law.

**(R3) Generation of the arena itself.** This is the requirement no prior programme satisfied. Hilbert’s own 1915 action lived on a spacetime he could not generate. Kolmogorov’s probability axioms presupposed the sample space. von Neumann’s Hilbert space was primitive. Wightman’s Minkowski space was an input. Haag–Kastler’s local nets were indexed by Minkowski regions taken as given. Causal sets, twistors, and spectral triples each take 2–3 independent structures as primitive. The McGucken Principle generates the Lorentzian arena (by constraint), the complex Hilbert space (by completion of amplitudes), the operator algebra (by co-generation with the McGucken Operator *D_M*), and the gauge structure (by covariantization), all from one law. The arena is no longer exogenous to the dynamics.

**What is solved.** The Sixth Problem, as Hilbert posed it, asked: by what minimal axiomatization can probability and mechanics be founded, and the wider physics built upon them? The McGucken answer is: by one law, *dx*₄/*dt* = i*c*, from which the complex Hilbert space (and hence the Born rule, hence physical probability), the Lorentzian manifold (and hence mechanics, both classical and relativistic), and the operator hierarchy (and hence quantum dynamics) are all derivable. Probability is no longer a separate axiomatic edifice (Kolmogorov) imported into physics by hand; it is a theorem about the spherical measure on the McGucken Sphere *S_M*. Mechanics is no longer a separate axiomatization (Newton, then Mach–Hertz–Boltzmann–Volkmann, then Einstein); it is the geodesic structure of the McGucken hypersurface. The unification Hilbert sought is achieved by derivation from one law, not merely by conjunction.

## 11. Gödel’s Shadow and Why It Does Not Fall on the Sixth Problem

The McGucken Principle asserts that the fourth dimension *x*₄ is expanding at the velocity of light: *dx*₄/*dt* = i*c*, with *x*₄ = i*ct*, as a spherically symmetric McGucken Sphere whose wavelength sets the action *h* and whose volume defines a nonlocality.

It is sometimes said—loosely, but persistently—that Gödel’s incompleteness theorems ended Hilbert’s programme tout court, and that no axiomatization of physics in Hilbert’s sense is therefore possible. This view is mistaken, but it is mistaken in an instructive way that deserves a section to itself. To dismiss it casually is to leave the deepest objection to the present paper unanswered; to engage it carefully is to show that the structure of the McGucken Principle is precisely what evades the obstruction.

## (a) The Königsberg Conference, September 1930

On 8 September 1930, the Society of German Scientists and Physicians convened in Königsberg, Hilbert’s birthplace. Hilbert delivered his retirement address *Naturerkennen und Logik* (Knowledge of Nature and Logic) by radio that evening, closing with the words now inscribed on his memorial stone in Göttingen: *Wir müssen wissen, wir werden wissen.* The day before, on 7 September, in a round-table discussion at the Conference on the Epistemology of the Exact Sciences held in Königsberg alongside that meeting, a 24-year-old logician from Vienna named Kurt Gödel had quietly announced a result whose written form, submitted to the *Monatshefte für Mathematik und Physik* on 17 November, would become known as the First Incompleteness Theorem. The Second Incompleteness Theorem appeared in the same paper, published in 1931. Hilbert in his radio address did not mention Gödel’s result; whether he had absorbed its full implications by 8 September is debated. What is not debated is that the theorems, once understood, dealt a blow to one specific branch of Hilbert’s programme: the branch that sought to prove the consistency of arithmetic by finitistic means.

## (b) What Gödel actually proved

Stated precisely, Gödel’s theorems are statements about formal systems satisfying certain conditions. Let *T* be a formal system that is (i) recursively axiomatizable, (ii) consistent, and (iii) sufficiently strong to formalize Peano arithmetic (or any system in which primitive recursion can be carried out and the Hilbert–Bernays derivability conditions hold). Then:

**First Incompleteness Theorem.** There exists a sentence *G_T* in the language of *T* such that neither *G_T* nor its negation is provable in *T*. Equivalently: *T* is incomplete.

**Second Incompleteness Theorem.** If *T* is consistent and satisfies the derivability conditions, then *T* cannot prove its own consistency: the statement *Con*(*T*), formalized within *T*, is not a theorem of *T*.

Gödel himself, in a note added to his 1931 paper in August 1963 ([46], p. 195), wrote that Turing’s work supplied a “precise and unquestionably adequate definition of the notion of formal system.” The Stanford Encyclopedia of Philosophy [30] reports that this definition is what convinced Gödel that the incompleteness theorems hold in full generality and refute the Hilbert programme.

## (c) Which programme? The crucial distinction

The Hilbert programme Gödel’s note refers to is the *metamathematical* programme—Hilbert’s *Beweistheorie*—whose target was a finitistic consistency proof of arithmetic and analysis. This is the programme of Hilbert 1922, 1925, and the Hilbert–Bernays *Grundlagen der Mathematik* volumes (1934, 1939). It is the programme of Hilbert’s *Second Problem* on the 1900 list (“the compatibility of the arithmetical axioms”), extended over the next three decades into a systematic attempt to ground all of mathematics in finitary metamathematics. Gödel’s theorems strike at this branch: they show that finitary metamathematics cannot establish the consistency of any system that encodes its own arithmetic.

The Sixth Problem is a different programme. It does not ask for a consistency proof of physics within physics by finitary means. It asks for an axiomatization of physics in the manner of Euclid for geometry: a small number of axioms (ideally one), from which the structure of physical theory follows by deductive reasoning. This is Hilbert’s axiomatic demand, repeated explicitly in 1918: the *Fachwerk von Begriffen* grounded in a deepening sequence of axiom layers, ultimately reducible to a layer whose internal consistency rests on the consistency of arithmetic.

|                         | Metamathematical programme                           | Sixth Problem                                                                        |
|-------------------------|------------------------------------------------------|--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------|
| Target                  | Finitary consistency proof of arithmetic / analysis  | Axiomatization of physics in the manner of geometry                                  |
| Object of consistency   | The formal system itself                             | The physical theory built on the axioms                                              |
| Means of proof          | Finitary metamathematics (Hilbert’s *Beweistheorie*) | Ordinary mathematical deduction                                                      |
| Hilbert’s 1900 problem  | Problem 2 (compatibility of arithmetic axioms)       | Problem 6 (axioms of physics)                                                        |
| Hilbert’s key text      | *Über das Unendliche* (1925); Hilbert–Bernays (1934) | Paris lecture (1900); *Axiomatic Thought* (1918); *Die Grundlagen der Physik* (1915) |
| Status after Gödel 1931 | Refuted in its original finitary form                | Untouched; the axiomatic demand is not a self-referential consistency claim          |

*Table 11.1. The two Hilbert programmes. Gödel’s theorems strike the metamathematical column; the axiomatic column — the home of the Sixth Problem — is unaffected.*

The conflation of the two programmes is the source of the “Gödel ended Hilbert’s programme” slogan. The refutation, properly scoped, applies only to the metamathematical branch; the axiomatic branch—the branch the Sixth Problem belongs to—stands intact.

## (d) Why the McGucken Principle is not subject to incompleteness

Even granting that the Sixth Problem is axiomatic rather than metamathematical, one might worry: any sufficiently rich theory *T* derived from the McGucken Principle will satisfy the hypotheses of Gödel’s First Theorem, and will therefore contain a sentence *G_T* independent of *T*. Does this not show that the McGucken derivation cannot be “complete”?

The answer is: the completeness Gödel’s theorem denies is not the completeness Hilbert’s Sixth demands. Three observations make this precise.

**(i) Different completeness.** Gödel’s First Theorem says that no recursively axiomatizable consistent theory containing Peano arithmetic can prove every arithmetic truth. The McGucken Principle does not promise a proof of every arithmetic truth; it promises a *derivation of the laws of physics* from a single source-law. These are different claims. Lorentzian signature, the canonical commutator, the Born rule, the Einstein field equations, and the second law of thermodynamics are all consequences of *dx*₄/*dt* = i*c*; whether the theory underlying them can decide every Gödel-sentence in its arithmetic fragment is a separate question, and one Hilbert never asked of physics.

**(ii) The principle is not a formal system.** *dx*₄/*dt* = i*c* is a single first-order linear differential equation in one unknown function on **R**⁴ over **C**. Its consistency reduces to the consistency of complex arithmetic and the differentiability of *x*₄(*t*) = i*ct* + const., both of which are facts about a fixed model (**C**, +, ·), not statements within a formal system that encodes its own metatheory. By Hilbert’s 1918 reduction (“for the fields of physical knowledge it is sufficient to reduce internal consistency to the consistency of the arithmetical axioms”), the consistency demand is met as soon as arithmetic is consistent—a presupposition no working physicist or mathematician questions in practice.

**(iii) Single-axiom systems are unaffected by independence incompleteness.** Gödel’s First Theorem is fundamentally a theorem about the impossibility of capturing all arithmetic truth within any one recursively enumerable axiom system. The McGucken Principle is one axiom; the question of whether some other axiom is settled directly (anything not implied by it is independent), and the question of whether *physical* consequences—the canonical commutator, Lorentzian signature, the Born rule, the second law—are derivable is answered constructively in §8 and §9 of this paper. The Gödel sentence is not a physical statement; it is a self-referential arithmetic statement, and physics has never demanded that its axioms decide such statements.

These three observations are the technical content of the standard philosophical position that Gödel’s theorems do not refute Hilbert’s axiomatic programme. The McGucken Principle exemplifies, in its strongest form, what such a programme can look like: *one* equation, with consistency manifest, generating the laws of physics by deduction. The completeness demand of the Sixth Problem is met not by deciding every arithmetic sentence (which is impossible) but by exhibiting a single source-law from which the propositions of physics—those Hilbert listed in 1900 and in 1918—follow.

**The McGucken Principle *dx*₄/*dt* = i*c* Reading of Gödel’s Condition G3: The Formal System *F_M* = (ℒ_M, ⊢_M).**

**McGucken Principle anchor.** The McGucken Principle *dx*₄/*dt* = i*c* states that the fourth dimension is expanding at the velocity of light in a spherically symmetric manner from every spacetime event, as a McGucken Sphere whose wavelength sets the action *h*. For Gödel’s theorem the Principle predicts the following: the formal system in which *dx*₄/*dt* = i*c* is written is a system of the real and complex fields, smooth functions and linear differential operators with one proper axiom; a system of that kind has a consistent, complete, decidable extension, and it therefore lies outside the hypothesis of Gödel’s First Incompleteness Theorem. Definitions 2–4, Proposition 24 and Corollary 25 of the companion paper [75] give the formal system and the statement; Lemma 11.5 and Proposition 11.6 below supply the decidable extension.

**Definition 11.1 (The McGucken formal language ℒ_M; Definition 2 of [75]).** The McGucken formal language ℒ_M is the multi-sorted first-order language with the following sorts, function symbols, predicate symbols, and constants. *Sorts:* *R*, the real numbers; *C*, the complex numbers; *P*, the four-coordinate carrier *E*₄, with elements (*x*₁, *x*₂, *x*₃, *x*₄) where *x*₁, *x*₂, *x*₃ ∈ *R* and *x*₄ ∈ *C*; *F*, the sort of smooth functions *f*: *P* → *C*; *O*, the sort of linear differential operators on *F*; [*K*, the sort of smooth curves *γ*: *R* → *P*, used by axiom 5 of Definition 11.3]. *Constants:* 0 and 1 in *R*; 0, 1 and i in *C*; the parameters *c*, ℏ ∈ *R* with *c* > 0 and ℏ > 0. *Function symbols:* the field operations of *R* and of *C* with their associated subtraction and division (the latter where defined); the inclusion of *R* in *C*; the coordinate projections π_j: *P* → *R* for *j* = 1, 2, 3, and π₄: *P* → *C*; the partial derivatives ∂ₜ, ∂₄, ∂_j: *F* → *F* for *j* = 1, 2, 3; operator composition *O*² → *O* and operator-on-function evaluation *O* × *F* → *F*; scalar-operator multiplication *C* × *O* → *O* and operator addition *O*² → *O*; [evaluation of a curve *K* × *R* → *P* and the derivative *d*/*dt* of a curve’s coordinate function]. *Predicate symbols:* equality on each sort, the ordering < on *R*, and complex conjugation as a unary function *C* → *C*. *Logical apparatus:* standard first-order logic with equality.

**Definition 11.2 (What ℒ_M does not contain; Definition 3 of [75]).** The following are explicitly absent from ℒ_M: (1) a sort *N* for the natural numbers and a successor function symbol *S*: *N* → *N*; (2) a primitive recursion operator, that is, a function symbol or term-builder taking a function *g*: *N*^k → *N* and a function *h*: *N*^(k+2) → *N* to a function defined by primitive recursion on them; (3) a Gödel-numbering function, that is, a term-builder from the formulas of ℒ_M to *N* definable within ℒ_M; (4) a provability predicate, that is, a predicate symbol asserting “the formula with this Gödel number is provable.”

**Definition 11.3 (The McGucken proof system ⊢_M; Definition 4 of [75]).** The McGucken proof system ⊢_M is standard first-order natural deduction over ℒ_M, augmented with: (1) the field axioms for *R* as an ordered field; (2) the field axioms for *C* as a field with conjugation, with i · i = −1; (3) the axioms of smooth-function calculus on *F*: linearity of ∂, the Leibniz rule, and Schwarz’s theorem on equality of mixed partials; (4) the axioms of linear differential operators: linearity, composition, and evaluation; (5) the Axiom stated as a sentence in ℒ_M: for all *t* ∈ *R*, *d*π₄(*γ*(*t*))/*dt* = i · *c*, where *γ*: *R* → *P* is the integral curve symbol with *γ*(0) at the origin. The McGucken formal system is the pair *F_M* = (ℒ_M, ⊢_M). The list (1)–(5) is the complete list of proper axioms of *F_M*.

**Condition G3 (Theorem 23 of [75], after Gödel [13] and Raatikainen [76]).** Gödel’s First Incompleteness Theorem concerns a consistent formal system *F* in which a sufficient part of elementary arithmetic can be carried out, and concludes that some sentence of the language of *F* is neither provable nor refutable in *F*. [75] states the arithmetic hypothesis as condition G3, in three parts. **(G3.1)** *F* represents the primitive recursive functions on ℕ: for every primitive recursive function *f* of *k* arguments there is a formula *A_f* of *F* in *k* + 1 free variables such that *F* proves *A_f* at the numerals of (*n*₁, …, *n*_k, *m*) whenever *f*(*n*₁, …, *n*_k) = *m*, and *F* proves the negation of *A_f* at those numerals whenever *f*(*n*₁, …, *n*_k) ≠ *m*. **(G3.2)** *F* supports Gödel-numbering of its own formulas: there is an injective computable function from the formulas of *F* to ℕ, and the predicate “*x* is the Gödel number of a formula of *F*” is representable in *F*. **(G3.3)** *F* defines in its formal language a provability predicate asserting “the formula with Gödel number *x* is provable in *F*.” The theorem requires all three parts.

**Proposition 11.4 (G3 fails for the McGucken formal system; Proposition 24 of [75]).** The McGucken formal system *F_M* = (ℒ_M, ⊢_M), specified in Definitions 11.1, 11.2, and 11.3, does not satisfy condition G3.

*Proof (Proposition 24 of [75]).* We verify each part of G3 by reference to the explicit specification of ℒ_M in Definition 11.1 and the explicit list of omissions in Definition 11.2.

*(G3.1) Representation of primitive recursive functions.* For the representation to be available through primitive symbols, ℒ_M must contain (i) a sort *N* for the natural numbers and (ii) function symbols for the building blocks of primitive recursive functions: the zero function, the successor function *S*: *N* → *N*, the projection functions, function composition, and a primitive-recursion operator taking *g* and *h* to the unique *f* defined by *f*(*x*, 0) = *g*(*x*) and *f*(*x*, *S*(*y*)) = *h*(*x*, *y*, *f*(*x*, *y*)). By Definition 11.2 (1)–(2), ℒ_M contains no sort *N* for the natural numbers as a primitive type, no successor function symbol *S*, and no primitive-recursion operator. The natural numbers appear in the framework only as substructures of derived objects (the indexing set of the Fock spaces produced by operation (O12); the Gaussian integers ℤ[i] as a subring of the complexified coordinate ring). These substructures are not equipped with a successor function in the language ℒ_M. That no formula of ℒ_M does the work of the absent symbols is Lemma 11.5 with Proposition 11.6. Therefore (G3.1) fails.

*(G3.2) Gödel-numbering of formulas.* Gödel-numbering requires an injective computable function from the formulas of ℒ_M to ℕ definable within ℒ_M, together with the apparatus to express “*x* is the Gödel number of a formula of ℒ_M” as a formula of ℒ_M. By Definition 11.2 (3), ℒ_M contains no such function. The standard Gödel-numbering construction uses prime factorization: the number of a formula is the product over its symbols of the *j*-th prime raised to the code of the *j*-th symbol. To define this function within ℒ_M requires (i) a representation of the natural numbers, which fails by (G3.1); (ii) the prime-counting and prime-power-extraction primitive recursive functions, which require primitive recursion, also failing by (G3.1). Therefore (G3.2) fails.

*(G3.3) Provability predicate.* A provability predicate in the language ℒ_M would be a formula that holds of the numeral of *n* in *F_M* if and only if there is a proof in ⊢_M of the formula with Gödel number *n*. Its construction requires a Gödel-numbering scheme (G3.2), which fails; representability of the primitive recursive relation “*y* is a proof of the formula with number *x*” (G3.1), which fails; and a provability predicate symbol or its definability within ℒ_M, absent by Definition 11.2 (4). Therefore (G3.3) fails. All three parts of G3 fail for *F_M*. □

The proof of (G3.1) uses the following lemma. Its role is this: condition (G3.1) quantifies over formulas, and a language can define arithmetic by formulas while having no primitive symbol for it. The language of Zermelo–Fraenkel set theory has the single symbol ∈, no sort *N* and no successor symbol, and ZFC satisfies G3. Lemma 11.5 gives a criterion on the theory itself, and Proposition 11.6 verifies the criterion for *F_M*.

**Lemma 11.5 (A theory with a primitive recursive consistent extension does not satisfy (G3.1)).** Let *T* be a theory in a first-order language with finitely many symbols, and let num(0), num(1), num(2), … be closed terms of that language, with *n* ↦ num(*n*) primitive recursive. Suppose *T* has a consistent extension *T*′ in the same language whose set of theorems is primitive recursive. Then there is a primitive recursive set *D* ⊂ ℕ that no formula represents in *T*: there is no formula *A*(*x*) such that *T* ⊢ *A*(num(*n*)) for every *n* ∈ *D* and *T* ⊢ ¬*A*(num(*n*)) for every *n* ∉ *D*. Consequently the characteristic function of *D* is a primitive recursive function that *T* does not represent, and (G3.1) fails for *T*.

*Proof.* The argument is carried out in ordinary arithmetic, outside *T*. Fix a primitive recursive numbering of the formulas of the language having the one free variable *x*; the map sending (*e*, *n*) to the number of the sentence obtained by substituting num(*n*) in the formula numbered *e* is primitive recursive. Let *D* be the set of all *e* such that *e* numbers a formula *B*(*x*) and *T*′ does not prove *B*(num(*e*)). Because the set of theorems of *T*′ is primitive recursive, *D* is primitive recursive. Suppose a formula *A*(*x*) represents *D* in *T*, and let *a* be its number. Since *T*′ extends *T*, *T*′ ⊢ *A*(num(*n*)) for every *n* ∈ *D* and *T*′ ⊢ ¬*A*(num(*n*)) for every *n* ∉ *D*. If *a* ∈ *D*, then *T*′ ⊢ *A*(num(*a*)), while the definition of *D* gives that *T*′ does not prove *A*(num(*a*)). If *a* ∉ *D*, then *T*′ ⊢ ¬*A*(num(*a*)), while the definition of *D*, with *a* the number of *A*, gives *T*′ ⊢ *A*(num(*a*)), so *T*′ is inconsistent. Both cases contradict the hypotheses, so no formula represents *D* in *T*. If a formula *A_f*(*x*, *y*) represented the characteristic function *f* of *D* in the sense of (G3.1), the formula *A_f*(*x*, num(1)) would represent *D*. □

**Proposition 11.6 (The McGucken formal system has a consistent, complete extension with a primitive recursive set of theorems).** Let the numerals of *F_M* be the closed terms 1 + 1 + ⋯ + 1 of sort *R*. Define the constant-function model of ℒ_M as follows: *R* is the real field ℝ with its ordering; *C* is ℂ = ℝ² with i = (0, 1) and complex conjugation; *P* is ℝ³ × ℂ with its coordinate projections; *c* = 1 and ℏ = 1; *F* is the set of constant functions *P* → ℂ, on which every partial derivative ∂ₜ, ∂₄, ∂_j is the zero map; *O* is the set of operators *f* ↦ *λf* with *λ* ∈ ℂ, with composition, addition and scalar multiplication given by the product and sum in ℂ; *K* is the set of curves *t* ↦ *a* + *bt* with *a*, *b* ∈ ℝ³ × ℂ, the derivative of a coordinate function being the corresponding coordinate of *b*; and *γ* is the curve *t* ↦ (0, 0, 0, i*ct*). Then the constant-function model satisfies axioms (1)–(5) of Definition 11.3, and the set *T*′ of all sentences of ℒ_M true in it is a consistent, complete extension of *F_M* whose set of theorems is primitive recursive.

*Proof.* Axioms (1) and (2) hold because ℝ is an ordered field and ℂ is a field with conjugation in which i · i = −1. Axiom (3) holds because the zero map is linear, satisfies the Leibniz rule 0 = 0 · *g* + *f* · 0, and commutes with itself. Axiom (4) holds because multiplication by a complex number is linear and composes by the product in ℂ. Axiom (5) holds because the fourth coordinate of *γ*(*t*) is i*ct* and its derivative is i*c*. The set *T*′ is consistent because it has a model, complete because every sentence is true or false in that model, and it contains the proper axioms of *F_M*. Every sort of the model is a finite power of ℝ and every function and predicate symbol is interpreted by polynomial equations and inequalities over (ℝ; 0, 1, +, ·, <). A sentence *σ* of ℒ_M therefore translates, by a primitive recursive map, into a sentence *σ*° of the language of ordered fields such that *σ* is true in the constant-function model exactly when *σ*° is true in ℝ. Truth of sentences of ordered fields in ℝ is decidable by Tarski’s theorem [77], and the cylindrical algebraic decomposition of Collins [78] decides it within a time bound doubly exponential in the number of variables, which is a primitive recursive bound. Hence the set of theorems of *T*′ is primitive recursive. □

**Corollary 11.7 (Gödel’s theorem does not apply; Corollary 25 of [75]).** Gödel’s First Incompleteness Theorem does not apply to the McGucken formal system. The system is not foreclosed from deductive completeness by Gödel’s argument.

*Proof.* Gödel’s theorem requires G3 as an antecedent. By Proposition 11.4, with Lemma 11.5 applied to *T* = *F_M* and the extension *T*′ of Proposition 11.6, G3 fails. The theorem’s hypothesis is not satisfied, so its conclusion is not forced. □

**Definition 11.8 (Deductive completeness; Definition 26 of [75]).** A formal system *F* is deductively complete if for every well-formed sentence *φ* in the language of *F*, either *F* ⊢ *φ* or *F* ⊢ ¬*φ*.

**Definition 11.9 (Generative completeness; Definition 27 of [75]).** A formal generative system with a primitive and a list of closure operations is generatively complete over a class of structures if every structure in the class is constructible from the primitive by a finite sequence of the closure operations.

**The McGucken Principle *dx*₄/*dt* = i*c* reading of Gödel’s theorem.** Gödel’s theorem is an external mathematical result about formal systems that satisfy G3. The formal system *F_M* carries one proper axiom specific to the theory, the McGucken Principle *dx*₄/*dt* = i*c* as axiom (5) of Definition 11.3, over the ordered field of reals, the complex field and the calculus of linear differential operators. By Proposition 11.6 that system extends to the complete theory of a model definable in real arithmetic, which Tarski’s theorem decides; by Lemma 11.5 it does not represent the primitive recursive functions; by Corollary 11.7 Gödel’s theorem places no restriction on it. The two completeness notions of Definitions 11.8 and 11.9 concern different objects. Gödel’s theorem addresses deductive completeness over a language that encodes arithmetic. The derivations of §8 and §9 are statements of generative completeness: the Lorentzian manifold, the complex Hilbert space, the bundles, the Clifford algebra and Dirac spinors, the Fock space, the operator algebra, the classical phase space, the spectral triple and the principal bundles with connection are each constructed from (McGucken Space *M_G*, McGucken Operator *D_M*) by a finite sequence of the closure operations (O1)–(O14) of Definition 7.1 (Theorem 11.11 below, with the operations defined in Definition 11.10; it is Theorem 18 of [75], Restricted Generative Completeness).

**The McGucken Principle *dx*₄/*dt* = i*c* Reading of Generative Completeness: the Closure Operations Defined and Theorem 11.11 Proved.** **McGucken Principle anchor.** The McGucken Principle *dx*₄/*dt* = i*c* states that the fourth dimension is expanding at the velocity of light in a spherically symmetric manner from every spacetime event, as a McGucken Sphere whose wavelength sets the action *h*. The Principle yields the source-pair of §6, and the arenas of physics are built from the source-pair by constructions of a few fixed kinds. Definition 11.10 states each kind of construction, and Theorem 11.11 exhibits, for each of nine arenas, the finite sequence of constructions that produces it, with the theorem of this paper that proves each step.

**Definition 11.10 (The closure operations (O1)–(O14) of Definition 7.1; Definition 9 of [75]).** Each operation takes mathematical structures, with the chosen data named, to a mathematical structure. (O1) Constraint imposition: from a set *X* with structure and a condition on its elements, the subset on which the condition holds, with the structure restricted to it. (O2) Quotient projection: from *X* and an equivalence relation on *X*, the set of the classes. (O3) Slicing along the flow parameter: from a structure carrying the coordinate *t* and a number *t*₀, the subset *t* = *t*₀. (O4) Bundle formation: from a manifold *M* and a fibre *F*, a vector space or a group, the product *M* × *F* with its projection onto *M*. (O5) Section formation: from a bundle *M* × *F*, the vector space of the smooth maps of *M* into *F*. (O6) Cotangent lift: from a manifold *Q* with coordinates *q_j*, the space of the pairs (*q*, *p*) with the form Σ_j *dq_j* ∧ *dp_j*. (O7) Complexification: from a real vector space *V*, the complex vector space *V* ⊗ ℂ. (O8) Representation formation: from a group *G*, a vector space *V* and a homomorphism ρ of *G* into the invertible linear maps of *V*, the representation (*V*, ρ). (O9) Quantization: from the McGucken Operator *D_M* the operator iℏ*D_M*; from the coordinate *x_j* the operator *q̂_j* of multiplication by *x_j*; from the momentum the operator *p̂_j* = −iℏ∂_j. (O10) Completion: from a vector space with a norm, its completion. (O11) Tensor product: from two Hilbert spaces, the completion of their algebraic tensor product. (O12) Fock construction: from a Hilbert space ℋ, the direct sum over *n* ≥ 0 of the symmetric, or of the antisymmetric, *n*-fold tensor powers of ℋ. (O13) Operator-algebra construction: from a Hilbert space ℋ, the algebra *B*(ℋ) of its bounded operators. (O14) Connection formation: from a product bundle *M* × *G* with *G* a group of Definition 8.7.5 and from functions *A*_μ on *M* with values in the Lie algebra of *G*, the connection of Definition 8.7.5. The chosen data are the condition in (O1), the relation in (O2), the fibre in (O4), the homomorphism in (O8), the norm in (O10), the statistics in (O12) and the functions *A*_μ in (O14).

**Theorem 11.11 (Restricted generative completeness; Theorem 18 of [75]).** With the McGucken source-pair (McGucken Space *M_G*, McGucken Operator *D_M*) as the primitive and the operations of Definition 11.10, each of the following nine structures is obtained by the finite sequence of operations stated, and has the properties stated in the result cited. The system is therefore generatively complete, in the sense of Definition 11.9, over the class of these nine structures. (C1) The Lorentzian manifold: (O1) with the condition Φ_M = 0 on the carrier with the parameter *t* gives the constraint surface, the set of the events (*t*, **x**), and the form *dx*₁² + *dx*₂² + *dx*₃² + *dx*₄² restricted to it is *d***x**² − *c*²*dt*² (Lemma 6.25). (C2) The Hilbert space of states: (O3) applied to (C1) gives the slice ℝ³; (O4) with the fibre ℂ and (O5) give the complex functions on it; (O1) with the condition of rapid decrease gives the Schwartz functions; (O10) with the norm of Theorem 8.4.2 gives *L*²(ℝ³) (Theorem 8.4.3). (C3) The vector bundles: (O4) applied to (C1) with the fibre ℂⁿ, and (O5) for their sections. (C4) The Clifford algebra and the Dirac spinors: (O13) applied to ℂ⁴ gives the algebra of the 4 × 4 complex matrices, which the matrices γ^μ of Theorem 8.5.3(i), obeying the relations with the form η of (C1), generate (Lemma 8.7.2(i)); (O4) with the fibre ℂ⁴ and (O5) give the spinor fields, on which the Dirac operator of Theorem 8.5.3 acts. (C5) The Fock space: (O1) with the conditions η(*k*, *k*) = −μ² and *k*⁰ > 0 on the wave vectors gives the mass shell; (O4), (O5) and (O10) with the norm of Definition 5.1(b) give the one-particle space ℋ₁; (O11) and (O12) give the Fock space of Definition 5.1(d), which carries the free fields of Theorems 5.4 and 5.7. (C6) The operator algebra: (O13) applied to (C2) or to (C5); (O9) gives the operators *q̂_j*, *p̂_j* with [*q̂_j*, *p̂_k*] = iℏδ_jk (Theorem 8.3.5) and the operator iℏ*D_M* = *Ĥ* − i*c* *p̂*₄ (Theorem 8.5.1). (C7) The classical phase space: (O3) applied to (C1) gives *Q* = ℝ³, and (O6) gives the space of the (*q*, *p*) with the form of Theorem 6.34. (C8) The data of a spectral triple: (O1) with the condition that *x*₄ be real on the carrier gives ℝ⁴ with the form *dx*₁² + *dx*₂² + *dx*₃² + *dx*₄²; (O2) by the translations of a lattice *L*ℤ⁴, *L* > 0, gives a torus; (O4) with the fibre ℂ⁴, (O5) and (O10) give the Hilbert space of the square-integrable spinor fields on the torus; the operator *D_E* of Theorem 9.6.20 acts on it with the properties (i), (ii) and (iv) of that theorem, and its resolvent is compact; the distance of part (iii) is that of ℝ⁴ before the quotient. (C9) The principal bundles with connection: (O4) applied to (C1) with the fibre *G*, and (O14) with functions *A*_μ; the field strength and its covariance are Definition 8.7.5 and Lemma 8.7.6.

*Proof.* In each item every step is the application of one operation of Definition 11.10 to structures already obtained, with the chosen data stated, and the number of steps is at most five. The properties are those proved in the results cited. Two points need an argument. In (C4), the sixteen products of the γ^μ are a basis of the 4 × 4 complex matrices by Lemma 8.7.2(i), so the algebra generated by the γ^μ is the whole algebra given by (O13). In (C8), *D_E* has constant coefficients, so it commutes with the translations and acts on the functions on the torus. For *k* in (2π/*L*)ℤ⁴ and a column *u*, *D_E*(*u* exp(i*k*·*x*)) = (Σ_μ *k*_μ γ_E^μ)*u* exp(i*k*·*x*), and the Hermitian matrix Σ_μ *k*_μ γ_E^μ has the square |*k*|² by Theorem 9.6.20(i), hence the eigenvalues ±|*k*|. The functions *u* exp(i*k*·*x*) span a dense subspace of the Hilbert space, by the theory of Fourier series. So the Hilbert space has an orthonormal basis of eigenvectors of *D_E* with the eigenvalues ±|*k*|; only finitely many *k* of the lattice have |*k*| below a given bound, so the eigenvalues of (*D_E* − i)⁻¹, which are the numbers (±|*k*| − i)⁻¹, tend to zero, and an operator that is diagonal in an orthonormal basis with eigenvalues tending to zero is compact. □

**The McGucken Principle *dx*₄/*dt* = i*c* reading of Theorem 11.11.** Each of the nine structures begins from the McGucken Principle *dx*₄/*dt* = i*c*: (C1) is the constraint surface *x*₄ = i*ct* itself; (C2), (C5) and (C6) are built on the amplitudes that advance with *x*₄ and on the operators that the McGucken Operator *D_M* yields under (O9); (C3), (C4) and (C9) are bundles over (C1); (C7) is the cotangent lift of a slice of (C1); and (C8) is built on the real section of the same four coordinates, whose Dirac operator restricts on *x*₄ = i*ct* to the Dirac operator of physics (Theorem 9.6.20(v)). The inputs beyond the source-pair are the chosen data: the number *n*, the mass *m* in μ = *mc*/ℏ, the statistics, the group *G* with the functions *A*_μ, and the length *L*. The theorem is called restricted because the class is the nine structures listed; Theorem 9.6.9(ii) states the same fact as the position of the source-pair in the order of generation, and a statement about every structure of mathematical physics is not made.

## (e) Hilbert’s own response: 1930 and the persistence of “wir werden wissen”

Hilbert delivered his Königsberg radio address on the same day Gödel’s result was being absorbed in the same city. Whether Hilbert had fully grasped the technical content of the First Theorem on 8 September 1930 is debated by historians; what is certain is that he did not retract the Sixth Problem, and he continued, with Bernays, to develop the programme through the 1934 and 1939 volumes of *Grundlagen der Mathematik*. Zach’s survey [48] records that, beginning with Gentzen’s consistency proof of 1936 [55], which used transfinite induction up to ε₀, relativized Hilbert programmes extending strict finitism became central to proof theory, so that the programme remained, in revised form, viable for many of its goals. The Sixth Problem was never one of those goals Hilbert renounced, because it was never a self-referential consistency claim.

Gödel himself, late in life, kept a small chamber in his Princeton cosmology adjacent to questions of physical foundations. His 1949 rotating-universe solution to the Einstein field equations — Gödel’s universe, with its closed timelike curves — was offered as a contribution to the foundations of physics, not as evidence that physics is foundationally impossible. The received “Gödel refuted Hilbert” slogan would have puzzled both men. The Hilbert programme Gödel refuted is not the Sixth Problem. The Sixth Problem remained open until, this paper argues, it was closed by exhibiting a single source-law for physics.

**Verdict.** The shadow of Gödel’s incompleteness theorems falls on the metamathematical wing of Hilbert’s programme, not on the axiomatization of physics. The McGucken Principle supplies the axiomatization in the most extreme form compatible with Hilbert’s 1900 and 1918 demands: one axiom, with consistency reducible to the consistency of arithmetic, generating the propositions of physics by deduction. Gödel’s obstacle is real, but it lies elsewhere; it does not block the completion of the Sixth.

## 12. Objections and Replies

The McGucken Principle asserts that the fourth dimension *x*₄ is expanding at the velocity of light: *dx*₄/*dt* = i*c*, with *x*₄ = i*ct*, as a spherically symmetric McGucken Sphere whose wavelength sets the action *h* and whose volume defines a nonlocality.

**Objection 1: The McGucken Principle is merely Minkowski’s *x*₄ = i*ct* reread.** If the framework produces no observable distinct from standard SR + GR + QM, then it is a foundational reframing rather than new physics.

*Reply.* The objection conflates Minkowski’s static algebraic identity with the McGucken Principle, which is a different and deeper statement. Minkowski wrote *x*₄ = i*ct* as a notational convenience for converting a Lorentzian signature to Euclidean form, and explicitly disavowed any physical reading of the fourth coordinate. The McGucken Principle *dx*₄/*dt* = i*c* asserts something Minkowski never claimed and that no textbook of special relativity contains: that the fourth dimension is *physically, dynamically, geometrically expanding* at the velocity of light, in a spherically symmetric manner from every spacetime event. Minkowski’s *x*₄ = i*ct* is the integrated algebraic shadow that one obtains from the McGucken Principle by differentiation in reverse, with the dynamical and geometric content stripped away; it is not the McGucken Axiom *dx*₄/*dt* = i*c* itself. The deeper physics, and the entire derivation lattice of §8–10, lives in *dx*₄/*dt* = i*c*, not in *x*₄ = i*ct*.

Even granting the weaker claim that the framework is a foundational reframing rather than new phenomenology, that is precisely what the Sixth Problem demanded. Hilbert did not ask for new physics; he asked for an axiomatic foundation of existing physics. A completion of the Sixth Problem that derives all of canonical physics from one law — regardless of whether it predicts new phenomena — is exactly what Hilbert demanded. That said, McGucken’s papers do offer falsifiable predictions: a no-graviton claim (gravity is purely spatial curvature with no quantum mediator, testable via Bose-Marletto-Vedral entanglement-via-gravity experiments), a Compton-coupling diffusion *Dₓ* = ε²*c*²Ω/(2γ²) with mass-independent zero-temperature residual, and a zero-dark-parameter cosmology (*w*₀ = −0.983 claimed within 1% of DESI-BAO; baryonic Tully-Fisher slope = 4; specific SPARC RAR fits). These are testable and refutable, and their resolution will determine whether the framework is *also* new physics, or only foundational.

**Objection 2: Lovelock 1971 does the work in deriving the Einstein field equations.** If the field equations follow from any 4D metric theory satisfying diffeomorphism invariance, then the McGucken Principle is not specifically responsible for them.

*Reply.* Lovelock’s theorem is a uniqueness lemma; it does not generate its own preconditions. Lovelock requires (a) a 4-dimensional manifold, (b) a Lorentzian metric, (c) diffeomorphism invariance, and (d) a divergence-free symmetric (0,2)-tensor built from the metric and its first two derivatives. The McGucken Principle supplies (a) by the four-coordinate structure of *x*₄; (b) by integration of *dx*₄/*dt* = i*c* producing the Lorentzian signature on *Σ_M*; (c) by the McGucken-Invariance Lemma, which gives Diff_McG(*M_G*) off shell and the full diffeomorphism group on shell (§9.2); and (d) by the on-shell conservation ∇_μ*T*^μν = 0 of Theorem 9, Step 4 (§9.2). Lovelock then closes the deduction by uniquely fixing the form of the field equations. This is no more “outsourcing” than Einstein invoking the Bianchi identities or Hilbert invoking the calculus of variations: a uniqueness lemma is an admissible step of the deduction, not a competing source.

**Objection 3: Why exactly four dimensions, why Lorentzian, why i, why c?** Each of these appears in the McGucken Axiom *dx*₄/*dt* = i*c* as a chosen primitive feature.

*Reply.* The answer is that the McGucken Axiom *dx*₄/*dt* = i*c* is one equation. The symbols inside it—*x*₄, the differential *d*/*dt*, the constants i and *c*—are not independent axioms, in the same sense that *F* = *ma* is one equation rather than four primitives {*F*, *m*, *a*, =}. The relevant comparison is to rival foundational programmes: causal sets require two independent primitives (a set and an order relation); spectral triples require three (algebra, Hilbert space, Dirac operator); Wightman QFT requires five or more (Lorentzian manifold, Hilbert space, operator-valued distributions, locality, Poincaré covariance). The McGucken Principle requires one equation, and the dimensional count, signature, complex unit, and rate are not tunable choices—they are the variables of the law. To pose “why four” or “why i” as objections to the framework is to ask for a derivation of the law itself, which is not what an axiomatization provides. Hilbert did not derive his geometric axioms; he stated them and showed what followed. The McGucken Principle does the same.

**Objection 4: The Lorentzian signature is imported via i, not derived.** The substitution *x*₄ = i*ct* is what produces the negative sign in *ds*²; without it one obtains a 4D Riemannian (Euclidean) manifold with no light cones.

*Reply.* Correct, and this is part of the law’s content. The Lorentzian signature is precisely what i in *dx*₄/*dt* = i*c* establishes. To demand a derivation of i from outside the law is to demand the law be derived; this is the same as objecting that Newton did not derive his second law from a deeper principle. The Lorentzian signature is a consequence of the specific form of *dx*₄/*dt* = i*c*, just as the existence of light cones is. To say “i imports Lorentzian signature” is to describe how the principle works, not to identify a flaw.

**Objection 5: Gödel’s incompleteness theorems ended Hilbert’s programme; nothing can complete it.**

**Reply.** §11 of this paper is devoted to a full treatment of this objection—the Königsberg conference of September 1930, the precise scope of Gödel’s First and Second Theorems, the distinction between Hilbert’s metamathematical programme (*Beweistheorie*, refuted by Gödel) and the axiomatization of physics demanded by the Sixth Problem (untouched by Gödel), and three technical reasons the McGucken Principle is not subject to incompleteness. We summarize: Gödel’s theorems show that no sufficiently strong formal system can prove its own consistency by means internal to itself. They do not show that physical theories cannot be founded on a minimal axiomatic base. The Sixth Problem’s demand is axiomatic, not metamathematical, and the McGucken Principle—a single first-order linear differential equation whose consistency reduces to the consistency of complex arithmetic—meets that demand in the most extreme form Hilbert’s 1900 and 1918 framings allow.

## 13. Conclusion

The McGucken Principle asserts that the fourth dimension *x*₄ is expanding at the velocity of light: *dx*₄/*dt* = i*c*, with *x*₄ = i*ct*, as a spherically symmetric McGucken Sphere whose wavelength sets the action *h* and whose volume defines a nonlocality.

Hilbert posed the Sixth Problem at the moment when classical physics seemed complete and quantum mechanics did not yet exist. Over the subsequent century, the problem was attacked by the greatest mathematical physicists of the era—by Hilbert himself, by Kolmogorov, by von Neumann, by Wightman, by Haag and Kastler, by Glimm and Jaffe—and each attack produced a regional axiomatization without supplying the source-law. The arena of physics remained exogenous to the axioms governing physics within it.

The McGucken Principle *dx*₄/*dt* = i*c* closes this gap. It is one equation. From it follow, by integration and differentiation, the McGucken hypersurface (which is Minkowski spacetime), the McGucken Operator (which is the source of every operator in physics from Schrödinger to Dirac to gauge), the complex Hilbert space (by completion of amplitudes whose complex character traces to the i in the law), and the operator algebra acting on it. Special relativity is the kinematics of the law’s geometry; general relativity is its variational extension; quantum mechanics is its complex-amplitude wave structure; the canonical commutator and Born rule are theorems of the same source.

This is what Hilbert asked for. “By a small number of axioms” — one. “As large a class as possible of physical phenomena” — the canonical content of twentieth-century physics, both classical and quantum, both mechanical and probabilistic. “In the same manner” as geometry — with the source-law generating the arena, as Euclid’s postulates generate the plane.

On 8 September 1930 in Königsberg, Hilbert closed his radio address with the words now inscribed on his memorial stone: *Wir müssen wissen — wir werden wissen.* We must know — we will know. The Sixth Problem was the most audacious of his twenty-three: that physics could be founded on axioms in the manner of geometry, that probability and mechanics could be reduced to a small primitive base, that the arena and the dynamics could be unified in a single deductive structure. For 126 years, the problem remained open. It is open no longer.

## Appendix A. Derivation of the Lorentz Transformation from *dx*₄/*dt* = i*c*

The McGucken Principle asserts that the fourth dimension *x*₄ is expanding at the velocity of light: *dx*₄/*dt* = i*c*, with *x*₄ = i*ct*, as a spherically symmetric McGucken Sphere whose wavelength sets the action *h* and whose volume defines a nonlocality.

To make the claim that special relativity descends from the McGucken Principle as a theorem rather than as a postulate-set, we exhibit the derivation in full. The argument occupies four short steps; no assumption beyond *dx*₄/*dt* = i*c*, holding with spherical symmetry from every event, and its integrated form *x*₄ = i*ct* is used.

**Step 1 (Four-velocity normalization).** A worldline parameterized by proper time *τ* has four-velocity *u^μ* = *dx^μ*/*dτ*. From *x*₄ = i*ct* and the McGucken Principle:

*u*₄ = *dx*₄/*dτ* = i*c* *dt*/*dτ* = i*cγ*.

Here *γ* denotes *dt*/*dτ*. Proper time is defined from the line element of §9.1 by *c*²*dτ*² = −(*dx*₄² + *d***x**²) = *c*²*dt*² − *d***x**², so that (*dτ*/*dt*)² = 1 − *v*²/*c*² and *γ* = *dt*/*dτ* = (1 − *v*²/*c*²)^−1/2: the factor *γ* is derived from *dx*₄/*dt* = i*c*. Squaring and using the quadratic form *dx*₄² + *d***x**² on the (*x*₄, **x**) chart:

*u_μu^μ* = *u*₄² + |**u**|² = −*c*²*γ*² + *γ*²|**v**|² = −*c*²,

the standard relativistic normalization, in agreement with the value of *γ* just derived.

**Step 2 (Time dilation).** The temporal component of *u^μ* is *dt*/*dτ* = *γ*. Inverting, *dτ* = *dt*/*γ*; for a moving clock, less proper time elapses per unit of coordinate time, exactly the time-dilation formula.

**Step 3 (Lorentz boost).** Let *S* and *S*′ be two inertial frames with relative velocity *v* along *x*¹. Each frame measures the same *dx*₄/*dt* = i*c* because the McGucken Axiom *dx*₄/*dt* = i*c* is a statement about the fourth dimension *x*₄, holding from every event and in every inertial frame. Demanding linearity, isotropy of *x*², *x*³, and the constancy of *dx*₄² + *d***x**² uniquely selects the rotation in the (*x*¹, *x*₄) plane:

*x*′₄ = *x*₄ cos*θ* − *x*¹ sin*θ*, *x*′¹ = *x*¹ cos*θ* + *x*₄ sin*θ*.

Substituting *x*₄ = i*ct* and fixing *θ* by the motion of the origin of *S*′ (*x*′¹ = 0 along *x*¹ = *vt* gives tan*θ* = −*x*¹/*x*₄ = −*vt*/(i*ct*) = i*v*/*c*, so that cos*θ* = *γ* and sin*θ* = i*γv*/*c*) reproduces, after using cos(i*φ*) = cosh*φ* and sin(i*φ*) = i sinh*φ*:

*t*′ = *γ*(*t* − *vx*¹/*c*²), *x*′¹ = *γ*(*x*¹ − *vt*),

the standard Lorentz boost. Length contraction *L* = *L*₀/*γ* follows by setting *dt*′ = 0; mass-energy equivalence *E*₀ = *mc*² follows from the temporal component of four-momentum, *p*₄ = *mu*₄ = i*γmc* = i*E*/*c*, by reading off the modulus.

**Step 4 (Constancy of *c*).** The invariant statement of the McGucken Axiom *dx*₄/*dt* = i*c* is that *x*₄ advances at rate i*c*: any inertial frame agrees on this rate, since it is the law itself. A light signal satisfies *dx*₄² + *d***x**² = 0, that is |*d***x**/*dt*| = *c*, and the boost of Step 3 preserves *dx*₄² + *d***x**², so |*d***x**′/*dt*′| = *c* in every inertial frame. The constancy of the speed of light, taken as Einstein’s second postulate of 1905, is in the McGucken framework a corollary of *dx*₄/*dt* = i*c* being a frame-independent statement.

**Remark.** Einstein’s 1905 derivation took the constancy of *c* as a postulate and derived the Lorentz transformation by demanding linearity. The McGucken Principle moves the postulate one level deeper: *dx*₄/*dt* = i*c* is the law, and the constancy of *c* is what the law states once *x*₄ is read off in the (*x*₄, **x**) chart. □

## Appendix B. Predictions and Falsifiability

The McGucken Principle asserts that the fourth dimension *x*₄ is expanding at the velocity of light: *dx*₄/*dt* = i*c*, with *x*₄ = i*ct*, as a spherically symmetric McGucken Sphere whose wavelength sets the action *h* and whose volume defines a nonlocality.

A complete axiomatization of physics, in Hilbert’s sense, must do two things: it must reproduce the canonical content of the existing theory (reproduction), and it must commit to definite statements wherever the existing theory is silent or in dispute (prediction). Hilbert’s first requirement is met by the derivation lattice of §8 and the explicit calculations of §9. We list here the principal commitments at which the McGucken *dx*₄/*dt* = i*c* framework departs from, or sharpens, the standard twentieth-century picture, so that the framework can be tested.

**(P1) No-graviton.** Because gravity in the framework is a theorem about the geodesic structure of the McGucken hypersurface and is not a separate quantum field, no spin-2 mediator is required. Bose–Marletto–Vedral entanglement-via-gravity experiments, if they detect coherent quantum entanglement induced purely by gravitational interaction, would falsify this commitment. As of 2026 these experiments remain technically out of reach, so the prediction is open.

**(P2) Compton-coupling diffusion.** The McGucken Operator yields a diffusion coefficient *Dₓ* = *ε*²*c*²Ω/(2*γ*²) with mass-independent zero-temperature residual, *γ* being the environmental damping rate; the total is *D*_total = *k*_*B* *T*/(*mγ*) + *ε*²*c*²Ω/(2*γ*²), the first term the thermal Einstein diffusion and the second temperature-independent ([62], Theorem 14). This distinguishes it from standard Brownian motion in cold-atom interferometry. Tests at the µK–nK regime over multi-second baselines are within reach of current atom-fountain technology.

**(P3) Zero-dark-parameter cosmology.** The framework asserts that the apparent late-time acceleration of the universe is the *x*₄-expansion read off in the Lorentzian chart, with *w*₀ = −1 + Ω_m(0)/(6π) ≈ −0.983 ([62], Theorem 18, item 5) and no separate dark-energy or dark-matter sectors. McGucken’s papers claim agreement within 1% of DESI BAO 2024–2025 results, a slope-4 baryonic Tully–Fisher relation, and specific SPARC RAR fits without free parameters. Future DESI, Euclid, and Vera Rubin Observatory data will sharpen this test directly.

**(P4) Pauli exclusion as 4π-periodicity.** The framework derives the spin-statistics connection from the 4π-periodicity of the spinor representation in Cl(1,3) acting on *x*₄-orientation. Pauli exclusion is therefore not an independent postulate of quantum mechanics but a theorem about how matter orients itself with respect to *x*₄.

**(P5) Tsirelson bound 2√2.** The framework derives the Tsirelson bound as the maximum violation of CHSH inequalities allowed by the spherical structure of the McGucken Sphere; experimental tightening of the Tsirelson bound below 2√2 (e.g. via three-party loophole-free Bell tests) would falsify the framework.

None of (P1)–(P5) is required to validate the completion of Hilbert’s Sixth Problem, which is a claim about the axiomatic foundations of *existing* physics. They are listed to emphasize that the framework is a physical theory in the Popperian sense, not merely an axiomatic re-presentation of known content.
